# Tiruviruttam

## 1. Poy ninra

*poy ninṟa jñānamum pollā oḻukkum aḻukkudambuminninṟa nīrmai iniyām uṟāmai  uyir aḷippānenninṟa yōniyumāyp piṟandāy imaiyōr talaivameynninṟu kēttaruḷāy adiyēn seyyum viṇṇappamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

uyir – all the created entities; aḷippān – to protect; enninṟa – many different types; yōniyumāy – births; piṟandāy – Oh one who incarnated!; imaiyōr – for nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam); talaivā – Oh one who is the Lord!; poynninṟa – not being truthful; jñānamum – knowledge; pollā oḻukkum – bad conduct; aḻukku – deficient; udambum – body; inninṟa – of this nature; nīrmai – quality; ini – from now onvards; yām – we; uṟāmai – such that we do not attain; adiyēn – I, who am your servitor; seyyum – make; mey – reality; viṇṇappam – appeal; ninṟu – remaining firm; kēttaruḷāy – please listen to, mercifully

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who incarnated in many births in order to protect all the creatures! Oh the lord of nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam)! We should be without these births which are the cause for our sorrow and which are with knowledge which is the opposite of truth (we should be without faulty knowledge), with wrongful deeds, physical forms which are full of impurity etc. You should stand firmly and hear my appeal to you from me, your servitor, for the sake of this.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) emperumān shows to āḻvār the greatness of his svarūpam (basic nature), rūpam (divine form), guṇam (auspicious qualities) as well as his wealth of nitya vibhūti (śrīvaikuṇṭam) and leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm). āḻvār realises the knowledge, activities and the way the nityātmās (permanent dwellers of nitya vibhūti) and muktātmās (those who have reached nitya vibhūti after being liberated from leelā vibhūti) live there. He also thinks about his situation in samsāram which is the exact opposite of those who dwell in nitya vibhūti and appeals to emperumān to sever his connection with prakruti (samsāram) which is a hurdle for his enjoying emperumān. āḻvār has been granted knowledge and devotion by emperumān without any cause. When such is the situation, why should he, like ordinary people, appeal to emperumān to sever his hurdles, just as it is said in bhagavat gītā ślōkam 7-29 “jarā maraṇa mōkśāya mamāśritya yadantiyē” (those who wish to rid themselves of old age and death should attain me)? Just as a beloved would remove all the dirt from her person before uniting with her beloved and just as a prince, who is to be coronated, gets rid of his diseases, āḻvār too wishes severance from samsāram as a prelude to enjoying emperumān. Is this desire arising out of his love towards emperumān or out of fear for samsāram? It is for both reasons, says the commentator, just like maharājar (sugrīva) had love towards perumāḷ (śrī rāma) and fear towards vāli. Why is the āḻvār projecting emperumān’s followers in the forefront in many pāsurams in this prabandam? When a creeper plant starts growing, it needs the support of poles before it can be taken on to a pandal (broad network of sticks). Followers of emperumān serve a similar purpose. Just as it is said in (a) tiruviruttam 1 “imaiyōr talaivā” (Oh the lord of nityasūris), (b) tiruvāimoḻi 3-7-10 “adiyār adiyār tam adiyār” ((we are the servitors of) the followers of the followers of emperumān’s followers), (3) tiruvāimoḻi 2-3-10 “adiyārgal kuḻāngaḷai udan kūduvadu enṟukolō” (when will I unite with the groups of emperumān’s followers?) (d) tiruvāimoḻi 10-9-11 “andamil pērinbattu adiyarōdu irundamai” (being united with nityasūris who are present in the great hall of śrīvaikuṇṭam which has unending happiness), they serve the role of ācāryan (teacher who guides) when attaining emperumān’s divine feet. They are also required to console when there is ūdal (love-quarrel) with emperumān. Since both in the first pāsuram “inninṟa nīrmai” (the nature of sorrow arising out of repeated births) and in the final pāsuram “azundār piṟappām” (will not get drowned in the reason for being born in samsāram), the meaning is similar, it could be taken that the entire prabandam is centred around āḻvār requesting emperumān to rid him \[āḻvār\] of his hurdles which prevent him from enjoying emperumān.*

**poy ninṟa jñānamum** – this term refers to the knowledge which considers that which is not self as the self, viz. considers body which is not the soul, as the soul. This is called as *anyatā jñānam* (inaccurate or erroneous knowledge); it is erroneous knowledge because it is about *prakruti* (primordial matter which is the raw material for all creations) and *prākrutam* (those which are derived from *prakruti*); instead of knowledge about true meanings, it is knowledge about untruths.  śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 6-5-87 says “*tatjñānam ajñānamantōnyaduktam*” (that by which we know emperumān is knowledge; all the rest are only false knowledge). How can we say that whatever is seen by the eyes in *prakruti* is false knowledge? It is not the way that *baudda* follower says as false or *māyāvādi* (one who is a follower of *advaitam*) says as *tuccam* (non-existing). The opinion is that whatever is seen in *prakruti* is not like *ātmā* (soul). Just as it is said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 2-13-100 “*yattu kālāntarēṇāpi nānyasamjñāmupaitivai pariṇāmādi sambhūtān tadvastu nrupadaccakim*” (that for which this world which keeps changing is present and that which does not change at all, though in the grip of time) *ātmā* does not change unlike the other objects. This remains the same all the time. Since the other objects keep changing constantly, āḻvār refers to them as false knowledge. ***ninṟa*** refers to the fact that these objects have been existing in this samsāram from time immemorial. ***jñānamum*** – the state where one who sees *prakruti*, which is insentient, as something great, leading to the state where self becomes equally insentient. The erroneous knowledge which results from such a state, including mistaking body for *ātmā*.

**pollā oḻukkum** – *bhagavat kainkaryam* (carrying out service to emperumān) will result from having truthful knowledge. When knowledge is erroneous, activities also become erroneous, and would result in not carrying out service to emperumān. The basic nature of ātmā is to carry out service to emperumān, just as lakśmaṇa had told śrī rāma in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 31-25 “*aham sarvam kariyśyāmi*” (I will carry out all services to you) and 31-22 “*kuruśva mām anucaraṇam*” (you should order me to carry out service to you). Instead of being like this, one thinks “I am doing” and indulges in all activities other than carrying out service to emperumān.

**aḻukkudambum** – the insentient *prakruti* which is the cause for the above two mentioned characteristics. We can also consider this term to refer to the physical form which is misconstrued as ātmā (*ahankāram* or ego) and those which are considered as one’s own, even though they are not (*mamakāram* or possessiveness). Even mahābhāratam could not state clearly the difference between *prakruti* and *puruśa* (body and ātmā) just as one cannot clearly separate hay from jackfruit with which it is stuck. However, āḻvār, since he has been showered with faultless knowledge and devotion by emperumān, differentiates between *prakruti* and *puruśa* through these three terms:

1. *poy ninṟa jñānam* – by terming prakruti as false, the correctness of ātmā has been established.
2. *pollā oḻukku* – due to the connection with physical form, one thinks “I am doing”; however, carrying out service to emperumān is always the basic nature of ātmā.
3. *aḻukkudambu* – if there is no connection between ātmā and the physical form (which is the effect of prakruti), ātmā is full of true knowledge.

**inninṟa nīrmai** – the nature which has got destabilised like this. ammangi ammāḷ would say that āḻvār is explaining his position as well that of the people of the world through these words just like a person, who has been hurt badly, would display his blood stained clothes. svāmy āḷavandār would say – just like the sages of daṇḍakāraṇyam who had been hurt by demons would show their scars to śrī rāma, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇa āraṇya kāṇḍam ślōkam 6-16 “*ēhi paśya śarīrāṇi*” (look at our forms), āḻvār is showing his ātmā which has been tortured by samsāram. *poy ninṟa jñānam . . . .* – just like a person who has injuries on his physical form would display them, āḻvār says “look at this injury; look at this hurt”, displaying the injuries inflicted on his ātmā.

**inninṟa nīrmai** – this is remaining in a situation when one does not listen to ācārya’s instructions and neither does he listen to the instructions of śāstras (sacred texts) which are authentic.

**ini** – The time when one thought “I am capable of taking care of myself; why should I depend on emperumān?” has gone. The situation now is “I do not want anything other than what has been given by you”

**yām** – āḻvār is making the appeal on behalf of all the people since he is able to feel the worthless situation of others and hence is stating this in plural.

**uṟāmai** – incongruent; not affected. Just as it is said in pūrvamīmāmsai “śāstra palam prayōktari” (only the person who is going to get benefitted should take efforts to attain it), emperumān asks of āḻvār “Since I am considering attaining you as my benefit, I have created several paths such as karmayōgam, jñānayōgam, bhaktiyōgam etc (the paths of carrying out deeds, gaining knowledge and devotion respectively); why don’t you attain me choosing one of those paths?” āḻvār responds saying “Please state the purpose of your taking various incarnations” periya tiruvandādi pāsuram 56 says “*oruvāṟu oruvan pugāvāṟu uṟumāṟum āyavar tām*” (in order to ensure that no sentient entity enters some path \[which may not lead to liberation from samsāram\] emperumān incarnated as the covherd boy, kriṣṇa). If emperumān takes such care of us, why should anyone follow any other path?

**uyir** – did you come here for the sake of pirāttimārs (consorts), tiruvadi (garudan), anantan (ādiśēśan) et al?

**aḷippān** – did anyone request you to come and incarnate as a human being? Was it not out of your mercy that your incarnations took place? emperumān himself says in bhagavat gītā 4-5 “*bahūni*” (many incarnations), confirming that he had taken up many incarnations.

**enninṟa yōniyumāy** – just as it is said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam “*ātmānam mānuśam manyē*” (I consider myself as a mere man), emperumān takes incarnations as per his wish.

**piṟandāy** – emperumān also takes a birth similar to other human beings, unlike his other incarnations such as narasimha when he appeared suddenly from a pillar or when he came to help the elephant gajēndran when the elephant called out to him. śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāṇḍam ślōkam says ”*tataśca dvādaśēmāsē*”(he remained in his mother kauśalyā dēvi’s womb for twelve months instead of the normal ten months that we all remain for). periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3-2-8 also reflects the same meaning “*panniru tingaḷ*” (twelve months in mother’s womb).

**imaiyōr talaivā** –  Just as it is said in tiruviruttam pāsuram 21 “*sūttu nan mālaigaḷ*” (holding nice garlands ready for adorning emperumān with …), isn’t emperumān one who is having nityasūris in paramapadam as those who are “Dear to me”? Did he incarnate here since he was not satisfied with nityasūris alone and wanted to invite those who have shortcomings in their knowledge here? When he became the controller for us too, he became one without any shortcoming.

sarvēśvaran told āḻvār “If nityasūris make an appeal to me like this, I would consider that to be a statement of fact. But how can I take your words, when you have been in samsāram from time immemorial, to be true? For how long will these words remain truthful? If I go to people thinking that they are truly calling out to me, they may ask for benefits other than me”. To which āḻvār responds saying . . . .

**mey** – it is not that way. Our appeal is indeed truthful. Just as stōtra ratnam ślōkam 57 says “*vijñāpanam idam satyam*” (this is a truthful appeal), our appeal too, to you, is truthful.

**ninṟu** – āḻvār started his appeal with the first pāsuram in tiruviruttam and ended with tiruvāimoḻi 10.7.10 “*mangavottu un māmāyai*” (rid us of this bondage in samsāram).

**kēttaryḷāy** – to listen to this is apt for emperumān. It is only till such time that āḻvār makes the appeal that āḻvār has any shortcoming. Once he has enunciated it, if emperumān does not initiate any action on it, the resulting lowliness is his and not āḻvār’s.

emperumān is puzzled that someone, who is in samsāram, is making an appeal without expecting any worldly benefit, which is much against the normal behaviour of a samsāri. His words are totally contrary to those of a samsāri. He wonders as to who it is, who is making such an appeal . . . .

**adiyēn** – āḻvār says that he is emperumān’s servitor. Had he said, “I”, it would have been at loggerheads with the words spoken thus far. Hence he says “śervitor”

**seyyum viṇṇappam** – Had he said “I” in the previous verse, he would have said “Words spoken” here. However, since he said “śervitor” he hays “Appeal made”.

vyākyātā (commentator for this pāsuram) periyavāccān piḷḷai shows further that this pāsuram explains arta pancakam (five meanings) – svasvarūpam (nature of self, the jīvātmā), parasvarūpam (nature of paramātmā, emperumān), upāya svarūpam (nature of means, the path which leads us to our goal), upēya svarūpam (nature of goal) and virōdi svarūpam (nature of hurdle). The words *poyninṟa jñānam* and *inninṟa nīrmai* explain the nature of hurdles. The words *enninṟa yōniyumāyp piṟandāy* reveal the nature of means. The words *imaiyōr talaivā* explain the nature of goal. The word *adiyēn* explains the nature of self. The words *seyym viṇṇappam* reveal that kainkaryam (to emperumān) is the benefit that one gets on attaining the goal.

We will take up the second pāsuram in the next article.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 2. Selu nir

*seḻunīr tadattuk kayal miḷirndāloppac cēyarikkaṇaḻunīr tuḷumba alamaruginṟana vāḻiyarōmuḻunīr mugilvaṇṇan kaṇṇan viṇṇāttavar mūduvarāmtoḻunīr adiyiṇaikkē anbu sūttiya sūḻkuḻaṟkē*

**Пословный перевод:**

muḻu – replete; nīr – having imbibed water; mugil – like a cloud; vaṇṇan – one who has the complexion; kaṇṇan – kriṣṇa, who is obedient (to his followers); mūduvarām – being ahead of all others in terms of time; viṇṇāttavar – nityasūris who dwell in paramapadam; toḻum – apt to be attained \[and vorśipped\]; nīr – having it as the basic nature; iṇai – being together; adikkē – at the divine feet; anbu – garland of love; sūttiya – one who offered; sūḻ – being curled; kuḻaṟku – she, who has locks; seḻum – beautiful; nīr – having water; tadattu – in the pond; kayal – fish; miḷirndāl oppa – like rolling; sē – reddish; ari – having lines; kaṇ – the eyes; aḻu – crying; nīr – tears; tuḷumba – to be agitated; alamaruginṟana – to be bewildered

**Перевод:**

**The divine feet of kriṣṇa who is having the complexion of a cloud replete with water, are constantly vorśipped by nityasūris, who came ahead of all others. A girl with curly hair and whose eyes roam around here and there like fish in a beautiful, water-filled pond, offers her garland of love to such divine feet. Her eyes which have reddish lines, are shedding tears and are bewildered since she is separated from emperumān. Let her condition, in which her love is blooming, remain forever.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had called out to emperumān as “imaiyōr talaivā” (lord of nityasūris), referring to emperumān’s quality of supreme masculinity. He wanted to attain emperumān immediately. Since that did not fructify, he assumed the pathos of a lady. Is it possible for a man to assume the mental make-up of a lady? śrī rāmāyaṇa ayōdyā kāṇḍam ślōkam 3-79 says “pumsām druśti cittāpahāriṇam” (śrī rāma would steal the eyes and minds of men). Since that was possible, this also is possible. While it is possible, how is it that āḻvār donned the role of a noble damsel instead of a normal lady? This is so because āḻvār felt supremely happy when united with emperumān, felt very dejected when separated from him and wanted to enjoy and perform service to emperumān with all his faculties \[of mind, body and speech\]. While this is acceptable, how is it that he also assumed the conduct of the mother as well as friend of the damsel? Just as excess water during severe flood breaches river and flows into its tributaries and canals before reaching the ocean, since the matter which āḻvār is experiencing (viz., emperumān) is without any limit, his supreme affection makes it possible for him to don various roles (of the damsel, her friend and her mother). How did āḻvār reach such a situation? emperumān had shown him the hurdles (inimical entities to be avoided) in samsāram, had prevented any taste for these in āḻvār and had also shown him his nityavibhūti (śrīvaikuṇṭam). After seeing the profound knowledge which those who dwell in nityavibhūti have as well as the exchanges between them and emperumān and the distinction which that place has, a craving developed in āḻvār which will not allow him to rest until he attained that place. Even after he developed a desire towards śrīvaikuṇṭam and prayed to emperumān to grant him that, why did emperumān not grant him his wish? emperumān delayed granting āḻvār his wish immediately, thinking in his divine mind “Let us create a distinction between āḻvār and other samsāris, make him unaware of anything other than us. We will make him not expect anything from us (other than ourselves), and to make him call out to us constantly, even if without any reason. We will then grant him servitude towards us, which is his basic nature. Then we will enable him to attain us. We will do all these when he remains steadfast that we alone matter”. After āḻvār was granted faultless knowledge about emperumān in the previous pāsuram, he now craves that he should attain emperumān immediately. Why should emperumān make him wait? When guests come to a house, they are provided food, keeping the inmates of the house hungry. In the same way, one can see that emperumān keeps those who are totally devoted to him, waiting, while granting the wishes of others who are not that devoted to him. We could see this (a) in the way he fulfilled kaikēyi’s wishes (of denying him the crown and sending him to forest) while making bharata to wait for fourteen years and (b) in the way he made sītāppirātti to wait while fulfilling the desire of maharājar (sugrīvan). What is the reason for fulfilling desires of those who are not that devoted to him, immediately? This is because the wishes of others last only for a short while. Will he not help those who are totally devoted to him? He does that as we could see from the way he helped bharata to carry only his sandals on his head and not the crown. While the nāyaki (leading lady) and her friend go to the garden to pluck flowers, the nāyaka (leading man) also, by chance, reaches that place. The friend, again by chance, leaves the garden, leaving the leading lady and man to be together. They unite with each other and separate too. The leading lady thought that separation is also a form of union only. Due to separation from her beloved, the lady is in distress. The friend, who returns, finds out all these and resolves that she should help the lady to regain her existence. She wishes her well saying “What situation is she in! This condition should continue like this” (meaning that the leading lady should continue to be affectionate towards her beloved). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**seḻunīrt tadattuk kayal miḷirndāl oppa** – (her eyes) are like fish which keep moving from left to right and back to left, in a pond which has pure water. The greatness of what is being compared with is so high that the simile needs a correction. In other words, the fish will not equal her eyes in all ways; they could, to an extent be like her eyes. The eyes are having such greatness.

**sēyarikkaṇ aḻunīr tuḷumba** – her eyes, which are similar to the ones mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 16-5 “*asitēkśaṇa*” (sītā with black eyes), are shedding tears copiously. While it is the leading man \[here, it is emperumān\] who should shed tears after looking at her beautiful eyes, she is shedding tears. The condition in which the eyes shed tears due to separation from emperumān is the basic nature of an ātmā (soul). Seeing such a sight is what is acceptable to her friends. Her friend thought “My desire has been fulfilled (of seeing her condition like this); her desire too should be fulfilled”. Even emperumān does not witness such a situation \[where those who separate from him shed tears and wail\], as brought out in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 33-4 “*kim artam*” (looking at the tears in the eyes of sītāppirātti, hanumān asked her “What is the reason for the tears in your eyes?”) śrī rāma learnt the reason for the tears through hanumān and did not directly know from her. Looking at the tears, the friend asks the leading lady “What is the reason for your tears? Thinking of whom are you in this condition?”

**alamaruginṟana** – her (the leading lady’s) eyes are bewildered.

**vāḻiyarō** – She tells the leading lady “Let this disheartening condition of yours sustain” (since the leading lady is pining for emperumān’s love). Is there anyone who complimented on seeing the disheartening condition of someone else? śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 67-15 “*yāmōśadivāyuśman*” jatāyu praised śrī rāma “May you live long” even though śrī rāma was in distress \[searching for sītāppirātti\].

**muḻu nīr mugil vaṇṇan kaṇṇan** – Unlike the clouds which are mentioned in mēghasandēśam 1-5 “*dūmajyōtis salilamarutām sannipāda*” (a mixture of fire and smoke), the complexion of kaṇṇan (kriṣṇa) was that of a cloud which did not have smoke but had only water. It appeared as if the cloud had imbibed entire water from the ocean, making the floor of the ocean visible. Such a dark cloud. emperumān who has such a complexion, totally enticed her. She was enticed not only by his complexion, but by his simplicity too. When a *sarpayāgam* (a ritual to bring all the snakes to one place) is held, just as all the snakes gather at that place, while everyone goes to kriṣṇa, he comes to her, falling for her. She got caught by such a person!

**viṇṇāttavar mūduvar** – Just as it is said in puruśa sūktam “*yatra pūrvē sādyās santi dēvā:*” and in yajur vēdam “*yatra ruśaya: pratamajāyē purāṇā:*” the nityasūris are far ahead of all others, in terms of time; they are ancient. Their sustenance is only through vorśipping emperumān’s divine feet.

**toḻu nīr** – they remain with vorśipping as their basic nature. It is just as it has been said in mahābharatam mausala parvam 166-40 “*nama ityēvādina:*” (they constantly say “Our salutations to you”)

**iṇai adikkē** – the two divine feet where each is an exact match for the other. If a person is very beautiful in one limb of his physical form he may think “Who is as beautiful as I am?” When a person is obedient (servitude) towards one person alone, he may think “Who is as obedient as I?” and when a person is controlling a few persons he may think “Who controls as much as I do?” *paratatvam* (being supreme) is a combination of all these, without any shortcoming. Did this leading lady not fall for such a supreme entity!

**adikkē anbu sūttiya** – vouldn’t the others, who have been won over by a person’s sheer simplicity, fall at the feet of that person! Why use the term *sūttiya* (to adorn)? It is similar to tiruvāimoḻi 4-3-2 “*vāsagam sey mālaiyē*”(garland strung with words).

**sūḻ kuḻaṟkē** – locks which are curled; alternatively, locks which attract others and make them to follow.

In the next article, we shall take up the 3<sup>rd</sup> pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 3. Kularkovalar

*kuḻaṟkōvalar madappāvaiyum maṇmagaḷum tiruvumniḻaṟpōlvanar kaṇḍu niṟkungol meeḷungol taṇṇanduḻāyaḻaṟpōladum cakkarattaṇṇal viṇṇōr toḻak kadavumtaḻaṟpōl sinatta appuḷḷin pōna tani nenjamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

taṇ – being cool; am – being beautiful; tuḻāy – divine tuḷasi; aḻal pōl – like hot; adum – roasting; cakkaram – having sudarśana disc in his hand; aṇṇal – sarvēśvaran, who is the lord; viṇṇōr – nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam); toḻa – to be vorśipped; kadavum – conducting; taḻal pōl – like fire; sinatta – having anger; appuḷḷin – behind that garutmān (garuda); pin – following; pōna – one who followed; tani – without any support; nenjam – mind; kuḻal – having locks; kōvalar – born in cow-herd clan; madam – being obedient; pāvaiyum – nappinnai pirātti (neeḷā dēvi); maṇmagaḷum – śrī bhūmi pirātti; tiruvum – and periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi); niḻal pōlvanar – being like shadow (to followers) (and invigorating); kaṇḍu – after seeing them; niṟkungol – will it stay there itself?; meeḷungol – will it return?

**Перевод:**

**One who dons the cool and beautiful divine tuḷasi garland, one who has the cakrāyudam (weapon in the form of disc) which roasts enemies like fire, is vorśipped by nityasūris. garuda is conducted by such emperumān and his anger is like fire. My mind went behind such garuda. After seeing nappinnaip pirātti who has beautiful locks and who incarnated in the clan of covherds, bhūmippirātti and periya pirātti, will my mind stay there itself or will it return to me?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, unable to bear the pain of separation from emperumān, the leading lady had assumed the pathos of her friend; she assumed the shyness typical of a girl and mentioned about her sorrows. Since she is sure that she can sustain herself only if she speaks to her friend, she asks her “My heart has gone behind him. Will it return or will it stay there itself?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kuḻaṟ kōvalar madappāvai** – covherds with flutes. Just like brāhmaṇas will carryout sandyāvandanam (vorśipping the conjunction of day and night) as their norm, this (playing of flutes) is typical of covherds. Alternatively, this term would refer to nappinnaip pirātti, who has beautiful locks and was obedient towards them.

**maṇmagal** – śrī bhūmip pirātti

**tiru** – periya pirātti.

periya pirātti will condone the faults of those who attain her. śrī bhūmippirātti will say “Why should one look at the faults and then condone them? It is better not to look at the faults”; nappinnaip pirātti will be the epitome of forbearance and forgiveness. She will make emperumān not to look at our faults. periya pirātti is the repository of all of emperumān’s wealth.  bhūmippirātti is the land where this wealth is nurtured. nappinnaip pirātti is the one who makes emperumān to enjoy the wealth.

**niḻaṟ pōlvanar** – these three pirāttis are like shadow for emperumān, never separating from him. Alternatively, they will be shadow for one another; another interpretation – they will be shadow (a source of invigoration) for their followers.

**kaṇḍu niṟkungol meeḷungol** – After looking at emperumān and his consorts in this condition, will my mind stay there permanently, taking them as its refuge? After looking at their greatness etc, will be mind be tossed about, just like a piece of crumb is tossed about by the waters of an ocean?

**taṇ am tuḻāy aḻaṟpōl adum cakkarattu aṇṇal** – one who has the divine tuḷasi which emits coolness and sweet fragrance and who also has the divine disc which emits hot winds like fire. Due to her separation from emperumān, even the cool tuḷasi appears like fire, burning her. As an example for the emission of heat from tuḷasi, she cites the divine disc which is fiery. In other words, even while citing an example, she could only think of entities which are closely connected to emperumān. She cannot think of anything other than matters related to him. The divine disc does not distinguish between followers and enemies. While it will be a decoration for followers, it would be a weapon for enemies, hurting both, one through its beauty and the other, through its state of weaponry. In the case of divine tuḷasi, it will be apt for followers. The lady says that even tuḷasi is hurting her, like the divine disc.

**aṇṇal** – by holding the divine disc such that it does not slip out of his hands, he made me submit to him totally.

**taḻaṟpōl sinatta** – having an anger which appears like a raging fire

**viṇṇōr toḻak kadavum** – emperumān conducts periya tiruvadi (garuda) such that they \[nityasūris\] worship him (emperumān). They are the ones who worship and he is the one who is vorśipped. Their sustenance is through vorśipping emperumān.

**appuḷḷin pōna** – she is not worried about her difficulties. She is celebrating the fact that there are those who are concerned about emperumān. She says that her mind went behind that garudan rather than behind emperumān himself, since he is loving towards emperumān. The commentator cites reference to periya tirumoḻi pāsuram 11-1-9 “*keṇḍai oṇ*” whose meaning is as follows: My eyes which are like carp (a kind of fish) will sleep and my complexion will be healthy only when beetles blow on me, the fragrance of tuḷasi from the garland submitted by śrīvaiṣṇavas who are *ananyaprayōjanars*. The term *ananyaprayōjanar* refers to those who carry out activities without expecting any benefit or those who do not know what benefit they will attain. Why should this term be brought in here? This is to emphasise that while there are many who are loving towards her beloved \[emperumān\], there are those, like the beetle, who are caring towards her without expecting any benefit.

**tani nenjam** – independent mind. Even when she tells it “Do not go behind emperumān” it goes behind him and does not return.

*svāpadēsam* (distinguished/internal meaning of the pāsuram): By referring to pirāttimārs and nityasūris, she \[āḻvār in feminine pathos\] affirms the superiority of emperumān and says that affection towards śrīvaikuṇṭanātan (the lord of śrīvaikuṇṭam) was created in her.

In the next article, we will see the 4th pāsuram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 4. Tani nenjam

*tani nenjam munnavar puḷḷē kavarndadu taṇṇanduḻāykkuini nenjam inguk kavarvadu yām ilam nī naduvēmuni vanjap pēycci mulai suvaittān mudi sūdu tuḻāyppani nanja mārudamē emmadāvi panippiyalvē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tani – distinguished; nenjam – heart; mun – already; avar – that sarvēśvaran’s; puḷḷē – the bird garuda himself (emperumān’s vehicle); kavarndadu – had stolen; ini – further; yām – we; ingu – in this place; taṇ – being cool; am – beautiful; tuḻāykku – for the divine tuḷasi; kavarvadu – for stealing; nenjam – heart; ilam – do not have; muni – having anger; vanjam – having deceit; pēycci – the demon pūtanā; mulai – bosom; suvaittān – kriṣṇa who drank; mudi – in his crown; sūdum – proffered; tuḻāy – divine tuḷasi; pani – having coolness; nanjam – being poisonous; mārudamē – Oh wind!; nī – you; naduvē – amidst these; emmadu – our; āvi – prāṇan (life); panippu – shivering; iyalvē – is it its nature? (is it correct?)

**Перевод:**

**garuda had already stolen my mind completely such that there is no connection between me and my mind. I do not have another mind with me so that his cool tuḷasi garland could steal from me. Oh wind which is coming with coolness resulting from pervading through the tuḷasi garland which adorns the beautiful crown of kriṣṇa who had drunk milk from the bosom of demon pūtanā who was full of anger and deceit! When I am already struggling like this, is it proper for you to come in between and make me to shiver further?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Just as āḻvār had addressed emperumān in the first pāsuram as “imaiyōr talaivā” (lord of nityasūris), he had mentioned about how garudan, tuḷasi and others, who reside in nityavibhūti, had troubled her (āḻvār in feminine mood) further, so far. Wind joined them now. All these kindled memories of emperumān more. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tani nenjam** – āḻvār had only his mind; but there are several entities such as garudan, tuḷasi, wind etc who were attempting to steal it. Thus, the implication is that those who create hurdles are more in number.

**mun avar puḷḷē  kavarndadu** – Looking at the majestic way in which emperumān was seated on garuda, I wanted to hug him with the garland on his shoulder. Before I could do it, didn’t garuda steal my mind!

**avar puḷ** – Because of  his distinguished connection with emperumān, did garudan steal it?

**puḷḷē** – gaurdan did not give scope to others in śrīvaikuṇṭam such as tuḷasi et al to steal my mind. He did that himself.

**kavarndadu** – my mind was insufficient for that garudan too, filling up only half of his stomach.

**mun** – garuda preceded tuḷasi, in stealing my mind.

**taṇ anduḻāykku** – for that cool tuḷasi garland.

**ini nenjam** – she tells tuḷasi “Even if you remain here firm like a tree, in order to steal my mind, what is going to happen? There is no purpose in staying here saying that you will not leave this place without taking her mind. Your desire will not be fulfilled”.

**ingu** – she states her position clearly.

**yām ilam** – my mind has already gone behind garudan. I, a sinner, am not endowed with several minds so that all of you could steal one mind each. I do not have another mind for tuḷasi garland to steal.

**nī naduvē** – Seeing that the divine tuḷasi, after coming to steal my mind, was standing like a tree, it appears that the wind came very fast. While tuḷasi, who too is connected to emperumān, is waiting, what is your urgency? While garudan, tuḷasi et al have direct connection with emperumān, your connection with emperumān is through them. In other words, you have connection with tuḷasi who has direct contact with his divine form.

**muni vanjap pēycci** – the demon pūtanā who came with lot of anger and with deceit.

**mulai suvaittān** – he was taking in her milk with plenty of saliva. periya tirumoḻi 1-3-1 says “*peṟṟa tāy pōl vanda pēycci perumulai ūdu uyirai vaṟṟa vāngi uṇḍa vāyān*” (he drank, without leaving anything behind, including her life, through the bosom of the demon pūtanā, who came in the form of his mother yaśōdā).

**mudi sūdu tuḻāy** – this wind is bringing the fragrance of tuḷasi from the crown of that kriṣṇa. Just as clouds imbibe the waters of ocean and precipitate that as rain everywhere, this wind is also carrying fragrance of tuḷasi and spreading it everywhere. He donned the tuḷasi garland as easily as he put an end to pūtana.

How did she (āḻvār in feminine mood) know that emperumān had tuḷasi on his crown?

**pani nanjam mārudamē** – the wind which is cool, is now blowing like poison. Just as pūtanā smeared poison on her bosom, the wind has come carrying tuḷasi’s fragrance \[when in separation, even tuḷasi is not welcome\]

**emmadu āvi** – my prāṇan (vital air) which is in distress due to garuda, tuḷasi, wind et al.

**panippu iyalvē** – It is natural for my prāṇan to be in shivers. She asks the wind “Could you, who would cause distress to his enemies, cause distress to his followers too?”

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished/internal meaning): Since āḻvār had earlier enjoyed sarvēśvaran through the faculty of his mind, all the materials which are connected with emperumān are now reminding him about emperumān. Since he is in separation from emperumān, he is in distress and he is speaking about how the cool breeze is troubling the leading lady who is separated from her beloved.

We will take up the 5th pāsuram in the next article.

## 5. Panippiyalvaga

*panippiyalvāga udaiya taṇvādai ikkālam ivvūrp panippiyalvellām tavirndu erivīsum andaṇṇanduḻāy panippuyal sōrum tadangaṇṇi māmait tiṟattuk kolām panippuyal vaṇṇan sengōl orunānṟu tadāviyadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

panippu – creating cillness; iyalvāga udaiya – having as its nature; taṇ – being cool; vādai – northerly wind; ikkālam – at the present time; ivvūril – in this place; panippu – making it cold; iyalvellām – all its basic nature; tavirndu – leaving aside; eri vīsum – started to emit fire; am – beautiful; taṇṇam – having coolness; tuḻāy – for the sake of divine tuḷasi; pani – cold; puyal – tears, like a rainfall; sōrum – shedding; tadam – expansive; kaṇṇi – this girl with eyes; māmai tiṟattukkolām – for the sake of beauty; pani – cool; puyal – like a cloud; vaṇṇan – sarvēśvaran, who has such a complexion; sengōl – order; oru nānṟu – today; tadāviyadu – became bent

**Перевод:**

**Making things cool is the nature of northerly wind. But even that wind, in this place and at this time, discarding its nature, is emitting fire everywhere. Why did the sceptre of sarvēśvaran, who has the quality of a cool, rain-bearing cloud, bend like this? Was it to destroy the complexion of this lady, who desired his beautiful, cool tuḷasi and who sheds tears like rain-bearing clouds?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) All the materials in this world are present to remind us about emperumān. But these thoughts are also hurtful. As said in cāndōjña upaniśat “na ca punarāvartate” (one does not return again), one can reach that land of no return only after getting rid of all the contractions to knowledge. It is only after reaching that place could one know about him and entities connected to him, fully. Wouldn’t those entities, which are connected to him, remind about him, only after that? How is it then that it was possible for āḻvār to be reminded of these entities, even while he was living in this world? The knowledge which āḻvār had, was not obtained due to his effort. That was granted to him by emperumān through his causeless mercy. Since he was granted faultless knowledge and devotion by emperumān, it was possible for him to attain the states of parabhakti (knowledge about emperumān), parajñānam (inability to sustain self when separated from emperumān) and paramabhakti (attaining emperumān) simultaneously, even while living here. Thus, the entities connected with emperumān are capable of reminding him about emperumān even when he is still here and they are capable of hurting him, too. This is the nature of his knowledge. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**panippu iyalvāga udaiya taṇ vādai** – the northerly wind which has cillness alone as its basic nature.

**ikkālam ivvūr panippu iyalvu ellām tavirndu eri vīsum** – Since it is emperumān who changes the basic nature of the wind, at that place and during that time, the wind is bewildered because of him. Many entities stood there, determined to destroy her, by changing their form and nature. When the form and nature of sarvēśvaran could change in her case, should one say anything about the entities which follow him! Protection is emperumān’s basic form and mercy is his basic nature. He changed his basic form from being protective to causing harm and his basic nature from being merciful to being cruel. Thus the wind also sheds its basic nature of being cool and emits fire.

**am taṇṇanduḻāy panippuyal sōrum tadam kaṇṇi** – She stood there, shedding tears copiously, after desiring sarvēśvaran’s cool and beautiful tuḷasi. Is desiring his divine tuḷasi a crime? Even if it were, should one, who has committed such a crime, suffer to this extent? Her eyes are pouring out tears, just like a rain-bearing cloud would let out water that it is bearing. If the tears in her eyes dry up, all the water in the ocean would not be sufficient to fill the eyes again.

**māmait tiṟattuk kolām** – Was it not for changing her complexion that emperumān and his entities changed their basic form and nature so arduously?

**panippuyal . . .** – there are two interpretations for these terms. *pani* would refer to coolness; *puyal* is cloud and *vaṇṇan* is one who has that complexion. Thus, this combination would refer to emperumān who removes the three types of anguish (*tāpatrayam* which would refer to anguish brought on by self (by indulging in forbidden activities); anguish brought on by nature (floods, cyclone, drought etc); anguish brought on by emperumān based on our karma) that samsāris suffer from. Alternatively, the term *vaṇṇan* could be taken as referring to nature; then this would mean emperumān who showers mercy without any reason, just like cloud showers rain without expecting anything in return.

**sengōl** – we suffer due to his orders. Let it be so. We may suffer, but nothing untoward should occur to his sceptre (his orders). In other words, even if the cool wind starts emitting fire, if it had been done under his orders, let me suffer because of that; however, his order should not be crossed.

**oru nānṟu tadāviyadē** – to attain her, his sceptre bent a little, today. The lady’s mother laments that it should not bend any further.

*svāpadēsam* (distinguished meaning): While for all the people, in samsāram (materialistic realm), while outside entities (those which are not connected with emperumān) are favourable, for āḻvār who cannot sustain himself without emperumān, even the entities which are close to emperumān are creating trouble.

## 6. Tadaviya ambum

*nadāviya kūṟṟam kaṇḍīr uyir kāmingaḷ jñālattuḷḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

**Перевод:**

**tadāviya ambum**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) tadāviya ambum murinda silaigaḷum pōgavittuk kadāyina koṇḍu olgum valliyidēnum asurarmangak kadāviya vēgappaṟavaiyin pāgan madana sengōl*

*tadāvudal* **tadāviya** *tadāviya*

1. arrow which has an expansive edge – the expansive eyes of the leading lady
2. that which can be felt only after it pierces us – we can feel only after the glance of the leading lady falls on us
3. arrow together with the bow – expansive eyes together with the eyebrows
4. crooked arrow – the glance which hurts us in an indirect \[hidden\] manner

**murinda silaigaḷum** *murinda* *ambugaḷum silaigaḷum* *ambum silaigaḷum* *ambu* *silaigaḷ* **pōgavittuk kadāyina koṇḍu** **olgum valli idēnum** **asurar mangak kadāviya vēgap paṟavaiyin pāgan** *paṟavaiyin pāgan* **madana sengōl nadāviya** **uyir kāṇmingaḷ jñālattuḷḷē** *svāpadēsam* [https://divyaprabandam.koyil.org](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/) [https://koyil.org](https://koyil.org/) [http://grantams.koyil.org](http://granthams.koyil.org/) [http://acaryas.koyil.org](http://acharyas.koyil.org/) [http://pillai.koyil.org](https://pillai.koyil.org/)

## 7. Jnalam panippa

*jñālam panippac ceṟuttu nannīrittuk kāl sidaindu neela vallēṟu poṟā ninṟa vānam idu tirumāḷ kōlam sumandu pirindār kodumai kuḻaṟu taṇ pūm kālam kolō aṟiyēn vinaiyāttiyēn kāṇginṟavē*

**Пословный перевод:**

idu – this; neelam – darkening; val – very strong; ēṟu – bulls; jñālam – earth; panippa – to tremble; seṟuttu – becoming angry; nal nīr – the exulting water; ittu – copiously increasing; kāl sidaindu – clawing with their feet; poṟā ninṟa – fighting; vānam – sky; vinaiyāttiyēn – I, with lot of sins; kāṇginṟa – seeing; idu – this; tirumāḷ – consort of śrī (mahālakśmi); kōlam – decoration; sumandu – to bear; pirindār – those who separated; kodumai – the cruelty (of their sorrow); kuḻaṟum – saying incoherently; taṇ – cool; pū – beautiful; kālangolō – is it the (rainy) season; aṟiyēn – I am not sure

**Перевод:**

**(Looking at the monsoon clouds) Are these the skies which appear like bulls which are raring to fight with each other with anger, such that the world trembles, letting out the water from their bodies and scratching the ground with their hoofs? (ōr) Are these the cool, beautiful clouds during rainy season which have taken the bluish divine complexion of sarvēśvaran, who is the consort of pirātti, revealing his cruelty due to separation? I, having lot of sins, am unable to know this (she says that looking at the leading lady’s sorrowful state is due to her sins)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) The leading man had told the leading lady that he will return once the monsoon starts. Since he did not return, though monsoon had set in, the lady starts feeling sorrowful. The lady’s friend, to console the lady who suffers after seeing the change of season and in order to make her sustain herself in separation, tells the lady “this is not the season that he had said that he would come by”, thus bewildering the lady into believing that it is not the monsoon time. She tells the lady that these are not clouds in the sky but they are dark coloured bulls which, due to the enmity between them, have risen to the sky since there isn’t sufficient space on the ground for them to fight with each other. The friend denies arrival of monsoon just as those \[bhauddas\] who deny existence of world by saying that the world which we see with our eyes does not exist. They will say that neither the world nor emperumān exists. However, the friend says that though monsoon has not arrived, emperumān exists, as does the world. Is it appropriate to tell a lie like this \[denying that monsoon has arrived\]? If she had not said this, the leading lady may not live due to separation from her leading man. If she does not live, emperumān (the leading man) too would not live and with him, the entire wealth \[of both spiritual realm and materialistic realm\] would become nonexistent. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**jñālam panippac ceṟuttu** – the leading lady tells her friend “You could see the entire world trembling on account of the chilly wind and rain, blasting here”. The friend tells the lady “It is not like that. This isn’t the rainy season at all. The world is trembling on seeing two bulls which are fighting with each other fiercely”

**seṟuttu** – standing with anger

**nannīr ittu** – since it had been raining for a while, all the dirt had been wiped off the ground and plenty of pure water has got collected which could be drunk. Looking at that, the lady asks her friend “What is the reason for such copious amount of water?” The friend responds saying “Since these are distinguished bulls which are fighting with each other, water gets generated out of their anger, due to their sweat”

**kāl sidaindu** – this is interpreted in two ways: *kāl* could mean either wind or feet. The lady tells her friend that the wind has stopped and it is raining heavily. The friend tells the lady that it is not like that; since the bulls are fighting with anger, they scratch the surface of earth with their hooves and feet.

**neela vallēṟu poṟā ninṟa vānam idu** – the friend tells the lady ”Don’t we see only the sky in which two bulls, which are dark and strong, are fighting with each other? If these are clouds, by now, vouldn’t they have become white in colour, after raining for such a long time?”

**tirumāl kōlam sumandu neela vallēṟu poṟā ninṟa vānam idu** – why is the friend saying this? She thinks that if she were to keep saying only false things, the friend may not believe her. Hence she feigns as if she too is doubting whether it is monsoon. It could also be taken to mean that the friend is saying this in order to convince the lady that since it is monsoon time, the leading man will come soon.

**tirumāḷ kōlam sumandu pirindār kodumai kuḻaṟu** – the clouds, assuming the complexion of emperumān, are telling the lady “We have come without fail, during this rainy season. But emperumān has not come yet”. In other words, they are telling her about his faults.

**taṇ pūm kālangolō aṟiyēn** – I do not know whether it is the cool, beautiful time, implying that this is the time to be united happily with the leading man.

**vinaiyāttiyēn kāṇginṟavē** – what is the reason for this situation? It cannot be said that he is the type who gives up on those who desire him or those who he desires; it cannot be said that the leading lady is not desiring him; it cannot be said that this is not the time for him to come; and it cannot be said that she will not attain him. What else can be the reason? Only reason is that it is because of my (the friend’s) sins; *math pāpamēva nimittam*. In other words, the friend says “Seeing this sorrowful condition of my leading lady is only due to my sin”.

*svāpadēsam* (distinguished meaning): Looking at the sorrowful condition of āḻvār in separation from emperumān, it becomes pertinent on the part of śrīvaiṣṇavas to sustain him even if they have to tell lies that something doesn’t exist \[in this case, saying that the monsoon season has not arrived\].

In the next article, we will take up the 8th pāsuram.

## 8. Kanginranagalum

*kāṇginṟanagaḷum kētkinṟanagaḷum kāṇil innāḷ pāṇkunṟa nādar payilginṟana idellām aṟindōm māṇkunṟam ēndi taṇ māmalai vēngadattumbar nambum sēṇkunṟam senṟu poruḷ padaippān kaṟṟa tiṇṇanavē*

**Пословный перевод:**

innāḷ – at the present moment; kāṇginṟanagaḷum – activities (of yours) which are seen; kētkinṟanagaḷum – words spoken (by you) which are heard; kāṇil – if one were to analyse; pāṇ – having the songs (of beetles); kunṟam – the hills of tiruvēngadam; nādar – you, who are having that hill as your dwelling place; payilginṟana – carried out in sequence; idellām – all these; māṇ – beautiful; kunṟam – gōvardana hill; ēndi – holding it aloft; taṇ – being cool; māmalai – the huge mountain; vēngadattu – of tiruvēngadam; umbar – nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam); nambum – apt to be liked; kunṟam – hill; senṟu – attaining it; poruḷ – tiruvēngadamudaiyān, the wealth; padaippān – to attain; kaṟṟa – taking efforts; tiṇṇanavu – deceitful; aṟindōm – we have come to know

**Перевод:**

**If we analyse the activities carried out and words spoken of, by the lord of the place with mountains, they appear to be feigned. What is the purpose of doing all these? It appears that he wants to reach the cool, huge hills of tiruvēngadam, which belongs to kriṣṇa who had lifted the distinguished gōvardana hill, and which is apt to be attained by nityasūris too. It appears that he is doing all these activities since he wants to remain on that hill and earn materials.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, the friend comforted the leading lady with different types of reasoning. The leading man then came there and spoke to the lady. The friend, looking at his actions, feels that they were not natural. She doubts whether these are pretences by the man in order to separate again from the lady. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kāṇginṟana** – even as he is standing, he would suddenly bend and hold on to the feet; he would feign as if he is prostrating.

**kētkinṟana** – feigning courteous words such as “I am your servant”, “I will massage your feet”, “My mind is bewildered” etc.

**kāṇil** – if one were to analyse. periya tiruvandādi pāsuram 36 says “*nenjennum utkaṇṇēl kāṇum uṇarndu*” (if it can be visualised through the inner eye of mind)

**innāḷ** – today

**pāṇ** – it appeared just like flattering

**pāṇkunṟa nādar** – the leading man who has tiruvēngadamalai, where beetles constantly keep humming, as his dwelling place. The lord of *kurinji nilam*, the land which encompasses mountains and allied lands. *kurinji* also refers to a meeting place. When this is the case, is it proper to separate? She (the leading lady’s friend) says that his activities do not appear to be those carried out by someone who wants to attain something.

**payilginṟana** – the way he united with her, it appears that he is going to give her up. Just as maharājar (sugrīvan) had made perumāḷ (śrī rāma), who had come to protect him, to suffer in lightning and thunder and was enjoying himself without knowing whether it was sunrise or sunset.

**māṇ kunṟam ēndi** – holding one mountain \[gōvardana\] aloft and standing atop another hill \[tiruvēngadam\] and protecting all. *mātci* (root for *māṇ*) – beautiful.

**ēndi** – lifting easily, without any effort.

**taṇ māmalai vēngadattu** – just as it is said in nācciyār tirumoḻi 8-3 “*kuḷir aruvi vēngadam*” it is cool, invigorating tiruvēngadam hill.

**umbar nambum** – just as amalanādipirān pāsuram 3 says “*mandipāy vadavēngada māmalai vānavargaḷ sandhi seyya ninṟān*”, tiruvēngadam is the apt place where nityasūris come every day and carry out divine worship. nityasūris have always vorśipped him as the supreme *paravāsudēvan*; now, since he is manifesting his simplicity here, contrary to his supreme nature, they like tiruvēngadam.

**sēṇkunṟam** – tiruvēngadam which has tall peaks

**poruḷ** – we have seen that they stand in front of you saying “You are the greatest entity”

**padaippān** – we have seen that these are readily available; we do not have to take any effort.

**kaṟṟa** – these pretences were  not seen in you earlier. From which teacher did you learn these?

**tiṇṇanavē** – from where did this harshness come in you, superseding the earlier softness?

In the next article, we will take up the 9<sup>th</sup> pāsuram.

## 9. Tinpunjudar

*tiṇpūnjudar nudi nēmiyam selvar viṇṇādanaiyavaṇpū maṇivalli yārē piribavar tām ivaiyōkaṇpūngamalam karum sudar ādi veṇmuttarumbivaṇpūnguvaḷai madamān viḻikkinṟa māyidaḻē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tiṇ – being firm; pū – being beautiful; sudar – being splendorous; nudi – being sharp; nēmi – with the divine disc; am selvar – śrīya: pati (consort of śrī mahālakśmi), who has beauty and wealth; viṇṇādanaiya – enjoyable, similar to śrīvaikuṇṭam; vaṇ – magnanimous; maṇi – renowned like a gemstone; valli – Oh leading lady, who is like a creeper plant!; pū – being beautiful; kamalam – lotus; vaṇ – magnanimous; pū – being beautiful; kuvaḷai – blue coloured Indian water-lily; karum sudar – a bluish effulgence; ādi – having; veṇ muttu – having tears of white pearls; arumbi – shedding them like buds; mā idaḻ – having lip similar to tender mango leaves; madamān – you, who are like an obedient doe; viḻikkinṟa – looking, by opening your eyes; ivai – are these; kaṇṇō – eyes!; ārē – who; piribavardām – will separate (from you who are like this)?

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who is like a beautiful creeper and who is full of enjoyment similar to the paramapadam (śrivaikuṇṭam) of sarvēśvaran who is wealthy and has the divine disc which is firm, beautiful, fiery and sharp which are fitting together! Are your eyes, mere eyes? No! They are like lotus flower, like the beautiful water-lily, emitting a bluish splendour. Whitish pearl like tear drops are welling up in those eyes. Your eyelids are like tender mango leaves. Who will separate from you who are opening eyes slowly, like an obedient doe?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) The leading lady tells the leading man “You are determined to leave me and go to tirumalai”. He tells her “I did not think that way. Did you not think of going there?” Further he tells her “After reaching the stage of being together with the followers of emperumān, is it not you (āḻvār) who is thinking of being together with emperumān? We have understood that you have not realised your greatness. You may not have understood about us. Would we too be like that?” An incident is cited here. Once emperumānār (bhagavad rāmānujar) had decided to go to tiruvēngadam and mentioned this to his disciples. piḷḷai uṟangāvallidāsar immediately went to his house. Seeing this, emperumānār sent a śrīvaidṇavar to see what was happening there. He returned and told emperumānār “He is reciting the pāsuram ‘kāṇginṟanagaḷum’ with tears in his eyes”. Immediately emperumānār told him “ṣouldn’t you have reminded him about the pāsuram ‘vaṇ pū maṇivalli yārē piribavardām’ and consoled him?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**tiṇpū** – the nature of divine disc is that it will not step back when it comes to annihilating the enemies of emperumān’s followers. The term *tiṇ* refers to firmness while *pū* refers to gentleness. In other words, divine disc is firm when it is a weapon and gentle when it is an ornament.

**sudar** – splendorous

**nudi** – sharp

**nēmi** – divine disc, cakrattāḻvār

**am selvar** – the beauty and wealth which accrue because of holding such divine disc

**viṇṇādu anaiya ….** – paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) which is as said in cāndōkya upaniśat 8-15-1 “*na ca punaravartatē*” (there is no returning from that place); only if someone who goes there returns would I separate from you and go to tiruvēngadam.

**vaṇmai** – quality of being magnanimous. He \[āḻvār\] was showered with faultless knowledge and devotion, in his mind, such that he could experience and enjoy. Instead of experiencing and enjoying that alone, he spread it to the whole world as tiruvāimoḻi prabandam (divine collection). When he refers to himself in his prabandam, he calls himself as *vaṇsatakōpan* (the magnanimous nammāḻvār).

**pū** – one which gives enjoyment

**maṇivalli** – being an ornament to itself. The word *valli* has two connotations here: she is clinging on to the neck of her beloved, just like a creeper will spread on to a supporting pole. Alternatively, while she should be holding on to a supporting pole, she is lying on the ground, being anguished in separation from her leading man.

**ārē piribavardām** – even if I do not have affection to the extent that you have, should I not be considered as one with knowledge?

**ivaiyō kaṇ** – aren’t they eyes which are so beautiful that they have to be protected from evil eyes?

**pūm kamalam** – eyes which are beautiful like a lotus flower.

**karum sudar ādi** – having an arrogance

**veṇ muttu arumbi** – just as a pearl forms in the oyster, sorrow was appearing in the beautiful eyes

**vaṇ pūnguvaḷai** – the magnanimously blossomed purple coloured water lily.

**madamān viḻikkinṟa** – even when being united, anticipating separation, fluttering the eyes like a doe.

**mā idaḻē** – the word *mā* could refer to either black or reddish colour. If it refers to being black, it would apply to the black eyes while if it refers to being red, it would apply to the reddish lips.

*svāpadēśam* – even when he is together with śrīvaiṣṇavas, āḻvār is sorrowful anticipating that they would separate from him and go away and wonders as to what will happen should they separate from him. This pāsuram is the result of their mocking at him because of his doubts.

In the next article, we will consider the 10th pāsuram

## 10. Mayon vadatiruvengadam

*māyōn vadatiruvēngadanāda vallikkodigāḷnōyō uraikkilum kētkinṟileer uraiyīr numaduvāyō adu anṟi valvinaiyēnum kiḷiyum eḷgumāyō adum toṇḍaiyō aṟaiyō idu aṟivaridē*

**Пословный перевод:**

māyōn – belonging to tiruvēngadamudaiyān (lord of tiruvēngadam), the one with amazing activities; vadatiruvēngadanāda – those who are dwellers of tirumalai which is on the northern side; valli – like a creeper plant; kodigāḷ – oh women!; nōy – (my) disease; uraikkilum – even if mentioned; kētkinṟileer – you are not listening; O – alas!; numadu – your; vāyō – mouth?; adu anṟi – or else; val vinaiyēnum – me, who has the sins (to say these words); kiḷiyum – even the parrot; eḷgum – to quiver (after hearing); āyō – is it the sound “āyō”?; toṇḍaiyō – is it the lips which are like the reddish fruit; adum – affected (like this); uraiyīr – please tell the reason for this disease; aṟivaridu – unable to ascertain

**Перевод:**

**Oh women who are like the creeper plant and who are dwelling in tiruvēngadam hills which are to the northern side and for which tiruvēngadamudaiyān, who has amazing activities, is the lord! You are not aware, on your own, of my disease of separation and are not listening to me when I reveal it to you. You should tell me as to what is troubling me. Is it your talk which is troubling me? ōr am I, having lot of sins, the reason for this? ōr is it the sound “āyō” which you make to drive away the parrots which are sitting on the trees? ōr is it your lips which are reddish like the fruit?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) This pāsuram comes under the classification of madiyudan paduttal in tamiḻ literature. This signifies that the leading man, who had separated the previous day, returns the next day and expresses his desire to the leading lady and her friends when they are guarding the paddy fields (from birds). The opinion is that he expresses his feelings to the friends and, bewildering them, tells them to inform his desire to the leading lady. Let us look at the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**māyōn** – one who has amazing activities. Why is he \[āḻvār\] talking about amazing activities here? While emperumān has *paramapadam* (śrīvaikuṇṭam) which is beyond one’s words and mind, he left that and took residence atop tiruvēngadam hills, as said in nānmugam tiruvandādi pāsuram 47 “*gānamum vānaramum vēdum udai vēngadam*” (tiruvēngadam which has forests, monkeys and hunter-tribe people). This simplicity, even when he is so supreme, is the reason which makes āḻvār to address him as one with amazing activities.

**vada tiruvēngadam nāda** – while there are places beyond tiruvēngadam too, on the northern side, āḻvār is calling this place as one on the northern side since this place has been sung in praise of, by most of the āḻvārs.

**vallik kodigāḷ** – both terms *valli* and *kodi* would refer to women. What is the need for using both terms? This is to affirm that they do not have any of the qualities of masculinity and are full of feminine qualities only. Since creeper plants need to cling on to a supporting pole, he is stressing on the need for holding on to emperumān for sustaining oneself. The leading man is the only supporting pole and all the other women look up to him as their support.

**nōyō** – being separated from her, I am neither able to live nor give up my life.

**nōy uraikkilum O kētkinṟileer** – Looking at my state, śouldn’t you all have come to me and comforted me saying “Oh, your situation has become like this”? Instead of that, I, who am affected with this disease, am coming to you and, I am asking you the reason for this disease. In other words, while you should have got this disease due to separation from me, I have been afflicted with this. This is very strange. You do not have to give any treatment for getting rid of this disease. It is enough if you listen to my outpouring.

**uraiyīr** – he is imploring those who are not listening to his words “Please listen” thus manifesting his desire towards her. After listening to him, the leading lady’s friends tell him “You have realised on your own that you have the disease. What is there for us to say?” To this, the leading man responds saying “I do not know the reason for this disease. You should tell me that”

**numadu vāyō** – is your blossomed face the reason for my disease?

**adu anṟi valvinaiyēnum kiḷiyum eḷgum āyō** – is it the sound “āyō” that you make to drive away the parrots (which have come to eat the grains in the field which you people are guarding)?

**kiḷiyum eḷgum** – the word *eḷgum* has two meanings – to leave and to be involved. In the former case, it would refer to the parrots leaving the place on hearing their words “āyō”and in the latter case, it would refer to the parrots being involved with what they were saying and staying put in their place.

**valvinaiyēnum** – I did not become attracted to you, only on account of your sound (when you speak); I was engaged with you on account of your sweet voice and your beautiful forms. The implied meaning is that sarvēśvaran is engaged with āḻvār like the parrot, listening to his pāsurams while the śrīvaiṣṇavas are engaged with āḻvār on account of the pāsurams as well the qualities of his soul.

**toṇḍaiyō** – is it because of your reddish lips?

**adum** – all these make me perish.

**aṟaiyō idu aṟivaridē** – I do not know as to which of these troubled  me and caused this disease. For not knowing the reason, he is saying the words *aṟaiyō* as if he were raising a battle cry.

*svāpadēsam* (distinguished meaning) – śrīvaiṣṇavas were engaged with the qualities of āḻvār’s ātmā (soul) as well as his *dēham* (physical form) such as his pāsurams, his splendour etc, without making any distinction among them.

We will take up the 11th pāsuram in the next article.

## 11. Ariyana yam

*ariyana yām inṟu kāṇginṟana kaṇṇan viṇṇaṇaiyāy periyana kādam porutkō pirivena jñālam eydaṟku uriyana oṇ muttum paim ponnum ēndi ōrō kudangai periyana keṇḍaikkulam ivaiyō vandu pērginṟavē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaṇṇan – kriṣṇa’s; viṇ aṇaiyāy – Oh one who is most enjoyable like paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam); porutku – for wealth; pirivu – separation; ena – even as this was being mentioned; periyana – being huge; kādam – being afar; O – how amazing!; jñālam – distinguished people with supreme knowledge; eydaṟku – to experience; uriyana – being apt; oṇ – beautiful; muttum – pearls of tears; pai – fresh (new); ponnum – gold with distinguished colour; ēndi – holding; ōrō kudangai – each one being as much as the palm of the hand; periyana – large; keṇḍai – of fish; kulam – schools of; ivai – these eyes; vandu – coming; pērginṟana – are flipping; O – how amazing!; yām – we; inṟu – now; kāṇginṟana – what we see; ariyana – are rare

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who gives happiness similar to kriṣṇa’s paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam)! I had merely said that it is normal for the head of a family to travel afar for earning wealth, but had not left you. On hearing this, your eyes which are capable of keeping the world under their control, which are as large as the palm of one’s hand, which are like the carp fish, are shedding tears of pearl and fresh gold with distinguished colour. How amazing is this! What I see here is very much a rarity.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) The leading lady was united with the leading man in total happiness, not knowing anything about separation. A person came and told the leading man about earning wealth in a different place. On hearing this, the leading lady thought “While I was thinking that we could be together like this for a long time, he is thinking of separating from me” and was distressed. Seeing this, the leading man was amazed, thinking “Even when I had not told her anything about leaving her, she is so distressed” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**ariyana** – even as we (the leading man says of himself and the lady) were together, why is she in such a tearful condition, wonders the leading man. He says that he had not seen this condition when they were together or when they were separated \[in earlier times\].

**yām inṟu** – by adding the numeral one to one thousand, will the value of one thousand increase considerably? By adding the quantum of water which is present under the hoof of a cow, will the quantum of water in an ocean increase? In the same way, when I was together with her, while I had lot of wealth, what else is needed? This was my state.

**kāṇginṟana** – There are four different reasons for separating from a beloved: to increase one’s level of knowledge, to earn more, to take part in a battle and to be with some other girl. None of these is applicable here, says the leading man.

**kaṇṇan viṇ aṇaiyāy** – just as cāndōkya upaniśat 8-15-1 says “*na ca punarāvartatē*” (one does not return from śrīvaikuṇṭam), only if someone leaves that place and returns does the question of my leaving you arise. That place (śrīvaikuṇṭam) does not undergo any change. You, who provide happiness which is much similar to that distinguished place of śrī kriṣṇa’s, should not undergo any change, like that place.

**pirivu ena O periyana kādam** – on hearing the word “Separation” she feels as if we have been separated by a very long distance with many oceans and mountains between us, with no one who could act as a messenger between us. She feels very distressed

**porutku** – the leading lady seems to say “If the purpose of separating from me is to earn lot of wealth, such wealth is available in plenty here itself”

**jñālam eydaṟku uriyana** – This wealth (which is available here) is such that those with distinguished knowledge can enjoy well. Its value will equal the wealth of nityavibhūti as well as leelāvibhūti (the spiritual and material realms). The wealth which you are seeking elsewhere is available here itself, she says.

**oṇ muttum** – pearls at their place of origin \[very pure\]

**paim ponnum** – fresh gold

**ēndi** – holding them, as if saying “When these are here, why do you have to go elsewhere for wealth? Accept these”

**ōrō kudangai** – the beauty of her eyes was such that those who see them, which are as large as one’s palm, would imbibe them.

**periyana** – they were so huge that the enjoyer (the leading man) would be totally satiated

**kudangai** – through her face, isn’t she offering wealth which could not be held in one’s palm!

**keṇḍai** – like fish which have an arrogance

**kulam** – they (her eyes) would match each other; nothing else will come anywhere near them.

**ivaiyō vandu pērginṟavē** – the eyes suffered similar to the sufferings of those who saw them. Even an inanimate object could flop over someone

**vandu pērginṟavē** – they are being spoken of as animate objects.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): āḻvār wanted to be together with śrīvaiṣṇavas all the time. He expressed his feelings in this pāsuram as though they would separate from him and go on their own.

## 12. Perginradu manimamai

*pērginṟadu maṇimāmai piṟangi aḷḷal payalai ūrginṟadu kangul ūḻigaḷē idellām inavē īrginṟa cakkarattu emperumān kaṇṇan taṇṇanduḻāy sārignṟa nannenjinār tandu pōna tani vaḷamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

īrginṟa – splitting (me) with (his) beauty; cakkarattu – having the divine disc; emperumān – one who is my svāmy (lord); kaṇṇan – kriṣṇa’s; taṇ – being cool; am – beautiful; tuḻāy – in divine tuḷasi; sārignṟa – being engaged with; nal – distinguished; nenjinār – mind; tandu pōna – given; tani – unique; vaḷam – wealth; maṇi – praiseworthy; māmai – beauty; pērginṟadu – is leaving; piṟangi – abundant; aḷḷal – being close; payalai – sickness due to love; ūrginṟadu – is spreading; kangul – night; ūḻigaḷē – grew like unending time; idellām – all these sickness etc; inavē – became hindrance

**Перевод:**

**kriṣṇa is holding the beautiful divine disc and is splitting me with his beauty. My mind desired such kriṣṇa’s cool and beautiful tuḷasi garland, and left me, following tuḷasi. Due to this, it made my beautiful complexion to change. Sickness due to love is spreading throughout my form. Nights lengthened to unending yugas (long periods of time). Entities such as breeze etc became hindrances for me.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In several pāsurams so far, āḻvār had considered śrīvaiṣṇavas as the leading man and composed the pāsurams. In this pāsuram, he is considering emperumān as the leading man. It is natural for union with emperumān to end with union with śrīvaiṣṇavas as also for union with śrīvaiṣṇavas to end with union with emperumān. In the pāsurams before the 6th pāsuram of tadāviya ambum, he had mentioned about his distress on account of separating from emperumān and from the 6th pāsuram tadāviya ambum till the 11th pāsuram he mentioned about his distress due to his doubt that the śrīvaiṣṇavas will separate from him. In this pāsuram, separation from emperumān is causing him distress. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pērginṟadu maṇimāmai** – the complexion in my distinguished form is changing. Just as it has been mentioned in the 5<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of tiruviruttam *māmai tiṟattukkolām*, the complexion of the form which he \[emperumān\] desired is receding. My complexion is getting destroyed as I keep seeing him. *maṇi māmai* refers to beautiful complexion. Is it proper for one to praise one’s own complexion? Since he \[emperumān\] praises her form saying “Oh the complexion! Oh the beauty!” there is nothing wrong in her praising it herself. Since she exists for his sake rather than for her sake, it is not improper in her praising something which is pleasing to him.

**piṟangi aḷḷal payalai** – instead of keeping the place, from where the complexion has left, barren, it has been filled up with paleness, a sign of sickness due to love. *piṟangi* refers to abundance, like a flood. *aḷḷal* refers to the fact that while desilting a well in which there is plenty of mud, the foot will sink when one steps on it, if emperumān himself comes to remove the paleness, he could not remove it. There is such an abundance of paleness.

**ūrginradu** – spreading fast, just like poison spreads. Just as someone comes surreptitiously when a powerful king leaves the private quarters of his queen or just as rāvaṇa came when iḷaiyaperumāl \[lakśmaṇa\] left the hermitage to go looking for śrī rāma, the paleness came spreading, when the beautiful complexion left me.

**kangul ūḻigaḷē** – night time felt as if it were lasting several ages; while the time spent before uniting with emperumān appeared like half a day and the time spent uniting with him appeared like a moment, the time separated from him appeared to last forever.

**idu ellām inavē** – apart from these, whatever affected her, whatever caused her distress are being mentioned. This includes entities such as love-bird and gentle breeze, which, in normal times, would have caused happiness. Whatever entity appeared to be sweet and favourable when sītāppirātti and śrī rāma were together in the forest, appeared to be inimical in the same place when they were separated.

**īrginṟa cakkaram** – affecting without any distinction between favourable and unfavourable persons; \[The divine disc\] affects because of its sentient quality. It will destroy those who are inimical towards it; it will destroy those who are dear to it, through separation, by going afar from them. It will kill those who are unfavourable to it through its power and bewilder those who are favourable through its beauty.

**cakkarattu emperumān** – through his beautiful divine hands and the way he held his beautiful divine discus, emperumān affirmed “He is my property” and enslaved me.

**kaṇṇan** – kriṣṇa. One could get the doubt whether he is with two shoulders. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam ślōkam *upasamhara sarvātman rūpamētac caturbhujam* – his beautiful form is with four shoulders.

**taṇṇanduḻāy sārginṟa** – my heart was fully engaged with the beautiful garland of tuḷasi which he had donned. Instead of falling at the feet of the primary entity \[bhagavān, emperumān\], my mind was engaged with the ultimate entity \[tuḷasi, likened to bhāgavatas, śrīvaiṣṇavas\].

**nannenjinār** – is it apt to refer to one’s heart as “*nalla nenju*”(good heart)? Even if they are one’s own children, if they are better than the parents in terms of engaging with emperumān, they should be praised. He \[āḻvār\] is in such a state that he is wandering without even realising that his dress is diśevelled. What he wants to do is to tell emperumān that emperumān is everything for him just as he had said in tiruvāimoḻi pāsuram “”*pūsum sāndu en nenjamē”* (my heart is the sandal paste for you), that he is all the enjoyment for him and hence he wants to be always with emperumān, singing and dancing about him.

**tandu pōna tani vaḷamē** – the loss of complexion is the wealth which emperumān left for him.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning) for the pāsuram: āḻvār is mercifully mentioning through the words of the leading lady, her inability to suffer separation from her leading man, since her heart has got fully engaged with him, as a result of which how she decayed being unable to bear the separation.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
