## 35. Palvayp piraippillai

*pālvāyp piṟaippiḷḷai okkalaikkoṇḍu pagal iḻanda mēl pāl disaippeṇ pulambuṟum mālai ulagaḷanda māl pāl tuḻāykku manam udaiyārkku nalgiṟṟai ellām sōlvān pugundu idu ōr panivādai tuḻāginṟadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pālvāy – having milk in its mouth; piṟai – crescent of moon; piḷḷai – child; okkalai – on the hip; koṇḍu – carrying; pagal – day time, the master; iḻanda – one who lost; mēl pāl – being on the western side; disai – as the direction; peṇ – who could be considered as a lady; pulambuṟum – crying; mālai – evening time; ulagu – worlds; aḷanda – one who occupied; māl pāl – with sarvēśvaran; tuḻāykku – fond of tuḷasi; manamudaiyārkku – those who have their minds \[on such tuḷasi\]; nalgiṟṟaiyellām – all those (including existence) which were given; sōlvān – to steal; pugundu – entering; idu – this; ōr – unique; pani – cold; vādai – northerly wind; tuḻāginṟadu – gently hugging

**Перевод:**

**The western direction is like a lady who has lost daytime, her husband, and was crying. She had on her waist, the moon who is like a child which had milk in its mouth. At this time, the cold northerly wind gently hugged people who were waiting to get the divine garland of tuḷasi from sarvēśvaran, who had seized all the worlds, with the intention of stealing from those people, whatever had been given by sarvēśvaran.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) The nāyaki’s mother gets worried on seeing the onset of evening, which is far worse than all the other sorrowful situations that the nāyaki has been in so far and wonders “How will this end for her?” All those things which the nāyaki enjoyed when she was together with the nāyakan, turned hostile for her now. All along the mother was distressed by the sorrows of her daughter. Now she feels sorry also for the lady who is in the western direction \[this is a simile for dusk time which brings with it, its own trails of sadness\]. This is similar to the situation of the pirātti who felt sorry for various naturally occurring events in tiruvāimoḻi 2-1 padigam “vāyum tiraiyugaḷum”. Everything that one looks at, becomes inimical. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pālvāyp pirāppiḷḷai** – The lady called as western direction, stood with the crescent moon on her waist, crying for having lost her husband, the daytime or sūriyan. She appears like one who has lost her husband recently, crying out for him, carrying her infant who cannot even suckle properly.

**pālvāy** – the child will wither if she removes her bosom from its mouth.

**pagal iḻanda** – this signifies the importance of the husband who has been lost (daytime or sūriyan). It is similar to losing someone such as manu or māndātā (these are great kings of the ikśvāku dynasty). When someone gets something of importance, it is said as “*pagaṟkandēn*” just like it is said in iraṇḍām tiruvandādi pāsuram 81 “*pagaṟkaṇḍēn nāraṇanaik kaṇḍēn*” (I saw śrīman nārāyaṇa who is like sun who never sets). Thus losing that *pagal* is very sorrowful.

**mēl pāl disaippeṇ** – this gives the importance of the *nāyaki*. When directions are explained, greatness is given to the western direction. The roar from the ocean on the western side is taken as the crying out of the lady in the western direction.

**ulagaḷanda . . . .**  – her mind is fully focussed on the divine tuḷasi garland which sarvēśvaran dons.

**mālai nalgiṟṟaiyellām** – all those which were given by the evening time ; vyākyātā calls this as giving existence and *santānasāpam* – in olden times, when kings waged war and won an enemy country, the king of that country would request the conqueror to leave at the least one son to continue the clan. Similarly, the evening time left her complexion behind for her.

**mālai – sōlvān . . . .** –  the northerly wind tries to steal the existence, complexion etc left behind by evening.  Similar to torturing those who torture others, the wind tries to steal existence.

**panivādai** – northerly wind which is poisoned. Since it belongs to the clan of *prāṇan* (vital air in the body; existence), it knows where the vital air is and it tries to steal it. When queried as to what it was doing, it would say “Are we destroying the world? We are only trying to take our relative with us” and tried to destroy her.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): Looking at the condition of āḻvār for whom even favourable entities appear to be inimical because of separation from emperumān, the śrīvaiṣṇavas are fearing as to what will happen to his existence.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 36. Tula nedum

*tuḻā nedum sūḻ iruḷ enṟu tan taṇ tār adu peyarā eḻā nedu ūḻi eḻunda ikkālattum īngu ivaḷō vaḻā nedum tunbattaḷ enṟu irangār ammanō ilangaik kuḻā nedu mādam iditta pirānār kodumaigaḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

than – belonging to self; taṇ – cool; tār adu – desire towards the divine tuḷasi; peyarā – as the reason; tuḻā – touching gently; nedum – completely; sūḻ – pervading; iruḷ enṟu – called as darkness; eḻā – without enervation; nedu – unending; ūḻi – kalpam (a day of brahmā); eḻunda – started; ikkālattum – during this time too; īngu – here; ivaḷ – this nāyaki; vaḻā – without missing; nedum tunbattaḷ enṟeṇṇi – thinking that she is suffering from great sorrow; irangār – he is not showing mercy; ilangai – in lankā; kuḻām – gathered in abundance; nedu mādam – huge mansions; iditta – one who destroyed; pirānār – the benefactor; kodumaigaḷ – cruel acts; ammanō – how terrible

**Перевод:**

**Night time came, like an unending kalpa kālam (the day of brahmā running into several millions of years) with such darkness and pervading everywhere, that one has to know objects only by feeling them and not by seeing them. She is unable to tolerate the distress of separation, thinking of emperumān’s divine tuḷasi garland while he remains unaware of her sorrows and is not showing mercy on her. How cruel is the quality of the benefactor who destroyed all the huge mansions in lankā!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) It is the basic nature of both the daughter of King janaka (sītāppirātti) and nammāḻvār to consider that all the benefits which they get are due to emperumān’s mercy and all the sorrows which they experience are due to their misfortune. When his existence cannot take place without emperumān’s presence, āḻvār doubts emperumān’s ability to protect him, considering his lowly situation. Just as it is said in śrī rāmāyaqṇam yudda kāṇḍam 26-24 “ēśaivāśam satē lankām svēnānīkēna marditum” (the army which went under the leadership of naḷa hastened (to destroy lankā) in which each monkey cried out “Only I will destroy” and went further) wind and night time competed with each other to destroy her (nāyaki, āḻvār in feminine mood). Let us go trou0gh the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tuḻā nedum sūḻ iruḷ enṟu** – the term *tuḻā* could mean both destruction and touching \[objects\] to feel; holding tightly on to an object which has been just found so that it does not slip out of one’s hands.

**sūḻ iruḷ** – darkness has pervaded everywhere, surrounding all places in such a way that no one can find a safe place to rest. While night time comprises 30 *nāḻigai* (one *nāḻigai* is 24 minutes; thus night time is 12 hours), āḻvār calls it as *nedum* (very long; unending) because for those in separation, it is called as *dīrkka yāmā triyāmā* (long period, composed of three yāmams, with each yāmam being about 3 hours duration).

**iruḷ enṟu** – the night time behaved like rāvaṇa who came in the form of a pious sage and once he knew that sītāppirātti was alone, changed his form to that of a demon; night time too set in like a normal event but started extending for a very long time.

**than taṇ tār adu peyarā** – making use of the opportunity when the *nāyaki* desired the cool, divine tuḷasi garland on  his divine shoulder.

**eḻā nedu ūḻi** – the time of *kalpam* which is unending. Even that *kalpam* will have an end but this \[night time\] is unending. Just like people from the malayāḷa dēśam will not leave a place until they complete the job for which they came, this night also seems to stay until it destroyed her.

**eḻunda** – appeared. Normally the term *ūḻi* would refer to *kalpam* only. Using *nigaṇḍu* (lexicon), the vyākyātā says *samvarda: praḷaya: kalpa:* to show that these terms have the same meaning. Thus, the night time extended like a *praḷayam* (deluge). śrī rangarāja stavam 2-41 says “*dayamānamanā:*” – Oh ranganātā! During the time of *praḷaya* your divine mind took pity, looking at the condition of the sentient and insentient entities (which were lying without name and form). If sarvēśvaran shows mercy, there will be an end to *praḷayam* too. However, since he is not showing his mercy, this night time, referred to as *praḷayam* is extending further.

**ikkālattum** – just as it is mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 28-18 “*sītātavēnyudgratam gruhītvā*”(sītāppirātti says I am going to end myself by hanging from the braid of my locks as a rope), she too is in a similar distressed condition.

**īngivaḷō** – is her condition similar to that of the samsāris (those who are in the materialistic realm) and their position in samsāram? They had lost their forms and senses during *praḷayam*. Here her existence is in question. There, it is related to primordial matter; here it is related to the ātmā. There it is *praḷayam* due to water; here it is *praḷayam* due to darkness. There, the water, which is visible, is destroying the form \[of sentient entities\], which is visible; here it is the invisible darkness which is trying to destroy the invisible ātmā. There, they did not realise the danger that they were in, only emperumān was aware of their condition; here, she fully knows the danger that she is in and she has the desire too for his protection.

**vaḻā nedum tunbattaḷ enṟu irangār** – he is not taking pity on her condition where she is in deep sorrow which will not go away. Just as it is mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 15-39 “*dukkēna bubudē sītām*” ((hanumān) recognised sītāppirātti who was in deep sorrow); hanumān could recognise sītāppirātti from her sorrowful mood. sarvēśvaran did not recognise the *nāyaki*’s sorrow. *karma* (deeds from previously encountered sins and virtues) would keep whittling down as it is experienced. But her sorrow is such that it will keep increasing as it is experienced. Since this sorrow is coming on the basis of *bhagavat ruci* (engagement with sarvēśvaran), it is not possible to alter it and it is not possible to make it to be without distinction. For one \[sarvēśvaran\] who is the opposite of all faults, is it not expected of him to remove the faults of those who are engaged with him and shower mercy on them? If affection \[from her\] is not needed, should her *ātmasvarūpam* (true nature of ātmā) too not be required? If he is steadfast in his stand that he will protect only during times of danger, is her anguished state of being separated from him, false?  Is she devoted to someone else for you not to show mercy on her? If it is a fault for us to move away from you, it is also a fault for you not to come towards us \[to protect us\].  If you come to us, our total dependence on you will be sustained and if you do not come to us, your state of being our lord will be harmed. Is it not the duty of the servants to create eminence for the lord? Creating eminence for one who is complete in all aspects \[such as sarvēśvaran\] is not to protect oneself under any situation. This has been demonstrated in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 31-22 when lakśmaṇa tells śrī rāma “*vaidarmyam nēha vidyatē*” (Make me your servant; there is nothing unrighteous in this. Besides I will be accomplishing my objective of being your servant). Due to the backing of śrī rāma’s powerful shoulders, just as one inserts one’s hand into the mouth of a poisonous snake, sītāppirātti, while being in aśōka vanam, told rāvaṇa as in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 22-16 “*tvam nīca: śaśavat smruta*:” (you, a lowly person, are said to be like a hare). For such a pirātti, the situation now is that she doubts his ability to protect her. The reason for this is because of her doubt on her own nature, similar to the condition in which sītāppirātti said as in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 38-48 “*mamaiva duśkrutam kincit…*” (it is because of the faults in me that the two brothers, śrī rāma and lakśmaṇa are neglecting me). If this is the case, why did she say that sarvēśvaran is not showering mercy on her? The vyākyāta explains this by saying that she has knowledge about his quality of showering mercy but not on her nature. In other words, she has not understood his anguish. His condition has been described in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 36-42  “*naivadamśān namaśākān*” (he does not even drive away the insects and flies crawling on his body). This is because of the reason mentioned in the same ślōkam 36-42 “*tvatgatēnāntarātmanā*” (his mind is fully engrossed in you), as told by hanuman to sītāppirātti. His mind is agitated like the agitation one sees in an ocean and not the agitation that takes place in the puddle of water under the hoof of a cow \[in other words, his distress is real and deep\]. While her condition was just as it was described in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 66-25 “*ūrdvam māsānam najīviśyē*” (I will not live for more than one month) his condition was described in sundara kāṇḍam 66-10 “*na jīvēyam kśaṇamapi*” (I cannot live for a moment more). Both spoke about killing themselves because of the desire that each knew of the other. The vyākyātā quotes a few references to prove that one cannot survive without the other, as in nānmugan tiruvandādi 7 “*inṟāga nāḷaiyēyāga inicciṟidu ninṟālum ninnaruḷ en pāladē nanṟāga nān unnai anṟi ilēn kaṇḍāy nāraṇanē nī ennai anṟi ilai*” (either today or tomorrow or even after a longer time than that, your grace will certain fall on me since for me, your servant, there is no existence other than through you and for you there is no one to be protected other than me). There is no nārāyaṇan without nāram (sentient entities). nārāyaṇan refers both to the nature \[being the lord etc\] and the qualities \[being with motherly forbearance, simplicity etc\]. One cannot speak of a complete person if there were no incomplete (or faulty) persons; there can be no lord without servants.

**irangār** – if he does not come, the quantum of sandalwood paste applied and its quantum which dries up and withers due to waiting for  his arrival, appear to be doubling.

**ammanō** – she says this word with the fear that she would have had if she had sighted a tiger or a snake. He is the entity who looks at everyone as “*suhrudam sarvabhūtānām*” (I am friendly towards all creatures), and she is scared of such an entity. taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli says “*ānandam brahmaṇō vidvān na bibhēti kutaścana*” (one who knows the extent of the happiness of *brahmam* (supreme being) does not fear anything). One who has known brahmam’s ānandam need not fear the sins that he has committed knowingly. Her situation is such that despite knowing about *brahmam*, she has to be scared. The final syllable *O* refers to the cruelty of her sorrow.

**ilangai . . . pirānār kodumaigaḷē** – while interpreting this, embār \[cousin of rāmānujar as well as the one who succeeded him in the ācārya guruparamparai\] would say “What happened to the one who destroyed the huge mansions of lankā for the sake of one lady, by bridging the ocean and shooting arrows which carried the names of śrī rāma and lakśmaṇa, and the one who destroyed the gardens such that all the coconut trees lost all their leaves and looked like tonsured heads?” tirumalai nambi \[maternal uncle of rāmānujar as well as one of his ācāryas\] would interpret this as “He destroyed lankā out of jealousy since it appeared more beautiful than ayōdyā and it was not due to a lady”.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 37. Kodungal silaiyar

*kodungāl silaiyar niṟaikōḷ uḻavar kolaiyil veyya kadungāl iḷaijñar tudipadum kavvaittu aruvinaiyēn nedungālamum kaṇṇan nīṇmalarppādam paravippeṟṟa todungāl osiyumidai iḷamān senṟa sūḻ kadamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

aruvinaiyēn – I, having cruel sins; nedungālamum – for a long time; kaṇṇan – kriṣṇa’s; nīḷ – being long; malar – like a flower; pādam – divine feet; paravi­ – after praying; peṟṟa – got as a daughter; todungāl – merely on touching; osiyum – quivering; idai – having a waist; iḷamān – this girl who is like a youthful deer; senṟa – the place that she went to; sūḻ – surrounded (by heat); kadam – the path; kodum – curved; kāl – having leg; silaiyar – having bow; nirai – cows; kōḷ – those who steal; uḻavar – farmers; kolaiyil – in harming; veyya – being cruel; kadungāl – having legs which will move fast; iḷaijñar – youthful hunters; tudi – small drum (tambourine); padum – created from; kavvaittu – had a sound

**Перевод:**

**I, who am having cruel sins, prayed to the long, lotus-like divine feet of kriṣṇa and got this daughter. She has a waist which will quiver by merely being touched and she jumps around like a youthful deer. This girl has, however, left me. The place that she went to is a cruel forest – which has people having curved bows, who steal cows as their profession, young people who kill others and who have legs which will carry them fast and from whose drums (tambourines) a sound is created.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) The nāyaki who was distressed after being separated from her nāyakan, could not wait till he returned and left on her own \[in search of him\]. She went through a cruel forest which had animals and people, capable of harming, sounds which caused fear in one, and paths which were dangerous. Her mother found out that her daughter was not in her bed and gets bewildered thinking “Alas! She has gone through a dangerous path. What is she doing?” In the case of the daughter in tirukkōḷūr (tiruvāimoḻi 6-7) decad, the mother knew as to where her daughter had gone. But in the case of this mother, she did not know as to where her daughter had gone and feels distressed. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kodum kāl silaiyar** – just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāṇḍam 6-21 “*yōdānām agni kalpānām pēśālānām māhēśuṇām*” which describes those who are guarding the city of ayōdyā (firebrand like skilful warriors), these people always carry their curved bows. The term *kodum kāl silaiyar* has two interpretations – it could refer to those who have bows with curved legs or it could refer to those who carry bows and who have cruel legs. In the former case, it would mean that they are constantly ready with drawn bow in order to harm others. For the bow, cruelty refers to its nature of sending another entity (arrow) to cause harm and does not go by itself to harm someone.

**silaiyar** – they keep carrying their bows, whether it is during the time of taking food or when they are in privacy with their wives. The *nāyaki* is one who has known sarvēśvaran who, just as it is mentioned in periya tiruvandādi 86 “*kai kaḻalā nēmiyān*” always has the divine disc on his hand. This is his authenticity. Since she has moved with him, she is not afraid of anything. That divine disc is one which sustains everyone while this (the bow carried by these people in the cruel forest) is a harmful entity. Just as it is said in śrī rāmāyaṇam adyōdyā kāṇḍam 60-20 “*nāhārayati santrāsam bāhū rāmasya samśritā*” (sītāppirātti does not fear the wild animals in the forest since she is protected by the broad shoulders of śrī rāma), those who are under the protection of his divine disc and divine shoulders need not fear anything. However, that is not the case here.

**nirai kōḷ uḻavar** – for these people, stealing cows is a profession. However she is someone who has known sarvēśvaran for whom, as it is said in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam “*gō brāhmaṇa hitāyaca*” – protecting cows and brāhmaṇas is normal.

**kolaiyil veyya** – these people exhibit anger when killing others. They kill just to see how their victims tremble when being killed. Alternatively they will torture their victims, ask them to work for them till they die. periya tirumoḻi 11-8-6 says “*idaiyan eṟinda maram*” (the tree cut down by the shepherds for their sheep would be fresh at one place and dry at another). These people will harm their victims similarly, by making them half dead and half alive, with pain. In the case of sarvēśvaran, though he also aims his arrows at his victims, it is only to carry out a beneficial act for them, just as it is said in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 24-24 “*sanjāta bhāśpa paravīra hantā rāmō muhūrtam vimanā babhūva*” ((on hearing sugrīva’s words) śrī rāma had moist eyes and was for a moment perturbed), after he killed vāli.

**kadungāl iḷaijñar** – if they were to harm someone, they will run a hundred miles and return a hundred miles, running. Such is the strength and speed of their legs. One would be fearful of them on just seeing them; there is no need for them to act. Through āyurvēda medicines, they will keep themselves youthful, always.

**tudipadum kavvaittu** – having the sound of drums (similar to tambourine). If she (*nāyaki*) goes to such a place, only her physical form will get destroyed. But if she stays in her place, her ātmā will get destroyed through the words of her mother.

**aruvinaiyēn** – it is not that he is not keen on coming to her; it is also not that she doesn’t have the desire to be united with him. They are not uniting with each other only because of my cruel sins \[says the *nāyaki*’s mother\]. This is similar to the śrī rāmāyaṇa ślōkam “*math pāpamēva atra nimittam āsīt*” – it is only due to my sin.

**nedungālam . . . .** – I had vorśipped kriṣṇa who would not tolerate any delay in surrendering to him \[vyākyātā says that the word *sakrut* in śrī rāma *caraka ślōkam* (ultimate hymn) *sakrudēva prapannāya* is to be considered as immediately; kriṣṇa doesn’t want even this delay\]. Just as dēvaki had carried out lot of penance to give birth to kriṣṇa, I had done lot of penance to beget her. However, not realising about herself or about my penance to beget her, she has gone. śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 66-3 speaks of the penance carried out by daśarata to beget śrī rāma “*mahatā tapasā rāma mahatā ca api karmaṇa*” (Oh rāma, you were born because of the great penance and great acts carried out by daśarata).

**nīṇmalarppādam** – his divine feet are such that they will go towards the place where his followers are present. They are so easily approachable. Those divine feet considered attaining his followers as their \[the divine feet’s\] benefit.

**malar** – soft; beautiful.

**paravippeṟṟa** – obtained after offering prayers of various types. It is similar to attaining a treasure after lot of efforts.

**todungāḷ osiyum idai** – her waist was such that it would snap if touched. The *nāyakan* enjoyed that waist merely by looking at it and not by touching it after realising its greatness. tiruvāimoḻi 6-7-8 “*osinda nuṇṇidai mēl kaiyai vaittu nondu nondu*” (keeping the hand on the already quivering slender waist (out of tiredness) and hurting it more and more). Her waist was such that one would doubt its existence. By looking at the form above and below the waist, one could surmise that waist should be there in between. If one were to keep the hand as a support for that waist, the hand would like a mountain in comparison with the wait; it was that slender.

**iḷamān senṟa** – she could not tolerate any of the difficulties which lay in that path

**sūḻ kadam** – the forest which had pervaded in a cruel way.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 38. Kadamayinagal

*kadamāyinagaḷ kaḻittut tan kāl vanmaiyāl palanāḷ tadamāyina pukku nīr nilai ninṟa tavam idu kol kudamādiya maṇṇum viṇṇum kulunga ulagaḷanda nadamādiya perumān uruvottana neelangaḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

neelangaḷ – blue Indian water lilies; immaṇṇum – this earth; viṇṇum – upper worlds; kulunga – to move in amazement; kudamādi – dancing with pots (in kriṣṇāvatāram); ulagu – worlds; aḷanda – measured (in trivikrmāvatāram); nadamādiya – one who roamed; perumān – sarvēśvaran’s; uru – with divine form; ottana – are equivalent; idu – the reason for this; kadamāyinagaḷ – three debts owed to celestial entities, forefathers and sages; kaḻittu – fulfilling them; than – one’s; kālvanmaiyāl – strength of legs; palanāḷ – for a long time; tadamāyina – known as ponds; pukku – entering; nīr – in water; nilai ninṟa – residing for a long time; tavangol – could be the penance

**Перевод:**

**In kriṣṇāvataram he danced with pots such that this earth and the upper worlds shook with amazement; in trivikramāvatāram, he measured all the worlds. The blue lilies are having a complexion much similar to the sarvēśvaran who roamed like this. The reason for this could be that they paid back their three types of debts, entered water bodies with the strength of their legs, stayed there for a very long time and carried out penance.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) When yōgis (those who are meditating) are in meditation for a very long time, they will turn their attention towards worldly matters for a short time, in order to engage more deeply in meditation once they resume it. In the same way, he too \[nammāḻvār\] turned his attention towards worldly objects in order to enhance his experience with matters relating to emperumān. However, since he has been showered with  knowledge and devotion without any illusion, even the worldly objects which he saw reminded him of emperumān and tortured him further. It is as difficult to turn the āḻvārs away from the path of vēdas, and towards worldly matters just as it is difficult to turn us away from worldly matters, and towards the path of vēdas. Just as it is difficult to turn us away from materialistic pursuit and towards matters relating to bhagavān, it is equally difficult to turn āḻvārs away from matters relating to bhagavān and towards materialistic pursuits. embār, the disciple of rāmānujar, mercifully mentioned these two. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kadamāyinagal kaḻittu** –  just as it is mentioned in manusmriti 6-35 “*ruṇānitrīṇyabākrutya”* – after fulfilling the three debts owed. yajūśi kāṇḍam says “*brahmacaryēṇa ruśibhya***:** *yajñēna dēvēbhya prajayāpitrubhya”* – through *brhamacaryam* (observing the tenets of being a bachelor), one pays back the debt owed to riśis (sages); through yāgams (rituals involving *agni*, fire) one pays back the celestial entities and through siring children, one pays back the forefathers. Similarly, the blue lilies pay back their debts through the distress caused to their bodies in hot season, at the hottest time. Here, the body, hot season and hot time are the debts owed.

**than kāl vanmai ….** – through their efforts (strength of their legs), through their penance, considering only their existence, even during cold times, whether it is water formed under the hoof of a cow or in large water bodies like ocean etc, they stay up to their neck and carry out penance. Did you get a similarity with the complexion of sarvēśvaran due to the strength of that penance?

**kudamādi** – dancing with pots in the courtyard such that everyone could steal his beauty \[with their eyes\], snipping the pride of covherd folk.

**immaṇṇum** – instead of benefitting some people or people in a street, he danced such that all the world could enjoy,

**kulunga** – if rudra (Sivan) dances, the world trembles in fear since he is the celestial entity for annihilation. But if sarvēśvaran dances, the word forgets itself and rejoices.

**ulagaḷandu nadamādiya** – when sarvēśvaran incarnated as trivikrama and measured the worlds, indra accepted his lands which he had lost to mahābali; mahābali felt happy that he had magnanimously donated the lands \[which were not his in the first place\]; sarvēśvaran felt that he had carried out the act of retrieval as a benefit for himself.

**uruvottana neelangaḷē** – was it through paying back the debts, entering water bodies through the strength of your legs and carrying out penance over long periods that you attained equality with emperumān in all aspects of his divine form?

The vyākyātā says that the knowledge that āḻvār had was such that even if he wanted to look at worldly objects due to separation from emperumān, even those objects remind him of emperumān only.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 39. Neelat tadavaraimel

*neelattadavarai mēl puṇḍarīga nedum tadangaḷ pōlap polindu emakku ellāvidattavum pongu munnīr jñālappirān visumbukkum pirān maṟṟum nallōr pirān kōlangariya pirān  empirān kaṇṇin kōlangaḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pongu – agitating; munnīr – composed of water from three sources, viz from river, from spring and from rain; jñālam – for earth; pirān – being the benefactor; visumbukkum – for the people in the upper worlds too; pirān – being the benefactor; maṟṟum – further; nallōr – for muktātmās and nityasūris, who enjoy sarvēśvaran’s guṇas (auspicious qualities); pirān – being the benefactor; kariya – having a dark bluish complexion; kōlam – having a divine form; pirān – being the benefactor; empirān – being the benefactor for me, his; tadam – huge; kaṇṇin – eyes’; kōlangaḷ – beauty; neelam – like a bluish gem; varai mēl – on top of the mountain; puṇḍarīgam – of lotus; nedum – expansive; tadangaḷ pōla – like ponds; polindu – full of beauty; emakku – for us; ellāvidattavum – at all places

**Перевод:**

**He is the benefactor of earth which has three types of agitating water – water from river, water from spring and water from rain; he is the benefactor of brahmā et al who are in the upper worlds; he is the benefactor of nityasūris and muktātmās; he has a dark bluish complexion; he is the benefactor for people like me. Such empeurmān’s beautiful eyes are like a huge pond filled with lotus flowers atop a huge mountain of bluish gem, shining in all the places.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, the blue coloured objects appeared like the divine form of sarvēśvaran and tortured her. The divine form established itself in a superior part, the divine eyes. āḻvār describes as to how the divine eyes tortured him such that he could not move away from them. As an example of how each part reminded of another and finally the whole, the vyākyātā cites śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 36-4 “gruhītvā prēkśamānā” (taking hold \[of that ring\] and looking at it, sītāppirātti thought of the finger over which the ring had been placed, and felt as if she had reached her husband). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**neelattadavarai mēl** – just as it is said in tirumālai 2 “*paccai māmalai pōḷ mēni*” (the divine form is like a mountain made of emerald), his divine form is described here as a hill made of bluish gem.

**puṇḍarīga . . .**  – his eyes were like huge ponds of lotus flowers which had occupied his entire divine form; his divine form appeared to be full of eyes. The eyes of the beholder would focus on only on those eyes and will not see any other limb. This is something similar to śrī rangarājastavam 1-98 “*krupayā parayā kariśyamāṇē sakalāngam*” (the divine eyes of periya perumāḷ wanted to shower mercy; hence they attempted to change all the limbs of periya perumāḷ into divine eyes). Those eyes were similar to the ones mentioned in cāndōjña upaniśat 16-7 “*tasyayatā kapyāsam puṇḍarīkśam ēvamakśiṇī*” (they eyes were similar to lotus flower which has just then blossomed on seeing sūriyan (sūrya)).

**emakku ellāvidattavum** – those eyes followed everywhere in such a way that there is no place on earth which had not been visited upon. If one were to close the eyes so that one need not see those eyes which torment the beholder, those eyes will enter the beholder’s eyes; they will pervade everywhere. They have come to win me over, in this way. This could be compared with [tiruvāimoḻi 7-7-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/10/thiruvaimozhi-7-7-1-ezhaiyar-avi/) “*sūḻavum tāmarai nāṇmalar pōl vandu tōnṟum kaṇḍīr*” (they will appear from all sides, like just then blossomed lotus flower) as well as [tiruvāimoḻi 8-9-1](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/05/thiruvaimozhi-8-9-1-karu-manikka-malai/) “*karumāṇikka malaimēl maṇittadandāmaraikkādugaḷ pōl*” (like huge forest of lotus flowers atop a hill of dark carbuncle).

**pongu munnīr jñālappirān** – he is one who keeps rendering help to those who are on this earth which is surrounded by agitating oceans which are made up of three types of water – from rivers, from springs and from rain. As an example of rendering help, the vyākyātā cites the instance when he kept the worlds inside his stomach during the time of deluge and protected all.

**visumbukkum pirān** – he is also the controller of dēvatās (celestial entities) such as brahmā who had given birth to these worlds.

**maṟṟum nallōr pirān** – he is the controller of nityasūris too.

**kōlam kariya pirān** – the divine form of emperumān is totally subservient to those divine eyes. It is with the divine form that emperumān subjugates the nityasūris and makes them bewildered always.

**empirān** – just as he is their lord, he is my lord too.

**kaṇṇin kōlangaḷē** – the distinguished eyes of one who is my benefactor. Just as lotus ponds have occupied the entire mountain of bluish gems, these eyes shine in all the places that I see.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 40. Kolappagal kaliru

*kōlappagal kaḷiṟonṟu kaṟpuyyak kuḻām virittaneelakkangul kaḷiṟellām niṟaindana nēriḻaiyīrjñālappon mādin maṇāḷan tuḻāy nangaḷ sūḻ kuḻaṟkēēlappunanindu ennaimār emmai nōkkuvadu enṟu kolō*

**Пословный перевод:**

kōlam – being beautiful; pagal – sūriyan (sūrya – sun); onṟu kaḷiṟu – an elephant; kal – in the mountain of sunset; puyya – as it entered; kuḻām – as a herd; virinda – expansive; neelam – blue coloured; kangul – called as night; kaḷiṟu ellām – all the elephants; niṟaindana – abounded; nēr – being apt; iḻaiyīr – those who have ornaments; jñālam – for bhūdēvi; ponmādu – and for śrīdēvi; maṇāḷan – śriya:pati, consort’s; tuḻāy – divine tuḷasi; nangaḷ – our; sūḻ – dense; kuḻaṟkē – locks; ēla – ahead; punaindu – stringing; ennaimār – our mothers; emmai – us; nōkkuvadu – protect; enṟu kol – when will it be

**Перевод:**

**A unique elephant, called as the most beautiful sūriyan, entered the western setting mountains. Immediately, a herd of elephants, called as night, with dark bluish colour, filled up the entire place. Oh my friends who are donning apt ornaments! On which day will our mothers apply the divine tuḷasi garland of aḻagiya maṇavāḷan, who is the consort of śrīdēvi and bhūdēvi, on our dense locks and decorate us?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) She (the nāyaki), who had spoken about the supreme being who controls everyone and everything, has reached a stage where she speaks about one noon time. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kōlappagal kaḷiru onṟu kaṟpuyya** – A unique elephant called as noon, which gives happiness to those who see it, entered the setting mountains on the western side. The term noon refers to sūriyan. Why is the noon giving happiness? During noon time, each of the five senses is focussed on its work, enabling the person to sustain himself/herself since the person could bear the anguish of separation \[when the senses are not united in torturing the person\].

**kuḻām virinda . . .** – just as a paramour enters the private quarters of the queen as soon as the king departs, night time, in large gathering, entered as soon as sūriyan left. Since the five senses now get together and torture the person, reminding her of separation from her *nāyakan*, āḻvār refers to the night time as elephants. But why refer to them \[elephants\] in plural? Since a night extends to many kalpams, when in separation, he refers to them in plural.

**nēriḻaiyīr** – Oh those who have donned distinguished ornaments! When the *nāyaki* is in distress (having been separated from her *nāyakan*) will her friends who have come to console her, bedeck themselves with ornaments? She is like a helpless woman who has been stomped by the elephant called as night. Her friends are coming to encourage her to sustain herself \[till her *nāyakan* returns\]; hence they dress themselves up nicely with ornaments etc.

**jñālappon mādin maṇāḷan** – if he were alone, I can feel distressed thinking that there is none to recommend to him about me. However, I am in a sorrowful state despite the eminent bhūdēvi and śrīdēvi being together with him. When someone is wealthy, they will be without fear since they have enough wealth to take care of their expenses. In the same way, the *nāyakan* feels that even if the *nāyaki* is not around, he has bhūdēvi and śrīdēvi with him.

**mādin maṇāḷan** – he is in a bewildered condition since he has bhūdēvi who gives him all that he enjoys. The *nāyaki* feels that the he will have bewilderment towards her too, just as he has towards bhūdēvi.

**tuḻāy . . .** – the *nāyaki* does not desire the garland which he had donned as a bachelor. She covets the divine tuḷasi garland which has got fully wrinkled in the union between sarvēśvaran and bhūdēvi. She is just like a prince who would dip flowers in a fragrant material to enhance its sweet smell. cāndōjña upaniśat says of him that he is  *sarvaganda: sarvarasa:* (he is the repository of all that is fragrant and all that is tasty). She is referred to in tiruvāimoḻi 10-10-2 as “*vāsam sey pūnguḻalāḷ*” (it is pirtātti who gives fragrance to flowers and tuḷasi). Thus the *nāyaki* desires the garland which has been vrinked in the union of the *divyadampati* (divine couple).

**nangaḷ sūḻkuḻaṟkēā ēlappunaindu** – there is no other remedy for her. She wants to don that garland before her existence is destroyed.

**ennaimār** – she is looking up to her mothers who are keen on separating her from her *nāyakan* to get her the remedy which she needs to sustain herself. It is similar to asking for water from a person who is shutting the channel which brings in water.

**emmai nōkkuvadu enṟu kolō** – he \[āḻvār in feminine mood\] is asking as to when he will get the protection which he needs.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 41. Enrum punvadai

*enṟum punvādai idu kaṇḍaṟidum ivvāṟu vemmai onṟum uruvum suvadum teriyilam ōngu asurar ponṟum vagai puḷḷaiyūrvān aruḷ aruḷāda innāḷ manṟil niṟai paḻi tūṟṟi ninṟemmai vankāṟṟu adumē*

**Пословный перевод:**

enṟum – always; idu – this; punvādai – gentle breeze; kaṇḍaṟidum – we have experienced; ivvāṟu – like this; vemmai – having cruelty; uruvum – form; suvadum – nature; onṟum – any entity; teriyilam – we are not aware of; ōngu – grown well; asurar – demons; ponṟum vagai – to be annihilated; puḷḷai – garuda; ūrvān – emperumān who conducts; aruḷ aruḷāda – not showing mercy; innāḷ – this day; manṟil – in the open; niṟai – being complete; paḻi – slander; tūṟṟi – to pervade; ninṟu – without feeling tired; van – cruel; kāṟṟu – breeze; emmai – us; adum – is harming

**Перевод:**

**We have experienced this northerly breeze. However, we have not known any entity like this which brings with it cruel heat, form and nature. This day, sarvēśvaran, who conducts garuda who annihilates the well grown demons, is not showering his mercy. This cruel breeze is spreading slanderous words in the open and is harming me, without moving out of this place.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, at the end, the nāyaki said “emmai nōkkuvadu enṟu kolō?” She was wondering as to who will be her protector. A northerly breeze affects such a nāyaki; wind, which is the sustaining entity for life, is harming her. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**enṟum punvādai idu kaṇḍaṟidum** – it is similar to having been born in prison and brought up only there. *punvādai* is gentle breeze.

**idu kaṇḍaṟidum** – we have not seen anything like this. In other words, we have not seen anything other than the northerly breeze which is harming. Why speak about the breeze which has been harming? Various other entities are also carrying on with what the breeze had been doing – harming her by amplifying the anguish of separation. They are all of the same species in so far as harming her is concerned.

**ivvāru . . .** – We have not known any other entity which is as cruel as this.

**ōngu asurar . . .** – the demons are not like those who are withering in separation from sarvēśvaran, are not like those who carry out all the services to him; they are the epitome of *rajas* and *tamas* (qualities of passion and ignorance, respectively). sarvēśvaran conducts garuda such that these demons are annihilated; he is not showing his mercy today so that this breeze could be harmed just like he makes the demons to perish under the hoofs of garuda.

**aruḷ aruḷāda innāḷ** – on this day when he did not shower his mercy, like a mother refuses to nurse her infant.

**manṟil niṟai . . .** – it is not only that the breeze did not let her unite with her *nāyakan*, it also brought a slander on her. It made the people say “It seems that she was suffering in separation from her *nāyakan* and the moment the breeze touched her, she is quivering; her qualities of shyness, fear etc are like this…”

**niṟai paḻi** – the world did not talk slanderously about her alone. It even spoke disparagingly about the *nāyakan* saying “She is withering in breeze; even then, he is not coming to her rescue”.

**ninṟemmai van kāṟṟu adumē** – it does not leave after distressing her on one occasion or in one type. It continues to do that.

**emmai** –  I did not go anywhere near it so that it could make an attempt to grab me. This strong breeze came to end my life.

**van kāṟṟu** – it is a breeze which did not have affability.

**adumē** – it is not even considering my femininity. It is not even thinking that it is torturing a lady.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): this pāsursm narrates as to how despite staying in samsāram for a very long time, as engagement with emperumān starts increasing, compatibility with samsāram starts disappearing.

## 42. Van karraraiya

*van kāṟṟaṟaiya orungē maṟindu kidandu alarnda menkāṟ kamalattadam pōl melindana maṇṇum viṇṇum enkāṟku aḷavinmai kāṇmin enbān ottu vān nimirnda tanpāṟ paṇinda en pāl empirāna tadangaṇgaḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

maṇṇum – the earth; viṇṇum – the upper worlds; enkāṟku – for my divine feet; aḷavinmai – are not apt; kāṇmin – please see; enbān ottu – as if saying so; vān – in the sky; nimirnda – growing; tanpāl – towards him; paṇinda – vorśipped; en pāl – (caused) towards me; empirāna – sarvēśvaran’s; tadam – expansive; kaṇgaḷ – divine eyes; van – bring cruel; kāṟṟu – wind; aṟaiya – to blow; orungē – towards one side; maṟindu kidandu – having fallen; alarnda – blossoming; men – soft; kāl – having a creeper; kamalam – lotus flowers’; tadam pōl – like ponds; polindana – were full of beauty

**Перевод:**

**I vorśipped the divine feet of sarvēśvaran’s, of which he said “See, neither the earth nor the upper words would equal my divine feet”. How were his divine eyes towards such me? They bent towards me, just like the lotus flowers with long creepers in a pond would, when a strong breeze blows, bend towards one side.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) She said that it is difficult for her to survive the onslaught of this breeze and that she would perish. As soon as she said this, he showered his mercy on her. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**van kāṟṟu . . .**  – *menkāl kamalam* refers to a lotus whose stem is so slender that it cannot withstand even a gently blowing breeze. *van kāṟṟu* refers to a breeze which blows hard without taking into consideration the gentleness of this lotus.

**orungē** – on one side alone. In other words, the divine mercy of emperumān was oriented towards her alone. If he changes his glance to some other direction, the lotus flowers (divine eyes) will lose their beauty.

**maṟindu kidandu alarnda** – if a samsāri (dweller of materialistic realm) comes towards him, sarvēśvaran looks at him with mercy. If he wishes to look at even pirātti, after that, he cannot. tiruvāimoḻi 2-6-2 says “e*ngum pakkam nōkkaṟiyān*” (he did not look in any other direction)

**menkāl kamalam** – śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 19-34 says “*prakrutayā dharma śeelasya rāghavasya*” (śrī rāma is one who, by his nature, treads the path of righteousness). In this ślōkam, the word *dharma* refers to śri rāma being of gentle nature. śrī rāma asks the monkeys “What is the way to cross this ocean which is like a hurdle between us and lankā?” śrī vibhīśaṇāḻvān, since he had recently carried out śaraṇāgati (act of surrendering) to śrī rāma and had got the benefit of that act, since he knew that śaraṇāgati is the means for all advantages and since he knew that it grants highest benefits, told śrī rāma to surrender to the ocean. The greatness of śaraṇāgati is that even an insentient entity will grant what has been asked for, when a person performs śaraṇāgati to it. Among the various means to captivate the hearts of great people, there is nothing higher than śaraṇāgati. If it can be effective with sarvēśvaran who does not have a clan or limitations, should one doubt whether it will be effective with those who are having restrictions? śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 19-31 “*śaraṇam … kartum arhati*” (the ocean will be inclined to do what śrī rāma had surrendered for). śrī vibhīśaṇāḻvān had seen the effectiveness of śaraṇāgati with the disciples of sage vasiśta \[srī rāma and lakśmaṇa\]. Hence he firmly believed that it will be effective with an insentient entity such as ocean too. The vyākyāta cites a tamiḻ proverb to buttress this belief – *erudu keduttārkkum ēḻē kadukkāy* – for the one who lost his bull, take seven gall-nuts to retrieve it. The story goes like this – a person had lost his bull; he went to another person and asked him whether he will be able to find it. The other person told him, mischievously, to eat seven gall-nuts and that he would find it. So the owner of the bull ate the gall-nuts and waited at the tank bund. The bull per chance came there. Thinking that he got the bull back because he ate the gall-nuts the person went home happily. On the way back home, a lady, who had not had a child till then, asked of him to giver her a remedy for it. This man told her to ask his friend who had suggested to him to eat the gall-nuts. That person told her too to eat seven gall-nuts. She did that and she conceived. In other words, a deed carried out with deep belief fructifies into desired action.

**menkāl kamalam** – there is no need for breeze to blow to move these lotus flowers. Their stems will sway on their own; they are that soft. In other words, for sarvēśvaran to shower his mercy with his divine eyes, it is not necessary that one should be an *ārtaprapannan* (one who craves to surrender to get mōkśam immediately). sarvēśvaran will do that on his own. Is it not required to surrender through one’s mind? vyākyātā responds to this doubt by quoting śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-3 “*mitrabhāvēna samprāptam na tyajēyam*” (I do not desert anyone who comes with a friendly appearance, even if he has a defect). Instead of analysing whether vibhīśaṇāḻvān has true friendliness or not towards him, śrī rāma says as in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-31 “*bala vīryam vināśanam*” (It is enough that he came with a friendly demeanour. If I do not accept him, my strength and valour will get devastated). If sarvēśvaran demands something from one who comes to surrender to him, which he does not have, then it would result in his not being easily vorśippable. sarvēśvaran has the nature of accepting whatever the person who comes to surrender to him, has. śrī rāma says in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-28 “*ārtō vā yadivā drupta parēśam śaraṇam gata*” (whether he is oppressed or arrogant, if one comes seeking protection from others …). If a person comes saying as in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 17-16 “*rāghavam śaraṇam gata*” (I take refuge under śrī rāma), it is enough for sarvēśvaran. When he heard such words from śrī vibhīśaṇāḻvān, śrī rāma immediately fully realised the tenderness in vibhīśaṇa’s heart. sugrīva thought “These are mere words; his thoughts are otherwise. Even though śrī rāma considers him as *ārta prapannan* he is indeed *druptan* only. He has something in his mind and speaks differently; he should be killed”. An *ārtan* is one who thinks that he should live only if he attains sarvēśvaran; otherwise, he would die. *druptan* is one who has arrogance in him and says that he has come to take refuge, not with his full heart but with an arrogance. Whether one is an *ārtan* or a *druptan* the benefit is the same. This is because the benefit does not accrue out of the nature of the one who surrenders but based on the greatness of the act of surrendering and the greatness of the one to whom one surrenders. How does *drupti* act like a means similar to *ārti*? While the one who has *ārti* has *ākincanyam* (not having anything on his own) as a natural behaviour, in the case of one who has *drupti* sarvēśvaran thinks “Since he does not have *ākincanyam*, let us go on our own and show him mercy”. Thus whether one is an *ārtan* or a *druptan*, if he comes to take refuge, as said in śrī rāmāyaṇam 18-28 “*parēśām śaraṇam gata*” (one who comes to take refuge from others), he will be protected, even if he is from the enemy side. periya tirumoḻi 1-9-3 says “*konṟēn* . . . *anṟē vandadaindēn*” (Oh the lord of tiruvēngadam! I killed many people and immediately, before even cleaning the blood stain, I reached you to take refuge). śrī rāma says in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-28 “*prāṇān parityajya rakśitavya*” – he should be protected even if one were to give his life, ireseptective of whether one who comes to surrender is *ārtan* or *druptan*. The above mentioned ślōkam ends with the worlds “*krutātmanā*” – one who has a disciplined mind, one who stands in the path of righteousness. Thus, śrī rāma gave refuge to śrī visbhīśaṇāḻvān not only to protect him but also to safeguard his nature of being one who does not cross the path of righteousness. If one surrenders to an ācāryan (guru; teacher) in order to practice the path of righteousness, he has to do like this only.

**menkāl kamalattadam poḷ polindana empirāna tadangaṇgaḷ**

Whose eyes are they?

**maṇṇum viṇṇum . . .** – they are of one who stands firmly saying “See that this world and the worlds above are too little to be measured by my foot”. He filled the entire ethereal layers with his divine form. If he had said that “I will keep my divine feet on your heads when I measure these”, just as rāvaṇa said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 36-11 “*na namēyam*” (\[rāvaṇa told mālyavān, who came to offer him good counsel that\] he would not bend before anyone even if he were to be cut into two), the people of the world would have refused. Hence he said “Look at this amazing act” \[and measured the worlds, keeping his divine feet on everyone’s head even before they realised it\]. But why say that here? This is to illustrate the fact that sarvēśvaran, on his own, without any of the worldly people asking him to or taking any efforts on their own, took incarnation as trivikrama and kept his divine feet on his own, on their heads. In other words, he took the efforts for them to get the benefit. This brings out his quality of *menkāl kamalam*, viz. without the sentient entity taking any effort, sarvēśvaran remains the means on his own.

**vān nimirnda than kāl paṇinda enpāl** – *ānukūlya sankalpam* is the steadfastness with which one remains as favourable to sarvēśvaran. For this, sarvēśvaran takes the effort himself. Initially \[during trikrama avatāram\] he bowed to king mahābali, then made mahābali to bow to him and kept his divine feet on mahābali’s head saying “You were favourable to me” and showed his mercy.

**than pāl paṇinda** – this is similar to *van kāṟṟu aṟaiya*

**en pāl** – this is similar to saying *orungē maṟindu kidandu alarnda*

**empirāna tadakkangaḷē** – he took the efforts for mahābali to get the benefit. That sarvēśvaran feels happy that the benefit is his, is clear from the blossoming of his divine eyes.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 43. Kannum sendamarai

*kaṇṇum sendāmarai kaiyum avai adiyō avaiyē vaṇṇam kariyadōr māl varai pōnṟu madi vigaṟpāl viṇṇum kadandu umbar appal mikku maṟṟeppāl yavarkkum eṇṇum idattaduvō empirānadu eḻil niṟamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kaṇṇum – divine eyes too; sendāmarai – are like reddish coloured lotus flower; kaiyum – divine hands too; avai – are like that lotus; adi – divine feet too; avai – are like that lotus; vaṇṇam – divine form; kariyadu – being black; ōr – unique; māl – huge; varai pōnṟu – like a mountain; empirānadu – sarvēśvaran, our benefactor, his; eḻil – beautiful; niṟam – complexion; madi vigaṟpāl – due to differences in intellect; viṇṇum – dwellers of svargam (heaven); kadandu – going past; umbar – brahmā et al, who live beyond these people; appāl – going further; mikku – going wondrously; maṟṟu – further; eppāl – pervading everywhere (through their knowledge); yavarkkum – for nityasūris too; eṇṇum – for thinking; idattaduvō – is it a matter?

**Перевод:**

**His divine eyes are like a reddish coloured lotus flower; his divine hands and divine feet are also like the lotus flower. His divine form is like a dark, huge mountain which is without any parallel. The beauty of such sarvēśvaran, who is a benefactor for us all, is past the entities who are dwelling in svargam, past brahmā et al, who live beyond them, and is also past the knowledge of nityasūris who pervade everywhere through their knowledge. Is his beauty a matter for them to think about!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) There is no avatārikai written by the vyākyātā for this pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

In the previous pāsuram, he enjoyed the divine eyes of sarvēśvaran by speaking of them as *empirāna tadangangaḷē*. The beauty of those eyes took him to the other limbs.

**kaṇṇum sendāmarai** – He had enjoyed his eyes separately. However, he wants to enjoy the eyes along with the other limbs and hence is speaking about them. Earlier he was fully absorbed in the eyes alone. Now, he is enjoying sarvēśvaran’s motherly forbearance and beauty.

**kaiyum avai** – being attracted by sarvēśvaran’s divine eyes, āḻvār wants to touch his divine form. Knowing this, sarvēśvaran embraces him with his divine hands. śri viṣṇu purāṇam 5-18-2 says “*samspruśya ākruśyaca  prītyā sugādam pariśasvajē*” (kriṣṇa lifted akrūrar \[kamsan’s minister\] who fell at his feet and embraced him tightly) – such divine hands.

**adiyō avaiyē** – The act of embracing  him leads to a feeling of intoxication. Due to this, on swooning, the place where he falls is the divine feet. jitantē ślōkam 1 says “*puṇḍarīkākśa nama:*” (salutations to sarvēśvaran who has lotus like eyes). In other words, don’t those who lose out to his eyes, fall at his feet!

**vaṇṇam . . .** – the divine form is the means for those who swoon and fall at his divine feet to get back to life. piḷḷaippiḷḷai āḻvān (one of the disciples of kūrattāḻvān) would say “Just like an infant which is suckling looks at the mother’s face keenly, those who fall at his divine feet look at his divine form”

***viṇṇum kadandu umbar appal mikku maṟṟeppāl yavarkkum madi vigaṟpāl eṇṇum idattaduvō empirānadeḻil niṟam***

**madi vigaṟpāl** – due to differences in the level of intellect. Even though there is difference in the intelligence levels of samsāris and dēvās (celestial entities), it is not possible to know. What is not possible to know? …

**viṇṇum . . .** – starting with brahmā who is the cause of samsāris and who has his place of birth at sarvēśvaran and who got his knowledge through sarvēśvaran, and extending to nityasūris, is it possible to know the beauty of sarvēśvaran, who is my benefactor?

**empirān** – āḻvār celebrates how sarvēśvaran manifested his beauty to him, as described in

- mahābhāratam mōkśa darmam (187-4) *nagrāhya kēnacit kvacit* – he cannot be estimated by anyone, in any way
- kēnōpaniśat 1-2 *yasyāmatam tasya matam* – one who thinks that he does not know (about emperumān), knows; one who thinks that he knows, does not know.
- śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 1-12 *rūpasamhananam lakśmīm saukumāryam suvēśatām dadru vismitākārā rāmasya vanavāsina:* – the sages dwelling in \[dandakāraṇya\] forest saw the physical build, its augustness and softness, and the fine arrangement of limbs, remaining in wondrous form. śri rāma’s softness of divine form is such that even if sītappirātti or lakśmaṇa looks at his form, it will wither away. Such a *darśan* (sight) which even the dwellers of his kingdom ayōdyā could not get, was manifested to these sages who dwell in forests.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 44. Niram uyar

*niṟam uyar kōlamum pērum uruvum ivai ivai enṟu aṟamuyal jñānaccamayigaḷ pēsilum angangellām uṟavuyar jñānaccudar viḷakkāy ninṟadanṟi onṟum peṟa muyanṟār illaiyāl empirāna perumaiyaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

niṟam – due to the beauty (of the divine form); uyar – wondrous; kōlamum – decorations; pērum – divine names; uruvum – auspicious divine forms; ivai ivai enṟu – saying that these are of such and such type; aṟam – righteously; muyal – attempting; jñānam – having devotion; samayigaḷ – those who are firmly engaged in the philosophy of paths of vēdams (sacred texts); pēsilum – even if they instruct; angangellām – in all those entities of beauty etc; uṟa – to attain; uyar – distinguished; jñānam – related to knowledge; sudar – splendorous; viḷakkāy – having intelligence; ninṟadanṟi – apart from being that way; empirāna – emperumān’s; perumaiyai – greatness; onṟum – in any way; peṟa – to attain; muyanṟārillai – they did not attempt

**Перевод:**

**People who are firmly established in the path of righteousness may instruct about the decorations, divine names, auspicious divine forms etc which had attained greatness due to emperumān’s beauty. However, it cannot be said that they have splendorous knowledge about emperumān’s beauty etc. They have not attempted to describe fully his incomparable greatness in any way.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Some people asked āḻvār “You say that sarvēśvaran is not an entity who could be attained as per our wish. Is it not possible to know about him?” He responds saying “It is not that way. With his mercy, we could know about him”. Let us go torugh the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**niṟam . . .** – beauty is what one gets as existence after holding on to sarvēśvaran. vyākyātā narrates about what hanuman said after looking at śrī rāma’s beauty and becoming subservient to him. śrī rāma and lakśmaṇa, searching for sītāppirātti, had approached the place where sugrīva and others were hiding. sugrīva was worried that they could be inimical people and sent hanuman to find out about them. hanuman came to their place, saw them, and forgetting his primary task, started singing paens on them, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 3-14 “*simhas skandau mahōtsāhau samadau ivagōvriśau āyatāśca suvrutāṣa bhāhava: parighōpamā:*” (lion-shouldered, full of fortitude, vigorous like bulls, lengthy and circular shoulders, looking similar to clubs). sarvēśvaran is that beautiful. hanuman realised that on merely seeing them, the purpose for which he came had become successful \[to befriend sugrīva and help him in getting back his wife and kingdom\]. *āyatā: ājānubhāhu* – śrī rāma’s divine arms came down to his knees and extended further beyond. hanuman found that both śrī rāma and lakśmaṇa, who is referred to as in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 28-24 “*rāmasya dakśiṇōbhāhu*” (right hand of śrī rāma) had not decorated themselves with any ornament. He continues, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 3-15 “*sarvabhūśaṇa bhūśārhā: kim artam na vibhūśitā:*” (while your physique is fit for being decorated with all ornaments, why have you not decorated with any ornament?) Why have you not worn the ornaments which have lost their beauty (since they are not decorating you)? Some kings would take a vow that they would not drink milk or eat betel leaves until they won over a particular king. Have you too taken a vow that you will not wear ornaments until you annihilate the enemies of sages in forests? ōr are you not wearing ornaments thinking that you could attract monkeys roaming in the forest such as me with your external beauty?

**pērum** – he has divine names, befitting this beauty, such as mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 7-3-10 “*magaranedum kuḻaikkādar*” (one who has ears similar to long fish).  The vyākyātā narrates a few incidents from the lives of our pūrvācāryars (preceptors). tirumalai nambi, the son of periya tirumalai nambi (who was bhagavat rāmānuja’s ācārya) was in his final days. He called kaṇiyanūr siṟiyāttān, the disciple of parāśara bhattar, and asked him “Could you tell me the divine name which parāśara bhattar used to constantly meditate on, as his refuge?” siṟiyāttān hesitated since he vasn’t sure about the specific divine name which he should recount. tirumalai nambi egged him on, saying “dāśaraty! This is not the time to keep quiet.” Immediately, siṟiyāttān responded saying “He used to recount many divine names, including nārāyaṇan. However, the one divine name in which he was much engaged with is *aḻagiya maṇavāḷapperumāḷ*” Hearing this, tirumalai nambi said “Instead of saying namperumāḷ, he has said *aḻagiya maṇavāḷapperumāḷ*, just like a *darmapatni* (wife who walks in the path of righteousness) refers to her husband. In any case, other than this divine name which bhattar used to meditate on, there is no refuge for adiyēn” and reiciting “*aḻayagiyamaṇavāḷapperumāḷē śaraṇam”* (I take refuge under aḻagiya maṇavāḷapperumāḷ) he breathed his last and ascended to paramapadam. Another preceptor, āccimagan, disciple of parāśara bhattar, was lying unconsciously, in his final days. bhattar went to his house and recited in his ears “*aḻayagiyamaṇavāḷapperumāḷē śaraṇam*”. On hearing this, previous memories came flooding to āccimagan; he recited the same and ascended to paramapadam.

**uruvum** – it is not only the divine name, even the auspicious divine form has similar effect, says the vyākyātā. āḷavandār’s son, sottai nambi, was in his last days. His disciples came to see him and asked of him “What are you constantly thinking about?” sottai nambi said “I will go to śrī vaikuṇṭam and have darśan of (audience with) śrī vaikuṇṭanātan. If his divine face is not as cool as namperumāḷ’s, I will pierce through śrīvaikuṇṭam and come back running to namperumāḷ”. Once, parāśara bhattar was near namperumāḷ who had mercifully come for taking *tirumanjanam* (divine bath). namperumāḷ asked him “What do you wish to have?” bhattar said “You should remove your divine *vastram* (dress) and show your divine thigh”. There was belief that if one were to have *ēkānta sēvai* (having darśan of namperumāḷ in private), he would ascend to paramapadam. On hearing this, namperumāḷ asked him “Are you worried whether you would get such a benefit?” bhattar responded saying “*nāyantē* (my svāmy (lord))! We are not worried about that. We are worried that we might lose your cool divine face and the beauty of the scar of *kastūri tilakam* (divine mark applied on namperumāḷ) \[i.e. We may not see these in paramapadam\]”

**uruvum** – divine form.

**ivai ivai enṟu** – saying in amazement, “This is such a decoration! This is such a divine name! This is such a beauty!”

**aṟamuyal** – those who are constantly engaged with the path of righteousness

**jñānaccamayigaḷ** – those who constantly meditate on *daharavidyā, cāndilyavidyā, satvidyā* etc (these are different modes mentioned in upaniśats for meditating on sarvēśvaran); due to that devotion and affection, they will focus on any one alone, such as beauty or divine name or divine auspicious form etc, not going to the others.

**pēsilum** – experiencing only one mode this way, and even if they narrate about it

**angangellām** – among those decoration, divine name, divine form etc

**uṟa** – what they had attained

**uyar jñānaccudar viḷakkāy ninṟadanṟi** – they will get distinguished knowledge about that one mode alone. Apart from that . . .

**perumaiyai onṟum peṟa muyanṟārillaiyāl** – they had not attempted to attain the knowledge about emperumān’s greatness. They could not say anything in full about his greatness other than what he had manifested to them out of his mercy.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 45. Perum kelalar

*perungēḻalār tam perum kaṇ malarppuṇḍarīgam nammēl orungē piṟaḻa vaittār ivvakālam oruvar nam pōl varungēḻbavar uḷarē tollai vāḻiyanjūḻ piṟappum marungē varappeṟumē sollu vāḻi mada nenjamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

perum kēḻalār – varāhapperumāḷ (divine wild boar) who has a huge form; tam – his; malar – blossomed; puṇḍarīgam – like a lotus flower; perum – expansive; kaṇ – divine eyes; nam mēl – upon us; orungē – completely; piṟaḻa – to join; vaittār – kept; ivva kālam – during this time; oruvar – anyone; nam pōl – like us; varum – that which happened; kēḻbavar – having the benefit; uḷarē – is there?; tollai – for a very long time; āḻi – cannot be crossed like the ocean –; Am – beautiful (being cruel); sūḻ – surrounding; piṟappum – birth; marungē – nearby (with those connected to me); varappeṟumē – will it occur?; madam – being obedient; nenjamē – oh heart!; sollu – tell; vāḻi – may you live long

**Перевод:**

**varāhap perumān (śriman nārāyaṇa who incarnated as the divine wild boar) with his huge divine form,  kept his divine eyes which were like blossomed lotus flowers, completely upon me. Is there anyone else, during this time, who had got the benefit just like we had obtained? When such is the situation, could the cycle of births, which is like a huge ocean surrounding us and which cannot be crossed, come near us? Oh my mind! You please tell. May you live long.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) There is no avatārikai for this pāasuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**perum kēḻalār** – In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had said that we cannot attain sarvēśvaran through our efforts and that he attained sarvēśvaran through his mercy. Some people asked āḻvār, who was joyous because of this “You seem happy that sarvēśvaran manifested himself to us who are standing in the middle of samsāram. Would the benefit of crossing this samsāram last eternally for us?” He responds through this pāsuram saying that for those who got total mercy through him, there is no shortcoming. varāha avatāram (incarnation as divine wild boar), who protected bhūmippirātti, the protected entity, was very huge. Just as it is mentioned in nācciyār tirumoḻi 11-8 “*māsudambil nīr vārā*” he let all the dirt gather on his divine form \[to appear as natural as a boar\]. Just as śrī rāma said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda Kāṇḍam 120-11 “*ātmānam mānuśam manyē*” (I am rāma, the son of daśarata and I am a human being), the radiance of this *varāha* was such that even the radiance of śrī vaikuntanātan was no match for this. Just as it has been said in tiruvāimoḻi 3-3-6 “*en mēḷ pāsam vaitta param sudar sōdiyai īśan vānavarkkenban enṟāl adu tēsamō*” he manifested himself to many, including monkeys, who had not known him. When such is the case, is it apt to say that he manifested himself to those who knew him?

**tam perum kaṇ malar** – his huge form of wild boar was full of eyes. Alternatively, his form was like a huge ocean and that ocean was full of lotus flowers (his divine eyes). śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-4-26 says “*mahā varāha: sputapadmalōcana*:” – he has a huge form of wild boar and has divine eyes just like blossomed lotus flowers.

**tam perum kaṇ malar** – he lifted bhūmippirātti who was surrounded by deluge thus carrying out an apt activity for her. He looked at bhūmippirātti with his cool eyes.

**nam mēl** – on us, her children.

**orungē** – the glance which he kept on us was not shifted to bhūmippirātti too.

**piṟaḻa vaittār** – he glanced towards us fully.

**ivva kālam** – it was a time when trees which had shed their leaves fully, became a great foliage with fresh sprouts and flowers.

**oruvar nam pōl varum kēḻbavar uḷarē** – Is there anyone else who has got benefitted like we had got? Since sarvēśvaran is not dependent on anyone, there is no benefit for him; since muktas and nityasūris are near him, there is no benefit for them too; since *bhaktas* (those who attempt to attain him through devotion) are fully focussed on *bhagavat viśayam* (matters related to bhagavān) they also do not have this benefit; śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 66-10 says “*najīvēyam kśaṇamapi vinātām asitēkśiṇām*” (I cannot sustain myself without sītā, even for a moment). Since she had this greatness, she too did not get this benefit. The opinion here is that that there is no one else, who had got such a benefit which he had attained, standing in a lowly situation \[being a samsāri\].

**tollai . . .** – when such is the situation, will the cycle of births, which is very ancient, which has grown without any shortcoming, which is full of faults and which surrounds us wherever we go, come anyvhevre near those who are related to me? āḷavandār had said in śtōtra ratnam 5 “*madanvayānām*” (those who are related to me); āḻvār says that no ill will befall him or those who are related to him.

**sollu vāḻi mada nenjamē** – Oh heart, you have been roaming around everywhere, giving your opinion on everything. Tell me on this. This āḻvār is going to say in the 66<sup>th</sup> pāsuram of this prabandam “*vaigundam annāḷ kuḻalvāy virai poḷ viṇḍugaḷ vārum malaruḷavō*”. Oh heart! You are obedient to me. May you live long.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 46. Madanenjam enrum

*madanenjam enṟum tamadenṟum ōr karumam karudi vida nenjai uṟṟār vidavō amaiyum appon peyarōn tadanenjam kīṇḍa pirānār tamadikkīḻ vidappōyt tida nenjamāy emmai nīttu inṟu tāṟum tiriginṟadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

appon peyarōn – that hiraṇyakaśipu’s; tadam – broad; nenjam – chest; kīṇḍa – tore; pirānār tamadu – benefactor, sarvēśvaran’s; adikkīḻ – under the divine feet as objective; vida – on sending (the mind); pōy – going (there); tidam – firmly; nenjamāy – having deep belief; emmai – us; nīttu – leaving; inṟu tārum – till date; tiriginṟadu – is roaming; nenjam – mind; madam enṟum – saying that it is obedient (to us); tamadu enṟum – saying that it is our limb; ōr karumam – an activity; karudi – thinking (of doing); nenjai – mind; vida – to send (on an errand)\\; uṟṟār – those who had planned; vida – to drop (that activity); amaiyum – will be apt

**Перевод:**

**I sent my mind with the intention of taking refuge under the divine feet of narasimhan, who had torn the broad chest of hiraṇyakaśipu. My mind which went like this, instead of returning to me, stayed firmly there itself, considering those divine feet as an apt place for itself. Those who think that our mind is obedient to us and is ours, and who are planning to send it on an errand, would do well to drop that firmness.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār praised his heart saying “vāḻi mada nenjamē”. His heart asked him “Why should you praise me like this? What did I do?” He replied saying “You have been here all along to explain the relationship between sarvēśvaran and me. Now, you have become the target of his mercy. You have become friendly with him. Was it not because of you that I too got this fortune?” Having celebrated like this, the previous experience (between āḻvār and sarvēśvaran) became like a dream, with the experience limited to internal faculty (mind). āḻvār desired external union and since that did not fructify, āḻvār became upset. Looking at his distressed condition, his divine mind asked him “What should I do here?” āḻvār told his mind “Go to the divine feet of naraśimhan who tore the heart of hiraṇyakasipu and inform him of my condition”. The mind too went, but did not return, which made āḻvār to become more afflicted. While he desired to be united with perum kēḻalār (sarvēśvaran in varāhāvatāram), why is he sending his mind to narasimhāvatāram? Since both the incarnations were benevolent towards followers, he sent the messenger to the latter. In the previous pāsuram when he called his heart as mada nenjamē (Oh obedient heart!) it too, not refusing, became favourable towards him and agreed to carry out his task. Now he is disgusted with the same heart. Why did he praise it earlier and why is he disgusted with it now? The mind created deep desire in him for sarvēśvaran and nurtured that desire to such an extent that āḻvār could not sustain himself without uniting with him. Instead of fulfilling that task by being together with him, the heart deserted him. Hence he is disgusted with it. The mind \[in all these vyākhyānams, mind and heart are interchangeably used\], apart from being the reason for creating a relationship with sarvēśvaran, should also be the reason for attaining mōkśam (uniting with sarvēśvaran in paramapadam). While sarvēśvaran is the reason for mōkśam, why is āḻvār saying that mind is the casue for mōkśam? Just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 1-127 “rarakśa darmēṇa balēna ca” ((lakśmaṇa vigilantly) protected (śrī rāma) (with an undespaired mind and) even by his virtue and strength), āḻvār’s mind should have stayed with him until he attained sarvēśvaran and told him “Whatever troubles you have had, I too had experienced with you”. While the words of those who say that only sarvēśvaran is the means \[for attaining him\] are uttered after discriminating between what is apt and what is inapt, there are certain requirements from the cētana (sentient entity) too who wishes to attain mōkśam. First he should exist. Even if he exists, he should engage with the means; he should know the puruśārtam (end-goal or benefit) which is apt for him and a heart which helps to develop ruci (interest / taste) in that benefit. Moreover, he should shun the other means, carrying out activities which alone will be apt for sarvēśvaran. When sarvēśvaran himself is the means and readily available, the reason for the cētana to continue in samsāram is because the requirements mentioned above have not developed in him. Thus, when the heart should have stayed with him until he attained his end-goal, since it did not do, he is upset with his heart. Let us go through the pāsiuram and its meanings*

**mada nenjam enṟum tamadenṟum** – there is a wrong impression among many that their minds are obedient to them. The mind has been provided by sarvēśvaran as an aid along with the physical form to enable the cētana (sentient entity) to reach his end-goal. Hence they may consider the mind as theirs. Moreover, apart from the physical form and the five senses, sarvēśvaran had provided the heart to propel the cētana towards the end-goal and to create an interest in it. This being the case, there is no fault in thinking that the heart is one’s own. If one were obedient to another and the other person becomes distressed, the obedient entity should stand by that person and tell him “I am also suffering the same difficulties which you suffered”. Also, one who is a *śēśan* (carrying out servitude) should reveal his master’s fame and good qualities. However, āḻvār says that the heart does not have these qualities.

**ōr karumam . . .** – some people may be bold enough to send their hearts on errands. If their hearts leave them without carrying out that errand, it is better not to feel sorrowful about it. It is better not to have an attachment to that errand.

If this is the case, let the heart remain like that. Why search for it? . . . .

**appon peyarōn**  . . . – being under his control and being his slave, isn’t this the target for that mind to be engaged in?

**appon peyarōn tadanenjam kīṇḍa pirānār tamadu adikkīḻ vida** – did I leave it in a place where I cannot be successful? Did I not leave it where the one who does benefits for us, is present? āḻvār feels that the benefit which sarvēśvaran did for his follower \[prahlādan\] is as good as having done for him.

**appon peyarōn** – prahlādan, who was sired by hiraṇyakaśipu, recited the divine names of sarvēśvaran. Getting angry with him because of this, hiraṇyan meted out punishments to his son, which he would mete out to his enemies. Thinking of those cruelties āḻvār is referring to him as *pon peyarōn*.

Here, the vyākyātā explains a few lines from periya tirumoḻi pāsuram 2-3-8 *paḷḷiyil ōdi vanda siṟuvan*

*paḷḷiyil ōdivanda* – whatever the children recite, which they had learnt from their school, is always joyous.

*than siṟuvan* – it would be joyous even if other children recite it. But if it is by one’s own child, it is more delightful.

*vāyil ōr āyira nāmam oḷḷiyavāgippōda* – the words recited by prahlādan were such that even if they had been recited by an enemy, one would have fallen at the enemy’s feet!

*onṟum ōr poṟuppilanāgi* – hiraṇyakaśipu had carried out *asahyāpacāram* (irreverence, being unable to bear) in the case of prahlādan. While he was related to prahlādan, being his father, not respecting that, he did to prahlādan what enemies would do.

*piḷḷaiyaiccīṟi* – he gave up prahlādan saying “He is not my son” just because prahlādan recited the divine names of sarvēśvaran.

āḻvār is mentioning a connection that he has with him.

**tada nenjam** – the huge chest \[of hiraṇyakaśipu} which had grown to become a prey for sarvēśvaran’s divine nails and divine teeth.

**kīṇḍa** – even when narasimhap perumān had not torn it, the physical form of hiraṇyakaśipu started melting on merely looking at that divine form and fury. Without any difficulty, narasimhap perumān tore that chest.

**pirānār** – would narasimhap perumān, who came running when prahlādan’s father himself turned inimical, be able to tolerate should āḻvār send a messenger due to his distressed condition in separation? While he had helped for prahlādan’s clarity of mind, he would indeed help for āḻvār’s distressed condition. Not only because prahlādan had deep devotion, as mentioned in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-19-88 ”*mattassarvam ahamsarvam*” (Everything is mine (being a form of bhagavān), I am everything (having bhagavān as antaryāmi)), he has devotion with clarity.

**tamadadikkīḻ vida** – leaving his heart under such narasimha perumān’s divine feet.

**pōy tida nenjamāy** – his heart, while going to sarvēśvaran as āḻvār’s messenger had thought “āḻvār is in tears. Will he sustain himself until I return?” But once it reached there, it seemed to have taken his \[sarvēśvaran’s\] nature. When sītāppirātti separated from śrī rāma for the second time (due to the disparaging words of a vaśerman), did he not sustain himself for long? Probably, the heart too took to this nature.

**emmai nīttu** – leaving me aside, completely.

**inṟu . . .** – let us at the least hear that the heart felt bad saying “Now that I have left him, to whom will he cry about his distress in separation? How bad that we have left him like this?” The heart was roaming around, like this.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
