## 47. Tiriginradu

*tiriginṟadu vadamārudam tingaḷ vendīmugandu soriginṟadu aduvumadu kaṇṇan viṇṇūr toḻavē sariginṟadu sangam taṇṇanduyākku vaṇṇam payalai viriginṟadu muḻu meyyum ennāngol en melliyaṟkē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vadamārudam – northerly wind; tiriginṟadu – is blowing; tingaḷ – moon; vem – cruel; tī – fire; mugandu – scooping a lot; soriginṟadu – is showering; aduvum – similarly, other harmful entities such as the evil cuckoo; adu – started harming like that moon; kaṇṇan – kriṣṇa’s; viṇ – in paramapadam; Ur – śrīvaikuṇṭam, his dwelling place; toḻa – to attain (thinking of vorśipping); sangam – bangle; sariginṟadu – is slipping; taṇ – cool; am – beautiful; tuḻāykku – in the divine tuḷasi (due to desire); vaṇṇam – natural colour; payalai – becoming pale; muḻu meyyum – throughout the physical form; viriginṟadu – pervaded; en melliyaṟku – for my soft-natured daughter; en āngol – will anything harmful happen (later)?

**Перевод:**

**The northerly wind is blowing everywhere. Moon is showering cruel fire. Other entities such as the evil cuckoo etc are also raining fire, just like the moon. Since my daughter is thinking of continuously enjoying śrīvaikuṇṭam, the dwelling place of kriṣṇa, her bangle is slipping out of her hands since her desire is not getting fulfilled. Due to her desire for his cool, beautiful divine tuḷasi garland, her natural complexion is fading and she is becoming pale. Where will all these end for my daughter?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) The nāyaki’s mother, looking at the distress of her daughter in separation (from the nāyakan) after being united with him, wonders as to what will happen to her, considering the greatness of various things which torment her as well as considering her softness. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tiriginṟadu vada mārudam** – Just like an elephant in rut would size up a man and roam around, the northerly wind was roaming here and there. Waiting for the moment when the *nāyakan*’s glance would move away from her face, it wanted to harm her and was roaming around. Another interpretation – normally, northerly breeze would be cool. But it changed its nature and blew hot.

**tingaḷ . . .** – moon saw the change in the nature of northerly breeze and thought “Why am I not doing anything?” Immediately, he too showered fire, on his part.

**vem tī** – fire and breeze joined together. The northerly breeze was like boiling water \[bringing in another aspect of heat\] instead of like fire.

**mugandu soriginṟadu** – both of them burnt the *nāyaki* without any shortcoming in the heat.

**aduvum adu** – just like northerly wind and moon, there are other entities too which harm those who are in separation – the sound of ocean, the Indian love bird (*anṟil*), breeze, sound from the bell around the bull’s neck etc. Since the harm created by these entities could not be described in words, she (*nāyaki*’s mother) is saying “*aduvum”*. Alternatively, if we consider the meaning for *tiriginṟadu* as roaming, *aduvum adu* would mean that the breeze too showered fire just like the moon.

**kaṇṇan . . .** – Is it necessary to say that there are some others too who are responsible for her position \[such as moon, wind etc\]? If someone becomes distressed and the bangles slip out, sarvēśvaran would incarnate himself and be easily approachable. She (the *nāyaki*) wishes to see him in his permanent residence of śrī vaikuṇṭam. Due to despondence, she started slimming and her bangles slipped out of her hands.

**kaṇṇan viṇṇūr** – Is it not sufficient to say *śrīvaikuṇṭam*? Why call it as kriṣṇa’s *śrivaikuṇṭam*? In śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 33-17 it says “*rāghavasya nivēśanē”* śrī rāma’s palace, to refer to the palace. This is similar to that \[to give emphasis\].

**taṇṇanduḻāy . . .** – śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 16-21 *patisammānitā* – sitā who was respected by her husband – thinking of that event when sītāppirātti received the garland from śrī rāma and donned it on her shoulders, the *nāyikā*’s physical form becomes pale.

**vaṇṇam . . . .** – her natural complexion became pale and spread throughout her form.

**ennāngol . . . .** – the *nāyikā*’s mother wonders “if even the northerly breeze could turn her complexion into a pale colour, if these things too happen (entire body becoming pale, bangles slipping out etc) what will happen to her?”

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): āḻvār sings through the pāsuram of those who were nearby, and who felt, on seeing his natural softness and distressed condition as well as the entity that he desired is confidential “Would we lose him forever?” seeing his condition in separation.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 48. Melliyal akkai

*melliyal ākkkaikkirumi kuruvil miḷirdandāngē selliya selgaittu ulagai enkāṇum ennālum tannaic colliya sūḻal tirumāl avan kavi yādu kaṟṟēn palliyin sollum sollāk koḷvadu uṇḍō paṇḍu paṇḍē*

**Пословный перевод:**

melliyal – being soft; ākkai – having a form; kuruvil – in a wound (i.e. in samsāram); miḷirdandu – being born; āngē – in that place; selliya – what is to happen; selgaittu – having that activity; kirumi – a worm; ulagai – matters relating to the world; en kāṇum – what will it know?; ennālum – through me, who has no intelligence; tannai – about him who is unlimited; solliya – explaining; sūḻal – amazing entity; tirumāḷavan – matter concerning śriya:pati (consort of śrīmahālakśmi); kavi – stōtrams (verses of praise); ādu – all of them; kaṟṟēn – I learnt (from him); paṇḍu paṇḍu – from time immemorial; palliyin – lizard’s; sollum – whatever it says; sollāk koḷvadu – recognising as true (whether it is favourable or unfavourable); uṇḍu – has been in vogue

**Перевод:**

**A worm, which has a soft form, is born in this samsāram which is like a wound. What will the worm, which keeps getting tossed about inside that wound, know about the world? sarvēśvaran, who has unlimited greatness, who is an amazing entity and who is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi, made me, who has no capability in anything, to speak about his qualities. I learnt from him all those words of praise \[about him\]. Is it not in vogue from time immemorial to consider the sounds of a lizard as subtly hinting about the future?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār felt as if he was deprived of strength, wondering as to why he was being kept in samsāram. sarvēśvaran thought in his divine mind “He feels that he is on fire while in samsāram because he feels that he is staying there for himself. If I tell him ‘You are here for my sake’ he will be able to sustain. Moreover, that is also the nature that he wishes to attain”. He told āḻvār “āḻvīr! Do not feel anguished. There is no connection for you with this samsāram; you are not living here for your sake; it is only for my sake. We want to bring out a few pāsurams (hymns) through you, which will appeal to me as well as to those who are with me. We wanted to bring out our qualities such as simplicity etc through those pāsurams. Is it not for this that we kept you here?” On hearing this, āḻvār thought “Then there is no hurdle. If it is going to be aligned to my true nature and it is the one which I am going to attain, there is no issue at all”. He is amazed that sarvēśvaran is accomplishing this task through him and speaks about sarvēśvaran’s quality of considering him as an object worthy of attention. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**melliyal ākkaik kirumi** – There is nothing among his svarūpam (true nature), *rūpam* (divine form), *guṇam* (auspicious qualities), *aiśvaryam* (wealth) etc which is known to me. He decided to carry out the task of explaining about him through me who knows nothing about him.

**melliyal ākkaik kirumi** – worm with a very soft nature of physical form. It is so soft that if the air which gets expelled when the eyelids close, blows on it, it will perish. It is so lowly that it cannot even be considered as a creature.

**kuruvil miḷirdandu** – being born in a wound called *krantippuṇ*. Why did the āḻvār say *miḷirndu* and not *piṟandu* (another word for being born)? The worm has only birth and death, with nothing called as ‘life’ in between the two. Hence he called it as *miḷirndu*. It could be called as *piṟandu* only if it takes a body and reaches an end goal, as described in *pancāgni vidyā* (a set of five activities which describes the journey of a soul through five different stages in its repeated cycle of births and deaths).

**āngē** – in that wound

**selliya selgaittu** – involved in some activity. Apart from being born and living, other activities such as being looked at with detestation by the others and death also take place in that wound itself. It thinks that it is becoming beautiful, over a period of time. It knows nothing other than the wound, just like some people will ask “Which is the tree where paddy grows?”

**ualagai en kāṇum** – what will that worm know of the world when its existence is as described above? āḻvār says that this is his status too.

**ennālum** – through me, who is the boundary for ignorance, incapability etc

**tannai** – did he want to convey one of his activities through me who is in the state of that worm? When *śruti* and *smruti* (sacred texts and their explanatory works respectively) returned empty-handed, after trying to measure just one quality of his, bliss, as mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat, he wanted to convey through me about him.

**solliya** – sarvēsvaran had intended to explain through āḻvār. Before he could even guess if it is possible through him, he told āḻvār about himself. What is the reason for this?

**sūḻal** – because he is the one with amazing activities.

**tirumāḷ** – since he is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi, he told āḻvār alone about about his activities and not to all.

**tirumālavan kavi** – it is to compose poems on the togetherness of sarvēśvaran and periya pirātti. This mentions the distinction with śrī rāmāyaṇam. While śrī rāmāyaṇam started by saying *sītāyāś caritam mahat* – great narrative about sītāppirātti, when it was sung, he alone heard it, without her presence. It is not like that here; both of them were together.

**ādu kaṟṟēn** – it could be *yādu kaṟṟēn* or *ādu kaṟṟēn*; if it is *yādu kaṟṟēn*, it would imply that whatever he \[āḻvār\] had learnt, it was his \[sarvēśvaran’s\] hymns. If it is *ādu karrēṇ*, it would imply that he had learnt every hymn about *tirumāl*.

**kaṟṟēn** – just as it has been mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 7-9-2 “*en mun sollum*” (recite in front of me), sarvēśvaran started reciting the *santai*, I continued after him (*santai* is a metof of learning in which the teacher recites a verse once and the disciple repeats twice, after the teacher). How was this completed? He responds saying . . . .

**palliyin sollum . . . .** – when a person with very little knowledge starts saying something, one with deep knowledge would correct it and complete it. Lizard would make some sounds such as *natu natu* which would be meaningless. However, learned people would make use of that sound for their favourable / unfavourable events. Similarly, I had said some gibberish which came to me at various points of time and he corrected them as if to refer to his auspicious qualities and accepted them.

**paṇḍu paṇḍē** – from time immemorial, we have seen respect for lizard’s sounds like this. sarvēśvaran made that to be seen in āḻvār’s matter, by all.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 49. Pandum palapala

*paṇḍum palapala vīngiruḷ kāṇḍum ippāyiruḷ pōl kaṇḍum aṟivadum kētpadum yāmilam kāḷavaṇṇa vaṇḍunduḻāyp perumān madusūdanan dāmōdaran uṇḍum umiḻndum kadāya maṇṇēranna oṇṇudalē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kāḷam – dark; vaṇṇam – having colour; vaṇḍu – beetles; uṇ – drinking (honey); tuḻāy – having divine tuḷasi; perumān – being a benefactor; madusūdanan – having annihilated the demon, madu; dāmōdaran – sarvēśvaran who has the (scar of) the rope tied around his stomach (by yaśōdā pirātti); uṇḍum – swallowed (during deluge); umiḻndum – spat out (during creation); kadāya – carried out (protected); maṇ – bhūmi (earth)’s; nēr – for the beauty; anna – matching; oṇ – beautiful; nudalē – Oh one who has forehead!; paṇḍum – earlier too; pala pala – different types; vīngu – growing; iruḷ – darkness; kāṇḍum – we have seen; i – this; pāy – spreading; iruḷ pōl – like darkness; yām – we; kaṇḍum – seen; aṟindum ilam – have not known; kētpadum ilam – have not heard of

**Перевод:**

**sarvēśvaran has the divine tuḷasi garland from which dark coloured beetles are drinking honey; he killed the demon madu and has the \[scar of the\] rope with which yaśōdāppirātti had tied him \[when he stole butter during his incarnation as kriṣṇa\] on his divine stomach. Oh one who has a beautiful forehead which is as beautiful as the earth which that sarvēśvaran swallowed during deluge, spat out during creation and protected! We have seen different types of nights in the past. However, have we seen or heard of or known a night which has spread out as much as this?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Earlier, āḻvār was wondering whether sarvēśvaran had not blessed him in a distinguished way. In the previous pāsuram, he realised that he has been showered with distinguished mercy by sarvēśvaran. He now wonders “Though he has showered his mercy, why is he still keeping me in samsāram which is a hurdle for enjoying him continuously?” Due to this distress of separation from sarvēśvaran, he assumes the mood of pirātti (consort of sarvēśvaran). After being united with sarvēśvaran and having separated from him, the nāyaki, unable to tolerate this distress, tells how she is suffering in the night time. ōr, these words could be taken to have been spoken by her friend. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**paṇḍum palapala vīngiruḷ kāṇḍum** – In earlier times too, she has spent several nights in distress due to separation from him. āḻvār says that just as sage mārkaṇḍēya has seen several deluges, he too has suffered in distress over several nights in the past.

**vīngiruḷ kāṇḍum** – we have seen several nights which had extended over a long time.

**ippāyiruḷ . . .** – However, we have not seen a night which extended for such a long time as this night. We do not see a place where we can rest in this night as it has pervaded everywhere, just like deluge.  We have not heard that even the daughter of janaka maharājar (sītāppirātti), who had also suffered in separation like us, would have suffered during a night like this. āḻvār says that he had in the past, as a samsāri, seen and suffered for many nights. Even after attaining knowledge granted by sarvēśvaran with causeless mercy, he had suffered for many nights. However, after being showered with distinguished mercy by sarvēśvaran, he has not suffered like this ever.

**kāḷa vaṇṇan** – The entity which casued harm for us through this dark coloured night is one which has a dark complexion. Just as it is said in periya tiruvandādi 26 “*iruḷanna māmeni*” (huge form which is dark), did he not manifest his dark form to make me like this? This darkness ,which is similar to the dark night when he, with a form which is as dark as this, had come in happier times and embraced me, is the reason for my sorrow.

**vaṇḍuṇ tuḻāy** – he explains the beauty of the divine form which came through decorations.

**madusūdanan** – he is the one who removes hurdles which come about due to enjoying such beauty, just like he removed the demon *madu*.

**dāmōdaran** – he is obedient towards those who are happy on seeing him, such that they could even tie him down to a mortar.

**uṇḍum . . .** – engaging in activities such as swallowing, spitting etc

**maṇṇēranna** – when it is considered that she is an entity to be protected by him, she is like bhūmi (earth). If this pāsuram is considered to have been recited by the *nāyaki*, the meaning will be – just like bhūmippirātti was protected by you from being annihilated during deluge, if I have to exist after being caught by this dark night, it will be with your assistance only; if it is to be considered as having been recited by the *nāyaki*’s friend, the meaning will be – you should understand that he is not one who will see his wealth being destroyed; he is one who will look at with consideration. Just as he had protected bhūmippirātti from deluge, he will not leave until he protects you too from the sadness of this night.

**oṇṇudalē** – I had cited an example (bhūmi) only to illustrate that you are his wealth. However, will that bhūmi match you? Don’t you alone have the beauty of form which will attract him?

## 50. Onnudal mamai

*oṇṇudal māmai oḷi payavāmai viraindu nam tēr naṇṇudal vēṇḍum valava kadāginṟu tēn navinṟa viṇmudal nāyagan nīṇmudi veṇ mutta vāsigaittāy maṇmudal sērvuṟṟu aruvi seyyā niṟkum māmalaikkē*

**Пословный перевод:**

valava – Oh charioteer, who is strong!; oṇ – beautiful; nudal – nāyaki who has a forehead, her; māmai – complexion’s; oḷi – radiance; payavāmai – before it gets discoloured; tēn – like honey; navinṟa – referred to as; viṇ mudal – for vibhūtis such as paramapadam; nāyakan – one who is the lord, his; nīḷ – long; mudi – on top of the crown; veṇ – white; muttam – pearls’; vāsigaittāy – arrangement; maṇ mudal – earth which is primary (for him); sērvuṟṟu – to reach; aruvi – having rivers; seyyā niṟkum – flowing in abundance; mā – huge; malaikku – for the tirumalai hill; viraindu – quickly; nam tēr – our chariot; kadāginṟu – conducting; naṇṇudal vēṇḍum – we should reach

**Перевод:**

**The nāyakan tells his charioteer “Oh charioteer who is strong! Before the complexion of nāyaki, who has a beautiful forhead, changes colour, drive the chariot fast \[to her place\]. We should go fast towards tirumalai hills which have the character of the whitish pearls which are present on the long crown of the lord of paramapadam and which have rivers which start from the peak and flow abundantly towards earth”**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, he had mentioned about how he had not seen a long night and suffered distress like this. Just as it is said in bhagavat gītā 10-9 “bodayanta: parasparam” (enlightening one another), the nāyakan comes to console her. The distance which śrī rāma had covered only by walking for fourteen years, was covered by him in one afternoon time when he realised the agitation that bharatāḻvān had. In the same way, the nāyakan is coming with a great urge. He tells the charioteer certain words then. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**oṇṇudal māmai oḷi payavāmai** – the beautiful complexion of *nāyaki* who has a beautifully radiant forehead. Radiance is the brightness in the complexion. Before that radiance becomes dull …

**oṇṇudal māmai** – the *nāyakan* and the *nāyaki* are like śrī rāma and sītāppirātti who had separated from each other for ten months. During the period of separation, the *nāyakan* was thinking about *nāyaki*’s beauty and sustained himself. As an example of such behaviour, the vyākyātā cites the words of śrī rāma in śrī rāmāyaṇam *candrakāntānanām subrūma* – ability to attract like the moon. The *nāyakan* thought that he cannot be patient should he lose that beauty and that he could not retrieve the radiance of that beauty should it become dim.

**viraindu nandēr naṇṇudal vēṇḍum** – our chariot should reach her fast. Help me in reaching her fast so that she doesn’t blame me in case she loses her complexion.

**valava** – the activity of the charioteer is similar to that of the charioteer in mahābhārata war.

**kadākinṟu naṇṇudal vēṇḍum** – our chariot should reach her with your ability to conduct the chariot.

**tēn navinṟa . . .** –  the *nāyakan* tells the charioteer that the place where they are going to would be beneficial for the charioteer too; that place, tirumalai, will be like that. The verse could be read as either *tēn navinṟa māmalaikkē* or *tēn navinṟa viṇmudal nāyakan*. In the former case, the word *tēn* would refer to beetles and *navinṟa* would refer to the humming sound made by them. In other words, the beetles are singing in tirumalai after drinking honey from the flowers there. In the latter case, just as it has been mentioned in śrī viṣṇu sūktam *viṣṇōr padē paramē madva utsa*: (honey is flowing from the divine feet of sarvēśvaran), it is the *viṇmudal nāyakan* who has been referred to as shedding honey.

**viṇmudal nāyakan** – he is the lord of paramapadam which is referred to as *viṇ*. Alternatively, he is the lord of all vibhūtis including *viṇ* (paramapadam).

**nīṇmudi . . .** – you would see that the place (tirumalai) is such that it would remove all the difficulties suffered during the time of separation. śrīvaikuṇṭanātan has a long crown; the crown has white pearls; tirumalai has the quality of those pearls. śrīvaikuṇṭam could be considered as a simile for the greatness of tirumalai. Compared to this place (tirumalai) which could be seen by the eyes, śrīvaikuṇṭam is a step lower. Since the greatness of tirumalai could be experienced through the eyes, its true nature will be clear to the eyes. Even though authentic texts confirm that such greatness is there in śrīvaikuṇṭam too, one could only hold a strong belief that the feature exists; only this \[tirumalai\] could be experienced through the eyes.

**maṇmudal sērvuṟṟu** – approaching down to the earth

**aruvi seyyā niṟkum malaikkē** – all our difficulties will be removed once we reach there. nācciyār tirumozohi 8-3 says *kuḷir aruvi vēngadam* – tiruvēngadam (tirumalai) which is with cool streams.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): looking at the anguish of āḻvār in separation, the followers of sarvēśvaran come quickly to console him. Just as pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi) forgot all about mitilā after attaining śrīnivāsan who is standing amidst cool streams, āḻvār also forgot about \[āḻvār\]tirunagari and the followers are rushing to see him in this state.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 51. Malai kondu

*malai koṇḍu mattā aravāl suḻaṟṟiya māyappirān alai kaṇḍu koṇḍa amudam koḷḷādu kadal baradar vilai koṇḍu tanda sangam ivai vērittuḻāyt tuṇaiyāt tulai koṇḍu tāyam kiḷarndu koḷvān ottu aḻaikkinṟadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kadal – ocean; malai – mantara hill; mattā – as the churning shaft; koṇḍu – making it; aravāl – through vāsuki, the snake; suḻaṟṟiya – one who churned; māyam – one with amazing activity; pirān – sarvēśvaran, the benefactor; alai kaṇḍu – making the waves to rise up; koṇḍa – taken (from the ocean); amudam – nectar; koḷḷadu – without accepting; baradar – pearl traders; vilai koṇḍu – taking money; tanda – offered; ivai sangam – these bangles; vēri – fragrant; tuḻāy – divine tuḷasi; tuṇaiyā – as support; tulai koṇḍu – searching a way for destroying; tāyam – share of relatives; koḷvān ottu – as if taking; kiḷarndu – agitatingly; aḻaikkinṟadu – makes a noise

**Перевод:**

**sarvēśvaran had done great benefits such as keeping the mantara mountain as the churning shaft, the snake vāsuki as the churning rope and churned the ocean to obtain nectar. The ocean did not accept that nectar which was taken from it in such a way that its waves rose up. Traders in pearl had taken payment from me (nāyaki) and given me bangles. The ocean, with the support of divine tuḷasi garland, with the thought of destroying me, is making huge noise in order to take these bangles from me, just like relatives would partake of their share in a property.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Before the nāyakan could reach her, the nāyaki heard the noise from ocean and reveals how it distressed her. śrī rāma wanted to cover the distance, which he had travelled over fourteen years by foot, within an afternoon. Knowing that bhartāḻvān would not be able to tolerate even that, he sent hanuman as his messenger to tell bharatan that he was on his way. Until someone is sent as a messenger to her, all the things which she sees appear to cause her harm. śrī rāma tells hanuman as in śrīrāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam ślōkam 125-17 “sangatyā bharata: śrīmān rājyēnārti svayam bhavēt l praśāstum vasudām sarvāmakhilām raghunandana: ll” (bharatāan who is full of the wealth of servitude and devotion, could continue to rule over ayōdyā should he wish to do so, since he has been ruling it for many years). There are two interpretations for this ślōkam – some learned people, looking at it superficially, would say that since bharatan has been ruling for a long time, he would have developed a desire and a hold on the kingdom. Thus, if bharatan wishes to continue to rule over the kingdom, śrī rāma would not wish to go to ayōdyā and would rather stay at sage bharatvāja’s āśram (hermitage) itself. Since he thought this way, he sent hanuman ahead of him on an errand. Our pūrvācāryars (preceptors) considered that this opinion did not gel with śrī rāma’s greatness. The correct meaning of this ślōkam, in our pūrvācāryas’ opinion, is this \[given as the reasoning of śrī rāma\]: as per daśaratan’s order and due to the boon which kaikēyi had obtained, we had given the kingdom to bharatan. bharatan followed us to the forest and said “Oh rāma! If you delay returning to ayōdyā even by a day, I will not be able to sustain my life”. We are now returning after fourteen years. When he is in a state of bliss upon seeing us, we will tell him “You get ready for the coronation”. In the supreme happiness of seeing us, due to bewilderment, he would accept it instead of saying “I will not”. daśaratan wanted to see us being coronated. But he could not live to see that. To reverse that, we will be ecstatic to see our child bharatan being coronated. Hence you go ahead, he told hanuman and sent him. What is the reason for some learned people interpreting in one way and our pūrvācāryars interpreting in the other? This is explained through śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam ślōkam 19-31 “śanair jagāma sāpēkśa:” (śrī rāma, before leaving for the forest, circumambulated all the materials which had been kept for his coronation, saw them respectfully …) In this ślōkam, those learned people saw the word sāpēkśa: as sāvēkśa: which means glancing from the corner of the eye. The meaning for this would then be that śrī rāma had a desire in being the King and made it known in his eyes. On hearing this, parāśara bhattar said “It is not to be considered that way. It should be considered as śrī rāma looking at them with despondence since he would not be present to see bharata’s coronation”, Before such sarvēśvaran could reach the place where the nāyaki was present, the ocean troubled her. Let us go torugh the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**malai koṇḍu mattā** – sarvēśvaran made the immovable mountain to move.

**aravāl** – he took the snake vāsuki, who has a gentle form, as the churning rope.

**suḻaṟṟiya** – the cleverness with which he used these. These entities (mountain and snake) kept churning on their own until nectar came out. This is similar to a potter rotating his wheel once, after which it keeps rotating on its own with that momentum. Since it is sarvēśvaran himself who rotated the mountain once, it kept rotating until the work was completed. Thus all the active and inactive states of all entities are under his control.

**māyappirān** – he has amazing activities. periya tirumoḻi 5-7-4 “*māyirungunṟam ….*” – keeping  the mantara mountain which was broad and tall, as the churning staff, keeping the snake vāsuki as the churning rope, he churned the ocean such that it made a noise like the trumpeting of an elephant and its waves rose to hide the sky.He churned in such a way that the garlands and the dress which he wore kept swinging from one side to the other.

**pirān** – he benefited me by manifesting the beauty of his divine form to me. He is āḻvār’s nectar.

**alai kaṇḍu . . .** – he churned he ocean in such a way that the sand at the bottom rose to the top and the sand at the top sank to the bottom, by planting the mountain mantara at the centre of the ocean’s heart like a mammoth pestle. He took away the nectar from the ocean. The ocean did not have the courage to go to him to retrieve its nectar from him. Just as it has been mentioned in nācciyār tirumoḻi 10-9  “*kadalē kadalē unnaik kadaindu kalakkuṟuttu..*” the ocean did not go to him to trouble him; instead it is creating distress for me. In this world, it has become natural for strong people to show their strength towards the weak ones.

**baradar** – the ocean is attempting to take the bangles which I had bought from the pearl traders after payment of money. Did we go near the ocean on our own?

**vērit tuḻāy tuṇaiyā** – it is torturing me by keeping the fragrant divine tuḷasi, which it received from sarvēśvaran, as a support. Just as sugrīva took śrī rāma’s support and challenged vāli, the ocean took divine tuḷasi’s support to torture me. śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam ślōkam “*tat garjat harivara: sugrīvō hēmapingala: tēna nātēna mahatā nirjagāma haṟiśvara: anumānya tatā dārām sugrīvēna samāgata:*” (sugrīva, who was with a reddish yellow hue, similar to gold, stood near the entrance to vāli’s cave and roared. When his wife tārā tried to prevent him from going, vāli comforted her and came out). *mahatā* – sugrīva displayed his valour; *nirjagāma* – he came out of his cave; *anumānya* – vali told tāra who tried to dissuade him from going out to fight with sugrīva “Will you not unite with a person like me only if I win over him?” As an example of this, the vyākyātā cites śrī rāmāyaṇam 30-39 “*tam druśtvā śatru hantāram maharśīṇām sukhāvaham* \| *babhūva hruśtā vaidēhi bhartāram pariśasvajē* \|\|” (becoming happy after seeing śrī rāma annihilating the enemies of the riśis, sītāppirātti embraced him well).

**tulai koṇḍu** – to destroy her, ocean took the support of divine tuḷasi.

**tāyam . . .** – even after a land had got changed hands four or five times, the relatives of the original owner of the property would stake their claim on that land. In the same way, the ocean, on seeing the bangles on *nāyaki*’s hands, claimed that these bangles came from it (from conces which are in the ocean), and is trying to take them away.

**aḻaikkinṟadē** – it is calling like a waylaying robber. When the omnipotent entity immersed a huge pestle inside it, churned it with the power of his shoulders and took away nectar from it, this ocean did not have the capability to challenge him and retrieve that nectar. Since I am a helpless lady, it is trying to harm me. The vyākyāta cites an example of how the sounds made by the ocean troubled the *nāyaki*, from a ślōkam in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam “*nisvanam cakravākānām niśamya sahacārinām puṇḍarīka visālākśī katamēśā bhaviśyati*” (on merely hearing the noise made by cakravāka birds when they fly, sītāppirātti will be joyous; what sort of difficulty is she in, who has eyes like lotus flower?) Just as the noise made by the birds brought memories of sītāppirātti to śrī rāma and troubled him, the sound made by the ocean troubled the *nāyaki*.

## 52. Alaikkum karum

*aḻaikkum karum kadal veṇ tiraikkē koṇḍu pōy alarvāy maḻaikkaṇ madandai aravaṇai ēṟa maṇ mādar viṇ vāy aḻaittup pulambi mulai malai mēl ninṟum āṟugaḷāy maḻaik kaṇṇa nīr tirumāl kodiyān enṟu vārginṟadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

alarvāy – having been born in a lotus flower; maḻaikkaṇ – having a cool glance, just like rain \[in terms of coolness\]; madandai – periya pirātti \[śrī mahālakśmi\]; azahikkum – calling out loudly; karu – bluish coloured; kadal – in the ocean; veṇ – whitish; tiraikkē – through the waves; koṇḍu pōy – inviting (the nāyakan); aravaṇai – on the mattress of tiruvanantāḻvān (ādiśēśan); ēṟa – on climbing; maṇmādar – bhūmippirātti; tirumāl – śriya: pati (consort of śrī mahālakśmi); kodiyān – cruel entity; enṟu – saying so; viṇ vāy – through the mouth of sky; aḻaittu – loudly calling; pulambi – wailing; malai – mountains; mulai mēl ninṟum – standing atop the bosoms; maḻaik kaṇṇīr – tears like rainfall; āṟugaḷāy – like rivers; vārginṟadu – created copiously

**Перевод:**

**The bluish ocean invited periya pirātti who was born in a lotus flower and who has a glance, as cool as the rain. Taking hold of the whitish waves of that ocean, periya pirātti, along with her lord, sarvēśvaran, started mercifully to ascend the mattress of ādiśēśan. At that time, bhūmippirātti, standing atop the mountains which are like her bosoms, calling out loudly from the sky, started wailing saying “The consort of śrī mahālakśmi is a cruel entity” and started letting down her tears of rainfall copiously, like rivers.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) This is in the classification of kāla mayakkut tuṟai (similar to pāsurams 7 and 18) which occurs due to bewilderment regarding time. nāyakan who had promised to return by monsoon time, is delaying his return. With the monsoon having arrived, nāyaki is in distress since he has not returned yet. Her friend tells her “This is not rainy season.  What you are seeing now is not rain, but the tears of bhūmippirātti (bhūdēvi)”, thus bewildering the nāyaki into thinking that it is not rainy season. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**aḻaikkum karum kadal** – in line with the saying “*apām api śuklam*” – the colour of water is white. However, when it is in abundance, it would appear to be dark. With the bottom portion of the ocean appearing dark and the top portion appearing white (because of the white coloured waves), it appeared like a place made with bluish gems having whitish steps. When śrī rāma was on the mālyavān mountain along with lakśmaṇa, he told lakśmaṇa looking at the clouds, as in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 28-4 “*śakyam ambaram āruhya mēgha sōpāna panktibhi*: \| *kutaja arjuna mālābhir alankartum divākara*: \|\|” (it appears that it is possible to decorate the supreme entity, who is at the centre of sūryamaṇḍala, through the steps of clouds, with wild jasmine and other flowers). He said that there is a deficiency in this. While garlanding the supreme entity, it is necessary to go with one’s wife, just as it is mentioned in śri rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 6-1 “*saha patnyā viśālākśyā nārāyaṇamupāgatam*” (śrī rāma meditated on śrīman nārāyaṇa along with sītāppirātti, who has wide eyes). While sītā was with us then, she is not here now to help us. This is the deficiency.

**veṇ tiraikkē koṇḍu pōy** –  with the help of those waves which were like hands, taking sarvēśvaran along

**alarvāy** – *alar* – lotus; periya pirātti has divine lips similar to lotus flower. She was born in the lotus flower.

**maḻaikkaṇ madandai** – after looking at the place of sleeping (ādiśēśan mattress) and periya pirātti, he has to follow her. When she looks, it is with cool eyes, just like rain falling on a dry place makes it cool. If he considers her divine mind, he has to follow her.

**aravaṇai Era** – getting atop ādiśēśan, along with periya pirātti.

**maṇ mādar** – bhūmippirātti (śrī bhūdēvi)

**viṇ vāy aḻaittu** – calling through sky, which is lke the mouth; alternatively, calling, standing in the sky. When the waves came up to the sky in agitation, it appeared that the earth was calling out.

**pulambi** – wailing, as if reciting several pāsurams

**mulai mēl ninṟum** – standing on top of mountains, which were like bosoms. Since earth is bhūmippirātti’s wealth, the mountains can be considered to be her bosoms, just as it has been said in siṟiya tirumadal “*kārār varaikkongai*” (completely dark mountains which were like bosoms).

**āṟugaḷāy** – when it rained, the water coming down from the mountains appeared like rivers which were full.

**maḻaik kaṇṇa nīr** – tears which were like rain water. In normal times, it would appear like rain water; now it appeared like her tears.

**tirumāḷ kodiyān enṟu** – we thought that if we see sarvēśvaran and periya pirātti united, it would be a great fortune; however, it is now distressing us. When he manifests his simplicity, it appears like cruelness to us.

**enṟu vārginṟadē** – as she wails mentioning his cruelty, her tears appear like abundant flow of water.

While it is the normal rainy season, the *nāyaki*’s friend tells her “While sarvēśvaran and periya pirātti united, śrī bhūmippirātti, unable to bear that, is shedding copious amount of tears which appear like rainfall”, making her bewildered into thinking that it is  not the rainy season but tears shed by bhūmippirātti.

When sarvēśvaran is united with periya pirātti, both bhūdēvii and neeḷādēvi would think that he has united with their soul and when he is united with bhūdēvi and neeḷādēvi, periya pirātti would think that he has united with her bosoms and shoulders. Thinking like this is their true nature. Since he is without any limit, everyone has to enjoy him together. When such is their true nature, how is it said that bhūdēvi is wailing on seeing sarvēśvaran being united with periya pirātti? Our pūrvācāryars prior to āḷavandār would explain this saying “bhūdēvi is in tears because when sarvēśvaran and periya pirātti are together, we are unable to help them by bringing them flowers, sandalwood paste etc” āḷavandār explained it by quoting tiruvāimoḻi 9-2-10 “*vadiviṇai illā malarmagaḷ*”- I have made such periya pirātti who is unparalleled in her beauty and soft nature to shiver in the cool water and being carried away by the ocean even as I remained as a mattress for her – and wept because of that. parāśara bhattar on hearing this would say “There is no need for all these explanations. The *nāyaki* on hearing such inapt explanation from her friend would have thought ‘How is this possible?’ By the time she analyses this and decides on it, the *nāyakan* would have returned. āḻvār would have mercifully said this, for this position”

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): Since āḻvār cannot sustain himself without attaining sarvēśvaran, he has to be consoled by pointing to events which take place nearby, which may not be true.

## 53. Varayina mulaiyal

*vārāyina mulaiyāḷivaḷ vānōr talaimaganām sīrāyina deyva nal nōy idu deyvattaṇṇanduḻāyt tārāyinum taḻaiyāyinum taṇkombadāyinum kīḻ vērāyinum ninṟa maṇṇāyinum koṇḍu vīsuminē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ivaḷ – this nāyaki; vār āyina – apt bodice with which to tie; mulayāḷ – having bosom; idu – this disease; vānōr – for nityasūris; talaimaganām – being the lord; sīr āyina – having auspicious qualities; deyvam – caused due to the deity; nal – distinguished; nōy – resulted as disease; deyvam – being related to that deity, śrīman nārāyaṇan; taṇ – being cool; am – being beautiful; tuḻāy – divine tuḷasi’s; tār āyinum – whether it is a bunch of flowers; taḻaiyāyinum – whether it is the petals; taṇ – cool; kombadāyinum – whether it is the stem; kīḻ – seen below it; vēr āyinum – whether it is the root; ninṟa – from which that divine tuḷasi plant sprouted; maṇṇāyium – whether it is the soil; koṇḍu – bringing that; vīsumin – blow it such that the wind from that \[object\] falls on her

**Перевод:**

**The nāyaki’s bosoms are apt to be tied with a bodice. The disease that she has is a distinguished one, caused by śrīman nārāyaṇan, the lord of nityasūris and the one who has auspicious qualities. Obtain the cool, beautiful flower of tuḷasi plant or its petals or its stem or its root or the soil from which the plant sprouted and blow it on her such that the wind from that falls on her.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Though the friend had said that it is not the rainy season, in the previous pāsuram, it was raining steadily like a true rainy season. Since the nāyakan who had promised to return before the rains had not returned, the nāyaki fell down unconsciously. Her relatives who had seen this were bewildered, not knowing the reason for her behaviour. They thought that this must be the act of a lowly deity and started engaging in activities to remedy the situation. An old, knowledgeable woman, who was passing that way and who knew her real condition, told them “The remedies which you are trying to carry out will not revive her. Carry out the remedy which I am going to tell you” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vārāyina mulayāḷ ivaḷ** – she is not in a position to accept the harsh remedies which you are carrying out towards a lowly deity. īsn’t her position such that without wasting time, you have to carry out a remedy immediately?

**vārāyina mulayāḷ ivaḷ** – bosom tied by a bodice; bosom sustained by a bodice. Her bosom could be sustained either by a bodice or by the *nāyakan*; it cannot be sustained by her. The vyākyātā explains a few verses from tirumangai āḻWar’s periya tirumadal “*ennivai tān vāḷā enakkē poṟaiyāgi*” (what is the use of these bosoms? They have become an unwanted load for me) starting with the verse

*kal navilum kāttagattu* – in the forest which is full of stones

*ōr vallik kadi malarin* – in a fragrant flower on a creeper

*nal naṟu vāsam* – with great fragrance

*maṟṟārānum eydāmē* – without anyone enjoying that fragrance

*mannum vaṟu nilattu vāḷānguguttadu pōl* – withering on the ground from which it blossomed, without being of use to anyone; similar to that

*ennudaiya peṇmaiyum ennalanum enmulaiyum* – my femininity, my qualities and my bosoms are \[wasted\] like those creeper, flowers and fragrance respectively.

*mannu malar mangai maindan* – sarvēśvaran who has been attained by periya pirātti, who resides on a flower

*kaṇapurattup ponmalai pōl ninṟavan than* – sarvēśvaran should come to tirukkaṇṇapuram, instead of being in śrīvaikuṇṭam, along with periya pirātti

*ponnagalam tōyāvēl* – if I am not embraced by his valorous chest and if my distress is not removed

*ennivaidān vāḷā* – should there not be a purpose for their \[bosoms’\] creation?

*enakkē poṟaiyāgi* – when two people are required to sustain them, what is the purpose in my sustaining them alone? They become unbearable for me. Had they been on my back, I would not have to see them losing their beauty.

*mūvāmaik kāppadōr marundu aṟivīr illaiyē* – there is none who can give a remedy to prevent them from aging and losing their beauty.

parāśara bhattar, on hearing this, said “He is asking whether there is no *āndarāḷikar* in this land” (āndarāḷikar is one who is an intermediary, helping the *nāyakan* and the *nāyaki* in sorting out their love-quarrel. nanjīyar would say that piḷḷai tirunaṟaiyūr araiyar and others who were listening to this explanation by bhattar would praise bhattar’s sharpness for four to five *nāḻigais* (about two hours), without speaking about any other subject. piḷḷai tirunaṟaiyūr araiyar thought that when he is much older than bhattar and has more knowledge too, others may wonder whether it is apt to praise parāśara bhattar like this. Hence he said “Oh child, earlier too ācāryars (teachers) have praised their śiśyars (disciples). In the tirunedundāṇḍagam pāsuram 14 *vaḷarttadanāl payan peṟṟēn* for which you gave certain explanations, the *nāyaki* had nurtured a parrot, taught it the divine names  of emperumān; when the parrot recited those divine names, did she not fall at its feet?” bhattar told him “Oh svāmy! You could praise me by keeping me in whatever position that you desire; but please do not deny us the opportunity of having you in our midst and enjoying your presence”.

The vyākyātā narrates another incident to show how sarvēśvaran becomes elated when someone who is totally devoted to him is praised. Once emperumānār (rāmānujar) was sitting near a *punnai tree* close to candira puśkaraṇi inside śrīrangam temple. tirvarangan \[śrī ranganātan\] had mercifully reached that place as a part of temple festivity. A few people who came there fell at the divine feet of emperumānār and vorśipped him. An officer of the kingdom, udaiyār subrahmaṇya  bhattar, saw this incident. Given below is the conversation between this officer and emperumānār:

He asked emperumānār “I want to ask you a question. Can I?”

“What is it?”

“In the presence of śrī ranganātar, a few persons fell at your feet. They were ignorant. But I did not hear you telling them not to do that. Why is it?”

“Others may ask this question. But your asking this question is not apt”

“What is the difference between others and me?”

“Are you not one who is carrying out service to the King? When someone comes to the court of the King to ask for some help, you would have seen him taking the sandals of the King and keeping them on his head. Would the sandals tell that man ‘Do not keep us on your head’? Is that man truly celebrating the sandals? No. Even if he praises the sandals, are they capable of helping that man? However, doesn’t the King, who is seeing this, feel happy and help the man?” \[In the same way, śrī ranganātan feels happy and helps the people who had fallen at emperumānār’s feet\].

**vārāyina mulayāḷ ivaḷ** – her bosoms are such that the bodice, through its connection with them, realises its true nature. Even the *nāyakan* realises his nature through embracing them. The *nāyaki* too cannot sustain herself without him, just as it has been mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 6-10-10 “*agalagillēn iṟaiyum*” (I cannot leave even for a moment) and in śri rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 53-31 “*na ca sītā tvayā hinā*” ((lakśmaṇa tells śrī rāma) sitāppirātti will not survive without you).

It is due to the connection between the cause of her disease \[sarvēśvaran\] and her disease that āḻvār says *vānōr talaimaganām sīrāyina.*

**sīr āyina** – widespread disease. It is such a rare disease that no one could say that (s)he will be able to cure her from the disease.

**deyva nōy** – it is a disease which has not been caused by any entity in this world.

**nal nōy** – a disease which one has to accept by even making lot of efforts. Alternatively, a disease caused by the supreme entity who is the lord of nityasūris, due to his greatness and auspicious qualities.

**deyvattaṇṇanduḻāy . . .** – make sure that you find a remedy such that it is aligned with your nature as well as the nature of the disease which is afflicting her. Just as it is said “*dēvatāntara katākśa kauleenā*:” (grace from other *dēvatās* (deities) will create major fault for the clan), do not approach other deities.

**deyvam . . .** – try to save her by bringing a material which, through its contact with the supreme entity, has his true nature, such as the leaves of tuḷasi, or its stem or its root or the soil from which the tuḷasi plant sprouted. With other medicines (obtained through the grace of other deities), only harm will result while medicines which are connected with sarvēśvaran, even if they are far removed from sarvēśvaran, will be the apt remedy. Just as it has been mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 3-7-9 “*endai pirān tanakku adiyār adiyār tam adiyār adiyār….*” it is enough if someone has direct contact with sarvēśvaran or contact through several of his followers in a lineage. That alone will do.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): This pāsuram brings out āḻvār’s belief that contact with other deities will be the cause of destruction of one’s existence and contact with any material which has even a remote connection with sarvēśvaran will be the cause for one’s existence

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 54. Visum siragal

*vīsum siṟagāl paṟattīr viṇṇādu nungatkeḷidu pēsumbadiyanna pēsiyum pōvadu ney todu uṇḍu ēsumbadiyanna seyyum emmīsar viṇṇōr piṟānār māsinmalaradikkīḻ emmaiccērvikkum vaṇḍugaḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

viṇṇōr – for nityasūris; pirānār – being their lord; ney – ghee; todu – stealing; uṇḍu – eating it; ēsumbadi – such that (enemies) ridicule; anna – activities like that; seyyum – one who carries out; em īsar – being our svāmy, his; māsu il – without any fault; malar – like a soft flower; adikkīḻ – under \[his\] divine feet; emmai – us; sērvikkum – having the ability to take us \[there\]; vaṇḍugaḷē – Oh beetles!; vīsum – spread out; siṟagāl – with wings; paṟattīr – you are flying; nungatku – for you; viṇṇādu – paramapadam which is in paramākāśam; eḷidu – will be easy (and natural); pēsumbadi – whatever is to be spoken (there); anna – those words; pēsiyum pōvadu – should speak (in our presence)

**Перевод:**

**Oh beetles! You have the capability to take us under the faultless, flower-like soft divine feet of sarvēśvaran who was spoken of derisively after he stole and ate butter from the houses of those who were in tiruvāyppādi (śrī gōkulam) and who is the lord of nityasūris. You are flying with widespread wings. It will be easy for you to reach paramapadam where he dwells. Please tell us the words which you are going to tell there.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, a lady who was going that side said “fan her with any material \[related to emperumān\] even if it is the soil \[from which his divine tuḷasi had sprouted\]” However, there was no need to do anything like that. It appeared that from her words and activities, one would know that she is conscious. When a person is afflicted with deep hunger, he would be able to tolerate it if he were asleep. However, it is not possible to bear that hunger if he were awake. In the same way, since she was unconscious in the previous pāsuram, it appeared to be normal. She had forgotten him for sometime. Since she is awake now, she can sustain herself only if someone comes from there \[from the nāyakan\] or someone goes from her side as a messenger to him and to await his arrival. It did not appear that anyone would come from there. From her side, there was none who could walk to his place. She is sending beetles which are near her, to go as her messenger to him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vīsum siṟagāl** – The term *vīsugai* refers to swaying, speed and being widespread.

**siṟagāl paṟattīr** – she says that she has to walk since she does not have wings. That is why she swooned, thinking of him, as in tiruvāimoḻi 7-2-4 “*itta kāl itta kaiyaḷāy*” (legs and hands are immovable)

**viṇṇādu nungatku eḷidu** – The moment they take to the skies she thinks that they can fly all the way to paramapadam. She is firm that paramapdam is above only, just as it has been mentioned in nācciyār tirumoḻi 10-2 “*mēlulagangaḷin mīdu pōy*” (going beyond the worlds above).

**nungatku eḷidu** – paramapadam is easy for you to reach. It is difficult only for us! She feels that all those who could fly would be able to reach paramapadam.

**pēsumbadi anna pēsiyum pōvadu** – please tell us what you are going to tell him and others who are there.

**ney todu . . .** – despite being the lord of the inexhaustible nityasūris, he incarnated on this earth. He felt that he could not sustain himself without materials such as milk, curd, butter etc which have had the touch of herd-girls who were very affectionate towards him. He had to steal them since they were not easily accessible to him and ate them; he was derided by people like śiśupālan et al who were inimical to him, because of such acts.

**emmīsar** – he has the eminence of being the controller of nityasūris; at the same time, he has the simplicity to consider that he could not sustain himself without materials connected with his followers; with these qualities, he made me to exist exclusively for him.

**māsin malaradi** – his divine, soft feet which are without any fault. It is not that he goes to someone only after that person sends him a messenger since he had not gone to that person, saying “Oh, I forgot completely”. Even if someone were to send him a message, he would put the words in the messenger’s mouth.

The vyākyātā explains the ślōkam 40-3 in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam “*yatā tam puruśa vyāgram gātrai: śōka abhikarśitai*: \| *samspriśēyam sakāmā aham tatā kuru dayām mayi* \|\|” (due to grief, my form has become very enervated. I wish to embrace that tiger among men, śrī rāma. Show mercy on me and fulfil my desire) \[this ślōkam tells how sītāppirātti informs hanuman about her condition and her desire, so that he could take that message to śrī rāma\]

*yatā tam puruśa vyāgram* – I don’t have to think that time has passed and that I have to tell him something to make him do it. He is like a male tiger who is capable of carrying out whatever he thinks of, the moment he thinks about it.

*gātrai*: – You could see that my physical form is becoming emaciated. It is not that I wish to get rid of this form, attain a new form and then go to him. I want to embrace him with this emaciated form itself.

*sa kāmāham* – I will not remain like him uttering words such as “If you come to the forest, I will not go; if you are lost, I will not search for you”

*tatā kuru dayām mayi* – even when there is no reduction in his power, I have not seen it coming up to me. With your mercy, you should make it happen for me

**emmaic cērvikkum vaṇḍugaḷē** – āren’t you the beetles who feel that you would realise your *svarūpam* (true nature) only if you unite me with him?

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): āḻvār is conveying his distress in separation to sarvēśvaran’s divine heart through the mode of sending messengers.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 55. Vandugalo vammin

*vaṇḍugaḷō vammin nīrppū nilappū marattiloṇpū uṇḍu kaḷittu uḻalvīrkku onṟu uraikkiyam ēnamonṟāy maṇḍugaḷādi vaigundam annāḷ kuḻalvāy virai pōl viṇḍugaḷ vārum malar uḷavō num viyalidattē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vaṇḍugaḷō – Oh beetles!; nīrppū – among the flowers in water; nilappū – among the flowers on land; marattil – on trees; oṇ – beautiful; pū­ – flowers; uṇḍu – drinking (honey); kaḷittu – becoming happy; uḻalvīrkku – you, who are roaming; onṟu – a word; uraikkiyam – I will say; vammin – please come; oru – unique; ēnamāy – as a varāham (wild boar); maṇ – earth’s; tugaḷ – dust; ādi – one who took it throughout his divine form; vaigundamannāḷ – on this nāyaki who is like paramapadam (being very much enjoyable), her; kuḻal vāy – on her locks; virai pōl – being very fragrant; viṇḍu – spread well; kaḷ – honey; vārum – flowing copiously; malar – flower; num – your; viyal – amazing; idattu – places; uḷadō – is it there?

**Перевод:**

**(This has been told by the nāyakan to the beetles)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) The nāyakan comes and unites with the nāyaki after she sends him her errand. Subsequently the time to separate from her came. In order to make her sustain herself even in separation, he praises her so that she thinks “Oh, such a person will not delay in returning”. This comes under the classification of nalam pārāttudal (similar to pāsuram 14). śrī rāma too praised sītāppirātti as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 26-20 “kulē mahati sambhūtē” (Oh one who was born in a great clan!) Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

Oh beetles! I am going to tell you all, who are drinking honey from flowers which are present on water, on land and on trees and are roaming happily. Please come and listen to me. Among the places where you keep roaming, have you seen the sweet fragrance on flowers, which you are seeing on the locks of this *nāyaki* who is as enjoyable as the paramapadam of sarvēśvaran who incarnated as a unique wild boar and took on all the dust which is present on this earth?

**vandugaḷō vammin** – Oh beetles! Please come here.

**nīrppū . . .** – some people would say that three types of flowers are referred to here while some others would say that there are four types of flowers. In the term *nilappū* (flowers on land), people would say that two types of flowers are included – those on plants and those on creepers. Added to the flowers on water and flowers on trees, there are four types of floves, according to these people. There is an old saying *nalla malarppoḻil nālum nuḻaivīrgāḷ* (may you enter all the four types of good orchards). I will say something to you, who are going around to wherever flowers are seen, being happy by drinking honey from them and having that as your main work. Please come.

**ēnam . . .** – he incarnated as the unique, great boar, trampling the earth. When that earth attained him, due to his pride, he took its dust all over his divine form.

**vaigundam annāḷ** – this *nāyaki* is very much concordant with his permanent abode of vaigundam, unlike this vibhūti (samsāram) where he comes occasionally to protect. She will not let any other enjoyment to come to her heart. She is saying just as āḷavandār had mercifully said in his stōtra ratnam ślōkam 5 “*sarvam yadēva niyamēna*” (by whomsoever everything is controlled…)

**kuḻal vāy virai pōl** – just like the sweet fragrance on her locks.

**vindugaḷ . . .** – Have you seen among the flowers on the wonderful earth where you roam around fully, any fragrance like this, such that you will not bother about passage of time?

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): śrīvaiṣṇavas enjoy the auspicious qualities of sarvēśvaran in his para, vyūha, vibhava etc forms (sarvēśvaran in paramapadam, in tiruppāṟkadal, in incarnations etc). They are asked “Among all the qualities which you enjoy there, have you seen any quality similar to the one that āḻvār has?” In other words, the greatness of *bhāgavata* (follower of sarvēśvaran) is higher than that of *bhagavān* (sarvēśvaran). vyākyātā quotes from taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli “*ambasyapārē bhuvanasya madyē nākasya pruśtē*” wherein *ambasya* is taken as being equivalent to vyūham, *bhuvanasya* is taken as being equivalent to vibhavam and arccai, and *nākasya* is taken as being equivalent to param. These are the three different types of flowers mentioned in the first line.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 56. Viyalidam unda

*viyalidam uṇḍa pirānār vidutta tiruvaruḷāl uyalidam peṟṟu uyndam anjalam tōḻi ōr taṇ tenṟal vandu ayalidai yārum aṟindilar am pūnduḻāyin in tēn puyaludai nīrmaiyināl tadaviṟṟu en pulan kalanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

am – beautiful; tōḻi – Oh friend!; viyal – amazing; idam – earth; uṇḍa – one who ate; pirānār – sarvēśvaran, the benefactor; vidutta – gave; tiru – praiseworthy; aruḷāl – due to mercy; uyal – for being uplifted; idam peṟṟu – getting an opportunity; uyndam – we got our lives back; anjal – do not fear; ōr – being unique; taṇ – cool; tenṟal – southern wind; vandu – coming and blowing gently; am – beautiful; pū – having flowers; tuḻāyin – available in divine tuḷasi; in – enjoyable; tēn – honey; puyaludai – cloud’s; nīrmaiyināl – due to its nature; en – my; pulan – senses; kalan – ornaments; tadaviṟṟu – touched; ayalidai yārum – none in the vicinity; aṟindilar – knew (it)

**Перевод:**

**(The nāyaki tells her friend)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) The nāyakan, after being united with the nāyaki for some time, separated from her. The nāyaki’s friend realised that there was a delay in his returning and that the nāyaki was in distress because of that. The friend thought “Her sadness due to the separation is so harsh; there is no sign of his returning; she will decide that there is no reason for her to be alive anymore. Thus, will we lose her?” The nāyaki realised her friend’s predicament. At that time, by chance, the nāyaki felt that there was a connection with a material which had a connection with him. She tells her friend “Without your knowledge, a connection happened just now, with him. Hence, you do not have to feat about my position”. She narrates the connection that she got, to her friend. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

Oh beautiful friend! Due to a distinguished merciful act of sarvēśvaran who is the benefactor of this amazing earth, I got an opportunity to uplift myself and live. Do not fear. The cool, southerly breeze, carrying honey from the flowers of divine tuḷasi, and with the nature of clouds, came here and blew gently on my senses and my ornaments. None here knew it.

The friend told *nāyaki* “If we have to get his mercy just as *bhūmi* (earth) had got, we should have something with us \[which he likes\]. But we do not know as to what it is” …

**viyal idam uṇḍa** – whatever the earth had during deluge, we have with us now, says the *nāyaki*. It is enough if he thinks that we are in danger. This is the only requirement which he expects from us. During the time of deluge, he had kept earth inside his stomach and protected it. Did earth have any other recompense with her to receive his help? Whatever distressed condition that the earth was in then, is seen in the *nāyaki* too now.

**pirānār** – he has only the quality of bestowing benefit \[to others\]. All that is required of the recipient to receive his mercy is to be an apt entity for that. He does not offer a help, expecting anything in return.

The vyākyātā narrates an incident from the life of emperumānār (bhagavat rāmānujar). Some śrīvaiṣṇavas came to offer their obeisance to emperumānār at the place where he had mercifully come. After falling at his divine feet, they asked him “What should we think of as our refuge if we have to come out of this ocean of samsāram and reach its shore?” vangipurattu nambi, one of rāmānujar’s disciples, told them to think of the words of kśatrabandu, just as it has been mentioned in viṣṇu darmam ślōkam 98-35 “*mūdōyam alpamati alpaviśēśtitōyam kaliśtām manōsya vinayair namayi prasangi* \| *ittam krupām kuru mayi praṇatē klēśa tvām stōtum ambuja bhavōpi hi dēvanēśa \|\|* ” (Oh the lord of everyone and everything! I have a lowly knowledge, indulging in only lowly activities; this mind of mine is looking for wrongful activities alone. My mind is refusing to obey me. Hence, you have to help me who is coming to you for help. Even *nānmukhan* (brahmā) does not have the knowledge to worship you completely). emperumānār, after listening to this, said “It is a difficult condition to attain. The word *praṇatē* (for me who is vorśipping you) indicates that only the one who thinks that he is *nirmama* (nothing belongs to me) should fall at the divine feet of the omniscient sarvēśvaran. If we think that we attain a benefit only by carrying out some activity, engaging in that activity will create a sorrow in us and will prevent us from getting that benefit. If we think that we have a qualification with us, that qualification will only stop him from bestowing a benefit on us without any reason. That qualification will not be the means to receive his mercy. Even though we should necessarily have the thought ‘you should be our means’ even that thought should come only from him. Even if one gets to the stage of *paramabhakti* (attaining sarvēśvaran), that *paramabhakti* will only end in his getting the taste to enjoy sarvēśvaran, but it will not, on its own, have the capability to grant him what he desires”. The śrīvaiṣṇavas then asked emperumānār “What else should we think of as our refuge?” He responded, saying “Only the words of *kāḷiyan* are apt to be considered as our refuge” \[*kāḷiyan* is a snake who had troubled the herd and herdsfolk at śrī gōkulam by remaining in the river yamunā\]. His prayer to kriṣṇa, who had come to drive him away from yamunā is seen in śrī viṣṇu purāṇam ślōkam 5-7-10 “*sōham tē dēva dēvēśa nārcanātau stutau na ca* \| *sāmārtyavān krupāmātra manōvrutti prasīdamē* \|\| ” (Oh the lord of dēvatās! I am incapable of praising or vorśipping you. For such a person like me, you should yourself shower your mercy; your divine mind should feel happy and show mercy on me). This ślōkam is explained in detail here, by the vyākyātā:

*sōham* – I, who had coiled myself around your divine form and indulged in activities which are not to your liking and had not freed myself from such activities; such a lowly person

*dēva dēvēsa*: your quality is such that not indulging in activities which are not to your liking alone is sufficient to qualify for your mercy. You consider our saying the words “You should shower your mercy” as “renouncing the undesired”. Just as it has been mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 5-6-4 “*nanju sōrvadōr venjina aravam veruvi vandu nin Saran enac caraṇā* …” (the snake *sumukhan* which spits poison and which is fiery and angry, out of fear for garuda, took refuge under you saying “I am surrendering to you”) Though its anger was against it, since it surrendered by merely saying the words “*śaraṇam*” it was granted that. *veruvi vandu* – it came running to him out of fear \[of garuda\]. The fear towards its enemy alone was with the snake

*nārcanātau stutaunaca sāmārtyavān* – \[*kāliyan* tells kriṣṇa\] I am capable of doing everything for myself but incapable if I have to do even a little bit for you \[that is my nature\]. You are such that you do not expect anything for yourself, from me; I am without any strength to do anything. kriṣṇa tells *kāḷiyan* “If you are capable of doing anything for yourself and incapable of doing anything for me, what is it that you want me to do?”

*krupā mātra manōvrutti* – You should show your mercy on me, says *kāḷiyan*. kriṣṇa tells him “For that you need remorse” *kāḷiyan* responds saying “But I do  not have even that remorse. Whatever you want to do for me with my feeling of remorse, you should do with your mercy itself” vyākyātā cites the reference from śrīrangarājastavam 2-97 “*balibhujiśiśupālē*” (*kākāsuran* and śiśupālan) \[*kākāsuran* was indra’s son who took the form of a crow in order to trouble sītāppirātti when she was with śrī rāma in the forest and śiśupālan was kriṣṇa’s cousin, who poured scorn on kriṣṇa all the time\]. Just as it is mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 38-34 “*sa tam nipatitam*” (that crow fell on earth and sought refuge) the crow experienced sorrow and sought refuge by falling on the earth. śiśupālan kept reciting your names \[though his intention was to abuse kriṣṇa, that he recited the names was sufficient\] to get recognised by kriṣṇa. *kāḷiyan* continues “Your mercy was refused for them because of the initial lowly thoughts that they had. However, since no such fault can be ascribed to me, your mercy can get manifested beautifully”.

**vidutta tiruvaruḷāl** – through the mercy showered by him; without expecting anything from us, treating our benefit as his own, and showering his mercy.

**vidutta** – allowed to come; given

**uyalidam peṟṟu uyndam** – the friend thought “She has no scope to live; I will not be able to see her alive”. The *nāyaki* tells the friend “An opportunity arose for my being uplifted. Instead of waiting for it to happen, we got it immediately and got our lives back”. Since the friend had known the *nāyakan* too, she had thought that she had lost both of them. Now, everyone has got uplifted.

**anjal** – do not fear

**am tōḻi** *–* you are the person who considers my benefit as yours. We are similar to the friendship between śrī rāma and sugrīva as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 5-17 “*ēkam du:kham sukham ca nau*” (our mirth and misery are one). Is it not enough to say *tōḻi* (friend)? Why address her as *am tōḻi* (beautiful friend)? Some people would look for same benefits in both theirs and their friends’. However, this friend is such that she does not look for any benefit for herself. She considers whatever benefit that the *nāyaki* gets as her own. This is why she is addressed as *am tōḻi*. śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-16 says “*suhrudō vā bhavadvidā:*” – śrī rāma tells sugrīva “Not all friends are like you”. śrī rāma further tells sugrīva “As soon as you saw me, you considered your elder brother \[vāli\] as your enemy, you had more confidence in me than in your brother and you tried to get answers to your confidential problems too. Such was your friendship with me” The *nāyaki* tells her friend “You considered my benefit as yours and my loss as yours; you were fearful of my loss; now, you do not have to fear”

**ōr taṇ tenṟal vandu** – earlier, the southern breeze was causing me harm; now a cool southern breeze came, tells the *nāyaki*. Breeze will be cool only when united; the *nāyaki* subtly hints that union did take place. The friend too accepts that and is worried as to what will happen if others around know about it. The *nāyaki* says . . .

**ayalidai yārum aṟindilar** – None in our vicinity knew about it.

The friend asks her as to what happened further? . . . .

**am pūnduḻāyin intēn** – the fine drops of honey collected from the flowers of cool, divine tuḷasi

**puyaludai nīrmaiyināl** – the southerly breeze assumed the quality of the cool clouds. This talks about the help rendered by the breeze when one had not expected it.

**tadaviṟṟu en pulan kalanē** – it gently touched all my sensory organs and all my ornaments. It touched my sensory organs as if to satisfy their thirst and the ornaments so that they do not slip out of my limbs. Thus the *nāyaki* tells her friend as to how the *nāyakan* united with her.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): āḻvār describes that he survived due to the knowledge of sarvēśvaran’s auspicious qualities. taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli says *ānandam brahmaṇō vidvān nabibhēti kutaścana* – one who knows about the joyfulness of brahmam (sarvēśvaran) does not fear anything.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 57. Pulakkundala

*pulakkuṇḍalap puṇḍarīgatta pōrkkeṇḍai valli onṟālvilakkuṇḍu ulāginṟu vēl viḻikkinṟana kaṇṇan kaiyālmalakkuṇḍu amudam suranda maṟikadal pōnṟavaṟṟālkalakkuṇḍa nānṟu kaṇḍār emmai yārum kaḻaṟalarē*

**Пословный перевод:**

pulam – visible; kuṇḍalam – having ear-rings; puṇḍarīgatta – on the face which is like a lotus flower; pōr – engaged in a war; keṇḍai – eyes which are like fish; valli onṟāl – with the nose which looks like a creeper; vilakkuṇḍu – being separated; ulāginṟu – are roaming; vēl – cruel, like the weapon spear; viḻikkinṟana – are looking; avaṟṟāl – through such activities; kaṇṇan – kriṣṇa’s; kaiyāl – with divine hands; malakkuṇḍu – being agitated; amudam – nectar; suranda – secreted; maṟi – throwing up waves; kadal pōnṟu – like an ocean; kalakkuṇḍa – agitated; nānṟu – during that time; kaṇḍār – those who saw; yārum – whosoever; ennai – me; kaḻaṟalar – will not admonish

**Перевод:**

**The eyes of the nāyaki which appear to be battling each other, present on her lotus-like face having beautiful ear rings, are separated by the nose, which is like a creeper. The two eyes are moving around, looking in a cruel way, like a spear. Just like the ocean which was agitated by the divine hands of kriṣṇa and secreted the nectar, I too am bewildered. Whosoever had seen me at that time, would not find fault with me.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) After being united with the nāyaki, when the nāyakan separated from her, his distress became more acute. Though he had to separate from her for a reason, just as it is mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 66-10 “na jīvēyam kśaṇamapi” (I cannot live even for a moment without her), his distress in separation was very difficult for him. Looking at his distress in separation and a lack of liveliness in him, the nāyakan’s friend told him “The way you are distressed about one issue, without engaging with other matters, does not fit well with your leadership qualities” and tried to bring him back to normal situation. The nāyakan tells him “You are speaking like this since you do not know how those who have been the target of her glance have suffered. If you too experience her glance, you would not speak like this” denying his accusation. Let us go torugh the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pulakkuṇḍala . . .** – The *nāyakan*’s friend had spoken about the condition of *nāyakan* without knowing the facts. The *nāyakan* tells him about the eyes of the *nāyaki* so that he will realise as to what would happen if he were to undergo a similar experience. He would then say “Oh my bad! What all did he suffer? How did he manage?” The ear rings of the *nāyaki* are capable of stealing all the sensory organs. They are present on her face which looks like a lotus flower. The term *kuṇḍalam* would also refer to a circular shape. If the text is *polakkuṇḍalam*, it would refer to golden ear-rings.

**puṇḍarigatta pōrkkeṇḍai** – the eyes look like carp fish which appear to be fighting. The face is capable of making a person to become immobile, more than any other limb. The eyes are the most prominent part, on such a face.

**valli onṟāl vilakkuṇḍu** – Just like keeping a cross-bar between two rutting elephants which are fighting, her nose is present \[between the two fighting eyes\]

**ulāginṟu** – since the nose is there between them, the two eyes, which are like fish, are unable to engage in direct combat with each other. Due to the limitation set by sarvēśvaran, they are unable to cross the ears too. Hence they are roaming between these two boundaries.

**vēl viḻikkinṟana** – their glance is like a sharp spear, capable of piercing through the other side.

**kaṇṇan . . .** – the milky ocean was churned by the divine hands of kriṣṇa such that the sand at the bottom was brought to its surface; the ocean gave him its life saving *amrutam* (nectar). The *nāyakan*’s majesty was similar to that ocean’s. The term *maṟikadal pōnṟu* is to be considered as *maṟikadal pōnṟavaṟṟāl*, thus bringing a distinction to the eyes (the eyes are like an agitating ocean). Once she looks at him, he too looks at her. Being engaged with his glance, she manifests her engagement with him, both inside her heart and outside, in her eyes. The *nāyakan* now reminisces that.

**avaṟṟāl** – those eyes create a scar in him, even now, through their glance.

**kalakkuṇḍa nānṟu kaṇḍār** – those who saw me that day when I was bewildered \[by her glance\]

**emmai yārum kaḻaṟalarē** – apart from you, a friend who knows my nature well, even those who have not known me, will not speak a word ill of me.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): śrīvaiṣṇavas were fully engaged with āḻvār’s *śrīvaiṣṇavaśrī* (wealth of āḻvār’s service). They could not be retrieved even by showing them matters relating to sarvēśvaran. This pāsuram depicts such a position of śrīvaiṣṇavas, who remained just like madurakavi āḻvār who said as in kaṇṇinuṇ siṟuttāmbu 2 “*dēvumaṟṟaṟiyēn*” (I do not know of any deity other than nammāḻvār). They will take a liking to matters relating to sarvēśvaran only because āḻvār liked them, just like the saying *bhavata: paramōmata:.*

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
