## 58. Kalaralam onre

*kaḻaṟalam onṟē nilam muḻudāyiṟṟu oru kaḻal pōy niḻaṟara ellā visumbum niṟaindadu nīṇḍa aṇḍattu uḻaṟalar jñānac cudar viḷakkāy uyarndōrai illā aḻaṟalar tāmaraikkaṇṇan ennō ingu aḷakkinṟadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

onṟu – being single; kaḻal talamē – divine foot itself; nilam muḻudu – entire earth; āyiṟṟu – pervaded; oru kaḻal – the other divine foot; pōy – growing; niḻal tara – as it gave shade; ellā visumbum – entire sky; niṟaindadu – pervaded completely; nīṇḍa – expansive; aṇḍattu – in the oval shaped universe; uḻaṟu – roaming fully; alar – blossomed; jñānam – knowledge; sudar – having radiance; viḷakkāy – being self illuminating like a lamp; uyarndōrai illā – not having anyone superior; aḻaṟu – in mud; alar – blossomed; tāmarai – like a lotus; kaṇṇan – sarvēśvaran who has divine eyes; ingu – at this place; aḷakkinṟadu – measuring; ennō – what is it?

**Перевод:**

**One divine foot measured such that the entire world came under it. The other divine foot grew, went up the sky such that it pervaded all the words above and gave shadow to the ones below. What is kriṣṇa, who keeps roaming all over the universe, who has a radiant, self-illuminating knowledge and who has eyes which are like lotus flowers which have blossomed in slush, measuring in my case?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār now narrates an incident carried out by sarvēśvaran by which the different types of fatigue experienced earlier get negated. He kept his divine feet on the heads of everyone, without discriminating between those who are favourable to him (his followers) and those who are not favourable to him (his enemies). Through this, he said that there is none who he considers as an enemy. āḻvār mercifully mentions that after doing all these activities, sarvēśvaran remained as if he had not done anything. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kaḻaṟalamonṟē nilamuḻudāyiṟṟu** – as soon as a drop of water \[from mahābali\] fell on \[sarvēśvaran’s\] divine hand, one divine foot spread out such that the entire earth came under it. *kaḻaṟalam* – kaḻal talam (foot)

**onṟē** –  without waiting for his thoughts on what is to be done, the foot, on its own, spread out fully \[and measured the earth\].

**oru kaḻal . . .** – the other divine foot

**niḻaṟara ellā visumbum niṟaindadu** – even as it entered its domain, with the confidence that he is there, it gave shade so that everyone can get under it; spread out everywhere.

**niḻaṟara** – niḻal tara; to provide shade. Just as a flood would flow towards low lying regions, it \[the divine foot\] went to all open spaces and pervaded. *vāsudēva taruccāyā* – shade of the tree vāsudēvan (sarvēśvaran). It became the shade of one tree which had pervaded everywhere. *nātiśītā . . .* – it will not be too cold; it will not be too warm; it will not allow the huge fire, *naragam*, hell, to come near. *sākimartam nasēvyatē* – even if another shade which is an exact match for this is seen, is it proper to take shelter under the other shade? Could one say that this shade is not good? periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 5-3-4 “*tirumālirunjōlai endāy un pāda niḻalallāl maṟṟōr iruppidam nān engum kāṇginṟilēn*” (Oh the lord of tirumālirum sōlai! Other than the shade of your divine foot, I do not see any shade elsewhere) – since you are the protector of your followers and eliminator of the enemies of your followers, I have not seen a shade where I could take refuge other than the shade of your divine feet.

The vyākyātā explains in detail, two ślōkams from śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 15-19 and 15-20 to explain how sarvēśvaran’s divine feet provide comforting shade and are the only refuge for all.  tārā tells vāli that she has heard from angadan (their son) who had gone to play in the forest, that sugrīva has got some apt companions; on being questioned, angadan had told her that it is perumāḷ who has come to sugrīva, along with his brother and that perumāḷ has given refuge to sugrīva. vāli asks her “Will he not give us refuge?” to which she recites ślōkam 15-19 “*nivāsa vrukśa: sādūnām āpannānām parā gati:* \| *ārtānām samśraya*: *caiva yaśasa: ca ēka bhājanam* \|\|”

*nivāsa vrukśassādūnām*: tārā tells vāli “He \[śrī rāma\] is like a shade for those who desire to take refuge under him. He offers to others flowers, fruits, shade etc while taking sunlight on himself. For those who stand under his shade and wish to utilise him for gaining materials, he has the quality of offering even himself. vāli asks her “I have not caused any harm to him. Is there any objection in standing under his shade?” tārā replies . . .

*āpannānām* – it is not sufficient to think that we will take refuge under him. We should be under the grip of difficulties. vāli responds “But we are in difficult condition only. The brother born with me is himself my enemy” tārā responds, saying . . .

*ārtānām* – “It is not enough if we are in difficulties. We should also think that we need a refuge” vāli tells her “If śrī rāma is the refuge for sugrīva, we will find out another who will be our refuge” tārā tells him . . .

*yaśasaccaikabhājanam*: “He is the only refuge. There is none like him”

The vyākyātā cites the reference of bhagavat gītā 7-16 “*ārta: jijñāsu artārtī jñānī . .* ” – one who is craving, one who is looking for knowledge, one who desires wealth and one who knows me – there are four types of people in the world. In the śrī rāmāyaṇa ślōkam cited above, the term *sādūnām* would refer to *jñāni* (as given above), the term *āpannānām* would refer to *kaivalyārti* (one who wishes to enjoy his own ātmā) and the term *ārtānām* would refer to both types of *artārti* (one who is looking for lost wealth and one who is looking for wealth for the first time). The vyākyātā raises a query: Would the words of a lady (tārā) have such deep meaning? He answers this himself quoting śrī rāmāyaṇam bālakāṇḍam 4-6 ślōkam “*vēdō’pabruhmaṇārtāya*” (capable of explaining the meanings of vēdas (sacred texts)) Since śrī rāmāyaṇam is capable of explaining the meanings of vēdas, every word in it has deep meanings. bhagavat gītā 7-17 “*ēka bhaktir viśiśyatē*” (since he is deeply devoted to me, he attains eminence). Such a devotee is very much attached to me and I am attached to him, says kriṣṇa. One should detest samsāram (life in the materialistic world) and break all ties with prakruti (repeated cycle of birth and death). This holds true for *ātmaprāptikāman* (one who desires to attain sarvēśvaran) and then to the one who is looking for lost wealth. This is true of the one who wishes to have wealth, for the first time, too.

**nīṇḍa . . .** – due to the reason of desiring to pervade the entire expansive universe, one who has a splendorous, well grown knowledge. He considered keeping his divine foot on the heads of all people as his benefit. The happiness which he obtained due to that act made his entire form to blossom.

**uyarndōrai illā** – Since all the entities including brahmā were held under the divine feet, there was none to say “I” \[he doesn’t have anyone superior to him\].

**aḻaṟalar tāmaraikkaṇṇan** – one who has his divine eyes similar to blossomed lotus flowers standing in slush. Just as cāndōjña upaniśat 1-6-7 says “*tasya yatā kapyāsam puṇḍarīkam ēvam akśiṇī* ” (having reddish eyes similar to inverted lotus flower)

**ennō ingu aḷakkinṟadē** – We could not see the joy of having measured. There should be a time interval for saying that he measured. Even that time interval was not seen. Since he stood in the same place, can we say that he measured? Only if a step has been moved after keeping one step could it be said that something has been measured. Thus, none saw him measuring. Alternatively, it could be said that even as he kept his divine foot on the heads of everyone, without any reason, he stood as if he had done nothing. āḻvār feels exhilarated in his divine mind, thinking as to what he cannot do.

**ennō ingu aḷakkinṟadē** – Will anyone measure if the entity to be measured is extremely small and the entity, which is measuring it, is too large? Thus, the opinion here is that since the people to be protected are very small, he stood as if he has nothing to do.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 59. Alapparum tanmai

*aḷapparum tanmaiyavvūḻi angangul andaṇṇanduḻāyk kuḷapperum kādalil nīḷiyavāyuḷa ōngu munnīr vaḷapperu nādan madusūdanan ennum valvinaiyēn taḷapperu nīṇmuṟuval seyyavāya tadamulaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

val – being cruel; vinaiyēn – as my daughter, who has sins; taḷam – like jasmine buds; peru – having greatness; nīḷ – without limit (being enjoyable); muṟuval – having soft smile; seyya – reddish; vāy – having lips; tadam – expansive; mulai – she, who is having bosoms; aḷappa – to measure; arum – difficult; tanmai – having nature; avvūḻi – more than that kalpam (a day of brahmā); am – beautiful; taṇṇam – being cool; tuḻāykku – for divine tuḷasi; uḷa – formed in (my) heart; peru – huge; kādalil – more than desire; nīḷiyavāy – being bigger; uḷa – is present; (ennum – she will say so); ōngu – being well grown; munnīr – made of three types of water; vaḷam – having richness; peru – being expansive; nādan – one who has land; madusūdanan – destroyer of demon madu; ennum – she will say so

**Перевод:**

**(The mother is speaking with reference to the state of her daughter)**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār was joyous, praising the incarnation of trivikrama, by saying “sarvēśvaran, who is difficult to attain in śrīvaikuṇṭam, incarnated here and made himself easily approachable”. While his separation from sarvēśvaran when he was in śrīvaikuṇṭam did not trouble him much, āḻvār felt very sad due to his separation from sarvēśvaran despite his coming here and taking residence. He attained the state of a pirātti (āḻvār in feminine form). Though there was a connection with sarvēśvaran, since he did not come to see the nāyaki (āḻvār in feminine form) during night time, which is the apt time for being together, she was unable to bear the distress of being separated from him now, after being united with him earlier. Her cry of “This night is harmful to me” is recited by her mother, in this pāsuram. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

Being the daughter of me has cruel sins, she has greatness of a soft smile similar to jasmine buds, reddish lips and expansive bosoms. She says “The night times, which are crueler than *kalpams* which are very difficult to measure, are longer and bigger than my desire which is present in my mind for the cool, divine tuḷasi. madusūdanan has the expansive earth which is surrounded by ocean, which is formed from the three types of water and which is huge”.

**aḷapparum tanmaiyavvūḻiyam kangul** – night times, which are as long as *praḷayam* (deluge) are difficult to measure even for sarvēśvaran who measured with his divine feet the immeasurable entities too. If it is possible for him to measure them, vouldn’t he have appeared during the night? These nights have as their nature, the quality of being immeasurable.

**ūḻiyam kangul** – *ūḻik kālam* (deluge) is the proof that this night is indeed of long duration.

vyākyātā raises a query: Is it not possible to recite a pāsuram on that which cannot be measured? He answers that himself, saying . . .

**andaṇṇanduḻāy . . . .** – Just as it has been mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 10-10-10 “*adaniṟ periya en avā*” (you, with your desire, surrounded me who has a huge desire to attain you). āḻvār’s desire pervaded even sarvēśvaran. vēdam returned empty handed, after trying to measure the joy of sarvēśvaran, as mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat “*yatō vācō nivartantē*”. The same is true of this \[measuring the length of night\] also. bruhadāraṇya upaniśat could only say in the negative when trying to describe sarvēśvaran, saying “*nēti nēti*” (he is not like this), but could not define clearly that he is like this. *andaṇṇanduḻāy* – the garland on his divine shoulders. Those divine garlands have the power to control over everything in my mind. These nights are larger than the affection that I have for those garlands.

**ōngu munnīr vaḷapperunādan madusūdanan ennum** – am I distressed because I am outside his boundaries of reign? Am I distressed because he is powerless? He is the lord of the opulent land which is surrounded by ocean which contains three types of water (water from rivers, water from springs and water from rains).

**madusūdanan** – he is the destroyer of demon *madu* who was like a weed for the entities which were to be protected \[sarvēśvaran’s followers\]. Am I suffering because I am not connected to sarvēśvaran? Am I suffereing because he is incapable of destroying my enemies?

**valvinaiyēn** – if her beauty \[these are the words of *nāyaki*’s mother\] were to be considered, there is no need for being distressed like this. The reason for her suffering, when actually it should he who should be suffering for not being together with her when she is so beautiful, is only because of my sin. This is similar to bharatāḻvān feeling distressed about what happened in ayōdyā which resulted in śrī rāma leaving for the forest, said that this is “*math pāpam Eva*” (only because of my sin).

What is the reason for the mother saying that it is her sin which is the reason for the *nāyaki*’s suffering?

**taḷapperu . . .** – Is there any deficiency in the *nāyaki*’s physical form? *taḷappu* has got shortened to *taḷa*. *taḷappu* denotes something similar to the buds of jasmine, something which is difficult to obtain. Just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 64-77 “*purēva mē cāru datim*” (sītāppirātti who had a beautiful row of teeth), it is the smile which is desired by the *nāyakan* and which melts his heart wherever he goes.

**nīḷ muṟuval** – it is not the boisterous laughter of narasimhap perumāḷ; it is the gentle smile of a lady. However, an entity must be available like this. The word *nīḷ* refers to the enjoyability of that smile.

**seyya . . .** – having reddish lips which will be suitable for that gentle smile. Alternatively, since the next word spoken about is *tada mulai*, referring to the hugeness of her bosoms, the word *seyyavāy* could be added to *tadamulai* to make it as *seyyavāy tadamulai* as a single word, in which case it would refer to the straightness of the bosom.

**tadamulaiyē** – due to the separation from sarvēśvaran, the bosom appeared like the ear of corn which has drooped. It appeared that the time frame set by the *nāyakan* was insufficient.

*svāpadēśam* (distinghiśed meaning) – this refers to the words of śrīvaiṣṇavas who speak of the distress of separation caused by the incongruence of the connection and knowledge of true nature of āḻvār with the time suitable for enjoyment.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 60. Mulaiyo mulumurrum

*mulaiyō muḻumuṟṟum pōndila moy pūnguḻal kuṟiya kalaiyō araiyillai nāvō kuḻaṟum kadal maṇṇellām vilaiyō ena miḷirum kaṇ ivaḷ paramē perumān malaiyō tiruvēngadam enṟu kaṟkinṟa vāsagamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

mulaiyō – if one considers her bosom; muḻumuṟṟum – even a little bit; pōndila – have not sprouted; moy – being dense; pū – being beautiful; kuḻal – locks; kuṟiya – are short; kalaiyō – if one considers her dress; araiyillai – has not been properly tied on the waist; nāvō – if one considers her tongue; kuḻaṟum – will not be clear; kadal – surrounded by the ocean; maṇ ellām – this entire earth; vilaiyō ena – will it be the price (for the eyes); kaṇ – the eye; miḷirum – huge; perumān – sarvēśvaran’s; malaiyō – if one considers tirumalai; tiruvēngadam – it happens to be tiruvēngadam; enṟu – saying so; kaṟkinṟa – learning; vāsagam – spoken word; ivaḷ paramē – is it apt for her in her state?

**Перевод:**

**The bosoms of this girl have not sprouted. Her locks which are dense and beautiful are short. The dress which she wears does not sit well on her waist. Her tongue has not grown out of her child-like gibberish way of speaking. However her eyes are sparkling such that one could say that even the earth, surrounded by ocean, is not equivalent in terms of compensation for them. Her words saying that sarvēśvaran’s divine hill is tiruvēngadam is indeed surprising.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, she \[the nāyaki\] had said “taḷapperu nīṇmuṟuval seyyavāya tadamulai vaḷapperu nādan madusūdan ennum”. Her mother says “What is surprising about her saying such words in her youthful state? īsn’t this how she has been thinking even in her childhood days?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**mulaiyō . . .** – these words are best said after reaching a certain stage \[in the development of a girl’s life\].

**mulaiyō muḻu muṟṟum pōndila** – it is not that her bosoms have not grown properly; even the place from where they sprout has not been identified \[she is such a young girl\].

**moy pūnguḻal kuṟiya** – the locks which are dense and beautiful.

**kuṟiya** – they are so short that if the *nāyakan* wants to arrange the locks into a bun and she, not liking that arrangement, wishes to let the locks down, he will not \[be able to\] fall at her feet \[to beg her to keep them as a bun\].

**kalaiyō araiyillai** – she does not even know as to where to tie her dress.

**nāvō kuḻaṟum** – if the mothers teach her a few words, she would pronounce them indistinctly, like gibberish. They would then teach her a few simple words such as “*iyar* {father), *ācci* (mother)” Her indistinct words sound better than their clear pronunciation.

**kadal . . .** – when parāśara bhattar was explaining this pāsuram, his disciple, nanjīyar told him “he (āḻvār) says that all the other limbs have not developed well. But he has spoken about the greatness of eyes alone. This appears contradictory” to which bhattar responded saying “It is not that way. Even here, he has stated only the condition of under-development”. “How is that?” “At a small age, the eyes will see objects fully; at a youthful stage \[adolescent age\], the eyes will see through glance only. Hence, here too āḻvār is speaking of very young age only” The splendour in her eyes is such that even if the entire earth, surrounded by ocean, is given as a compensation, it will not be a match.

**miḷirum** – even if the waves keep pushing against the shore, they cannot cross it. This is similar to that. When united with *nāyakan*, a girl will look at him with tenderness and love. Unlike that, this girl would look at her mother and her beloved, equally.

**ivaḷ paramē** – Is it apt for her age? It is because of her friends, just as it has been mentioned in periyāḻvār tirumoḻi 3-7-2 “*sāyvilāda kuṟundalai*” (even though they are young without any shyness or reverence, my girl is joining with a few girls and appears like one possessed).

**perumāḷ malaiyō tiruvēngadamenṟu** – is it apt for her age to disregard para and vyūha forms of sarvēśvaran and focus on arcā form, and that too saying that tiruvēngadam is the divine hill for his residence? vēdas set out to measure one quality, the joy of sarvēśvaran, and returned empty handed. But the eminence of arcāvatāram is not like that.

**kaṟkinṟa vāsagam** – the words spoken by her; alternatively, the words taught by her mothers so that they could hear them from her mouth. tiruvāimoḻi 6-5-7 says “*ivaḷ vāyanagaḷ tirunda*” (to appear clearly from her mouth). The verse could be arranged as *ivaḷ paramē perumāḷ malaiyō tiruvēngadamenṟu kaṟkinṟa vāsagamē* in which the meanings would be as given till now; alternatively, the words could be arranged as *perumāḷ malai tiruvēngadamenṟu kaṟkinṟa vāsagamē O ivaḷ paramē* in which case, the sound *O ivaḷ paramē* would indicate astonishment.

*svāpadēsam* (distinguished meaning): Just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāṇḍam 18-28 “*bālyāt prabhruti susnigda*:” (amiable since childhood) and tiruvāimoḻi 2-3-3 ”*aṟiyākkālattuḷḷē*” (at that age when I did not know about servitude), this pāsuram speaks about how āḻvār’s engagement with matters relating to emperumān has always been his sustaining force.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 61. Vasagam seyvadu

*vāsagam seyvadu nam paramē tollai vānavar tam nāyagan nāyagar ellām toḻumavan jñālam muṟṟum vēyagamāyinum sōrāvagai iraṇḍē adiyāl tāyavan āykkulamāy vandu tōnṟiṟṟu nam iṟaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tollai vānavar tam – for nityasūris who have been present from time immemorial; nāyagan – as the lord; nāyagar ellām – all entities such as brahmā et al; toḻumavan – being vorśipped; jñālam muṟṟum – entire world; vēy agam āyinum – sufficient space for planting bamboo pole; sōrā vagai – not leaving \[even that\]; iraṇḍē adiyāl – with only two steps; tāyavan – one who measured; nam iṟai – śriya:patim (consort of śrī mahālakśmi), being our lord; āykkulamāy – in the clan of herdsfolk; vandu tōnṟiṟṟu – that he came and incarnated; vāsagam seyvadu – to narrate it; nam paramē – is it possible for us?

**Перевод:**

**Is it possible for us to speak about the incarnation, in the clan of herdsfolk, of one who is the lord of nityasūris who have been present from time immemorial, who is vorśipped by brahmā who is the lord of celestial entities, who measured this world with just two steps without leaving space even for a bamboo pole to be planted and who is our lord?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, the nāyaki’s mother, after hearing her saying “perumān malaiyō tiruvēngadam enṟu kaṟkinṟa vāsagamē” elucidated it. On hearing this explanation, āḻvār returned to his original form \[from his feminine form\]. Once he realises his true nature, since he is always engaged with kriṣṇāvatāram, he is once again amazed \[at the simplicity of that avatāram\] . Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vāsagam seyvadu nam paramē** – apart from being unconscious, as mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-1 “*ettiṟam*” (how amazing!), is it possible for us to praise sarvēśvaran? Why can’t we praise him through pāsurams? . . .

**tollai vānavar** – just as it has been mentioned in accidram 42 “*pratamajāyēpurāṇā*:” (they have been here from the beginning) he is the controller of nityasūris.

**āykkulamāy vandu tōnṟiṟṟu vāsagam seyvadu nam paramē** –  without any distinction between vēdams (sacred texts) and those who follow the vēdams, none can speak about him. vēdams, which desired to speak about his quality of happiness, returned empty handed, as mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat 6 “*yatō vācō nivartantē*” (mind and words came back without being able to speak). puruśa sūktam says “*tasya dīrā*: *parijānanti yōnim*” (only those who are well versed would know about his incarnations)

– *tasya yōnim*: sarvēśvaran is without any karma (result of sin or virtue from past deeds). Yet, due to his quality of simplicity, he takes birth; the most prominent among wise will not know about it despite engaging deeply with it; they will keep coming back repeatedly, trying to immerse in it. sarvēśvaran himself says, as in śrī bhagavat gītā 4-9 “*janmakarmaca mē divyam*” (my birth and karma are divine) and in śrī bhagavat gītā 7-24 “*parambhāvam*” (superior state). For āḻvār, to remain unconsicious, being amazed at his simplicity, is a naturally occurring phenomenon.

**tollai vānavar tam nāyagan** – he is superior to nityasūris

**nāyagar ellām toḻumavan** – even celestial entities such as brahmā et al, who take pride in saying “We control the fourteen worlds” will remove that pride from their minds and worship him. They, who are termed as being without a birth, will worship sarvēśvaran who takes birth in this world. They, who are not visible to human beings, will come after the killing of demon rāvaṇa and worship him.

**jñālam . . .** – he took over the entire area of earth, without leaving space for even planting a bamboo shoot, with his two steps. Just as duryōdanan had said “I will not give pāndavas, the space even for planting a bamboo shoot”, sarvēśvaran, for the sake of his follower indra, said that he will not give mahābali even the space for planting a bamboo shoot.

**iraṇḍē adiyāl tāyavan** – he took three steps of land as alms from mahābali and measured the entire space with two steps, keeping the third step on mahābali’s head to imprison him.

**āykkulamāy vandu tōnṟiṟṟu** – he had taken hold of the entire earth under his divine foot and came down to earth as the child of one of the entities caught under his divine foot. Is it possible to praise this simplicity through a pāsuram?

**āykkulamāy** – just as bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat 5-5 says “*bālyēnatiśtāsēt*” (leaving aside the qualities of silence and not being silent, one becomes a brahmā jñāṇi (knowing about sarvēśvaran)), if a samsāri gains even a little bit of wisdom, he will not manifest his expertise. When such is the case, what is surprising when sarvēśvaran, who is omniscient, hides all his supreme nature etc and does not reveal about himself? śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍa, 1-7 says “*jajñē viṣṇu: sanātana*:” (viṣṇu, the eternal entity, was indeed born) – when those who attained him and those who have been there from time immemorial do not take birth here, is it possible to praise him when he is born here?

**vandu tōnṟiṟṟu** – didn’t śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-3-2 say “*āvirbhūtam*” (he appeared)? \[bhagavān’s incarnations are unlike ātmās assuming bodies, by their karma\]

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 62. Iraiyo irakkinum

*iṟaiyō irakkinum īngōr peṇpāl enavum irangādu aṟaiyō ena ninṟu adirum karungadal īngivaḷ tan niṟaiyō ini un tiruvaruḷāl anṟik kāpparidāl muṟaiyō aravaṇaimēl paḷḷi koṇḍa mugil vaṇṇanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

karum kadal – ocean which has a dark complexion; irakkinum – even if beseeced; ōr – without any support; peṇpāl enavum – even if the person happens to be a lady; īngu – in her matters; iṟai – even a little bit; irangādu – is not taking pity; aṟaiyō ena – like those who make a huge noise; ninṟu – remaining firmly; adirum – it is making a noise; O – Oh, how cruel!; aravaṇai mēl – on top of the mattress, ādiśēśan; paḷḷikoṇḍa – one who has reclined; mugil vaṇṇanē – Oh lord, who has the complexion of cloud!; īngu – at this place; ivaḷ than – her; niṟaiyō – modesty; ini – henceforth; un tiruvaruḷāl anṟi – other than by your mercy (with any other entity); kāppu aridu – difficult to protect; muṟaiyō – (is this) apt?

**Перевод:**

**The dark ocean, despite being beseeced by her, is not taking any pity on her, even though she is without any support. The ocean is like those who are making a war-cry, being very firm. How cruel is this! Oh lord, who is having the complexion of cloud and who is reclining on the mattress, ādiśēśan! Her situation is such that she cannot be protected by anything other than your mercy, henceforth. Is her condition proper?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār was reminiscing about kriṣṇavatāram. He felt saddened that he had not been born in this world, during that time. Due to the pain of this separation, he returned to his earlier feminine mood (as nāyaki). Her mother says that the sounds of the ocean aggravated her distress of separation. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**irakkinum īngōr peṇpāl enavum iṟaiyō irangādu** – this ocean is not taking even a little bit of pity on her despite her falling at its feet, saying “taking refuge is indeed a great act which manifests her state”, despite hinting “she is without any help and has no other refuge”. This is similar to revealing our weakness to those who harm us, thus giving them an opportunity to harm us even more. vyākyātā cites śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam ślōkam 21-8 “*adya mē maraṇam vāta taraṇam sāgarasya vā*” (śri rāma says that today either he should cross the ocean or die). Even after śrī rāma performed śaraṇāgati (surrendered) to the king of ocean, he did not come. However, the moment he strung the arrow on his bow, he came helter-skelter. Thus, the ocean will not take pity for śaraṇāgati, but will, for the arrow. It will not consider śaraṇāgati but will take pity when faced with death.

**aṟaiyō ena ninṟu adirum kadal** – the ocean behaved like sugrīva who sounded the war-cry on vāli saying “Will you come out of your hiding to fight with me?”

**karum kadal** – the ocean appears as if its anger inside is visible on its surface. Just as the saying goes “*sivappum karuppum sinam*” (red and black colours are manifestation of anger)

**īngu . . .** – the modesty of this girl who is in such a distressed state.

**un tiruvaruḷ anṟik kāpparidāl** – she has lost her senses due to her deep love for you; without your mercy, which is the manifestation of your true nature, she will not revive. She has crossed the stage when she could have corrected herself, due to fear of being ridiculed by the people of the town.

**muṟaiyō** – śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 39-30 says “*tat tasya sadruśam bhavēt*” ((sītāppirātti tells hanuman) if śrī rāma comes here and takes me with him after destroying lankā, it will be appropriate for his valour). Instead of waiting like that for him, here, the *nāyaki* is calling out to him to protect her; this is her situation now.

**aravaṇai . . .** – her beauty and the arrangement of the mattress are such that even if she had dressed herself in saffron robe (indicating renunciation) she should have been accepted.

**aravaṇai mēl** – while she is lying on the hard floor, you are looking out for a soft mattress.

**mugil vaṇṇanē** – when a person was reading out this line, embār (disciple and successor of ramānujar) \[sarcastically\] told him “even after she has called out so much, if he is not going to turn in her direction, he should be a great sage. Hence, instead of saying *mugil vaṇṇan*, call him as *mugil vaṇṇar* (the end syllable *ar* in *vaṇṇar* is an honorific term, indicating respect while the end syllable *an* in *vaṇṇan* indicates someone who is of same status or lower status). Even if one gets hit with stick, the person who is hitting should be addressed respectfully” embār is one who takes delight in the togetherness of perumāḷ and pirātti; here, he takes the side of pirātti when they are separated \[and shows his anger on perumāḷ who is delaying in uniting with pirātti\].

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): This pāsuram illustrates how the distress in separation makes one to destroy one’s *svarūpam* (true nature) to attain the end benefit, instead of waiting for the appropriate time, in line with one’s *svarūpam*.

## 63. Vannam sivandula

*vaṇṇam sivanduḷa vānādamarum kuḷir viḻiya taṇmen kamalattadam pōl polindana tām ivaiyō kaṇṇan tirumāḷ tirumugam tannōdum kādal seydēṟku eṇṇam pugundu adiyēnōdu ikkālam irukkinṟavē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vaṇṇam – due to colour; sivanduḷa – becoming red; vān nādu – nityasūris who are in paramākāśam (śrīvaikuṇṭam); amarum – to sustain; kuḷir – refreshing; viḻiya – having glance; taṇ – being cool; mel – being soft; kamalam – lotus flowers which have blossomed; tadam pōla – like a pond; polindana – being splendorous; ivaidam – these divine eyes; tirumāḷ – being śrīya:pati (consort of śrī mahālakśmi); kaṇṇan – puruśōttaman (best among all cētanas), who is obedient to his followers; tirumugam tannōdu – with the divine face; kādal seydēṟku – one who is desirous, my; eṇṇam – with that intention; pugundu – entered \[on their own\]; adiyēnodu – with me, the servitor; ikkālam – during the present time; irukkinṟavē – are present

**Перевод:**

**The eyes \[of sarvēśvaran\] are reddish in colour, are the sustaining force for nityasūris who are in śrīvaikuṇṭam due to their refreshingly cool glance and are splendorous like cool, soft lotus flowers which have blossomed in a pond. These eyes entered my heart on their own, when I was with the intention of desiring the divine face of śriya:pati, the obedient kaṇṇan, and are present without leaving me.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) She had asked him whether it was correct (muṟaiyō?) in the previous pāsuram. The nāyakan came immediately and looked at her with comforting eyes. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vaṇṇam sivanduḷa** – the divine eyes of sarvēśvaran are reddish as a natural corollary of not having seen āḻvār. With this term *vaṇṇam sivanduḷa*, it is opined that these were like this even earlier. The distress of separation is similar to what śrī rāma had said in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 66-10 “*najīvēyam kśaṇamapi*” (I cannot live even for a moment (without sītāppirātti)). The implication is that the reddiśness in the eyes which happened due to not seeing āḻvār, has now gone.

**vānādu amarum kuḷir viḻiya** – those in *tripādvibhūti* (śrīvaikuṇṭam which is three times bigger than *leelāvibhūti*, the materialistic realm) sustain themselves through these divine eyes. The divine eyes are cool, so that they \[nityasūris\] could sustain themselves. Sustenance for them is through eating, dressing and living, because of the divine eyes. They are *dārakam, pōśakam* and *bhōjñam* (water as sustaining material, food as nourishment and betel leaves as enjoyment) for nityasūris.

**taṇ . . .** – the divine eyes appear like a pond whose surface is full of just blossomed lotus flowers which are cool and soft.

**tāmivaiyō** – just as it has been mentioned in cāndōjña upaniśat 1-6-7 “*tasya yatā kapyāsam puṇḍarīkam ēvam akśiṇī*”, we have heard through those who recite vēdams that sarvēśvaran’s divine eyes are like just blossomed lotus flowers. Would they be like these?

**kaṇṇan tirumāḷ** – should be rearranged as *tirumāḷ kaṇṇan* – śriya:pati, the consort of śrī mahālakśmi, who is like an obedient attendant of his followers.

**tirumugam tannōdum kādal seydēṟku** – for me who had desired that such *nāyakan* should come with his beautiful, divine face and glance at me with his cool divine eyes.

**eṇṇam pugundu** – entering, as I had intended. Alternatively, considering my desire as his own.

**adiyēnodu** – āḻvār says as in jitantē ślōkam 1 “*jitam*” (success to you). The true nature of the word *aham* (I) appears to be *adiyēn* (your servitor). The term *adiyēn* has two meanings: āḻvār says that he has lost out to the beauty of the divine eyes and has become a servant. *adiyēn* also refers to the natural servitude (of jīvātmā). What is the *pramāṇam* (authentic source) for saying that servitude is the natural quality of jīvātma? taittirīya upaniśat nārāyaṇavalli 5-1 says “*ōmityātmānam yunjīta*” (one should meditate on ātmā, by reciting the praṇava mantram, *Om*) as well as in the vēdic saying *ōnkārō bhagavān viṣṇu* – viṣṇu is identified by the mantram *Om*.

**ikkālam** – I do not know if the reason for the benefit that I had attained \[of his divine eyes entering me\] had happened earlier.

**irukkinṟavē** – just as it has been said in cādōjña upaniśat 8-15 “*na ca punarāvartatē* ” (those who reach śrīvaikuṇṭam do not return), it appears as if mōkśam has been attained. It appeared that he would not separate again from her. It appeared that he had come right in front of her.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 64. Irukkar moliyal

*irukkār moḻiyāl neṟi iḻukkāmai ulagaḷanda tiruttāḷiṇai nilattēvar vaṇanguvar yāmum avā orukkā vinaiyodum emmodum nondu kaniyinmaiyil karukkāy kadippavar pōl tirunāmaccol kaṟṟanamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

irukku – being mentioned in rig vēdam (one of the four parts of sacred texts); Ar – completely (explaining the true nature of supreme entity); moḻiyāl – with puruśa sūktam; neṟi – the supreme path of vorśipping supreme entity; iḻukkāmai – without mistake; nilattēvar – śrīvaiṣṇavas, who are referred to as the dēvatās (celestial entities) of earth; ulagu – world; aḷanda – one which measured; tiruttāḷiṇai – divine feet; vaṇanguvar – will worship \[and attain\]; yāmum – we too; avā – desire; orukkā – not allowing to reach; vinaiyodum – with sins; emmodum – and with us; nondu – grieving; kani inmaiyil – due to absence of fruit; karukkāy – unripened fruit; kadippavar pōl – those who eat; tirunāmaccol – divine names without following the meanings; kaṟṟanamē – learnt

**Перевод:**

**brāhmaṇas who are referred to as celestial entities on earth, keep vorśipping the two divine feet \[of  sarvēśvaran\] which measured all the worlds, through puruśa sūktam seen in rig vēdam, which explains the true nature of the supreme entity, without transgressing from the path of righteousness. We grieve at our sins and our nature which are a hurdle to realise our desire of vorśipping in a similar way. Consequently, we learnt only to recite the divine names of sarvēśvaran, just as those who eat only unripened fruit since ripened fruit is not available.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the earlier pāsuram āḻvār had said that sarvēśvaran had glanced at him with his divine eyes and showered his mercy on him. He analysed this saying “Why did he glance at me with his divine eyes instead of at his favourable ones?” He concludes saying “I had recited his divine names superficially and not even wholeheartedly. It could be because of this” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**irukkār moḻiyāl** – Due to the distinguished knowledge of those who had carried out *prapatti* (śaraṇāgati, or act of surrendering) to śarvēśvaran, through the lines mentioned in rig vēdam, the lands where crops grow to a meagre extent as well as arid lands became fully grown lands. The vyākyātā says that there is no need to feel bewildered that in vēdams, terms such as śambhu et al denote rudra, brahmā et al as eminent entities. This is because the distinguished divine word of nārāyaṇan as mentioned in places describing sarvēśvaran’s *svarūpam, rūpam, guṇam, vibhūti* (true nature, divine form, auspicious qualities and wealth respectively) is superior to everything else. In other words, he says that in nārāyaṇa anuvākam, only sarvēśvaran is spoken of. In yajur āraṇyakam, the verse *sarvē vēdā yatraikambhavati* refers to the fact that only nārāyaṇan is being recited in everything \[including the defining names of all entities\]. Since all the terms which refer to eminent entities in this nārāyaṇa anuvākam speak only about nārāyaṇan, it should be considered that all the terms refer only to him.

**neṟi iḻukkāmai** – not deviating from *bhagavat ārādanam* (vorśipping of sarvēśvaran), which is the path of righteousness,

**ulagaḷanda tiruttāliṇai** – his nature is such that he would say “Whether these entities (both sentient and insentient) do or do not know, these are all my property; I will not let go of them”. When all the entities were not even aware of, did he not redeem them all, by measuring everyone and everything?

**nilattēvar vaṇanguvar** – though the brāhmaṇās are living in this *samsāram*  (materialistic world), just as they have been referred to in tiruviruttam 79 *viṇṇuḷārilum sīriyar* (they are more eminent than nityasūris), they are considered as dēvatās \[celestial entities\] on earth.

**yāmum** – I, who am not having knowledge on śāstrams just like they have

**avā orukkāvinaiyodum emmodum** – my sins prevent me from engaging with matters related to sarvēśvaran with the same desire which I have towards worldly matters. I am grieving about those sins and about me, who is having those sins. Grieving because of the fact that these are there and I am there, carrying on with these sins.

**kani inmaiyil karukkāy kadippavar pōl** – even though I do not have the qualification which they have in vorśipping, without deviating from the path of righteousness, I recited only the divine names, just as one who does not have access to fruits, eats only the unripened ones.

**tirunāmaccol kaṟṟanamē** – even when I recited those divine names, I did so without knowing their meanings. I had not even known their greatness. However, sarvēśvaran showered his mercy upon me, without any reason.

Alternative explanation:

**avā . . .** – there is a sin which enables the desire which is there for worldly matters to be diverted towards matters related to sarvēśvaran. It is referred to as *bhakti* \[devotion\]. Could *bhakti* be termed as sin? Since it is the cause for suffering when one does not attain bhagavān, it can be termed as a sin.

**kani inmaiyil karukkāy kadippavar pōl** – just as those who eat the unripened fruit when ripened ones are not available, āḻvār had to make do with reciting divine names. He wanted to see sarvēśvaran with his eyes and embrace him with his hands.  In such a situation, what could he do with only reciting of divine names?  There is another interpretation for the word *kani inmai*. Just as people would eat an unripened fruit before it becomes ripened, before nityasūris could enjoy him \[sarvēśvaran\], āḻvār destroyed him by only speaking about him.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 65. Karruppinai

*kaṟṟuppiṇai malarkkaṇṇin kulam venṟu ōrōkarumam uṟṟuppayinṟu seviyodusāvi ulagamellām muṟṟum viḻungi umiḻnda pirānār tiruvadikkīḻ uṟṟumuṟādum miḷirnda kaṇṇāy emmai uṇginṟavē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kanṟu – being youthful; piṇai – doe’s \[female deer’s\]; malarnda – blossomed; kaṇṇin – eyes’; kulam – gathering; venṟu – being victorious; ōrō karumam – one task; uṟṟu – determining; payinṟu – attempting (to do that); seviyodu – with ears; usāvi – discussing (that task); ulagamellām – all the worlds; muṟṟum – completely; viḻungi – swallowing (during the time of deluge); umiḻndu – spitting out (during the time of creation); pirānār – benefactor, sarvēśvaran’s; tiruvadikkīḻ – under his divine feet; uṟṟum – fitting (with me); uṟādum – not fitting; miḷirnda – looking radiantly; kaṇṇāy – having the name ‘eyes’; emmai – us; uṇginṟa – (two entities) are harming me

**Перевод:**

**The eyes of the nāyaki, emerging victorious over the eyes of a rangale of deer \[a herd of deer\], determining to carry out a job and attempting to carry it out, engaged in a discussion with her ears. Despite being present under the divine feet of sarvēśvaran who benefitted the worlds by swallowing them (during the time of deluge) and by spitting them out (during the time of creation), the two entities of the nāyaki, called as eyes, are engaging and disengaging with me, harming me in the bargain.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) The nāyakan, caught in the glance of nāyaki, narrates how her eyes harmed him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kaṟṟuppiṇaimalar** – deer which is young; the eyes of such a young deer.

**malarkkaṇ** – eyes which are like flowers, viz. well opened eyes.

**kulam venṟu** – being victorious over the eyes of all young deer.

**ōrō karumam . . .** – thinking of a specific task only, and discussing with ears on that specific task. Instead of engaging with several tasks at the same time, without attachment with any of those, thinking intently about one task alone; just as it has been mentioned in [śrī bhagavat gītā 10-9](http://githa.koyil.org/index.php/10-9/) “*bōdayanta: parasparam*” (discussing with each other), glancing at only the *nāyakan*. When he looks at her, she will change her glance and appear to look elsewhere, just as it has been mentioned in tirukkuṟaḷ 1094 “*yānōkkungālai nilan nōkkum nōkkākāl tān nōkki mella nagum*” (when I look at her, she will avoid my look and look at the ground below; when I am not looking at her, she will look at me and smile gently).

**ulagamellām . . .** – despite being under the divine feet of the protector of all the worlds. The eyes will cause harm in spite of being under his boundary.

**uṟṟum uṟādum miḷirnda** – the eyes will look at him, questioningly. If he looks at her with ecstasy, they \[the eyes\] will change their glance to some other direction and shine lustrously.

**kaṇṇay emmai uṇginṟavē** – A harmful object, allegedly called as eyes, will harm me.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): This is the situation of śrivaiṣṇavas who are looking at āḻvār’s situation. The verse *kaṟṟuppiṇaimalarkkaṇṇin kulam venṟu* refers to innocence regarding what is to be done. *ōrō karumam uṟṟup payinṟu* refers to *prapatti* (śaraṇāgati, the act of surrendering to sarvēśvaran). Is this not related to knowledge? How is there a connection with the previously stated innocence? Even though *prapatti* is manifestation of knowledge, there is a lack of focus in it, due to innocence. The act of *prapatti* arises out of one’s true nature as a jīvātmā and is not to be considered as an effort taken by the jīvātmā to attain the means.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 66. Unnadu urangadu

*uṇṇādu uṟangādu uṇarvuṟum ettanai yōgiyarkkum eṇṇāy miḷirum iyalvinavām eri nīr vaḷi vān maṇṇāgiya emperumān tanadu vaigundamannāḷ kāṇṇāy aruvinaiyēn uyirāyina kāvigaḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

eri – for fire; nīr – for water; vaḷi – for air; vān – for sky; maṇ – for earth; āgiya – as indvelling soul; emperumān tanadu – for emperumān who is my benefactor, his wealth; vaigundam annāḷ – she, who is like śrīvaikuṇṭam (having enjoyability); kaṇṇāy – being called as eyes; aru – cruel; vinaiyēn – I, having sins; uyirāyina – life-giving airs; kāvigaḷ – red water lily; uṇṇadu – foregoing food; uṟangādu – foregoing sleep; uṇarvu – knowledge (about true nature of ātmā); uṟum – those who have attained; ettanai yōgiyarkkum – for even the distinguished people who carry out meditation; eṇṇāy – to think about; miḷirum – being radiant; iyalvinavām – they have that nature

**Перевод:**

**She is like śrīvaikuṇṭam, which is the abode of sarvēśvaran who remains as the indvelling soul of the five elements, fire, water, air, sky and earth.Her two eyes are like red water lily and are the life sustaining airs for me.  Her eyes, which are radiant, are such that even great yōgis, who keep constantly meditating on sarvēśvaran, foregoing their food and sleep, would keep thinking of.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) This pāsuram also reveals the words spoken by the nāyakan who is engaged with the beauty of the nāyaki’s eyes. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**uṇṇadu . . .** – unable to sustain themselves through the pleasures available in this world, with the intellect that this world and its materials are to be renounced, being firm in their minds that only the ātmā which is the goal to be attained is apt, considering that even that entity ātmā is not independent but a conduit for obtaining knowledge about sarvēśvaran – even for such yōgis

**eṇṇāy miḷirum iyalvinavām** – if such people, as described above, were to become the target of these eyes, even they will discard the supreme entity who they can attain as their nature, and consider these eyes as eminent.

**eri . . .** – the causal factors of the five elements are representative of the effect factors too \[whatever is enjoyed through these five entities\]. sarvēśvaran has all these as his wealth.

**emperumān** – sarvēśvaran’s vibhūtis (spiritual and materialistic realms) are the benefits for one. Apart from his auspicious qualities, his vibhūtis are also apt for him.

**tanadu vaigundam annāḷ** – unlike the materialistic realm which is created and destroyed repeatedly, she is like his nityavibhūti (śrivaikuṇṭam) which is never destroyed and which is his natural abode as well as confidential to him.

**kaṇṇay . . .** – when such a lady came near me, her eyes, which are the most important limb in her form, which are like the entity ātmā which does not change or does not get destroyed, and which, unlike the ātmā, remain independent and cause harm, indeed harmed me.

**aruvinaiyēn** – I have carried out such sinful activities that the entity which should protect me, is harming me.

**uyirāyina kāvigaḷē** – when they are with her, they remain as her eyes; when she comes near me, they are like my life.

**uyirāyina kāvigaḷē** – it appeared as if life took a form to harm me.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): Thus, for the śrīvaiṣṇavas who are enjoying the *śrīvaiṣṇavaśrī*  (wealth of service to sarvēśvaran) of āḻvār, this gives their position of *dēvum maṟṟaṟiyēn* (we do not know of any other deity). They cherish him because of connection with sarvēśvaran. This states how his *śrīvaiṣṇavaśrī* is so complete that they do not cherish sarvēśvaran but cherish him. Those who are engaging with *caramāvati* (ultimate position) will not engage with *pratamāvati* (primary position) \[in other words, ācāryan takes precedence over sarvēśvaran, which is the underlying principle of śrīvaiṣṇavam\]

## 67. Kaviyum neelamum

*kāviyum neelamum vēlum kayalum palapalavenṟu āviyin tanmai aḷavalla pārippu asuraicceṟṟa māviyam puḷ valla mādavan gōvindan vēngadam sēr tūviyam pēdai annāḷ kaṇgaḷāya tuṇai malarē*

**Пословный перевод:**

asurai – demons; seṟṟa – one who annihilated; mā – having greatness; viyam – having amazing activities; puḷ – garuda; valla – capable of conducting (as his vehicle); mādavan – śriya:pati (consort of śrī mahālakśmi); gōvindan – sarvēśvaran, who tends to cows, his; vēngadam – in his divine abode of tiruvēngadam; sēr – living permanently; am – beautiful; tūvi – having wings; pēdai annāḷ – nāyaki who is like a female swan; kaṇgaḷāya – called as eyes; tuṇai – together; malar – like flowers (these); pārippu – spread; kāviyum – red lily; neelamum – blue lily; vēlum – spear; kayalum – fish; palapala – and various other entities; venṟu – emerging victorious; āviyin – ātmā’s; tanmai – svabhāvam (true nature); aḷavalla – cannot be contained (it will go beyond)

**Перевод:**

**This nāyaki is like a female swan which is permanently residing in tiruvēngadam. This is the abode of sarvēśvaran, who conducts garuda who annihilates demons. He has great eminence and has amazing activities, who has śrī mahālakśmi constantly on his chest and who tends to cows. Such nāyaki’s eyes are like flowers which match each other well. Their expansive nature wins over many entities such as red lily, blue lily, spear, fish etc and are beyond the nature of ātmā.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In this pāsuram too, the nāyakan keeps talking about the beauty of her eyes. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kāviyum . . .** – If one were to look for an entity which matches her eyes, one has to refer to śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāṇḍam 1-18 “*viṣṇunā sadruśā: vīryē sōmavat priya darśana: kāla agni sadruśā: krōda kśamayā pritvī samā:*” (matching śrī mahāviṣṇu in valour, candiran (candra – moon) in attracting others, agni in anger, bhūmi (earth) in patience); as mentioned in this ślōkam, in each attribute, one has to compare with several entities and even then, one will have a doubt whether these would be an exact match for that attribute. In this ślōkam, in the verse *viṣṇunā sadrusō vīryē* he compared paratvam (supreme) only to the quality of valour. Here, it is not so. By its \[eye’s\] reddish colour it was superior to red lily, by its dark colour it was superior to blue lily, by its sharpness it was superior to a spear and by its innocence it was superior to fish; thus it was superior to several entities.

**āviyin tanmai aḷavalla pārippu** – is destroying entities such as flowers etc, which will in any case get destroyed in course of time, a sign of victory? The eyes are capable of destroying even ātmā which is indestructible.

**āviyin tanmai aḷavalla** – not stopping merely with the entity of ātmā, going towards the entity which is the sustaining force for it – in other words, her limbs are capable of bewildering even sarvēśvaran.

**asurai . . .** – one who is capable of conducting garuda who destroys the demons along with their clan, who is  great and is equivalent to emperumān himself, having amazing activities.

**mādavan** – more than the greatness of being the conductor of garuda, sarvēśvaran has the greatness of being the consort of śrī mahālakśmi.

**gōvindan** – due to that union with śrī mahālakśmi, he is simple to attain; that simplicity made him come down to tirumalai and be easily approachable to his followers, making that easy approacability as a sign of supremacy. The *nāyaki* is like *tūviyampēdai*  – the female swan which has beautiful wings, which will live only in that tiruvēngadam, belonging to gōvindan and not in any other place. Alternatively, *tūviyampēdai* could refer to śrī mahālakśmi, and in this case, *nāyaki* is similar to śrī mahālakśmi. What is the necessity to speak about *garudavāhanan*, *lakśminātan et al*? He wanted to talk about sarvēśvaran’s easy approacability, spoke about supremacy which is the basis for the quality of easy approacability and finally he came into the  boundary of simplicity.

**kaṇgaḷāya tuṇai malarē** – being referred to as eyes, they became two flowers which matched each other.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): āḻvār is enjoying the pāsuram of śrivaiṣṇavās who say that as per āḻvār there is no limit to those who enjoy the simplicity of one who stands in tirumalai \[sarvēśvaran\].

## 68. Malarnde olindila

*malarndē oḻindila mālaiyum mālaippon vāsigaiyum pulandōy taḻaippandal taṇḍuṟa nāṟṟip poru kadal sūḻ nilandāviya emperumān tanadu vaigundam annāy kalandār varavedirkoṇḍu van konṟaigaḷ kārttanavē*

**Пословный перевод:**

poru – agitating; kadal – ocean; sūḻ – surrounded by; nilam – bhūmi (earth); tāviya – one who measured; emperumān tanadu – sarvēśvaran’s; vaigundam annāy – one who is (enjoyable) like śrīvaikuṇṭam!; van – being cruel; konṟaigaḷ – konṟai trees; kalandār – sarvēśvaran who united (with you); varavu – arrival; edir koṇḍu – expecting; kārttana – are forming buds; mālaiyum – as garlands (string); mālai – made as garland; pon vāsigaiyum – golden strings; pulam – on the earth; tōy – falling; taḻai – well grown; pandal – as a bower; taṇḍu – in the branches; uṟa – fitting; nāṟṟi – making it to hang; malarndēyoḻindila – have not blossomed fully

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who gives joy like śrīvaikuṇṭam which belongs to sarvēśvaran, who had measured this earth surrounded by agitating oceans! The cruel konṟai trees are waiting with buds, for the arrival of sarvēśvaran who had united with you. They are not seen with blossomed flowers on their branches which come down like strings of garlands or garlands with golden strings, to the ground from the bower on which they have spread.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) The nāyakan, who had united and later separated from the nāyaki, told her before leaving “I will come when the konṟai flowers blossom (konṟai is a tree belonging to the legume family, with elongated clusters of yellow flowers)” The konṟai flowers started blossoming with the arrival of that season. Since the nāyakan had not returned, the nāyaki felt distressed. Her friend sees the flowers, knows that the season has arrived, but still tells her “They are only in the process of blossoming but have not blossomed yet. He will certainly return at the correct time; do not worry” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**malarndē oḻindila** – the friend tells *nāyaki* “This is not the season when he said he would return” The *nāyaki* tells her “How do you say so even after seeing that the flowers have blossomed?” The friend says “You could only see them trying to blossom; they have not blossomed fully”

**mālai . . .** – only if they come down in strings as garlands or as golden strings through those garlands, could one say that they have blossomed. They should come down to the earth in such a way that those who see them would have their sensory organs stolen by them.

**poru kadal** – \[friend’s words\] aren’t you matching his indestructible *nityavibhūti* (spiritual realm)? Will he allow you to get destroyed? He has one vibhūti (leelā vibhūti) which keeps getting formed and destroyed repeatedly and one vibhūti (nitya vibhūti) which is indestructible.  Measuring this earth which is surrounded by ocean, he is the lord who brought all the worlds under him.

**tanadu vaigundam annāy** – is it possible for you not to have *svarūpa jñānam* (knowledge about your true nature)? āren’t you matching his nityavibhūti? Only if he gives up that nityavibhīūti, would he give you up too. Will he allow you to get distressed?

**kalandār** – even if he does not know your true nature, will he not know his true nature? Would he, who had united with you and knows your importance, let go of you? Know well that his identity is that he is *kalandār* (one who united with you).

**varavedir koṇḍu** – even plants which do not know any distinction between correct and incorrect, were ready to blossom as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam ślōkam “*akālapalinō vrukśā*” (thought it was not the season, trees blossomed flowers). Thus, when these flowers are waiting for the arrival of *nāyakan* to blossom, why are you upset?

**van konṟaigaḷ** – the konṟai trees were anxiously waiting, deciding to blossom only when he comes and not otherwise

**kārttana** – they formed buds. When the embryo is formed, it would change colour. This indicates that. It could also be taken to refer to rainy season.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): this pāsuram states how those who were nearby comforted āḻvār, saying that due to his knowledge of sarvēśvaran’s auspicious qualities and due to the fact that sarvēśvaran had entered his mind, sarvēśvaran will not remain without coming during the time which is apt for enjoyment.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 69. Karerrirul

*kārēṟṟiruḷ segilēṟṟin sudarukku uḷaindu velvān pōrēṟṟu edirndadu puntalai mālai bhuvaniyellām nīrēṟṟaḷanda nediya pirān aruḷāvidumē vāṟēṟṟiḷa mulaiyāy varundēl un vaḷaittiṟamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

iruḷ – darkness; kār – black; ēṟu – bull; segil – reddish; ēṟṟin – bull (refers to sūriyan (sūrya)); sudarukku – effulgence; uḷaindu – losing out; velvān – to win (against that bull); pōr – battle; ēṟṟu – accepting that; pul – negligible; talai – duration; mālai – in the evening time (dusk); edirndadu – fought; bhuvaniyellām – throughout the earth; nīr ēṟṟu – accepting water (given as sign of giving alms, from mahābali); aḷanda – one who seized; nediya – one who had unlimited friendliness; pirān – sarvēśvaran, the benefactor; aruḷāvidumē – will he not shower his mercy?; vār – with bodice; ēṟṟu – being held; iḷa mulaiyāy – Oh one who has youthful bosoms!; un vaḷaittiṟam – regarding your bangles; varundēl – do not grieve

**Перевод:**

**The bull of darkness had lost out to the bull of reddiśly splendrous sūriyan. However, in order to win against that bull, it came during dusk, which lasts only briefly. Will sarvēśvaran, who had accepted water, as a sign of receing alms from mahābali, and measured all the worlds, not show his mercy on you at this time of dusk? Oh one has held her bosoms in a bodice! You need not grieve regarding your bangles.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Next, dusk came and started tormenting her. Since the time after dusk is the happy time of night, and since the nāyakan had not come there to be with her, the nāyaki feels distressed. Her friend tells her “This is  not dusk. Two bulls are raring to fight with each other”, making her feel bewildered about time and trying to console her. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kārēṟṟiruḷ** – darkness looks like a black bull.

**segilēṟṟin sudarukku uḷaindu** – surrendered to the rays of reddish bull, called as sūriyan, after losing to it. That dark bull told itself “Should we lose like this such that we would perish? We should also win once” and waged a fight with the reddish bull, donning the garland (or evening) \[In tamiḻ language, both garland and evening are referred to by the same word, *mālai*\].

**pun talai mālai** – *sandyā* (dusk) time which lasts only briefly; dusk which has minute buds of embers of darkness. Is it possible to ascribe buds to evening? Just as it has been mentioned in the saying *nakanibhasunjatiyāntamōvallaya*: where darkness is compared to a creeper, it is acceptable to say that darkness has buds.

**bhuvani . . .** – śouldn’t one, who knows sarvēśvaran’s qualities, remain patiently? Is he not one who kept his divine foot, which are like the flowers for nityasūris, on everyone’s head, without any reason, treating indra as the cause for doing that way? Will he, who is the benefactor for all, leave you behind? Would one, who comes and helps entities who are engulfed by *tamas* (ignorance) and complain to him \[reference is to indra et al\], remain idly when *tamas* (darkness) spreads in the world (during dusk)? Would one, who comes seeking alms to reclaim land which others claim as their own \[mahābali\] leave you, who are his wealth, and that too when he does not have to seek alms? Would one, who does not let go of anything on this earth which is full of forests and highlands, let you to suffer? Would one, who hastened when the earth was in trouble, remain patiently when a lady is in trouble?

**vārēṟṟu iḷa mulaiyāy** – would he, who keeps his divine feet on forests and highlands, not keep them on your bosoms?

**aruḷāvidumē** – would one, who transforms himself to help those who have the quality of *satvam* (goodness) occasionally, not help you who is full of *śudda satvam* (pure goodness)?

**bhuvani . . .** – he is the great benefactor who feels that he has not done anything even after keeping his divine feet on the heads of everyone, when he measured the earth consequent to accepting water as a sign of taking alms.

**aruḷāvidumē** – even if his affection for you is lost, would even his mercy, which is a corollary of his true nature, not be showered on you?

**vārēṟṟu iḷamulaiyāy** – oh one who has bosoms which have to be held by a bodice! Would he allow you to bear your bosoms which are to be borne either by the bodice or by him?

**iḷa mulaiyāy** – though you have not reached adulthood, your bosoms are such that they have to be sustained by bodice. Just as children cry out for food during times of hunger, your bosoms are heaving, crying out for worldly pleasures.

**varundēl un vaḷaittiṟamē** – you do not have to grieve for the bangles slipping out of your hands. If you wish, you could grieve for entities on his hands slipping out. Just as it has been said in tiruppallāṇḍu 2 “*appāncasanniyamum pallāṇḍu*” (long live that conch, pāncajanyam), praise his conch \[our bangles are representative of the conch since they are made of conch\].

In the previous pāsuram, how āḻvār sustained himself through the knowledge of sarvēśvaran’s auspicious qualities and through sarvēśvaran entering his mind, was mentioned. In this pāsuram he says that even that is not required; mere knowledge that he is the protector and we are the entities to be protected is sufficient. *varundēl un vaḷaittiṟamē* – this is similar to the saying in bhagavat gītā 18-66 ślōkam “*mā śuca*:” (do not grieve). Through the words *aham tvā*, the true nature of both (sarvēśvaran and āḻvār) has been explained and only after that has *mā śuca*: been said.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 70. Valai vay

*vaḷaivāyt tiruccakkarattu engaḷ vānavanār mudimēl taḷaivāy narungaṇṇit taṇṇanduḻāykku vaṇṇam payalai viḷaivān miga vandu nāḷ tingaḷ āṇḍu ūḻi niṟka emmai uḷaivān pugundu iduvōr kangul āyiram ūḻigaḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vaḷai – being bent; vāy – having mouth; tiruccakarattu – having the divine disc; engaḷ – being our svāmy; vānavanār – sarvēśvaran, who is the controller of paramapadam, his; mudimēl – donned atop his divine crown; taḷaivāy – having the opportunity; naṟu – having fragrance; taṇ – being cool; am – being beautiful; kaṇṇī – strung as a garland; tuḻāykku – for the divine tuḷasi; vaṇṇam – colour; payalai viḷaivān – to make it become pale (losing colour); idu – this; ōr – one; kangul – night; nāḷ – as a day; tingaḷ – as a month; āṇḍu – as a year; ūḻi niṟka – as a kalpam (life time of brahmā), apart from transforming; emmai – us; uḷaivāṇ – to trouble; migavandu – increasing repeatedly; pugundu – entering; āyiram – many; ūḻigaḷ – increased as kalpams

**Перевод:**

**I desired the cool, beautiful tuḷasi garland which is fragrant and has the nice opportunity of decorating the divine crown of our lord sarvēśvaran, who has the divine discus which has curved mouth and who is the controller of paramapadam. As a result of this, in order for the complexion in my form to lose its colour and become pale, this one night appears to extend beyond one day, one month, one year and many kalpams, troubling me.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Due to the craving arising out of the desire of obtaining the garland of tuḷasi which had been worn by śrīvaikuṇanātan \[sarvēśvaran\], this pāsuram narrates the difficulty in spending time. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vaḷaivāy tiruccakarattu** – divine disc which has a bent mouth. Alternatively, one who has *vaḷai* – śrī pāncajanyam (divine conch) and *vāy* – divine disc which has mouth. Reference is made to śrī rāmāyaṇa ślōkam yudda kāṇḍam “*tamasa: paramōdātā Sanka chakra gadādara*:” (one who is beyond the darkness of ignorance, one who is supreme and who adorns the divne weapons of conch, disc, mace etc).

**engaḷ vānavanār mudimēl** – having the opportunity of donning the divine crown of śrīvaikuṇṭanātan who is having divine weapons, divine garlands and who is the lord of *tripādvibhūti* (nityavibhūti, the spiritual realm) which is three times larger than leelā vibhūti (materialistic realm); the garland has been strung by nityasūris such as ādiśēśan, garudan et al.

**naṟungaṇṇit taṇṇanduḻāykku** – the divine garland of divine tuḷasi which is fresh.

**vaṇṇam . . .** – desiring that divine garland, and becoming pale since it has not been obtained; losing colour.

**miga vandu . . .** – the night which came, first became a day; that day then became a month; that month became a year; that year became a kalpam. Thus a single night extended to several kalpams, in order to torment me.

**miga vandu** – each part came, getting worse than the previous. Thus, several nights came this way. How one night turned out to be!

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): this pāsuram explains the benefit that āḻvār desired similar to the benefit which sītāppirātti attained in śrī rāmāyaṇam as mentioned in ayōdyā kāṇḍam 16-21 “*pati sammānitā sītā*” (sītāppirātti who was given a happy message by her husband) and how it became extremely difficult to spend time on not attaining that benefit.

## 71. Uligalay

*ūḻigaḷāy ulagēḻum uṇḍān enṟilam paḻam kaṇḍu āḻi kaḷām paḻavaṇṇam enṟēṟku ahdē koṇḍu annai nāḻivaḷō enṟum jñālam uṇḍān vaṇṇam solliṟu enṟum tōḻigaḷō uraiyīr emmai ammanai sūḻginṟavē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ūḻigaḷāy – being the controller of all the worlds ruled by time; ēḻu ulagum – (during deluge) all the seven worlds; uṇḍān – ate (to keep them in his stomach and protect them); enṟilam – we did not say that; paḻam kaṇḍu – looking at the fruit; kaḷāppaḻa vaṇṇam – the colour of berry fruit; enṟēṟku – to me who said so; ahdē koṇḍu – presuming another meaning for that word; annai – (my) mother; ivaḷ – this girl; nāḻ – is clever; enṟum – saying so; jñālam – earth; uṇḍān – one who swallowed; vaṇṇam – complexion; solliṟṟu – said; enṟum – saying so; tōḻigaḷō – Oh my friends!; emmai – regarding me; ammanai – mother; sūḻginṟa – saying it repeatedly; uraiyīr – please tell

**Перевод:**

**Oh friends! I had not revealed the name of my nāyakan explicitly by saying “He is the lord of all the worlds ruled by time and he protected the seven worlds in his stomach during deluge”. Instead, looking at the colour of berry fruits, I had said “The colour of ocean is the colour of this fruit”. My mother, who listened to my words, said “This girl is very clever! She is referring to the complexion of the one who swallowed all the worlds” Please tell me a remedy since my mother is accusing me repeatedly and hurting me.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) The nāyakan who had clandestinely united with the nāyaki and separated from her, sends a few persons to sell fruits to the place where the nāyaki resides. These fruits resemble the complexion of his divine form and would help her to lessen her distress in separation. Looking at those fruits, the nāyaki sustains herself. Her mother notices this and chides her. The nāyaki tells her friends about this excessive act of her mother’s. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ūḻigaḷāy ēḻulagum uṇḍān enṟilam** – Had I said “During deluge, he swallowed and protected the seven worlds”, I could be accused of talking about him (sarvēśvaran). However, I had not said “He is the controller of all entities such as time etc; he is the great benefactor who swallowed all these entities during deluge”. Those who say that sarvēśvaran does not have auspicious qualities will give a different meaning to the words *satyam, jñānam* etc which are mentioned in taittirīya upaniśat ānandavallee “*satyam jñānam anantam brahma*” (the supreme entity is permanent, omniscient and unlimited). In the same way, the *nāyaki* too feels happy to praise the greatness of his auspicious qualities by saying indirectly about his complexion etc. She has a right to say this. Her mother too has a right to deny what the *nāyaki* had meant. The right for the *nāyaki* comes from the basis that she cannot remain quiet about someone who has carried out a beneficial act for her; one with intellect cannot keep quiet. Her mother, on the other hand, felt that the *nāyaki*’s thoughts would torment her later.  She thought that deep involvement with the qualities of a person would lead to worries later on.

**paḻam kaṇḍu** – when one looks at an entity in this world, it is natural to identify it by its name. Whether it is *ghatam* (pot) or *patam* (cloth), it is called by its name. Is identifying a material by its name, an offence?

**āḻi kaḷām paḻavaṇṇam enṟēṟku** – I merely said that the colour of the berry fruit is similar to the colour of the ocean. However, my mother presumed “She is talking about the one who has the complexion of ocean \[sarvēśvaran\]”

**ahdē koṇḍu** – Instead of accepting what I had said, she thought that there was an inner meaning to it.

**annai** – was she a mother right from the time of her birth? It appears that she had not grown up to the stage of a young girl (before becoming a mother). If there is a fault, it could be chided. But when there is no fault, is it proper to see as to who is controlling and who is being controlled?

**nāḻivaḷō enṟum** – we are seeing the fruits and speaking about them. Does she not understand what I am saying? Why is she taking a different meaning for it? Is she having a skill in talking meaninglessly? *nāḻ* refers to skill.

What is the greatness of the *nāyaki*?

**jñālam uṇḍān vaṇṇam solliṟṟu ennum** – sarvēśvaran had kept all the worlds during the time of deluge, inside his stomach to protect them. Reminiscing that quality, my daughter could not bear separation from that sarvēśvaran.

**tōḻigaḷō uraiyīr** – Oh friends! Don’t you stand with me in my happy moments and sad moments? You are not like my mother.

**emmai ammanai sūḻginṟavē** – just like a snake in the house will torment even when it is not biting, whenever someone comes into the house or leaves the house, my mother is tormenting me.

**sūḻginṟa** – my mother is exaggerating things three times. While I am seeing something and speaking about the same thing, my mother torments me saying that there is some other meaning to what I am saying. Only you have to offer me a remedy for this.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): this pāsuram says that it is not possible to sustain oneself without looking at entities which are equivalent to sarvēśvaran and that it is not possible to redeem someone who is having overflowing affection towards sarvēśvaran.

## 72. Sulginra kangul

*sūḻginṟa kangul surungāviruḷin karundiṇimbaip pōḻginṟa tingaḷ am piḷḷaiyum pōḻga tuḻāy malarkkē tāḻginṟa nenjattoru tamiyāttiyēn māmaikkinṟu vāḻginṟavāru iduvō vandu tōnṟiṟṟu vāliyadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

sūḻginṟa – pervading everywhere; kangul – caused by the night; surungā – not contracted (huge); karu – being black; iruḷ – darkness’; tiṇimbai – strength; pōḻginṟa – splitting; am – being beautiful; piḷḷai – favourable like a son; tingaḷum – candiran too; pōḻga – let him split; vāliyadu – strong; vandu tōnṟiṟṟu – it rose up (in the sky); tuḻāy malarkkē – only for the divine garland of tuḷasi (worn by sarvēśvaran); tāḻginṟa – languishing; nenjattu – having heart; oru tamiyāttiyēn – I, who am lonely, without anyone to assist; my; māmaikku – natural complexion; inṟu – during this time; vāḻginṟa āṟu – the way I live; iduvō – is it like this?

**Перевод:**

**Let candiran, who rose up to split the black coloured darkness which occurred in the night and which pervaded everywhere, split me too. The beautiful and strong candiran, who appeared like a son, seems to have risen up only for splitting me. I am alone, with no support, longing for the divine tuḷasi garland worn by sarvēśvaran, with a languishing heart. Is this the way to live for my complexion?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) When the nāyaki had to sustain herself by looking at objects which were similar to the nāyakan, during the time of separation, the night time entered and started tormenting her. When she was thinking “Who will protect me from the torment of separation?” candiran (candra – moon) appeared. piḷḷai amudanār used to narrate a story here. One ascetic brāhmaṇa was walking through a forest when a cow started following him. He was wondering as to who will protect him from the cow when a tiger happened to come there; it killed the cow and ate it up. It then came in front of the brāhmaṇa, twitching its tail. The brāhmaṇa thought “I could have at the least saved my life from the cow. Now it appears that there is no way that I could survive this”. The nāyaki’s situation is similar to this. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**sūḻginṟa** – just as praḷayam (deluge) pervades everywhere, this \[darkness\] pervaded everywhere, without leaving a place where one can take rest away from it.

**kangul surungā iruḷin** – the darkness of the night, without any contraction. There are limits when it is expected to come with full denseness and when it is expected to come without being dense. This did not follow that principle. It was fully dense.

**karum tiṇimbai** – having a black colour. Through the word *tiṇimbai*, strength is indicated. It is as if the petals outside the flower of darkness have been removed and the strong diamond-like darkness inside is manifested.

**pōḻginṟa . . .** – candiran who comes splitting and tearing apart such darkness. Since I do not have any right over anything outside of myself, let that candiran harm me too.

**pōḻginṟa** – darkness and candiran are natural enemies. Thus, in the presence of candiran, darkness, aside from being torn apart, is being destroyed physically. The strength of darkness and the youthful state of candiran are indicated.

**tingaḷ am piḷḷai** – candiran who is like a beautiful child. Initially he appears as a favourable entity, but later turns out to harm us. He is favourable since he is driving out darkness, but is a harmful entity because he reminds the *nāyaki* of her loneliness. śrī rāmāyaṇam kiśkindā kāṇḍam 1-71 “*padmakōśa palāśāni druśtvā driśtir hi manyatē* l *sītāyā nētra kōśābhyām sadruśām iti lakśmaṇa* ll” (Oh lakśmaṇa! Since these petals of lotus flower are like the eyes of sītā, when I look at them, I am reminded of her eyes and it hurts me) In other words, when one is in distress, differences are not noticed. Would it be proper for the tongue, which is supposed to eat only tasteful food, to eat poison? In other words, without realising the danger which is going to come from candiran, is it proper to call him as being beautiful? śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 43-24 says “*paśya lakśmaṇa …*” (for the sake of this sītā, I will catch that deer; protecting her is our duty), in other words *darśi saumitrī* – look, lakśmaṇa – they saw the situation at that point of time and did not think of what would happen later.

**tuḻāy malarkkē . . .**  – desiring to obtain the divine tuḷasi garland which decorated his divine shoulder, and having a mind deeply engrossed with that.

**tuḻāy malarkkē tāḻginṟa** – not thinking of the situation when it does not occur that way

**oru tamiyāttiyēn** – for her loneliness, even the loneliness of sitāppirātti cannot equal. The *nāyaki* was distressed by both the dark night and candiran. The term *oru* (one) signifies that there is no simile for her condition.

**māmaikkinṟu vāḻginṟavāṟiduvō** – was it for this that we felt happy that candiran has come to remove the distress caused by the dark night?

**vandu tōnṟiṟṟu vāliyadē** – she laments “Just as candiran came to get rid of the darkness of the night, isn’t there a sūriyan (sun) who would get rid of the candiran?”  She says that candiran himself would stay firmly and keep harming.

**vāliyadu** – strong

The verses should be arranged this way to convey the sequential meaning: *tingaḷ am piḷḷaiyum pōḻga* – let that also come and harm; *vandu tōnṟiṟṟu vāliyadē* – over and above that darkness, this candiran too appeared to harm; *māmaikkinṟu vāḻginṟavāṟiduvō* – is this the way for my complexion to survive?

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): Just as it has been said in periya tiruvandādi 24 “*iruḷanna māmēni*” (divine form which resembles darkness), the dark night reminded āḻvār of sarvēśvaran’s divine form and tormented him. On top of that, candiran reminded him of sarvēśvaran’s divine face and divine conch and tormented him even further.
