## 73. Val vennilavu

*vāl veṇṇilavu ulagārac curakkum veṇ tingaḷenṟum pāl viṇsuravi suramudirmālai paridivattam pōlum sudar adalāḻippirān poḻilēḻu aḷikkum sālbin tagaimaikolām tamiyātti taḷarndaduvē*

**Пословный перевод:**

veṇ tingaḷ enṟum – being referred to as the whitish moon; viṇ – moving in the sky; suravi – cow; vāl veṇ – being very whitish; nilavu – moon; pāl – milk; ulagu – world, considered as the (receptor) vessel; Ara – to be full; surakkum – secreting; sura – secretion; mudir – matured; mālai – night time; tamiyātti – this girl, without any support; taḷarndadu – has crumbled; paraidivattam pōlum – looking like the sun’s halo; sudar – having radiance; adal – battle-ready; āḻi – one who has the divine disc; pirān – sarvēśvaran, who is the benefactor; ēḻ poḻil – the seven worlds; aḷikkum – protection; sālbin – the eminence of protection; tagaimai kolām – is it the true nature?

**Перевод:**

**This matured evening (night) is filling up the milk of whitish moon in the container of world. It also has the secretion of the cow which is roaming in the sky as moon. During this time, this girl, who is without anyone to help her, is lying in a crumbled state. Is this due to the nature of sarvēśvaran who is having the divine disc which has the radiance of the halo of the sun, and who has the greatness of protecting all the worlds?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) The moon rose to be a huge entity, harming the nāyaki, who swooned, unconsciously. Her mother, seeing this, said “Look at the disgrace which has befallen righteousness” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vāl veṇṇilavu . . .** – the *nāyaki*’s mother is having doubts on the capability of sarvēśvaran in protecting people, after looking at her daughter’s oppressive state. Whitish moon, which looks like a cow, is in the sky, secreting its white light which looks like milk, into the world, which looks like a container for holding that milk.

**viṇ suravi** – the moon is  in the sky, ensuring that the deluge of the *nāyaki*’s oppression does not come anytere near it.

**paridi vattam . . .** – there is an enemy for the moon in the sky. sarvēśvaran who showers benefits on all, has the divine disc which has the radiance of sun’s halo and which is always battle-ready.

**poḻil ēḻaḷikkum sālbin tagaimai kolām** – is this the way of protecting, by one who is the protector of all the worlds? He is at the eminence of protecting all the worlds. Is this his nature? Those who considered him as the protection are in a good position. Even when deluge comes, those who have deep faith in him will escape. However, is this the way to protect, having his divine hands and divine disc? We offer our salutations to his earlier state of protection \[this is said in derision\].

**tamiyātti taḷarndaduvē** – Will he, the protector be like this if someone within his sphere of protection remains like this \[like the *nāyaki*\]? The mother says that despite her \[mother\] being together \[with the *nāyaki*\], the *nāyaki* considers herself to be alone, without any support. This is because she considers only the sarvēśvaran as the protector and the rest as those who worship other dēvatās (deities). In the last stages of one’s life, when the messengers of yama (the deity for righteousness) surround the person, they will not come for help. They will merely say “Whatever has been destined to happen, has to happen” and cause distress only.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): This pāsuram states the doubt that those, who saw āḻvār suffering from the distress due to separation,  had in the protective ability of sarvēśvaran.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 74. Talarndum murindum

*taḷarndum muṟindum varu tiraippāyil tirunedungaṇ vaḷarndum aṟivuṟṟum vaiyam viḻungiyum mālvaraiyaik kiḷarndum aṟidarak kīṇḍeduttān mudi sūdu tuḻāy aḷainduṇ siṟu pasum tenṟal andō vandu ulāginṟadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

taḷarndum – crumbling down; muṟindum – breaking down; varu – coming; tirai – having waves; pāyil – in the mat of milky ocean \[tiruppāṟkadal\]; tiru – due to beauty; nedum – without limit; kaṇ – divine eyes; vaḷarndum – closing; aṟivu – thinking of protecting the worlds; uṟṟum – engaged in; vaiyam – worlds; viḻungiyum – (during deluge) eating them; māl – huge; varaiyai – gōvardanam; kīṇḍu – (from the earth) uprooting; kiḷarndu – raising; maṟidara – upside down; eduttān – one who lifted, his; mudi – in the divine crown; sūdu – donned; tuḻāy – in divine tuḷasi; aḷaindu – pervading; uṇ – having stayed with it; pasum – without contact with any other entity; siṟu tenṟal – gentle breeze; vandu – reaching; ulāginṟadu – is blowing

**Перевод:**

**sarvēśvaran is lying on the divine mat of tiruppāṟkadal in which the huge waves are rising, crumbling and breaking down repeatedly. sarvēśvaran has closed his beautiful, divine eyes and meditating on protecting the worlds. He keeps all the worlds in his divine stomach during the time of deluge and lifts up the gōvardana mountain from earth, holding it upside down to protect all the herd and herdsfolk. A gentle breeze has pervaded the divine tuḷasi which is decorating his long divine crown and, without contacting any other entity, is blowing here, making me feel happy.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Southerly breeze was blowing then. The nāyaki feels saddened seeing this saying “The southerly breeze has set in; but the nāyakan has not come” Her friend tells her “Do you think that this is merely southerly breeze? When the fragrance of the palace starts spreading, it would indicate the arrival of the prince. In the same way, along with this breeze you could smell the fragrance of divine tuḷasi. This is a hint that the nāyakan is coming. You need not feel sad” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**taḷarndum . . .** – tiruppāṟkadal (milky ocean) has huge waves which rise up, crumbling down on their own weight as well as breaking down due the effect of wind. sarvēśvaran is reclining on that mat, with his beautiful eyes which indicate that he is the sarvēśvaran, closed, thinking of ways to protect the worlds.

**aṟivuṟṟum** – when all entities get troubled by deluge, he awakens thinking “When I am lying here only to protect them, would I allow them to suffer?”

**vaiyam viḻungiyum** – keeping the worlds in his divine stomach, during the time of deluge and protecting them.

**māl varai . . .** – it is not only when the entire world is suffering that he comes and rescues. Even when a small place suffers \[such as gōkulam\] he runs in to help the people. Without any effort, he uprooted the huge mountain of gōvardanam and held it upside down so that all the herd and the herd-folk could stand under it. Such sarvēśvaran’s . . .

**mudi sūdu tuḻāy** – her thoughts are on the divine tuḷasi on his divine feet, just as it has been mentioned ini tiruvāimoḻi 4-2-1 “*tāḷiṇai mēlaṇi taṇṇanduḻāy*” (the divine, cool tuḷasi applied on top of his divine feet); he considers that as *gruhṇāti śirasāsvayam* – he considers that which has been offered to his divine feet, as having been offered to his divine crown. Hence the friend says this way. The breeze pervades the divine tuḷasi which has been laid out on his divine crown, takes the fragrance from there and without entering any other entity, comes to the place where the *nāyaki* is present and blows there. The friend asks the *nāyaki* “Do you have the time for feeling sad?” The pāsuram says “*mudi sūdu tuḻāy*”. The reason for all the explanation thus far has been to indicate that the breeze approaches him \[sarvēśvaran\], pervades the place fully and brings the fragrance to her.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): By this, the pāsuram explains how those who were near āḻvār comforted him \[when he was suffering from separation from sarvēśvaran\] by telling him about the qualities of sarvēśvaran lying in tiruppāṟkadal,  protecting the worlds by keeping them in his divine stomach and carrying out *gōvardanōddāraṇam* (lifting the gōvardana mountain) to protect the people. āḻvār sustains himself by meditating on such qualities.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 75. Ulaginra kendai

*ulāginṟa keṇḍai oḷiyambu emmāviyai ūduruvak kulāginṟa venjilai vāṇmugattīr kuni sangidaṟip pulāginṟa vēlaip puṇari am paḷḷiyammān adiyār nilāginṟa vaigundamō vaiyamō num nilaiyidamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

ulāginṟa – roaming; keṇḍai – innocent, like fish; oḷi – having a radiance; ambu – eyes which are like arrows; em āviyai – my prāṇan (life); ūduruva – crumbling it by piercing; kulāginṟa – bent; vem – cruel; silai – having eye brows which are like bow; vāḷ – having a radiance; mugattīr – oh those who have faces!; num – for you; nilai – dwelling; idam – place; kuni – being bent (to the right side); sangu – conces; idaṟi – pushing (to the shore); pulāginṟa – being a matter for sensory perceptions; vēlai – having waves; puṇari – ocean; am – beautiful; paḷḷi – having as mattress; ammān – lord’s; adiyār – nityasūris, his devotees; nilāginṟa – place of dwelling; vaikundamō – is it śrīvaikuṇṭam?; vaiyamō – or, is it leelāvibhūti (materialistic realm)?

**Перевод:**

**Oh you people who have radiant face having splendorous eyes which are like arrows piercing our lives and which look like fish which keep roaming, and eye-brows which are like cruel and bent bows! Is your place of dwelling śrīvaikuṇṭam which is inhabited by the followers of sarvēśvaran who has as his mattress the divine milky ocean (tiruppāṟkadal) where conces curved to the right side are pushed to the shore by the waves or is it this earth?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) This pāsuram describes the nāyakan celebrating the nāyaki when they are united. Alternatively, it could be taken as a natural doubt raised by the nāyakan \[after looking at her eyes\]. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**ulāginṟa keṇḍai** – the innocence of the eyes looks like roaming fish.

**oḷiyambu** – when the *nāyakan* looks at her, she would not look in his direction; when he is not looking at her, she would look at him. Without his being aware of it, the arrow will come and pierce him.

**oḷiyambu** – he cannot escape from her eyes

**emmāviyai ūduruva** – this will not pierce anyone else. It will pierce us only \[says the *nāyakan*}. Even that, it will not do superficially on the skin, but will pierce the *prāṇan* \[vital air; life itself\]; even that, it is not superficially but deep down.

**kulāginṟa venjilai vāḷ mugattīr** – \[eyebrows are\] constantly bent so that when an arrow has to be shot, one need not bend the bow, string it and then take aim; by merely looking at a person, the arrow will be shot immediately. Also, instead of having the bow in the hand, having the bow on the face itself. Oh, such people! Another interpretation: the term *vāḷ* could be considered as radiance; in this case the meaning will be – Oh one who has a face with a bent bow and radiance! Her bow \[eyebrows\] is tormenting him.

**kunisangu . . .** – pushing ashore conces which are curved to the right.

**pulāginṟa** – making a noise, the ocean which has agitating waves and is a mattress for sarvēśvaran.

**pulāginṟa** – it appears to be the time of dawn since the conces keep emitting radiance constantly. Such sarvēśvaran who has this ocean as his bed, his

**ammān . . .** – instead of remaining constantly at paramapadam \[śrīvaikuṇṭam\] where sarvēśvaran holds the court so that both he and the nityasūris could spend their time together, he enters tiruppāṟkadal which roars because of his presence and stays there similar to those who set up a house in a place of their liking, with the purpose of protecting the words and reclines. This makes nityasūris to be amazed at his simplicity. \[The *nāyakan* asks the *nāyaki*\] Is your residence śrīvaikuṇṭam which is the dwelling place of such nityasūris or is it the leelāvibhūti (materialistic realm) which he visits occasionally and which keeps getting created and destroyed similar to the arrangement of pandal etc made during festival times? Just as it is said in tiruvāimoḻi 3-9-9 “*vānavar nādu*” śrīvaikuṇṭam is the place where the nityasūris would draw the rules and regulations \[since nityasūris are *paratantrars* (totally dependent on him) he allows them to run that realm as per their desire\]. However, leelāvibhūti (materialistic realm) has cētanas (sentient entites) who are *svatantarars* (independent) having ego and possessive qualities. Hence he does not let them rule it as they wish; instead, he rules it by himself. piḷḷai tirunaṟaiyūr araiyar would say *“adiyār nilāginṟa vaigundamō anṟikkē avanadāna vaiyamō?*” (is it śrīvaikuṇṭam ruled by nityasūris or this leelāvibhūti ruled by sarvēśvaran?)

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): those who saw āḻvār said “There is none like āḻvār in both the vibhūtis”. Those who are in nityavibhūti are constantly in engagement with bhagavat viśayam (matters relating to sarvēśvaran). Those who are here (leelāvibhūti) are constantly engaging with worldly matters. āḻvār is distinct from both of them. He is without permanent engagement with bhagavat viśayam \[since he is still here\]. He is not sustaining himself with food, water etc \[like the cētanas who are here\]. Thus this explains how he is outside the nature of both vibhūtis.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 76. Idam poy virindu

*idam pōy virindu ivvulagam aḷandān eḻilār taṇḍhuḻāy vadam pōdil naiyum madanenjamē nangaḷ veḷvaḷaikkē vidam pōl viridal idu viyappē viyan tāmaraiyin tadam pōdu odunga mellāmbal alarvikkum veṇ tingaḷē*

**Пословный перевод:**

idam – at various places; pōy – going; virindu – pervading; ivvulagu – this world; aḷandān – one who measured; eḻil – by \[his\] beauty; Ar – being full; taṇ – cool; tuḻāy – divine tuḷasi; vadam – in the garland; pōdil – in its beauty; naiyum – crumbling; madam – having stubbornness; nenjamē – Oh mind!; viyan – amazing; tāmaraiyin – lotus’; tadam – broad; pōdu­ – flower; odunga – to contract; mel – small; āmbal – lily flower; alarvikkum – one who makes it to blossom; veṇ – white; tingaḷ – candiran (moon); nangaḷ – our; veḷ – pale; vaḷaikkē – on the bangles; vidam pōl – like poison; viridal – spreading its rays; idu – this \[activity\]; viyappē – is it of any surprise?

**Перевод:**

**Oh mind which is craving for the beautiful divine tuḷasi garland of sarvēśvaran who measured the world at all places with his divine foot! Is it any surprise that the whitish moon, which makes lotus to close its petals and lily to blossom, is spreading like poison on top of my pale bangles?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) During the time of moon rising, the nāyaki feels very distressed. She tells her mind “It is the nature of candiran (moon) to carry out simple tasks such as to make the lotus to close its petals and to make lily to blossom. Do not grieve regarding this’. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**idam pōy virindu** – just as flood will fill up all the low lying areas, \[sarvēśvaran’s\] divine foot permeated wherever there was space. Beautiful, cool divine tuḷasi is there on the divine form of the one who measured all the worlds; the divine garland is there as the sustenance for that divine tuḷasi. Oh mind, which is stubbornly desirous of that garland since it is beautiful and cool on account of being in constant contact with is divine form!  You are languishing because the divine tuḷasi is not attained and you are not listening to me when I say that it is not easy to obtain that!

**nangaḷ . . .** – is it any surprise that the white rays of the candiran are spreading on my bangles made of conch, like poison?

**veḷ vaḷaikkē** – it is natural for harming something when an entity is capable of harming it. Since candiran is whitish and my bangles too are, seeing a congruence in the colour, it could be harming.

**viyan tāmarai . . .** – when someone does not know the distinction between a great and a lowly entity, what will he not do? When candiran is capable of making the great and wondrous lotus flower to contract and the lowly lily flower to blossom, is it a surprise that it is spreading like poison on our bangles?

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): āḻvār is narrating about how in a situation when he could not get the beautiful divine tuḷasi garland which sarvēśvaran wore when he mercifully measured the worlds, even favourable objects \[such as candiran\] turn out to be harmful.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 77. Tingalam pillai

*tingaḷam piḷḷai pulambat tan sengōl arasupattasengaḷam paṟṟi ninṟu eḷgu pun mālai tenpāl ilangaivengaḷam seyda nam viṇṇōr pirānār tuḻāy tuṇaiyānangaḷai māmai koḷvān vandu tōnṟi naliginṟadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tingaḷ – candiran (moon); am – beautiful; piḷḷai – son; pulamba – weeping (due to the sounds of birds); sengōl – one who was ruling with his sceptre; than arasu – his king sūriyan (sun); patta – fell down (sun set); sem – reddish; kaḷam – battle field; paṟṟi – attaining; ninṟu – remaining; eḷgum – engaging with; punmālai – short duration of dusk; tenpāl – in the southern direction; ilangai – lankā; vengaḷam seyda – made it into a cremation ground; viṇṇōr – by celestial entities such as brahmā et al; pirānār – lord, who is apt to be vorśipped; nam – worn by our cakravarty tirumagan (son of emperor daśarata); tuḻāy – desire which was created in the divine tuḷasi; tuṇaiyā – as helping hand; nangaḷai – us; māmai koḷvān – to destroy the natural complexion; vandu – reaching; tōnṟi – rising; naliginṟadu – is tormenting

**Перевод:**

**It was the time of dusk when the King sūriyan, who has been ruling righteously with his sceptre, was about to meet his end in the reddish battlefield \[of western horizon\] and son of dusk, candiran, was about to rise, crying. It \[the candiran\] is trying to take our desire for the divine tuḷasi garland, worn by śrī rāma, who created a battlefield in the southern place of lankā, as its support and is trying to torment us by destroying our natural complexion.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) This narrates the words of the nāyaki who is feeling saddened by the onset of dusk. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tingaḷam piḷḷai** – the beautiful son, candiran. Just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 66-3 “*mahatā tapasā*” (by superlative control), he is a son sired after lot of difficulties.

**pulamba** – could not wail with a loud tone since there were other entities such as ocean, birds etc which were making lot of noise at that time.

**than sengōl . . .** – the short duration of dusk held on to the reddish sky where its king, sūriyan, who was ruling righteously with his sceptre, had fallen down. The redness could be compared to the earth which had become reddish due to the flood of blood shed in a battle. Just as maṇḍōdari called out in the battlefield where rāvaṇa had fallen and just as tārā had called out, along with her son angadan, in the battlefield where her husband vāli had fallen, this evening time too is remaining in that battlefield, where she had lost sūriyan, and is engaging with that place; just as āḻvār had sung in tiruvāimoḻi 2.1 decad *vāyum tiraiyugaḷum* when it appeared to him that every entity which he saw at that time was commiserating with him. The *nāyaki* too feels despondent, after losing sūriyan who has been mentioned as *pagaṟ kaṇḍēn* in iraṇḍām tiruvandādi 81 (I saw śrīman nārāyaṇan who is like the sun which never sets).

**tenpāl . . .** – in the southern side where great people \[reference to kubēran and other dēvatās\] had lost, in lankā, where wise people will not venture, making it to look reddish with blood.

**vengaḷam** – making it look like a cremation ground and places allied to it. It is similar to tirumoḻi 10-9-1 “*ilangai oḷḷeri maṇḍi uṇṇa* ”(making the effulgent agni (lord of fire) to pervade lankā and eat it up fully)

**nam viṇṇōr pirānār** – just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 117-13 “*bhavān nārāyanō dēva*: ” (brahmā tells śrī rāma “You are the lord śrīman nārāyaṇan” sarvēśvaran carried out acts of benefaction to everyone such that brahmā et al all stood and praised him.

**tuḻāy tuṇaiyā** – taking my desire to obtain the divine tuḷasi garland lying on his divine shoulder, as the means.

**nangaḷai . . .** – the dusk, instead of calling us to comfort it during its sorrowful moment \[of losing sūriyan\], is calling us to torment us.

**nangaḷai māmai koḷvān** – for those who covet others’ complexion, is complexion a necessity at all? Instead of calling us to support it during its difficult time, it is craving for tormenting us, who are already in distressed condition.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār’s distress in not getting the garland which sarvēśvaran wore at the time he measured the worlds, and which he desired, was narrated. In this pāsuram, āḻvār’s distress in not getting the garland which sarvēśvaran wore after annihilating rāvaṇa, which āḻvār desired, has been narrated.

## 78. Naliyum naraganai

*naliyum naraganai vīttiṟṟum vāṇan tiṇ tōḷ tuṇinda valiyum perumaiyum yān sollum nīrttalla maivaraipōl poliyum uruvil pirānār punai pūm tuḻāy malarkkē meliyum mada nenjinār tandu pōyina vēdanaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

naliyum – troubling; naraganai – the demon narakāsuran; vīttiṟṟum – destroying; vāṇan – the demon bāṇāsuran’s; tiṇ – strong; tōḷ – shoulders; tuṇitta – severed; valiyum – strength; perumaiyum – eminence (which followed it); yān sollum – to be informed by me; nīrttalla – do not have that nature; maivarai pōl – like a black mountain; poliyum – shining (beauty); uruvil – having divine form; pirānār – kriṣṇan, the lord; punai – donning; pū – being beautiful; tuḻāy malarkkē – for the divine tuḷasi flower; meliyum – withering; madam – being stubborn; nenjinār – mind; tandu pōna – given; vēdanai – (this) agony

**Перевод:**

**The strength and greatness of sarvēśvaran’s acts in the annihilation of narakāsuran and severing the thousand shoulders of bāṇāsuran are not of that simple a nature to be narrated by me. The agony of my heart, pining with stubbornness for the divine tuḷasi garland which is worn by kriṣṇa who looks beautiful like a black mountain, is like this.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) The nāyaki who was engaged with kriṣṇāvatāram (incarnation as kriṣṇa) desires to obtain a material connected with it. This pāsuram narrates that. Let us go through the pāsruam anda its meanings:*

**naliyum naraganai** – the demon narakāsuran imprisoned those who were apt to be part of sarvēśvaran’s retinue.

**naliyum** – their trouble \[of being imprisoned\] was like his \[sarvēśvaran’s\]

**vīttiṟṟum** – felling him – killing him.

**vāṇan . . .** – bāṇāsuran, the demon, who imprisoned uśai (uśā, his daughter) and aniruddan (grandson of kriṣṇa) instead of allowing them to be together.

**tiṇ tōḷ** – the demon was thinking that he had very powerful shoulders after taking refuge of some other deity \[and not sarvēśvaran\]. The vyākyātā explains a few verses from taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli 2 to distinguish this from one who takes refuge under sarvēśvaran. “*ānandam brahmaṇō vidvān*” – one who knows about the joy of brahmam (sarvēśvaran); *na bibhēti kutaścana* – will not fear anything; *ēśaśyēvānandayāti* – due to that courage, he will not respect entities which his intellect respects. Due to the false courage that the demon bāṇan had about his taking refuge under some other deity, he did not show reverence to those entities which his intelligence accepted as reverential. The strength and greatness \[of sarvēśvaran\] who severed the shoulders of such bāṇāsuran.  Alternatively, the greatness of such strength.

**yān sollum nīrttalla** – is it something which would be narrated by me at this time?

**nīrttalla** –it \[the narration\] is not of that simple a nature. Could I narrate today about his quality of annihilating his enemy, his manliness and his greatness? If his true nature were spoken about, then it will be impossible to blame him for the sorrow that I am facing due to his absence.

Then, was it due to someone else?

**maivarai . . .** – āḻvār says that it is the gift given by the heart by not analysing whether something is attainable or unattainable and desiring it.

**maivarai** – the divine form is like a black mountain.

**poliyum uru** – since there is nothing equivalent to the divine form’s beauty, it cannot be explained through an example. If among the entities which exist, one were to consider the best to attempt to match the beauty of his divine form, that would be found wanting, viz. everything else is inferior.

**pirānār** – just as jitantē ślōkam 5 says “*bhaktānām*” (for the sake of followers), his divine form exists for the sake of followers.

**punai . . .**  – only for the divine tuḷasi garland which he has donned.

**meliyum mada nenjinār** – if one desires something which is possible to get, yet if it is  not obtained, there will be sorrow. But what will happen if one desires to get something which is impossible to get? This is what the stubborn heart did for me, leading to this agony.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 79. Vedanai venpuri

*vēdanai veṇpurinūlanai viṇṇōr parava ninṟa nādanai jñālam viḻungum anādanai jñālandattum pādanaip pāṟkadal pāmbaṇai mēl paḷḷi koṇḍaruḷum sīdanaiyē toḻuvār viṇṇuḷārilum sīriyarē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vēdanai – one who is described by vēdas (sacred texts); veṇ – whitish; puri – having strands; nūlanai – having divine sacred thread; viṇṇōr – nityasūris (permanent dwellers of śrīvaikuṇṭam); parava ninṟa – being vorśipped by; nādanai – being the lord; jñālam – leelāvibhūti (materialistic realm) including earth; viḻungum – one who swallows; anādanai – one who does not have a lord for him; jñālam – earth; tattum – one who brought under; pādanai – having divine feet; pāṟkadal – in tiruppāṟkadal (divine milky ocean); pāmbu aṇai mēl – on top of the divine bed of divine ādiśēśan; paḷḷi koṇḍu aruḷum – one who is reclining mercifully; sīdanaiyē – sarvēśvaran who has coolness as his natural quality; toḻuvār – those who worship; viṇṇuḷārilum – more than nityasūris; sīriyarē – are eminent

**Перевод:**

**sarvēśvaran is described by the vēdas, he wears the divine sacred thread with white strands and is vorśipped by nityasūris. He swallows all the worlds, doesn’t have a lord for himself and measures all the worlds and keeps them under his divine feet. He reclines on the divine mattress of ādiśēśan in the divine tiruppāṟkadal and has coolness as his true nature. Those who worship him are more eminent than the nityasūris.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār is mercifully speaking about those who are able to enjoy firmly all the matters related to bhagavān, unlike himself who becomes faint on thinking about him. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**vēdanai** – āḻvār thinks of vēdas which keep thinking, just like himself, only about sarvēśvaran and which do not have a beginning or end.

**veṇ puri nūlanai** – just as it has been  seen in the previous pāsuram *mai varai pōl poliyum uru*, he  has a dark form; vēdas too describe such divine form. He has a whitish divine thread which contrasts with that dark form.

**viṇṇōr parava ninṟa nādanai** – even nityasūris, in a disorderly way, keep calling out to such form

**jñālam viḻungum anādanai** – he does not look at his eminence in being praised by those who have no shortcomings in them \[nityasūris\]; he thinks constantly of samsāris (dwellers in materialistic realm) and protects them by keeping the world in which they live, in his divine stomach, during deluge. He has no one who can tell him “You are not supposed to do this” \[he has none above him who can order him\].

**jñālam . . .** – instead of protecting once and letting go, like a worm making movements such that it forms an alphabet by accident, he has the divine feet which encircled the world fully, protecting them.

**jñālam . . .** – even after carrying out all the activities for samsāris, including measuring their worlds and protecting them, he thinks “We have not done anything for them”

**pāṟkadal . . .** – he keeps reclining on tiruppāṟkadal (the divine milky ocean) thinking about the protection of the worlds; despite doing everything, he thinks “We have not done anything for them”; he retrieves the worlds which indra \[the lord of celestial entities\] had lost out to demons,and reclines.

**sīdanai** – if one were to think about his reclining on tiruppāṟkadal, all the heat \[anguish\] gets removed; he has such a cool nature. Just as it has been mentioned in narāyaṇa sūktam *tāpārtō jalaśāyinam* – he reclines on water in order to remove the heat of samsāris.

**sīdanaiyē toḻuvār** – those who worship him without expecting some other benefit \[instead of himself\]; those who worship him without becoming faint hearted on hearing his divine name.

**viṇṇuḷārilum sīriyarē** – unlike those who are permanently enjoying sarvēśvaran from *that* place \[nityavibhūti\], those who are in samsāram, which is the exact opposite of nityavibhūti, and having the activity of vorśipping him as a constant behaviour, are more eminent than nityasūris.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 80. Sirarasandu

*sīrarasāṇḍu tan sengōl silanāḷ seleeikkaḻinda pārarasottu maṟaindadu nāyiṟu pāraḷanda pērarasē em visumbarasē emmai nīttu vanjitta ōrarasē aruḷāy iruḷāy vandu uṟuginṟadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

sīr – very well; arasu – kingdom; āṇḍu – governing; than – his; sengōl – rule; sila nāḷ – some time; seleei – conducting; kaḻinda – after spending; pār – on the earth; arasē – with the kings; ottu – being equal \[to them\]; nāyiṟu – sūriyan (sun); maṟaindadu – set; iruḷāy vandu – darkness, with its true form, came; uṟuginṟadu – is contacting; pār – earth; aḷanda – one who seized; pēr – having a great quality; arasē – oh lord!; em – being in close proximity to us; visumbu – for paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam); arasē – oh one who is the lord!; emmai – us; nīttu – leaving; vanjittu – through deceit; ōr – being unique; arasē – oh one who became the king!; aruḷāy – show your mercy

**Перевод:**

**The sūriyan (sun) set like kings who rule very well for some time and then leave. Darkness which followed that, engulfed me fully. Oh my lord who measured this earth! Oh the lord of paramapadam which is close to us! Oh the lord who deceitfully left me! You should show your mercy on me.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) The sun had set and darkness engulfed fully. This pāsuram reflects the words of the nāyaki in distress due to that. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**sīrarasu āṇḍu** – ruling the kingdom well. When the pāṇḍava king darman left the country for *vanavāsam* (to dwell in the forest), the people of the country followed him with all their ritualistic paraphernalia, saying “if darmaputran does not rule the country we too would not stay here”; ruling the kingdom in such a noble way.

**than sengōl silanāḷ seleei** – not ruling for an insignificant period but for a long time, in an eminent way.

**kaḻinda pār arasu ottu** – ruling like respectable kings such as māṇḍhātā (one of the kings in the ikśvāku dynasty, a lineage to which śrī rāma belonged)

**maṟaindadu nāyiṟu** – when sūriyan is around, it would appear as if *nāyakan* is around, comforting her. However, with sūriyan setting and night approaching, the sensory organs are focussing only on her separation from the *nāyakan* and are frightening her. In the daytime, each sensory organ would be involved in matters related to it and distress due to separation would not be intensely felt.

**maṟaindadu** – sūriyan is common to all people; however, to the *nāyaki*, he was more significant since she was looking at his disappearance just like she would keep looking at her *nāyakan* when he was leaving her.

**pāraḷanda pērarasē** – if sūriyan, who has been caught in the web of darkness, has to be redeemed, it would require the presence of the same entity who had redeemed earth from the clutches of mahābali.

**aḷanda** – just as he had measured the entire expanse of earth with his divine feet, couldn’t he measure this darkness too? Just as it has been mentioned in tiruviruttam 33 “*iruḷār vinai keda”* (to annihilate the sins of darkness) vouldn’t he be ruling to get rid of darkness?

**pērarasē** – he is  not one who carries out lowly and simple acts; all his activities are great ones.

**em visumbarasē** – after acquiring knowledge about matters relating to sarvēśvaran, paramapadam (śrīvaikuṇṭam) appears to be much closer to āḻvār than samsāram. Just as it has been mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 6-8-1 “*ponnulagu āḷīrō*” (āḻvār tells the birds that he would give them śrīvaikuṇṭam itself to rule), āḻvār had gifted śrīvaikuṇṭam as the first gift to those who were to unite him with sarvēśvaran.

**emmai nīttu vanjitta ōrarasē** – tirukkurugaippirān piḷḷān would explain this by saying “As if he had crowned himself separately for the two vibhūtis, it appears that he had taken over this vibhūti only to separate me and to destroy me”. bhattar would explain this by saying “When he separated from me, he had said ‘I will not leave you’; once he separated me from him, he seemed to say ‘Who can equal me in this nature?’ It appeared as if he had coronated himself separately for this behaviour saying ‘There is none to equal me in dismissing those who will not separate’ ”

**aruḷāy** – when nothing exists, there will be none asking for mercy to be showered. Those who are in paramapadam will not desire anything since they are deeply engaged with the qualities of sarvēśvaran; those who are in samsāram will sustain themselves only through food, water etc. Could he not shower his mercy on someone who, staying here, is in the status of those who are in paramapadam? Those in paramapadam would be fully devoted to enjoying the auspicious qualities of sarvēśvaran while those in samsāram would be fully devoted to *śabdādi viśayam* (matters relating to sensory perceptions such as sound, touch etc). sarvēśvaran has to shower his mercy on samsāris who have only knowledge about his auspicious qualities but not the experience with them.

What is the need to shower his mercy here, now?

**iruḷāy vandu** – instead of a tiger disguised as a cow, now the real tiger itself has come in the form of darkness. In other words, instead of fearing deluge, darkness has come to cause harm.   For regular samsāris, deluge would cause harm; for those in separation, darkness itself does that much of harm.

**uṟuginṟadē** – darkness has come to envelop her fully. Before that, she wants to unite with sarvēśvaran who is referred to in periya tiruvandādi 26 as “*iruḷanna māmēni”* – the huge form of sarvēśvaran which is dark.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): āḻvār mercifully says that since the time of stay in samsāram creates ignorance, he wants sarvēśvaran to redeem him soon from it.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 81. Uruginra kanmangal

*uṟuginṟa kanmangaḷ mēlana ōrppilarāy ivaḷaip peṟuginṟa tāyar meynondu peṟārgol tuḻāy kuḻal vāyt tuṟuginṟalar tollai vēngadamāttavum sūḻginṟalar iṟuginṟadāl ivaḷāgam mellāvi eri koḷḷavē*

**Пословный перевод:**

mel – being soft; āvi – (her) vital air; eri – the fire of separation; koḷḷa – is swallowing her; ivaḷ – this nāyaki’s; āgam – form; iṟuginṟadu – is perishing; mēlana – what is going to happen; uṟuginṟa – approaching closely; kanmangaḷ – deeds to be carried out; ōrppilarāy – without enquiring about them; tuḻāy – divine tuḷasi; kuḻal vāy – on her locks; tuṟuginṟinar – they are not applying; tollai – being ancient; vēngadam – in the pond of tiruvēngadam; āttavum – giving her a bath; sūḻginṟilar – they are not attempting; ivaḷai – this distinguished nāyaki; peṟuginṟa – those who begot; tāyar – mothers; mey nondu – with the body in pain; peṟār kol – did they not give birth to?

**Перевод:**

**The fire of separation is slowly destroying the nāyaki’s soft life. Her mothers do not seem to know the distinguished activities which will remove her distress. They are not applying the divine tuḷasi, worn by sarvēśvaran. They are not giving her a bath in the pond at the ancient tiruvēngadam. It appears that her mothers did not give birth to her, after suffering severe pain in their bodies \[implying that she is not born to them but probably adopted\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Darkness had set in everywhere. The nāyaki has become unconscious, thinking about him. Even after seeing her position, her relatives do not seem worried about it; they say “We will do the appropriate remedy at the appropriate time”. An old, knowledgeable woman, seeing this, says “Her position is sinking; her eminence is so great; when such is the case, why are they so lackadaisical? Are they not her parents?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**uṟuginṟa** –  The old lady is wondering whether there is no activity to be performed by them \[relatives of *nāyaki*\] considering the relationship between them and the *nāyaki*.

**uruginṟa kanmangaḷ mēlana ōrppilarāy** –  the *nāyaki* is not well. They have to take some actions for that. Though the time has come now for taking those actions, they seem to be undecided about the actions.

**ivaḷaippeṟuginṟa tāyar** – should her mothers not be worried about her situation? Do they have to think whether their actions would be beneficial or not? Is it not enough that she is the daughter that they had sired?

**mey nondu peṟār kol** – didn’t her mothers give birth to her after suffering pangs of pain during labour? ōr, did they adopt her from someone and bring her up?

What is the reason for the old lady to say like this?

**tuḻāy kuḻalvāyt tuṟuginṟalar** – The time for carrying out the action which had been mentioned in tiruviruttam pāsuram 53 *maṇṇāyinum koṇḍu vīsuminē* has passed. Now, it is not sufficient to blow divine tuḷasi or any of its connected entities such as stem or root or soil. Now they have to decorate her locks with tuḷasi such that it is fully covering her locks.

**tollai . . .** – they do not seem carry out any of the remedial measures which the people of their clan had done in the past.

**vēngadam āttavum** – the place which is full of stones and forests, appears to be a pond for the *nāyaki*. Just as it has been said in mahābhāratam mōkśa darmam 4-50 *ēśa brahma praviśtōsmi* – they are not immersing her in the pond of parabrahmam (sarvēśvaran) to give her a bath.

**sūḻginṟalar** – they may not do that. They are not taking the initial steps for that; they are not enquiring about what is to be done. Would enquiring about it be sufficient to improve her situation? Since it is a matter related to sarvēśvaran, the minute they take the first steps, it would start yielding the benefits.

Should the disease as well as the remedy be fully analysed before doing anything?

**iṟuginṟadāl ivaḷ āgam** – would her nature tolerate such a lengthy process? Her form is crumbling.

**mellāvi eri koḷḷavē** – is it proper to look at the process when her soft ātmā is getting burnt by the fire of separation?

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): this pāsuram narrates the way āḻvār is immersed in matters relating to bhagavān such that it becomes difficult both for those who try to sustain him and for sarvēśvaran \[the protector\].

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 82. Erikol sennayiru

*erikoḷ sennāyiṟu iraṇḍudanē udayam malaivāy viriginṟa vaṇṇattu emperumān kaṇgaḷ mīṇḍavaṟṟuḷ erikoḷ sendīvīḻasuraraippōla empōliyarkkum virivasolleer iduvō vaiyamuṟṟum viḷariyadē*

**Пословный перевод:**

emperumān – sarvēśvaran, who is my lord; kaṇgaḷ – divine eyes; erikoḷ – having fire; udayam malaivāy – in the mountains where it rises from; udanē – simultaneously; viriginṟa – spreading; sem – having reddish rays; iraṇḍu nāyiṟu – twin sūriyans’; vaṇṇantta – having the true nature; erikoḷ – having flame; sem – reddish; tī – like fire; avaṟṟuḷ – in the splendour of the sūriyan; mīṇḍu – again; vīḻ – falling; asuraraippōla – like demons (known as mandēhar); empōliyarkkum – like us who are affectionate (in matters related to bhagavān); viriva – appeared before us; vaiyam muṟṟum – throughout the world; viḷariyadu – calling out; iduvō – was it for this?; solleer – please tell

**Перевод:**

**The divine eyes of sarvēśvaran appear like two sūriyans, spitting fire, which have risen at the same time over the mountains. Just like demons die in the splendour of sūriyan’s flames, sarvēśvaran’s divine eyes are harmful for us who are devoted to him.  Please tell us whether it is for this that the world is calling out to that sarvēśvaran.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) jīyar \[nanjīyar\] would mercifully say that this pāsuram is not to his liking. This is because, just as it is mentioned in periya tirumoḻi 7-1-9 “tāyāyaḷikkinṟa taṇḍāmaraikkaṇṇā” (Oh one who has divine eyes similar to cool, reddish lotus flower and who showers mercy like a mother!), sarvēśvaran’s eyes which are soft are described in this pāsuram as being fiery. We could experience what āḻvār is saying only if we too are suffering in distress, just like āḻvār. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**eri . . .** – The divine eyes of sarvēśvaran are similar to two reddish sūriyans which are spitting fire and spreading it when they rise over the mountains.

**mīṇḍu . . .** – when the sūriyan rises, demons, known as *mandēhars*, will hold on to his chariot, preventing him from going further. The divine water offered by brāhmaṇās when they offer *arjñam* will provide a splendour to sūriyan. The demons will fall into and get destroyed in that fire. Similar to those demons who fall into that roaming sūriyan’s fire and get destroyed . . .

**empōliyarkkum viriva** – for those like me who desire him and become sorrowful, they cause harm just like they do for the demons.

**solleer iduvō vaiyam muṟṟum viḷariyadē** – is this the result for the world calling out to him? Please tell. Is it to fall into that fire that the world is calling out to him? īsn’t she the one who had thought in tiruvāimoḻi 2.1 decad *vāyum tiraiyugaḷum* when she felt that the entire world was suffering from the same sorrows that she was suffering from?

Alternatively

**mīṇḍavaṟṟuḷ erikoḷ sendīvīḻasuraraippōl empōliyarkkum** – just like those demons who fall into that flame of sūriyan’s splendour and perish, these eyes are harmful for people like me.

**viriva solleer** – tell me a refuge. In this context, the term *viriva* refers to being expansive and thus implies a refuge.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 83. Vilarikkural anril

*viḷarikkural anṟil menpēdai mēginṟa munṟil peṇṇai muḷarik kurambai idu iduvāga mugil vaṇṇan pēr kiḷarikkiḷarip pidaṟṟum mellāviyum naivumellām taḷariṟkolō aṟiyēn uyyalāvadu ittaiyalukkē*

**Пословный перевод:**

mel – having a soft nature; pedai – with female bird; mēginṟa – being united together; viḷari – in a high pitch; kural – voice; anṟil – nightingale bird’s; munṟil – in the frontyard; peṇṇai – on top of the palm tree; muḷari – made of lotus flower; kurambai – nest; idu iduvāga – tormenting permanently; mugil – like cloud; vaṇṇan – sarvēśvaran who has a form like that; pēr – divine name; kiḷarik kiḷari – taking it repatedly; pidaṟṟum – babbling; mel – soft; āviyum – vital air; naivum – (body) crumbling; ellām – since all these happened; ittaiyalukku – for this girl; uyyalāvadu – to be alive; taḷaril kolō – is it possible; ariyēn – I am not sure

**Перевод:**

**There is a palm tree in the frontyard of nāyakí’s house where a male nightingale bird, with a high pitch, is living with its female nightingale which has a soft nature, inside a nest made with lotus flowers. Her mother says that she is not sure if the nāyaki will survive since she keeps babbling the divine names of sarvēśvaran, since she has a soft nature and since her form is crumbling \[due to separation from sarvēśvaran\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, sarvēśvaran’s divine eyes tormented the nāyaki. This pāsuram narrates her sadness at seeing two nightingale birds sitting on a palm tree in the frontyard of her house. The two birds had locked their beaks when the female bird leaves. The male calls out after her. Seeing this, the nāyaki goes into a state of stupor. Her mother, hearing the bird’s high pitched calls, says “The sound of the bird is like this. Her \[the daughter’s\] distress due to separation is like this. Her softness is like this. Will I see her happy with her desire fulfilled?” When parāśara bhattar was narrating this, his disciple nanjīyar asked him “Could you please narrate an incident similar to this from śrī rāmāyaṇam?” bhattar responded saying “When lakśmaṇa left sītāppirātti alone in the forest \[when she was pregnant\] she let out a cry. Sage vālmīki who heard her cry of distress had a mental makeup similar to the nāyaki’s mother” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**viḷarikkural anṟil** – the term *viḷari* refers to a high pitched voice. The male nightingale bird calls with such a high pitched voice.

**men pēdai** – the softness of the female nightingale bird is such that when being united, touching it would appear to be similar to touching a flower. If there is separation in such a soft natured entity, the distress would be impossible to tolerate.

**mēginṟa** – being permanently united, without any separation. It would remind one of *anapāyini* (śrī mahālakśmi who will not separate from emperumān even for a moment).

**munṟil peṇṇai** – the term *munṟil* would refer both to frontyard and entrance. Some people asked vangipurattu nambi when this pāsuram was being recited “Will anyone have a palm tree in the frontyard of the house?”  nambi responded saying  “You are asking about that! īsn’t that the reason as to why her matters are like this, where even the tender leaves are snapping?” Just as it has been said in periya tirumoḻi pāsuram 11-2-1 “*munṟil tani ninṟa peṇṇai mēl kidandīrginṟa anṟilin kūttaip pirikka kiṟpavar yārkolo*?” (who has the cleverness to remove the nest of the nightingales, which are always together, from the palm tree seen at the entrance?) vouldn’t her relatives be busy in bringing the *nāyakan* back? The *nāyaki* is not the type who goes around separating and uniting entities.

**muḷarik kurambai** – the male nightingale will build the nest with lotus leaves and flowers, to be apt for the soft nature of the female bird. Alternatively, *muḷari* would refer to thorns and the nest would be built with thorns to protect the birds from outside attack.

**idu iduvāga** – after building nest like this and not tormenting.

**mugil vaṇṇan pēr kiḷarik kiḷarip pidaṟṟum** – repeatedly babbling the divine name of sarvēśvaran.

**mellāviyum** – even though she was reciting the divine names, did it make her feel peaceful? That did not happen because of the tiredness caused in her heart.

**naivum**– her physical form too was crumbling

**ellām** – since all these happened

**taḷariṟkolō (ittaiyalukku uyyalāvadu)** – survival for this girl

**taḷariṟkolō** – without even this blabbering, the only way to survive is to perish. I do not know whether she can survive without perishing.

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): those who saw āḻvār found that his form was getting tormented a lot. They said “Would we see him sustain himself until he reaches his end goal of attaining sarvēśvaran? It appears that there is hope only after he leaves this physical form”

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 84. Taiyanallargal

*taiyanallārgaḷ kuḻāngaḷ kuḻiya kuḻivinuḷḷum aiyanallārgaḷ kuḻiya viḻavinum angangellām kaiya ponnāḻi veṇsangodum kāṇbān avāvuvan nān maiyavaṇṇa maṇiyē muttamē enṟan māṇikkamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

taiyal naḷḷārgaḷ – gems of women; kuḻāngaḷ – association; kuḻiya – huge; kuḻuvinuḷḷum – in gatherings; aiyanallārgaḷ – wise men; kuḻiya – huge; viḻavinum – in celebrations; angangellām – at all those places; maiya – like black pigment; vaṇṇā – one who has a form (such that the eyes of the beholder would feel cool); maṇiyē – one who is easily approachable; muttamē – one who is cool like a pearl; en than – as a lord for me; māṇikkamē – one who is eminent like a carbuncle; kaiya – in the divine hands; pon – radiant; āḻi – with divine disc; veṇ – whitish; sangodum – with śrī pāncajanyam (divine conch); nān – I; kāṇbān – to worship (you); avāvuvan – desired

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who has a divine form like a black pigment which is comforting for the beholder! Oh one who is easily approachable! Oh one who has a cool nature similar to pearl’s! Oh one who is like a carbuncle and who is my lord! Among the huge gatherings of women who are like gems and among the festive gatherings of wise men, I desire to see your divine hands holding the radiant divine disc and the divine white conch.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) This pāsuram says how the nāyaki, in a state of bewilderment, runs here and there, calling out to sarvēśvaran, since she is not able to see him. This is similar to the way śrī rāma wandered in the forest, looking for sītāppirātti, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam āraṇya kāṇḍam 60-36, 37 “kvacit udbhramatē vēgāt kvacit vibhramatē balāt \|\| kvacit matta iva ābhāti kāntā anvēśaṇa tatpara: \|” (somewhere by forcefulness of illusion and somewhere else by the poverfulness of allusion and elsewhere, due to the love for his impassioned ladylove, he was running like a madman). Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tiyanallār . . .** – there are women who are like gems; there are huge gatherings of such women. When āḻvār sees such gatherings, he would presume that kriṣṇa is amidst them, ready to begin the dance called as *kuravai* (a dance in which two people will hold their hands tightly together and spin around, holding their feet at the centre).

**aiya nallār . . .** – just as the wise saying goes “*aṟavōr arundavattōr aiyar*” (eminent are those who follow righteous path and who carry out deep penance). *aiyanallār* are even better than such people.  *avargaḷ kuḻiya viḻavinum* – places where yajñams (ritualistic events) are held, where they will be present in large numbers. In such places as well as in places where people similar to daṇḍakāraṇya riśis are gathered. Places such as the one mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 57-13 “*samājēśu mahatsuca*” (great assembly of wise men).

**angangellām** – in places similar to these

**kaiya . . .** – āḻvār desires to see sarvēśvaran without distinction between his incarnations where he had hidden his divine disc and conch (such as kriṣṇāvatāram) and those where he had displayed them. He had hidden his divine weapons only from demonic entities. What is the hurdle for me to see him with such divine weapons? ḍidn’t arjunan tell him in bhagavat gītai 11-46 “*tēnaiva rūpēṇa caturbhujēna*” (I desire to see your form with four shoulders, as before)?

**avāvuvan nān** – Does desire have eyes? I desire only “To see”. I do not wish to think “He is difficult to see” If he is difficult to be seen, did he create the desire in  you to see him and punish you?

**maiya vaṇṇā . . .** – he says that it is the eminence in the entity that created the desire in him. When someone loses an eminent entity, will that person wait to get it back?

**maiya vaṇṇā . . .** – the entity is capable of making the beholder’s eyes to be cool, as if black pigment has been applied.

**maṇiyē** – even when the entity is like this, it has to be experienced fully.

**muttamē** – if the entity were embraced, all the thirst would evaporate.

**enṟan māṇikkamē** – absolutely inestimable.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 85. Manikkangondu

*māṇikkangoṇḍu kurangu eṟivottu iruḷōdu mutti āṇipponnanna sudar padumālai ulagaḷanda māṇikkamē en maradagamē maṟṟoppāraiyillā āṇipponnē adiyēn adi āvi adaikkalamē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kurangu – monkey; māṇikkam – gem \[carbuncle\]; koṇḍu – taking it; eṟivu ottu – equivalent to throwing; iruḷōdu – with darkness; mutti – getting close; āṇipon anna – like a distinguished gold; sudar – radiant sūriyan (\[sun\]; padum – suffering; mālai – dusk (appeared); ulagu – worlds; aḷanda – one who seized; māṇikkamē – Oh one who is virtuous!; en – being my lord; maradagamē – one who is invigorating like an emerald gem!; maṟṟu – over and above; oppārai – those who are equal; illā – ohe who does not have; āṇipponnē – Oh one who is like distinguished gold!; adiyēn – this servitor, my; adi – being your servitor; āvi – vital air; adaikkalam – is to be protected (by you)

**Перевод:**

**Oh one who measured all the worlds! Oh one who is virtuous and is like an invigorating emerald gem to me! Oh one who is like distinguished gold, without anyone being your equal! Just like a monkey threw a carbuncle gem, the dusk time has thrown out sūriyan who is like eminent gold. This ātmā (soul) of mine, your servitor, has to be protected by you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) This pāsuram reveals the words of the nāyaki who is saddened by the appearance of night. This could also be treated as the words of the nāyaki’s mother who is lamenting the nāyaki’s position. Let us go through the pāsuram and its words:*

**māṇikkam koṇḍu kurangu eṟivottu** – Just like a monkey would throw a gemstone of carbuncle. A monkey, without knowing the great value of a gemstone, throws it out such that it reaches a place where it will be of no use to anyone. Alternatively, being an animal, it throws the gemstone where it cannot be used even by the monkey itself and the gem remains losing its shine.

**iruḷōdu . . .** – coming with darkness. The evening time appeared, destroying sūriyan who is like an incomparable, eminent, reddish gold. Just like the carbuncle gem was destroyed by the monkey, sūriyan was destroyed by darkness.

Instead of merely speaking about evening, she now speaks about sarvēśvaran’s great activities.

**ulagaḷanda māṇikkamē** – he is one who pushed aside entities such as mahābali when indra et al, who think that they are the protectors, withdrew, being unable to fight with such mahābali. He is an entity who is virtuous, who measured the worlds which are full of forests and mountains.

**māṇikkamē** – he transformed himself though he is priceless and inestimable \[for the sake of indra et al\].

**en maradagamē** – he is invigorating when embraced.

**maṟṟoppāraiyillā āṇipponnē** – he is eminent without any simile to compare him with – like distinguished gold.

**adiyēn** – āḻvār calls himself as *adiyēn* \[servitor\] manifesting his enjoyability \[by sarvēśvaran\].

**adiyāvi** – If these are the *nāyaki*’s words, this would refer to the *ātmavastu* (her soul) which is subservient to sarvēśvaran. It is so much subservient to sarvēśvaran that even offering it to him would tantamount to being an act in excess \[it belongs to sarvēśvaran; hence there is no need even to offer it to him\]. It is to be offered as soon as one is bewildered; once offered, it would be so severed from him/her that it would make him/her shrink from thinking “I offered an entity of mine to him”. It will be similar to [stōtra ratnam 53](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/02/sthothra-rathnam-53/) “*atavā kinnu samarppayāmi tē*” (What can I offer you?) and [tiruvāimoḻi 7-9-10](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/11/thiruvaimozhi-7-9-10-udhavik-kaimmaru/) “*uṟṟeṇṇil aduvum aṟṟu āngavanṟannadu*” (if I wanted to call it as mine, even that *ātmavastu* happened to be his). The vyākyātā (periyavāccān piḷḷai, commentator for this prabandam) narrates an incident when kūrattāḻvān was sent by bhagavd rāmānujar to tirukkōttiyūr nambi (rāmānujar’s ācāryan) to find out whether it is *jñātrutvam* (being with knowledge about matters relating to sarvēśvaran) or *śēśatvam* (being a servitor of sarvēśvaran) which is *ātmasvarūpam* (the true nature of ātmā). nambi mercifully informed kūrattāḻvān “Oh my child! Where he should have said *ennuḷḷān* \[within me\] as in [tiruvāimoḻi 8-8-2](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2019/04/thiruvaimozhi-8-8-2-adiyen-ullan/) ‘*adiyēnuḷḷān udaluḷḷān*’ (he is within me), he had said *adiyēnnuḷḷān* to signify that it is servitude which is the true identity for ātmā”

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 86. Adaikkalattongu

*adaikklattōngu kamalattalarayan senniyennum mudaikkalattūṇ munnaranukku nīkkiyai āḻisangam padaikkalamēndiyai veṇṇeykkanṟu āycci van tāmbugaḷāl pudaikkalandānai emmānai ensollip pulambuvanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

adaikkalattu – as an entity to be protected; ōngu – grown well; kamalattu – (having been born in the divine navel) lotus’; alar – born in a flower; ayan – brahmā’s; senni ennum – known as head; udai – having an unpleasant smell; kalattu – in the vessel; ūṇ – food; mun – in earlier time; aranukku – for rudra; nīkkiyai – one who removed; āḻisangam – divine disc and divine conch; padai – divine weapons; kalan – as ornaments; ēndiyai – one who donned; anṟu – during the time of kriṣṇāvatāram (incarnation as kriṣṇa); veṇṇey – for butter; āychi – yaśodāp pirātti; van – strong; tāmbugaḷāl – ropes; pudaikka – to hit; alandānai – one who had trouble; emmānai – one who is my lord; en solli – by speaking about what deficiencies; pulambuvan – could I call?

**Перевод:**

**brahmā was born in the lotus flower which originates from the divine navel of sarvēśvaran who is the refuge for all. rudra took his food from the skull (head) of brahmā \[which was plucked by rudra\] which had an unpleasant smell. sarvēśvaran removed that curse on rudra; he holds the divine weapons of disc and conch; when he incarnated as kriṣṇa, he stole butter and was hit by strong ropes by yaśōdā \[his mother\]. With what deficiencies in him  could I call him, who is my lord?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, he had narrated the way darkness had tormented him. He says in this pāsuram that however much darkness surrounds and torments, sarvēśvaran’s true nature is such that one cannot blame him about anything. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**adaikkalattōngu** – growing under sarvēśvaran, to whom he has surrendered, brahmā is like *nikśēpam* (treasure)

**kamalattalarayan** – *nikśēpam* refers to a treasure which is hidden. However, that term refers here to his being looked at by sarvēśvaran such that there is no deficiency in him. Apart from that, brahmā has the fortune of having been born directly to sarvēśvaran.

**senni . . .** – while brahmā is considered as the *lōkaguru* (ācāryan for the world), he is the father of rudra, who plucked one of his heads and stood as one who has committed a serious crime. brahmā stood in sadness, having his head severed. He \[sarvēśvaran\] removed both their sorrows.

**ayan senni ennum mudaikkalam** – that skull \[severed head of brahmā\] became a vessel with a foul smell. Since it was having an unpleasant smell \[due to its contact with brahmā’s flesh\] wherever rudra went, he was followed by birds of prey such as eagle etc.

**mudaikkalattu ūṇ mun aranukku nīkkiyai** – \[due to the curse of brahmā\], rudra had to eat his food by begging for alms, from that skull. rudra, who is the deity for annihilation, had to suffer like this. sarvēśvaran removed this curse for rudra, in an earlier time. He thought “Someone who was created by me should not suffer for a long time like this” and removed that difficulty. Through this , it has been brought out that for those who are born in samsāram, being attracted by karma and the resultant physical form, difficulties etc are common to all and that these are applicable for these two too (brahmā and rudra). One stood with his head severed; one stood having committed that heinous act; sarvēśvaran stood as one who removed both their sorrows.

**āḻi sangam padaikkalam ēndiyai** – he constantly holds his divine weapons such as disc and conch since he cannot search for them to protect samsāris \[such as brahmā and rudra\] when they are wandering in difficulties. He has the nature of wearing these divine weapons as divine ornaments in order to carry out the activity of protection.

**veṇṇey . . .** – though he is capable of removing the difficulties of brahmā, rudra et al and has the necessary implements for that, when he gets into any difficulty from his followers, he cannot extricate himself from such difficulty. While he is the controller of brahmā, rudra et al and remains as the supreme entity, he cannot sustain himself without a few materials which have a contact with his followers. While he is *avāptasamastakāman* \[he does not have any unfulfilled desire\], he feels that there is some deficiency and if he cannot get it directly, he goes to the extent of stealing it. Even while stealing it, he makes sure that he leaves evidence behind to be caught; when caught, he does not take any action to extricate himself from such situations.

**anṟu** – a day comes when he gets tied down and looks as a very simple person! Such a day too occurs! I had missed that day when he had himself tied down and looked so simple and I am praying for attaining him today!

**āycci** – he could not seek a remedy for the difficulty posed by a herdsvoman!

**van tāmbugaḷāl** – whatever ropes she \[yaśōdā\] could lay her hands on, turned out to be short to tie him. Hence she took more ropes and hit him with those. The anger towards a person would be present to the same extent as affection towards that person. It is pertinent here to recall [periya tiruvandādi 38](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2018/11/periya-thiruvandhadhi-38/) pāsuram “*idaicci kumaittiṟangaḷ . . .*” (the difficulties that he had undergone at the hands of yaśōdā)

**pudaikkalandānai** – even when he was in such difficulties he did not take any remedial action.

**alandānai** – the term *ala* refers to blossoming. As she \[yaśōdā\] hit him with the ropes, his face became more and more blossomed. [periya tirumoḻi 5-9-7](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2025/04/periya-thirumozhi-5-9-7/) says “*kaṇṇiyār kuṟungayiṟṟāl katta vettenṟirundān*” (as he was tied with short ropes which were tied together, he stood as if agreeing to that act)

**emmānai** – as he was being tied and hit, he shed copious amount of tears from his eyes and his dark divine form became pale; with such an act, he made me to be subservient only to him. Just as it has been mentioned in [tiruvāimoḻi 5-10-3](https://divyaprabandham.koyil.org/index.php/2017/09/thiruvaimozhi-5-10-3-peyyum-punguzhal/) “*paiyavē nilaiyum*” (the way he stood trembling in fear), it was this which made me lose my steadfastness.

**en sollip pulambuvanē** – I desired for him now and am wailing since I could not attain him. I was not there when he carried out his activites in kriṣṇāvatāram and I cannot point out to any deficiency in him. I am unable to attain him now because of the result of my past deeds. He has no shortcoming in him for me to blame him.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 87. Pulambum kanai kural

*pulambum kanai kural pōḻvāya anṟilum pūngaḻipāyndu alambum kana kural sūḻ tirai āḻiyum āngu avai nin valam puḷḷadu nalam pādum idu kuṟṟamāga vaiyam silambumbadi seyvadē tirumāḷ ittiruvinaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tirumāl – Oh the consort of śrī mahālakśmi!; nin – as your vehicle; valam – having strength; puḷḷadu – periya tiruvadi’s (garudan’s); nalam – auspicious qualities; pādum idu – this singing; kuṟṟamāga – as a wrongful act (using this as a reason); ittiruvinai – this lakshmi; kanai kural – with an illegible sound; pulambum – crying; pōḻ – splitting (affecting girls in separation); vāy – having mouth; anṟil – nightingale bird; pū – being beautiful; kaḻi – in the salt pan; pāyndu – flowing copiously; alambum – splashing; kanam – heavy; kural – having sound; sūḻ – expansive; tirai – having waves; āḻiyum – ocean too; āngu avai – with such harmful entities; vaiyam – the world; silambumbadi – to cry out; seyvadē – you are making!

**Перевод:**

**Oh the lord of śrī mahālakśmi! The nightingale bird is crying out with a loud voice, splitting the hearts. The ocean, with its waves, is splashing around, to flow copiously into the salt pans. This lakshmi (my daughter) is singing on the strong garuda, your vehicle, about his auspicious qualities. Looking at this, the world is abusing her. You are making all these to happen!**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) The nāyaki’s mother tells sarvēśvaran “During this time when she is separated from you, harmful entities are tormenting her. You have made her to call out to you which is being seen by the people of the world!” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pulambum kanai kural** – the nightingale bird is speaking about various matters, with a heavy voice, splitting the hearts of those who have separated from you.

**pūngaḻi . . .** – expansive ocean which is flowing copiously into the salt pans, splashing its waves, making uproarious sound

**āngavai** – for those who are in separation, there are many harmful entities which torment, such as the bell tied to the neck of bull, rising moon, gentle breeze etc. Alternatively, it is the entities which have already been mentioned here – the call of nightingale and the sound from ocean. All these are tormenting her.

What is her fault for these entities to harm her?

**nin valam puḷ . . .** – she sang about garuda, who has a great strength, matching yours, about his qualities to carry you on his shoulders. śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 5-30-1 says “*garudōvāruṇam catram tadaiva maṇiparvatam* \| *sapāryanca hriśīkēśam leelayaiva vahan yayau* \|\|” – after the death of narakāsuran, kriṣṇa left the town of prājñōtiś carrying several things. At that time garuda carried kriṣṇa and maṇiparvatam, varuṇa’s eminent umbrella on his back as if it was a sport for him.  The *nāyaki* sang about such gardua. Just as it has been mentioned in stōtra ratnam 41 “*tvadangri sammardakiṇānga śōbhinā*”, having carried out kainkaryam to sarvēśvaran and carrying out the scars of footprint on his shoulders, she too wanted to wear the scars of their union. Hence she sang a few words about garuda. Making this out as a fault . . .

**vaiyam silambumbadi seyvadē** – without any distinction between elders and youngsters, just because they lived in the same place as the *nāyaki*, they pointed fingers at her and blamed her. How could they do this?

**tirumāḷ** – are you doing all these because she is not your consort?

**ittiruvinaiyē** – is she in any way inferior to those who desired you? Is she capable of remaining as patient as sītāppirātti who was crowded around by the demonic women sent by rāvaṇa? Will you allow the people of the town when they blame her condition of being tormented by ocean etc?  Will it not be your fault if your consort is distressed?

*svāpadēśam* (distinguished meaning): Those who are near āḻvār are asking sarvēśvaran “Will it not be a deficiency for you if you delay coming to him when he is so distressed, being unable to overcome separation from you?”

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 88. Tirumal uruvokkum

*tirumāl uruvokkum mēru ammēruvil senjudarōn tirumāl tirukkait tiruccakkaram okkum anna kaṇḍum tirumāl uruvōdu avan cinnamē pidaṟṟā niṟpadōr tirumāl talaikkoṇḍa nangatku engē varum tīvinaiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

mēru – the mountain mēru; tirumāl – the consort of śrī mahālakśmi; uruvu – with his divine form; okkum – will be equivalent; ammēruvil – on the peak of that mēru mountain; sem – reddish; sudarōn – sūriyan (sun) with his rays; tirumāl – the consort of śrī mahālakśmi, his; tirukkai – on his divine hand; tiruccakkaram – with divine sudarśana cakram (divine disc); okkum – will be equivalent; anna kaṇḍum – despite seeing entities which are equivalent to sarvēśvaran’s divine form and divine weapon; tirumāl – the consort of śrī mahālakśmi, his; uruvōdu – with the divine form; avan – his; cinnamē – only divine weapons; pidaṟṟā niṟpadu – calling out; tiru – eminent; māl – bewilderment; talaikkoṇa – developed (firmly); nangatku – for us; tīvinai – cruel sins; engē varum – how will it happen?

**Перевод:**

**The mēru mountain is a match for the divine form of sarvēśvaran, who is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi. sūriyan, with his reddish rays, on top of the mēru mountain, is a match for the divine sudarśana cakram held in the divine hand of sarvēśvaran. Thus, even though we are able to see the entities which equal the divine form and the divine disc of sarvēśvaran, we desire to see the divine form of sarvēśvaran and his divine disc. When we call out to him, which is like an eminent bewilderment for us, how will cruel sins reach us?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Even though the people of the town blamed her, the nāyaki (āḻvār) mercifully says in this pāsuram “There is no shortcoming in us, even though the whole town has been blaming us” What has been said in this pāsuram is this – sarvēśvaran’s divine form is without any equivalent entity, at all times. If a simile for that is seen, instead of consoling oneself saying “We are able to see a simile for an entity which is non-pareil”, it will create a craving in us to see the original entity itself. For such a person like us, is there any fault? Alternatively, when looking at his activity of creating all the entities and remaining as the sustaining force for them, instead of stopping with that, it would create a desire to see his distinguished divine form. For such a person like us, is there any deficiency? Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tirumāl uruvokkum mēru** – the mountain mēru, through its huge size and radiance, matches the divine form of śrīya:pati (consort of śrī mahālakśmi).

**ammēru . . .** – sūriyan, who is traversing that mountain mēru

**tirumāḷ tirukkait tiruccakkaram okkum** – just as it has been said in tiruppallāṇḍu 2 “*vadivār sōdi valattuṟaiyum sudarāḻiyum*” (divine disc having a radiance which pervades sarvēśvaran’s entire form, residing on his divine right hand, burning enemies), sūriyan will look like that divine disc.

**anna kaṇḍum** – thus seeing entities which resemble sarvēśvaran

**tirumāḷ . . .** – crying out incoherently to see the divine form which is like the mēru mountain and his distinguished weapons which are described as in tiruvāimoḻi 6-4-9 “*valakkaiyāḻi idakkaiccangam*” (divine disc on the divine right hand and divine conch on the divine left hand)

Alternatively,

**tirumāḷ tiruvokkum mēru . . .** – mēru resembles the divine form of sarvēśvaran; sūriyan, who is traversing it, resembles the divine disc on his divine hand; these two represent all the entities which sarvēśvaran had created and for which he is the sustaining force.

**anna kaṇḍum** – even though they \[mēru and sūriyan\] remain as his physical form as per the relationship described in *ādārā – ādēya* (sustaining – being sustained), *niyantru – niyāmya* (controller-controlled) and *śēśi – śēśa* (owner-owned) modes…

**tirumāl . . .** – instead of being satisfied with seeing entities for which he is the sustaining force, calling out with a desire to see his distinguished divine form and distinguished divine identities.

**tirumāl** – just as it has been mentioned in bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat 4-4-5 “*nidityāsitavya:*”-  meditating on him without any break, āḻvār attained the state which is true nature for him.

**talaikkoṇḍa** – *talaikkoḷḷudal* refers to being firm in a matter and not dropping it since some people spoke against it. Not considering the way some people had blamed her \[in the previous pāsuram\], remaining firm in matters related to sarvēśvaran, just like diamond cannot be destroyed by fire.

**nangatku** – for us, who are involved in matters related to sarvēśvaran. Just as the saying goes “*avaśyam anubhōktavyam krutam karma śubhāśubham*” (everyone has to necessarily experience the results of their deeds, whether good or bad), are our deeds capable of creating any fault in us?

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 89. Tivinaikkaru nanjai

*tīvinaikkaru nanjai nalvinakku innamudattinaip pūvinai mēviya dēvi maṇāḷanaip punmai eḷgādu āvinai mēykkum vallāyanai anṟu ulagu īradiyāl tāvina ēṟṟai emmānai ejñānṟu talaippeyvanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

tī – cruel; vinaikku – (followers’) sins; aru – being rare; nanjai – being like poison \[as an antidote\]; nalvinaikku – for good deeds; in – sweet; amudattinai – being like nectar; pūvinai – lotus flower; mēviya – dwelling aptly; dēvi – for periya pirātti (śrī mahālakśmi); maṇāḷanai – as her consort; pumnai – lowly act; eḷgādu – without despising; āvinai – cows; mēykkum – one who tends to; val – being eminent; āyanai – as kriṣṇa, the covherd; anṟu – during the time of mahābali; ulagu – worlds; īradiyāl – with two steps; tāvina – one who seized; ēṟṟai – being eminent; emmānai – sarvēśvaran, who is my lord; ejñānṟu – when; talaippeyvan – will I approach?

**Перевод:**

**sarvēśvaran is the rare poison for the cruel sins of his followers; he is the sweet nectar for their good deeds; he is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi who dwells on a lotus flower; without considering as a despicable act, he tended to cows, having been born in the clan of covherds and measured all the worlds with just two steps; when will I reach such sarvēśvaran?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār says “We have reached the stage of being distinct from samsāris; when will we mingle with the people who have attained him and are experiencing him?” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**tīvinaikky aru nanjai** – he \[sarvēśvaran\] is the intolerable poison for the bad deeds which are a hurdle in attaining him.

**nalvinaikku** – for bhakti (devotion) which is carried out without expecting any benefit from him

**innamudattinai** – he gives unlimited joy and he is the ultimate goal.

Next he explains as to why he removes our sins and creates an engagement in us, with him.

**pūvinai mēviya . . .** – due to his union with periya pirātti; there is nothing which is more eminent than his being the consort of periya pirātti.

**punmai . . .** – despite having that eminence, since he is together with periya pirātti, he is easily approachable for his followers.

**punmai eḷgādu āvainai mēykkum** – without thinking whether it will be apt for his eminence as the consort of periya pirātti, he tended to cows without considering it as a despicable act.

**vallāyanai** – if even a drop of water (rain) falls on the covherds, he would not tolerate it and hold a mountain as an umbrella for them.

**anṟulagu . . .** – just as he protected one village, he protected this earth.

**ēṟṟai** – he stood proudly after getting the worlds under his divine feet. He considered the benefit thus obtained as his and not as that of the people of the worlds.

**emmānai** – just as sarvēśvaran felt proud and eminent after his victories, āḻvār too felt eminent after losing out to him for such activities of sarvēśvaran’s.

**ejñānṟu talaippeyvanē** – when sarvēśvaran’s activites are like these, āḻvār knows that he is bound to attain him but is not sure as to when. Just as it has been said in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 24-37 “*apīdānīm sa kālassyād*” ((kausalyā dēvi tells śrī rāma) may that time be just now), though kausalyā knows that śrī rāma would come at the end of fourteen years, she could not wait for those fourteen years to end and wished that that day had come then itself. āḻvār too is in the same state.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
