## 90. Talaippeydu yan

*talaippeydu yān un tiruvadic cūdum tagaimaiyināl nilaippeyda ākkaikku nōṟṟa immāyamum māyam sevvē nilaippeydilāda nilaimaiyum kāṇḍōṟasurar kuḻām tolaippeyda nēmi endāy tollai ūḻi surungaladē*

**Пословный перевод:**

asurar – demons’; kuḻām – for their clan; tolai – destruction; peyda – one who made; nēmi – one who has the divine disc; endāy – Oh my svāmy (lord)!; yān – I; talaippeydu – attaining; un tiruvadi – your divine feet; sūdum – donning on my head; tagaimaiyināl – due to the nature; nilaippu eyda – having firmness; ākkaikku – to get the form; nōṟṟa – carrying out good deeds (for many thousands of births); i – this; māyamum – amazement; māyam – amazing form; sevvē – very well (uniform); nilaippeydu ilāda – not having firmness; nilaimaiyum – position; kāṇ dōṟu – whenever I think of; tollai – being ancient; ūḻi – kalpam (brahmā’s lifetime); surungaladu – is not shrinking

**Перевод:**

**Oh my svāmy who has the divine disc and who destroys the clan of demons! I have obtained a form by carrying out some good deeds in the past many thousands of births due to which I could attain you and don your divine feet on my head. When I think of these amazing activities as well as the condition of not reaching you soon enough, I realise that the time to reach you and experience you is extending without any shrinkage.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram āḻvār was lamenting “ejñānṟu talaippeyvanē” (when will I attain you?) sarvēśvaran asked him “Why cannot you remain with the belief that connection with sarvēśvaran will yield the result of uniting with him?” āḻvār says “I could remain like that If I do not have any desire in you, like the samsāris” He says that it is impossible for him to spend time without thinking of sarvēśvaran. What is he saying in this pāsuram? If I think about the facts that I have obtained a physical form which is capable of attaining you and that it is uncertain, due to the unbearability of not being with you, the time to attain you seems to extend forever. Alternatively, since I have obtained a nature by which I cannot sustain myself through any entity other than you, and since this nature came about because of my presence in samsāram, time is not sufficient to think about the fact that this is not uniform. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**talaippeydu** – attaining you

**yān** – I who am remaining without any means

**un tiruvadi** . . . – the amazement that I have obtained a form due to good deeds carried out in many thousands of births earlier; as a result I could sustain that form by attaining your divine feet

**māyam sevvē nilaippeydilāda nilaimaiyum** – due to the amazing nature of this *prakruti* (samsāram), that nature cannot be taken as being firm.

Alternatively,

**talaippeydu . . .** – I approached you and attained the nature of sustaining myself only with nothing else other than your divine feet and the amazement attained due to that.

**ākkaikku nōṟṟa immāyamum** – Just as śrī viṣṇu darmam says “*janmāntara sahasrēśu*” (after taking thousands of births), vouldn’t this nature come in only with carrying out good deeds over several thousands of births? Wouldn’t this nature result only after obtaining such a form? Thus, getting such a form is also a part of bhakti (devotion)

In this thought process, while it appears that carrying out good deeds in the past births is the reason for the present situation, the distinguished nature of donning your divine feet on my head (*tiruvadiccūdum tagaimaiyināl nilaippeyda*) can be a nature primarily only because of the distinction of having *satva guṇam* (only purely good quality) \[the form which the human beings acquire is made of three qualities, viz. *satva, rajas* and *tamas* (purely good, passion and ignorance respectively)\].

**māyam sevvē . . .** – since this form is under the control of three qualities \[as seen above\], the nature spoken of earlier will not come to the surface when the body is under the control of *rajas* or *tamas* component, but will be true only if *satva* component is predominant. Thus, the form is not uniform always and is a cause of amazement.

**kāṇ dōṟu** – whenever this \[feature of three qualities\] is thought of. In other words, it could be said that he keeps thinking of this continuously.

**asurar . . .** – Oh my svāmy who destroyed the clan of demons and who has the divine disc! Am I consoling myself that you are without ability, that you cannot be attained or that I am suffering due to my deeds?

**tollai . . .** – this time which is ancient is not shrinking \[viz., it is not reducing in terms of its length\]; it is extending further. Alternatively, if I keep thinking about this amazing nature, time does not seem to shrink; time is not passing.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 91. Surunguri venney

*surunguṟi veṇṇey toduvuṇḍa kaḷvanai vaiyam muṟṟum orunguṟavuṇḍa peruvayiṟṟāḷanai māvali māttu irunguṟaḷāgi isaiya ōr mūvadi vēṇḍiccenṟa perungiṟiyānai allāl adiyēn nenjam pēṇaladē*

**Пословный перевод:**

surungu – contracted (with the aid of deceptive ropes); uṟi – hoop like structure, hung from ceiling to keep pots etc; veṇṇey – butter \[kept in a pot inside that hoop like structure\]; todu uṇḍa – one who stole and ate; kaḷvanai – one who incarnated as kriṣṇa having the quality of stealing; vaiyam muṟṟum – entire world; orungu – in a small corner; uṟa – fitting well; uṇḍa – swallowed; peru – huge; vayiṟṟāḷanai – having stomach; māvalimāttu – to māvali; iru – huge (small); kuṟaḷāgi – as vāmana \[dwarf\]; isaiya – making (him to) accept; ōr – unique; mūvadi – three steps; vēṇḍi – seeking; senṟa – one who went with a beautiful gait; perum kiṟiyānai allāl – other than the one who is a great means; adiyēn – a servitor only for him; nenjam – mind; peṇaladu – will not like

**Перевод:**

**My heart will not like anyone other than sarvēśvaran who ate butter from the hoop by stealing, who kept all the worlds inside corner of his stomach, who went to mahābali, seeking alms as a dwarf and made him agree to giving him three steps of land and who is a great means \[for attaining himself\].**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had felt miserable about time not shortening \[to attain sarvēśvaran\]. A few samsāris, on seeing this, told him “We are engaged with matters relating to samsāram and are not complaining that time is not lengthening or shortening. We are enjoying the matters relating to samsāram even if time lengthens or shortens. Why don’t you change your involvement from matters relating to bhagavān and engage with worldly matters?”  āḻvār says “Yes, I could do it provided I get a heart which is capable of doing that; my heart knows nothing other than bhagavān. Even this heart I can engage with, provided he had not stolen it” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**surunguṟi veṇṇey** – it was a hoop made with deceptive ropes \[so that its location would not be known easily\].

**veṇṇey toduvuṇḍa** – he stole the butter and ate it, without disturbing the marks which had been made by the owners of butter.

**kaḷvanai** – he stole the concept of stealing itself; thus some people \[who had lost their butter\] said “This could not have been stolen by people; did Gods steal our butter? Did we not keep butter inside that?”

**vaiyam . . .** – while his stomach got filled up with butter, when he swallowed all the worlds \[during deluge\], it did not fill up even half his stomach. The implication here is that he would be able to sustain himself with a material touched by his followers while his mind was not much enthused about protecting the worlds.

**māvali māttu** – he approached and attained a demonic entity; āḻvār says that sarvēśvaran should have attained him who desired only sarvēśvaran and none else.

**irunguṟaḷāgi** – being a huge dwarf \[The word *iru* which means huge is used as a *viparītalakśaṇam* here – meaning exactly the opposite\]. His short stature was such that among the dwarfs of the world, the shortest dwarf would appear like trivikraman in his presence.

**isaiya** – he did all the work to make mahābali to agree to his request; making mahābali’s acceptance to be under his control. Though there were others (sukrācāryar) who did not want mahābali to accede to his request, sarvēśvaran made mahābali to accept.

**ōr mūvadi vēṇḍiccenṟa** – instead of asking for two steps and measuring with both the steps, he asked for three steps and measured with two steps so that he could imprison mahābali with the third step.

**vēṇḍicccenṟa perungiṟiyānai allāl** – he went in such a way that his quality of *sarva nirapēkśam* – ‘not desiring anything’ was not manifested. He went such that mahābali thought “Unless I give him what he wants, he cannot sustain himself”. He hid his quality of *īśvaratvam* – controlling everyone. He went asking for alms to make it appear that one who does not have any desire unfulfilled, came with a desire. He took such a form that it created mercy in mahābali.

**perungiṟiyān** – a hugely clever person.

**adiyēn** – āḻvār is not like mahābali, about to give something. He is not like indra \[head of celestial entities\] too, waiting to obtain one. He is one who has lost out to that activity of sarvēśvaran’s and made himself as a servitor to sarvēśvaran.

**nenjam pēṇaladē** –  he will not desire even the supremacy of the supreme entity, just like hanuman did in śrī rāmāyaṇam uttara kāṇḍam 40-15 “*bhāvō nānyatra gaccati*” (my thoughts will not go to anyone other than you0

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 92. Penalamilla arakkar

*pēṇalamillā arakkar munnīra perum padi vāy nīṇagar nīḷeri vaittu aruḷāy enṟu ninnai viṇṇōr kānilam tōyndu toḻuvar nin mūrtti pal kūṟṟil onṟum kāṇalumāngol enṟē vaigal mālaiyum kālaiyumē*

**Пословный перевод:**

munnīra – surrounded by ocean; perum – expansive; padi – vibhūti (materialistic realm); pēṇ – protecting; nalam illā – not having the benefit; arakaar – inhabited by demons; nīḷ – having the greatness of not being able to be destroyed by anyone; nagar – in lankā; nīḷ – not being burnt by any other entity; eri – fire; vaittu – making use of; aruḷāy – protect (us); enṟu – beseeching; ninnai – you; viṇṇōr – celestial entities; nilam – on earth; kāl tōyndu – keeping their legs firmly; vaigal – every day; kālaiyum mālaiyum – during dawn and dusk; toḻuvar – will worship; nin mūrty – in the beauty of your divine form; pal kūṟṟil – among the many forms; onṟu – at the least one form; kāṇulāmāngol enṟē – is to see (that they worship)? (No)

**Перевод:**

**The celestial entities worship you everyday, both during dawn and dusk, planting their feet firmly on earth, asking you to save them by setting fire to lankā which is inhabited by demons. lankā is a small part of the expansive earth, surrounded by ocean. The demons, who do not have the quality of protection, inhabit it. Are they doing this considering that they would like to see one form among your many divine, beautiful forms? (No, they are doing this to serve their own interests).**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār had said in the previous pāsuram “perungiṟiyānai allāl adiyēn nenjam pēṇaladē”. Here he says that dēvas (celestial entities) who are limited \[by their abilities\] but are higher than samsāris, are not involved even to this extent with sarvēśvaran. Their deficiency is that while we attempt to attain sarvēśvaran by destroying our sense of possessiveness, they try to gain advantages for themselves even if they have to make him transform himself. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**pēṇalam . . . (munnīra perum padiyaip pēṇalamillā arakkar)** –  your realm, composed of many different parts, which is surrounded by ocean.

**nīṇagar . . .** – they will request you to destroy a small part of your realm. Since they know that harming anyone or anything is against the grain of your divine mind, in order to show pity on them, they will use apt words. They will say “Your vibhūti will not survive if they survive”. They will tell sarvēśvaran “They are harming riśis (sages) who are meditating on your divine auspicious qualities, dwelling in one place and celestial entities”.

**pēṇalamillā** – sarvēśvaran considers that protecting others is his benefit. The demons will not think that others should live, as they do.

**pēṇalam . . .** – demons who do not have the benefit of protecting sarvēśvaran’s realm.

**munnīra perum padi vāy nīṇagar** – since that place \[lankā\] is like a refuge for those who fear for their safety, it became the refuge for the entire cruel clan of demons. Since it is well surrounded by ocean on all sides, it is impossible for some to enter it. It is a huge island with the ocean serving as a moat.

**nīṇagar** – the place has been reinforced by the benefits of boons granted by celestial entities.

**nīḷeri vaittaruḷāy** – the celestial entities will beseech sarvēśvaran to use his arrows which are capable of destroying the power granted through their boons. Just as it has been mentioned in tiruvāimoḻi 4-2-8 “*ilangai nagar ambu eri uyttavar*” (fire which was shot off the arrows from śrī rāma), they prayed to sarvēśvaran to burn the entire place.

**ninnai** – you are one who is deeply engrossed in protecting all the worlds. They will shed tears of distress through their eyes to change your mind when you are against destroying a place in your realm.

**viṇṇōr** – these celestial entities do not dwell in the place where nityasūris \[who dwell in paramapadam\] constantly experience sarvēśvaran; they do not dwell on earth where sarvēśvaran comes down, manifesting his simplicity amidst his greatness. They dwell somewhere in between, in svaragam, like an attic.

**kāl nilam tōyndu** – aren’t they the people who will come here, degrading themselves, since this is the place where they do not like to place their feet.

**toḻuvār** – unlike rāvaṇa who said as in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 36-11 “*na namēyam* . .” (Even if I am broken into two pieces, I will not bend before anyone), the celestial entities will stand before you, vorśipping you.

**nin . . .** – are they vorśipping you to get the benefit desired by those who worship you? It is certainly not to experience the divine form that they are vorśipping you. Are they vorśipping you to see the procession of your beautiful form \[as during a festival in temples\] which is one part among the many parts of your divine form?

**anṟiṟē** – they are not like the great people mentioned in stōtra ratnam 56 “*sakrut tvadākāra vilōkanāśayā triṇikrutān uttama bhukti muktibi:*” (the followers who consider their great qualilties as inferior to a blade of grass if they could see your divine form once).

**vaigal mālaiyum kālaiyumē** –  the vorśipping carried out by celestial entities is not for the purpose of seeing your divine form which your followers will worship for; yet they will keep vorśipping you incessantly, for a much longer time  \[in order to destroy the demons and enable them to live peacefully\].

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 93. Kalai veyyorku mun

*kālai veyyōṟkumun ōttuk kodutta kangul kuṟumbar mālai veyyōn pada vaiyagam paravuvar anna kaṇḍum kālai nanjñānattuṟai padindādik kaṇpōdu seydu mālai nannāvil koḷḷār ninaiyār avan maippadiyē*

**Пословный перевод:**

kālai – during dawn; veyyōṟku – for sūriyan (sun); mun – earlier itself; ōttukkodutta – one who ran away; kangul – called as night; kuṟumbar – mischievous person; mālai – during dusk; veyyōn – sūriyan; pada – as he sets; vaiyagam – on earth; paravuvar – will spread; anna – at that type of change in time; kaṇḍum – even after seeing; kālai – during sunrise; nal – (related to bhagavān) being distinguished; jñānam – knowledge; tuṟai – on the banks; padindu – fitting well; ādi – taking a bath; kaṇ – (sensory organs such as) eye; pōdu seydu – blossoming well (such that they do not engage with worldly matters); mālai – sarvēśvaran who is bewildered (with his followers); nal – eminent (in being devoted to praising sarvēśvaran); nāvil – tongue; koḷḷār – those who do not accept; avan – that sarvēśvaran’s; mai – being black like pigment; padi – divine form; ninaiyār – will not keep in their hearts

**Перевод:**

**The mischievous person, called as night, runs before sūriyan rises in the morning. But he returns in the dusk time, once sun sets. Despite seeing time flying away like this, every day, there are people who do not think of sarvēśvaran alone, in the morning, which is when knowledge about sarvēśvaran is full, who do not take a bath on the bank of that knowledge and who do not keep away from engaging with worldly matters through their eyes and other sensory organs. Such people, who do not recite the divine names of sarvēśvaran from their tongue, cannot keep the divine form of that sarvēśvaran, which is dark like a black pigment, in their hearts.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Celestial entities, due to their pride of calling themselves as īśvaran (one who controls everything) have forgotten sarvēśvaran. Are the others vorśipping him? Without making any distinction between one and the other, for everyone, it is only the worldly matters which are of interest. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**kālai . . .** – when sūriyan rises in the morning with his powerful rays, he drives away darkness; during night, darkness overpowers sūriyan. Despite seeing this activity, just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 105-24 “*nandanti udita ādityē nandanti astamitē ravau*” (people are delighted when the sun rises and are happy when the sun sets), they feel happy when it dawns so that they could go in search of worldly matters to feed their sensory organs; they are again happy when sun sets since the time has come to experience worldly matters without others knowing about them.

**kālai . . .** – it is the time of dawn. The mischievous people such as night will run away from sūriyan who rises at that time, since his rays fall on them. When it is dusk, they will destroy the mighty and powerful sūriyan and take away his expansive kingdom. They will make earth to be ecstatic since the time to sleep arrives.

**anna kaṇḍum** – Despite seeing this happen every day, since *bhagavat viśayam* (matters relating to sarvēśvaran) does not yield itself to sensory organs, they become ignorant of it and perish. Even though *bhagavat viśyam* is not visible to the eyes etc, it is strange that they are not able to perceive it with their minds too.

**kālai** – it is that time of the day when *satva* (good) matters rise to the surface.

**nan jñānam** – if it is knowledge, it cannot be related to anything other than *bhagavat viśayam*. The vyākyātā cites the following references to stress this:

mudal tiruvandādi 67 “*oṇḍāmaraiyāḷ kēḷvan oruvanaiyē nōkkum uṇarvu*” – one’s mind will look at the consort of śrī mahālakśmi alone.

śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 6-5-87 “*tat jñānam ajñānamatōnyadu:kam* ” – only that which leads to knowing about sarvēśvaran is referred to as knowledge. All the rest are ignorance only.

śrī viṣṇu purāṇam 1-9-41 “*vidyānyāśilpanaipuṇam*” – faculties other than knowledge do not bestow mōkśam (liberation from samsāram) on ātmā.

There is no distinction between knowing other \[worldly\] matters and sewing rag clothes.

**tuṟai padindādi** – entering this water called as *jñānam* (knowledge) and taking a bath

**kaṇ pōdu seydu** – *pōdu seydal*  refers to closing like buds as well as blossoming. When worldly mattes are seen, the eyes will close like buds.They will blossom when the matters are related to sarvēśvaran are seen.

**mālai** – All that is required to be done is to keep constantly meditating on sarvēśvaran; once this is done, sarvēśvaran will come on his own and fall on us head over heels; he is to that extent infatuated with us.

**nal nāvil** –  *nal nā* refers to avoiding all the unwanted worldly matters and talking about matters relating to sarvēśvaran. āḻvār says that despite being provided with a tongue which is apt to speak about sarvēśvaran, the samsāris are going for outside \[worldly\] matters. śrī viṣṇu darmam – 1 says *sā jihvā* – tongue is there only to speak about him.

**ninaiyār avan meyppadiyē** – no one thinks about the divine form \[of sarvēśvaran’s\] which cannot be given up even if he does only harmful activities towards us instead of being infatuated with us and carrying out benevolent activities. sūrppaṇakā says in śrī rāmāyaṇam, after looking at the divine form of śrī rāma \[and lakśmaṇa} *taruṇau* – youthful entities;  despite being severed of her nose and ears by śrī rāma, this is how she described to rāvaṇa of their physical beauty. Other than being filled with happiness on seeing their divine forms, for what did she have to describe them this way, despite being harmed by them?

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 94. Maippadi meniyum

*maippadi mēniyum sendāmaraikkaṇṇum vaidikarē meyppadiyāl un tiruvadi sūdum tagaimiyinār eppadi ūrāmilaikkak kuruttāmilaikkum ennum appadi yānum sonnēn adiyēn maṟṟu yādu enbanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vaidikarē – those devotees who know your true nature as mentioned in vēdas (sacred texts); mai – black pigment; padi – having its nature; mēniyum – divine form; sem – reddish; tāmarai – like lotus flower; kaṇṇum – having eyes; un – your; tiruvadi – divine feet; sūdum – to be donned on their heads; tagaimiyinār – have the nature; Ur – approaching the town; A – cows; eppadi – how; milaikka – moo (sound made by cows; also called as ‘low’); kuruttu A – blind cow; milaikkum – will also moo (like the former); ennum appadi – just like the idiom used in the world; yānum – I too; sonnēn – said (about you); adiyēn – I, in a devotional trance; maṟṟu – beyond that; yādenban – what will I say?

**Перевод:**

**You have a divine form which is dark in complexion like black pigment and reddish, divine eyes which are like lotus flower. Only those people who know your true nature as mentioned in vēdas, are qualified to don your divine feet truly on their heads. When cows return home after grazing during the day, the cows will moo \[in anticipation of seeing their calves\]. Hearing this, a blind cow too will moo. In the same way, I too said a few words about you. What else can I, who am in devotional trance, say beyond this?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) sarvēśvaran asked āḻvār “You say that since celestial entities have a pride that they are eminent and since samsāris are ignorant, they lose out on bhagavat viśayam (matters relating to sarvēsvaran). Do you have any deficiency?” āḻvār responds “You have created a deficiency in me. You had showered faultless knowledge as well as devotion. You showered that knowledge with devotion. Due to the devotional trance, in my state of bewilderment, I did not praise you”. In tiruvāimoḻi decad 1-5 vaḷavēḻ, āḻvār had said that due to his being without any qualification, he would not say anything. This is similar to that. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings.*

**maippadimēniyum** – divine form which is like black pigment. The divine form which is soothing on the eyes of the beholder. vyākyātā narrates an incident which occurred during the time of bhattar (son of kūrattāḻvān). bhattar was explaining this pāsuram. piḷḷai viḻuppararaiyar and āppān tiruvaḻundūraraiyar were in the audience. bhattar said for this verse “The divine form which causes infatuation in the hearts of those who think about sarvēśvaran” nanjīyar, bhattar’s disciple quoted periya tirumoḻi 10-10-9 “*innār enṟu aṟiyēn*” (I do not who this entity is, who is holding the divine disc and divine conch). bhattar asked as to who made that remark. The araiyars said that it was nanjīyar who made the remark. bhattar prasied him, saying “We did not think of that. You were born in a place which is hundreds of miles away from here and you are explaining this to us so well!”

**sendāmaraikkaṇṇum** – the divine eyes which bring out the inner, hidden, auspicious qualities.

**vaidikar . . .** – here, the term *vaidikar* refers to one who is firmly immersed in the first part of vēdas \[the first part of vēdas deals with deeds to be carried out and the second or end part of vēdas deals with the supreme entity\]; they will involve with the cluster of deeds to be carried out for various purposes and will not meditate on sarvēśvaran’s *svarūpam* (true nature), *rūpam* (divine form), *aiśvaryam* (wealth) etc. Alternatively, just as bruhadāraṇyaka upaniśat 4-4-5 says “*nidityāsitavya*:”(constantly meditating on the auspicious qualities of sarvēśvaran, without a break) – those who are with unchanged devotion towards sarvēśvaran.Unlike those who are involved with carrying out deeds and are clear in their minds, these people will be in a state of bewilderment since they keep thinking constantly of sarvēśvaran’s auspicious qualities. Only such people have the ability of holding on to your divine feet, truly.

sarvēśvaran asks of āḻvār “That may be so. If only such people are apt to attain our divine feet, how did you speak about us?” As a response to that, āḻvār says

**eppadi . . .** – when cows return home in the evening, after grazing, they will make a loud noise, called as mooing. Not knowing as to why they are mooing, even a blind cow will make a similar noise.

**appadi yānum sonnēn** – without thinking “Who am I to speak about sarvēśvaran when only knowledgeable people can speak about him” I too spoke a few words about you, like those blind cows.

**adiyēn maṟṟu yādu enbanē** – am I, in a state of devotional trance towards you, capable of speaking clearly about you and completing it? Alternatively, am I, leaving you after speaking in an inapt way about you, capable of speaking anything matching your auspicious qualities and completing it?

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 95. Yadanum or akkaiyil

*yādānum ōr ākkaiyil pukku angāppuṇḍum āppaviḻndum mūdāviyil tadumāṟum uyir munnamē adanāl yādānum paṟṟi nīngum viradattai nal vīdu seyyum mātāvinaip pituvai tirumālai vaṇanguvanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

uyir – jīvātmā (soul); munnamē – from time immemorial; yādānum ōr – in something or other; ākkaiyil – physical form; pukku – entering; angu – in that form; āppu uṇḍum – getting tied down to it (due to prārabda karma (past deed which has started yielding result)); āppu aviḻndum – getting liberated from it (due to destruction of prārabda karma); mūdu – being ancient; āviyil – in the subtle form; tadumāṟum – will keep fluctuating; adanāl – due to that repeated act; yādānum – some matter (related to prākrutam, the causative matter); paṟṟi – holding on to; nīngum – moving away (from matter related to sarvēśvaran); viradattai – (my) vow; nal vīdu seyyum – one who liberated me; mātāvinai – being affectionate like mother; pituvai – being well-intentioned like father; tirumālai – the consort of śrī mahālakśmi; vaṇanguvan – I will attain

**Перевод:**

**jīvātmā (soul) has been, from time immemorial, entering some physical form, getting tied down to it, and at one point of time, getting liberated from it. It would then enter a subtle form and keep going to svargam and naragam. With that bondage (to that form) as the reason, it has been holding on to some matter or the other related to worldly pursuits and leaving matters related to sarvēścvaran. However, sarvēśvaran made me give up my firm vow (to keep vacillating among these sates, mentioned here); he has been like a mother to me, being affectionate, and he has been like a father to me, being well-intentioned about me. I will attain such sarvēśvaran.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Instead of leaving sarvēśvaran showing his inadequacy in either his devotional trance or inaptness in meditating on him, he bowed down to the divine feet of sarvēśvaran since he created engagement in āḻvār to be fully devoted to matters relating to him and distaste in worldly matters. vyākyātā narrates an event regarding nanjīyar with reference to this pāsuram. When parāśara bhattar was in tirukkōttiyūr, one śrīvaiṣṇava, rāmānujadāsar, approached him and asked him to mercifully explain tiruviruttam to him. bhattar told him “Due to my separation from perumāḷ (periya perumāḷ, śrī ranganātan, at śrīrangam) I am in a state of distress and I am not in a frame of mind to say anything”. Turning to nanjīyar, bhattar told him “You please explain to him”. nanjīyar also started explaining tiruviruttam to him. Another person, vaḷavan pallavataraiyan, joined them in this. He was a follower of tirukkōttiyūr nambi (one of the ācāryars of śrī rāmānujar).  When this pāsuram was about to be explained, vaḷavan pallavataraiyar was in tears. Looking at this, nanjīyar asked him “I have not started explaining this pāsuram. Why are you in this state already?” pallavataraiyar replied “When tirukkōttiyūr nambi was instructing me about tiruviruttam, he had mercifully told me to recite this pāsuram in front of sarvēśvaran everyday. I was reminiscing that”.  Hearing this, nanjīyar asked him “Could you recollect a few words which tirukkōttiyūr nambi had mercifully told you about this pāsuram?” pallavataraiyar said “I do not remember that. All that I remember is reciting this pāsuram” nanjīyar felt very happy that tirukkōttiyūr nambi liked this pāsuram so much and spoke in praise of that for a few hours. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**yādānum ōr ākkaiyil pukku** – entering some *śarīram* (physical form), and praising it as *mamāyam dēha*: (this is an amazing form), even if it is a lowly form. Having taken such a form, due to firm bondage with it, an inability to leave that form and getting involved with it fully. Since it is referred to as *ākkai*, as per the root of the word *dēham* which is *diha upacayē*, dēham attains *upacayam* or growth. Thus, it keeps growing.

**pukku** – for *cētana* (sentient entity), attachment to an insentient entity is not his own nature but comes out of *karma* (previous deeds). Thus, ātmā, a *cētana* entity, enters a physical form which is *acēta* entity.

**angāppuṇḍum** – due to his \[*cētana*’s\] association with previous deeds and being habituated to it, he will get bonded to the physical forms. Despite knowing through the words of an ācāryan (teacher) or through śāstras (religious texts) and through direct experience, that these \[the physical forms) are lowly, wondering as to what he will do if he were to give up his forms, he will be unable to give them up.

**āppaviḻndum** – not knowing that his bondage with the physical form is due to his karma, he will be unable to get rid of it; once karma gets destroyed, the physical form will get annihilated.

**mūdāviyil** – through this, he is reminiscing early times. The term *āvi* refers to *prāṇan* – vital  air. Since prāṇan is distinct from acit, the subtle form is now referred to as *āvi*. This is considered as an activity of *prakruti* (primordial matter) and also as a part of *prakruti*. This will keep following until liberation. It will help the cētana in his movements and non-movements.

**tadumāṟum** – as described in *pancāgni vidyai* (the journey of ātmā through five stages in its repeated cyle of births), the jīvātmā will enter clouds \[after svargam/naragam\], enter earth through rains, enter foodfrains,  enter a cētana’s form through intake of food and enter the womb of a lady during their union. In all these states, he keeps stumbling without being able to stay firmly in any place. During his journey, there will be places which are known as well as unknown to him.

**uyir** – the ātmā, which is full of knowledge and full of joy, and is permanent, suffers like this.

From when has this stumbling and existing been like this?

**munnamē** – this has been there from time immemorial.

**adanāl** – since the association (with acētana) has been there for a very long time, he thinks that the acētana entity is his guide.

**yādānum paṟṟi** – thinking that only matters other than those related to sarvēśvaran should be attained; accepting all such matters without analysing their virtues and faults.

**nīngum** – only matters relating to bhagavāṇ are to be given up.

**viradattai** – even if some people instruct “Whatever you are holding on to are faulty. Only matters related to bhagavān are apt to be held on to” and even if what they say are correct, I have taken a vow that I will give them up \[matters relating to bhagavān\]. Just as sarvēśvaran has taken a vow, as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 18-33 “*ētat vratam mama*” (even if the person who comes to me is my enemy, I will not give him up; this is my vow), the jīvātmā has taken a vow that he will give up sarvēśvaran; just as rāvaṇa had said in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 36-11 “*na namēyam*” (even if I am split into two, I will not bow to someone; my body will only fall backward).

**nal vīdu seyyum** – removing the present situation and making me pray, as in tiruviruttam 1 *inninṟa nīrmai inyām uṟāmai* – holding his divine feet and asking him to relieve me from this samsāram. Before developing an involvement with him, severing my interest in matters related to vordly pursuits.

**mātāvinaip pituvai** – the mother and father, who are not really our mother and father, are responsible for this physical form and for our being involved with samsāram. However, sarvēśvaran is the one who relieves us from this. What is the need to mention both?

**tirumālai** – since he is the consort of śrī mahālakśmi,  as said in mahābhāratam *pitā mātā ca mādava*: – both father and mother are mādavan, the consort of śrī mahālakśmi, he is mentioning both.

**vaṇanguvanē** – it is not possible for us to carry out a beneficial act to sarvēśvaran matching  his beneficial act towards us in creating a distaste in us for worldly matters and affection towards him. The only action that we can do is to submit the entity which was his, always (ātmā).

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 96. Vanangum turaigal

*vaṇangum tuṟai palapalavākki madivigaṟpāl piṇangum samayangaḷ palapalavākki avai avaidōṟu āṇangum palapalavākki nin mūrtti parappi vaittāy iṇangu ninnōrai illāy ninkaṇ vētkai eḻuvippanē*

**Пословный перевод:**

vaṇangum – to attain (other deities); pala pala – many; tuṟaigaḷ – various deities who instruct; ākki – created; madi vigaṟpāl – due to difference in intellect; piṇangum – disagreeing; palapala – of many types; samayam – philosophies; ākki – created; avaiyavaidōṟu – among all those philosophies; ninmūrtti – as your forms; palapala – of many types; aṇangum – deities; ākki – created as being apt to be attained; parappi vaittāy – you created expansively; iṇangu – (with those deities) appearing to be a match; ninnōrai – those who are an equal to you; illāy – Oh, one who doesn’t have!; ninkaṇ – towards you; vētkai – desire; eḻuvippanē – I will create

**Перевод:**

**You have created many different paths for people to attain their goals; you created many different philosophies due to differences in the levels of intellect and deities, who are all part of your form, for those philosophies. Even though those deities are a part of you, you have none who is equal to you. I will create desire in the minds of samsāris, towards you.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) In the previous pāsuram, āḻvār had submitted the ātmā which belongs to sarvēśvaran back to him since he could not match the benefit that sarvēśvaran had carried out for him. The benefit that sarvēśvaran had done for āḻvār was that he had stopped the interest in worldly pursuits and created interest in matters related to him, when āḻvār was unable to seclude himself without speaking about it or could not remain without speaking either (about these two – refraining from worldly pursuits and engaging with matters relating to bhagavān). In this pāsuram, he says that all the ingredients to attain sarvēśvaran are not available here. Since you \[refers to sarvēśvaran\] have given up, does it mean that I too \[refers to āḻvār\], who am capable of carrying out your tasks, should give them up? When all the cētanas \[sentient entities\] were lying like acētana entities during the time of deluge, you had granted the qualities which are exclusive to cētanas and the knowledge to choose between what is to be acquired and what is to be discarded. The reason for granting them this was for them to decide as to what is the end goal for them and how to attain that. However, since that knowledge is common with engaging with worldly pursuits and being firmly rooted in them, due to their association with karmas (deeds, both good and bad) since time immemorial, with that knowledge these cētanas have been engaging with worldly pursuits. Thus, since you made them engage with worldly pursuits, your situation has become similar to a mother who was unable to prevent her child from falling into the well and was accused of having pushed that child into the well. Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

sarvēśvaran asked āḻvār “You are saying that I had pushed these cētanas in samsāram. What can I do for it?” āḻvār responds . .

**vaṇangum tuṟai . . .** – samsāris have the qualities of *rajas* (passion) and *tamas* (ignorance) in them.  When they get an involvement based on these qualities, they would be told to attain the deity which is predominant for those qualities, by instructors who were created by you. The word *tuṟai* refers to sacred water and through that, it refers to celestial entities.

**madi . . .** – due to bewilderment in their knowledge as well as differences with one another, when one person enunciates his philosophy, another person takes it as something which opposes his philosophy. You created many such philosophies to cater to such feelings of mistrust and misunderstanding.

**avai avai . . .** – you created many deities who are spoken of highly in those philosophies.

**nin mūrty parappi vaittāy** – just as you had mentioned in bhagavat gītā 7-21 “*yōyō yāmyām tanumbhaktyā*” (for whatever reasons someone worships whichever deities …), you created many deities who realise their powers from you. Just as the saying goes “*paisal kaisunōy mukkaisu*” (even the small sufferings of a child are serious diseases), you had created lack of knowledge in these cētanas, you created those who tell such cētanas that attaining only lowly deities are apt for them and you also created pāsurams (hymns) in those śāstras (sacred texts) extolling those deities and you created the deities too.

**iṇangu ninnōrai illāy** – if you create a maze of such philosophies and deities for those philosophies, there is none through whom these could be  avoided. There is none who could stand equalling you, none who is like you. On hearing this, sarvēśvaran asked āḻvār “That may be. What are you going to do now?”

**nin kaṇ vētkai eḻuvippanē** – I will stop these activities of yours which you are carrying out due to your unlimited independence; I will stop them \[samsāris\] from experiencing the results of their activities which they are indulging in due to the faults in their knowledge; I will make them run towards you with desire, just like me. This has arisen out of āḻvār getting faultless knowledge even while he was in samsāram.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 97. Eluvadum minde

*eḻuvadum mīṇḍē paduvadum pattu enai ūḻigaḷ pōyk kaḻivadum kaṇḍu kaṇḍu eḷgalallāl imaiyōrgaḷ kuḻām toḻuvadum sūḻvadum sey tollai mālaik kaṇṇārak kaṇḍu kaḻivadōr kādal uṟṟārkkum uṇḍo kaṇgaḷ tunjudalē*

**Пословный перевод:**

eḻuvadum – the way of creating; mīṇḍē – the same way; paduvadum – the way of perishing; pattu – being born; enai ūḻigaḷ – for a very long time; pōy – passing all these; kaḻivadum – (later) perishing; kaṇḍu kaṇḍu – seeing repeatedly; eḷgalallāl – other than despising; imaiyōrgaḷ – nityasūris’; kuḻām – their gathering; toḻuvadum – vorśipping; sūḻvadum – going in circumambulation; sey – carrying out; tollai – ancient; mālai – sarvēśvaran; kaṇ Ara – such that eyes get satisfied; kaṇḍu – worship; kaḻivadu – such that time passes; ōr – being unique; kādal – due to desire; uṟṟārkkum – those who are fitting aptly; kaṇgaḷ – their eyes; tunjudal – closing (sleeping); uṇḍō – could it happen?

**Перевод:**

**Other than giving up the repeated cycles of taking birth and perishing after sometime, for those who desire to see to their eyes’ content, sarvēśvaran who is surrounded and vorśipped by nityasūris, will hurdles such as closing the eyes (sleeping) occur?**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) sarvēśvaran tells āḻvār “You had said in tiruviruttam 91 perungiṟiyānai allāl adiyēn nenjam pēṇaladē and in the previous pāsuram nin kaṇ vētkai eḻuvippanē, appearing very worried. If you keep meditating on these aspects, you will keep feeling distressed, unavoidably. If you look at other samsāris you would find that they are totally devoted to worldly pursuits, enjoying themselves, spending their time eating and sleeping. Could you not follow their practice, forgetting about me and engaging with worldly pursuits?” āḻvār says “They are indulging in all these, since they do not know you” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**eḻuvadum . . .** – there are two interpretations. First interpretation: either being born and perishing immediately or being born, living for sometime and perishing. Just as it has been mentioned in cāndōjña upaniśat 5-108 “*jāyasva mriyasva*” (being born and dying) some people die within moments of being born while some are born at the time of creation and die at the time of deluge. To āḻvār, both appear identical.

**kaṇḍu kaṇḍu eḷgal allāl** – if one were to analyse these, they will only appear to be fit to be ridiculed. Would anyone give up his/her involvement with you? \[asks āḻvār of sarvēśvaran\]. *eḷgai* is despising/ridiculing. If one looks at the fault in this repeated cycle of taking birth and perishing, one would feel detachment towards them.

Second interpretation: **eḻuvadum . . .** – repeated rising and setting \[of the sūriyan, sun\]. Even if one were to see these for many days

**eḷgalallāl** – instead of being involved with you, is your matter such that we could move away from you? Here, *eḷgai* refers to being involved. The implication here is that having developed desire for you and having waited for such a long time, instead of waiting patiently for the remaining time too, to attain you, could we say “We have been waiting for a long time; there is no sign of attaining sarvēśvaran; hence we will move away”? If there are faults in your matters, it may be apt to give you up. When you are distinctly superior to everything and since there is a natural relationship between us, can we leave? Reference is made to tiruvāimoḻi 2-5-4 “*eppoḻudum nāḷ tingaḷ . . .*” (sarvēśvaran is new to his followers, every second, month, year, epoch etc)

**imaiyōr . . .** – sarvēśvaran is one who has been constantly vorśipped and followed by the gathering of nityasūris who cannot keep their mind on anyone other than you, just as it has been mentioned in mahābhāratam mōkśa parvam 16-4 “*nityānjaliputā hruśtā*:” (feeling happy, with joined palms) and in śrī bhagavat gītā 11-40 “*nama*: *purastād ata pruśtatas tē*” (offering salutations from the front and from the rear) and considering this to be the sole purpose of their existence.

**kaṇṇārak kaṇḍu** – just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam sundara kāṇḍam 40-3 “*gātrai: śōkābhikarśitai:*” (sītāppirātti tells hanuman ‘with my limbs emaciated by grief, I wish to touch that śrī rāma, the tiger among men’) the desire will be satiated only if sarvēśvaran is seen. In other words, only that desire will get fulfilled on seeing him. What comes subsequently is the strong affection towards him which follows the jīvātmā’s nature of being subservient to him.

**uṟṟārkkum** – for those who have that affection firmly.

**uṇḍō . . .** – we have heard that the fully independent perumāḷ (śrī rāma) had slept; however, have we ever heard that iḷayaperumāḷ (lakśmaṇa) who followed him to the forest, had ever slept? Have we ever heard before that those who are involved with sarvēśvaran had closed their eyes \[slept\]?

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 98. Tunja munivarum

*tunjā munivaraum allādavarum tudara ninṟa enjāpppiṟavi idar kadivān imaiyōr tamakkum tan sārvilāda tanipperu mūrtty tan māyam sevvē nenjāl ninaipparidāl veṇṇey ūṇ ennum īnaccollē*

**Пословный перевод:**

enjā – without contraction; piṟavi – happened in samsāram; idar – sorrow; kadivān – to remove; tunjā – not having contraction; munivarum – eminent sages such as sanaka et al; allādavarum – others such as brahmā et al; tudara – to follow; ninṟa – one who remained mercifully; than – for himself; sārvu ilāda – one who does not have (another entity) as protection; tani – being unique; peru – without any limitation; mūrtti than – sarvēśvaran, who has such a nature, his; veṇṇey – butter; ūṇ – ate (by stealing); ennum sol – word such as this; māyam – only amazement; imaiyōr tamakkum – even for nityasūris who do not have any contraction in their knowledge; sevvē – very well; nenjāl – through mind; ninaippu aridu – will be impossible to think of

**Перевод:**

**sarvēśvaran is one who is vorśipped and followed by eminent sages such as sanaka et al who do not have any contraction in their knowledge as well as by other samsāris such as brahmā et al, in order to remove the sorrows which happen due to samsāram; he does not have anyone to protect him; he is unique and does not have any limitation. The amazing activity which is indicated by the words “He stole butter and ate” is impossible even for nityasūris to think of.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Some people told āḻvār “You are constantly involved with sarvēśvaran and are speaking about it, you are trying to correct others through your experiences and make them to turn their heads towards sarvēśvaran; you are feeling distressed due to all these. Instead of doing this, since kriṣṇāvatāram is very dear to you, why do you not spend your time meditating on the narration of kriṣṇa stealing butter?” They do not know the extent of his involvement in such matters. In tiruvāimoḻi 1-3-1 “ettiṟam” did he not remain unconscious for months together, reminiscing about sarvēśvaran’s act of stealing butter and getting beaten up by yaśōdāppirātti, after being tied down to a mortar? āḻvār says that one cannot even think about such an act \[of sarvēśvaran’s\] Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**enjāppiṟavi idar kadivān tunjā munivararum allādavarum tudara ninṟa** – *tunjā munivar* is one who is similar to the one mentioned in śrī bhagavat gītā 2-69 ”*nisā sarvabhūtānām*” (when it is night time for all living beings, the enlightened will be awake). In the earlier pāsurams, āḻvār had said “My mind will not go after anyone other than sarvēśvaran” “Sleep will not befall those who are involved with matters relating to sarvēśvaran” “I will make all the sāmsāris to be involved with him like I am”. Some people, who heard him, told him “Why are you getting distressed like this, thinking of others? īsn’t kriṣṇāvatāram very dear to you? Don’t you keep reminiscing his act of steaing butter? Why don’t you continue to think about that?” āḻvār responds saying “If I think of other activities of sarvēśvaran, I might probably exist. However, I cannot think of the depth of this particular activity; I cannot sustain myself too; thus instead of thinking of this activity and getting caught, it would be better not to think of it”

**tunjā munivarum** – just as samsāris are immersed in worldly pursuits such as *śabdam* etc (the five senses such as sound etc), eminent sages such as sanaka et al are immersed in matters related to ātmā and īśvaran, and they are without the qualities of *rajas* (passion) and *tamas* (ignorance). Just as it has been said in śrī bhagavat gītā 2-69 *yā nisā* – when all the other living beings sleep during night, they will be awake. Though sarvēśvaran had created them in samsāram, due to their eminent deeds over many births, they have developed detachment towards samsāram and are mumukśus (those desirous of *mōkśam*, liberation). They are such eminent entities that looking at their nature, samsāram would decide “They are not the people for us; we will seek out those who are apt for us”

**allādavarum** – unlike such eminent entities as sanaka et al, due to variations in their virtues and vices, these people will be under the control of their karma (past deeds), being celestial entities such as brahmā et al. They will have a mixture of karmabhāvanai and brahmabhāvanai (activities based on their karma and involvement with sarvēśvaran respectively). When their satva quality comes to the fore, they will attain the mode of brahmabhāvanai  and on those few occasions they will think “samsāram is to be given up; only sarvēśvaran should be attained”. They will take efforts to attain sarvēśvaran during those occasions. During all the other times, they will be like samsāris only.

**enjāppiṟavi** – in order to avoid the sorrows caused by the unending births which keep coming without any contraction

**tunjā munivarum allādādavarum enjāppiṟavi idar kadivān tudara ninṟa** – since this sorrow cannot be cured by samsāri, since he has no strength on his own and since it has been coming from time immemorial, he has to attain an omnipotent entity at an opportune time.

**than sārvilāda** – while brahmā et al attain sarvēśvaran in order to get rid of their sorrows, sarvēśvaran does not have an entity who he has to attain.

**imaiyōr tamakkum sevvē nenjāl ninapparidāl** – those in samsāram, despite knowing that they are engaged with lowly activites, do not have the capability to give them up; but, nityasūris are those who constantly experience and enjoy sarvēśvaran. However, there is an amazing activity which even the nityasūris cannot think of. What is that activity? sarvēśvaran, who is the refuge for brahmā et al, who has no unfulfilled desire in him, treats those materials which have a connection with his followers \[gōpikās, the covherd girls in śrī gōkulam\] as his sustenance. If he cannot get such a material directly, he will obtain it even by stealing and will eat it. During the act of stealing, he will ensure that he is caught redanded; he will allow himself to be tied down to a mortar and get beaten up for that; his divine form will get drained of its complexion and become pale and he will remain in a state of fear. Is it possible for anyone to even think of this activity? It will be a source of amazement even to sarvēśvaran himself.

The vyākyātā narrates an incident from the life of piḷḷai uṟangāvillidāsar (a disciple of bhagavad rāmānujar). A person stole the milk kept for the king and drank it up. He was caught by the guards, tied to a tree and was being beaten up. dāsar was going that way when he saw this incident. On enquiring, he was told as to what had happened. When he heard this, he was reminded of kriṣṇa stealing milk, getting caught, tied to a mortar and beaten up for it. He became emotional on thinking of these incidents. He told the guards “Let him go; I will take responsibility for his action”

**sevvē nenjāl** – a few people, such as śiśupālan et al could narrate a few words about sarvēśvaran on the basis of their enmity with him. However, it is not possible to narrate his auspicious qualities.

The pāsuram should be rearranged to read like this to get sequential meaning:

*tunjā munivarum allādavarm enjāppiṟavi idar kadivān tudara ninṟa than sārvilāda tanipperu mūrtti udaiya veṇṇey ūṇ ennum īnaccollāna mayāmaānadu imaiyōr tamakkum sevvē nenjāl ninaipparidāl*

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 99. Inac collayinumaga

*īnaccollāyinumāga eṟitirai vaiyam muṟṟum ēnatturuvāy idanda pirān irum kaṟpagam sēr vānattavarkkum allādavarkkum maṟṟellāyavarkkum jñānappirānai allāl illai nān kaṇḍa nalladuvē*

**Пословный перевод:**

īnaccol – (my) word is without any benefit; āyinum – even if it is; āga – let it be; eṟi – throwing up; tirai – having waves; vaiyam muṟṟum – all the worlds; ēnattu uruvāy – with the form of a wild boar; idanda – one who pierced and removed (from nether world); pirān – as benefactor; jñānam – having complete knowledge; pirānai allāl – other than śrīman nārāyaṇa, the lord; irum – having the greatness (of fulfilling wishes made under it); kaṟpagam – the \[celestial\] wish-fulfilling tree, kaṟpagam; sēr – desirous of reaching it; vānattavarkkum – for celestial entities; allādavarkkum – nityasūris, who are distinct from the celestial entities; maṟṟu ellāyavarkkum – other entities such as manuśya (human) et al; illai – there is no other means to uplift; nān – I; kaṇḍa – ascertained; nalladu – benefit (is only this)

**Перевод:**

**Even if my words are without any benefit, let them be so. I would still say this. There is no means other than śrīman nārayaṇan, who in the form of a wild boar, pierced and protected all the worlds which have oceans with rising and agitating waves. This is true for celestial entities who are desirous of reaching the celestial tree kaṟpagam; this is true for nityasūris, who are different from the celestial entities; this is true for other creations such as humans, animals etc. This is my ascertained view.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) Seeing this āḻvār deeply involved with bhagavat viśayam (matters relating to sarvēśvaran) and falling unconscious, śrīvaiṣṇavas told him “Due to your involvement with bhagavat viśayam, we are unable to determine when you will fall unconscious and when you will be in normal state. We are unable to have complete faith in you because of that”. The vyākyātā narrates a few incidents about those who are deeply engaged with sarvēśvaran, falling unconscious. He starts with the incident from śrī rāmāyaṇam when bharata had swooned, remaining unconscious, and kausalyā dēvi is trying to wake him up, as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 87-9 “adya rāja kulasya ..” (the life of this royal ikśvāku clan is dependent on you). Just as people will get perturbed when the stone used by an alchemist (one who converts iron into gold) falls on the ground, the queen mothers were worried on seeing bharatan falling down unconscious \[on hearing about the departure of śrī rāma for the forest\]. They tell him “We thought that we would be alive, treating you as the leader of this kingdom.  The cakravarty (daśarata, the emperor) has passed away, śrī rāma has left for the forest. We are alive because of you. We are holding on to our lives in the hope that śrī rāma and the other two will return after seeing the sorrow on your face. If you are not there, will they even look in this direction? ” Just as it has been mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 87-9 “putra vyādi na tē  kaścit śarīram paribādatē” they ask him “Oh child! I hope that no ailment is afflicting your body” śrī rāmāyaṇam ayōdyā kāṇḍam 59-4 says “api vrukśā:” (even trees were withering due to the sorrow of śrī rāma leaving them) – thus, when everyone in ayōdyā was in distress, bharatan fell down unconscious suddenly  as a result of which his mothers had to ask him “Oh son! What is your disease?” Since they have only satva guṇa (the quality of goodness) it is difficult to know as to when they will swoon and when they will be aware. āḻvār is one who will remain unconscious for six months, just reminiscing kriṣṇa’s quality of saulabhyam (easy to approach). The next incident involves kūrattāḻvān (disciple of bhagavat rāmānujar). Whenever kūrattāḻvān starts his discourse on tiruvāimoḻi, piḷḷai uṟangāvilli dāsar would become very emotional; shedding tears copiously, he would speak about sarvēśvaran’s auspicious qualities. Seeing this, kūrattāḻvān would tell him “Unlike us who are well versed in śāstrams, having learnt śrī bhāśyam etc, your crumbling nature on thinking about the auspicious qualities of bhagavān is incredible. Your birth is indeed a great one!” The same kūrattāḻvān was once narrating periya tirumoḻi pāsuram 3-7-1 “kaḷvankol yānaṟiyēn”, keeping a little girl on his lap. In this pāsuram the mother of parakāla nāyaki (tirumangai āḻvār in feminine mood) would be wailing about sarvēśvaran having taken away her daughter. Just then, the husband of that little girl came there to take her home. Seeing this and reminiscing the pāsuram, kūrattāḻvān fell unconscious. Such kūrattāḻvān was telling piḷḷai uṟangāvilli dāsar that he had a strong heart while dāsar was having a soft heart due to which he was crumbling on merely thinking of the auspicious qualities of sarvēśvaran. When nanjīyar was still an advaiti, tirumalaidāsar, a śrīvaiṣṇava, told him about the greatness of parāśara bhattar and made him to attain the divine feet of parāśara bhattar. One day, the two of them were sitting inside śrīrangam temple, learning tiruvāimoḻi. kūrattāḻvān happened to pass that way. Seeing him, nanjīyar started to rise. kūrattāḻvān signalled to him to sit and asked him “While samsāris will spend their time eating, is this what you people are doing?” nanjīyar responded saying “We have learnt tiruvāimoḻi at the divine feet of parāśara bhattar” emperumānār (bhagavad rāmānujar) would spend the day time in writing / teaching śrī bhāśyam. At night, before going to bed, he would ask his disciples to learn santai of tiruvāimoḻi (a mode of learning where the ācāryan (teacher) would recite a verse once and the siśyan (disciple) would repeat it twice/thrice). When he starts reciting a verse first time, he will be very much involved in it and will be elated. When he repeats it, he would crumble and shed tears continuously, requiring several towels to wipe them. When prasādam (food offered to periya perumāḷ) would be taken to periya perumāḷ’s sannidi, he would leave instruction with those who are offering the prasādam to show him the prasādam items before as well as after offering to perumāḷ. Not realising why he had told them this way, they would show him the prasādam items, both before and after offering to periya perumāḷ. After six months had passed this way, he told periya perumāḷ “Oh śrīranganātā! Would you not show me the mark of having eaten this prasādam? “ kidāmbiyāccān (one of rāmānujar’s disciples and the one who used to cook food for him) was learning tiruvāimoḻi from kūrattāḻvān. One day he came late to rāmānujar; following is their conversation: “Why are you late?” “I had gone to learn tiruvāimoḻi from kūrattāḻvān” “What did he teach today?” “He taught tiruvāimoḻi 5.10 decad piṟandavārum” “Could you say a few words from that?” “There is no word to recount” “What did you listen to? What did he say?” “He started to teach. He recited the pāsuram. Then reminiscing the devotional trance of āḻvār, he started weeping for a long time. Then he told us, ‘What an involvement this āḻvār has! Could I, having no involvement with bhagavat viśayam, explain about āḻvār’s involvement?’ With that he stopped” “What a supreme entity is āḻvān!” The vyākyātā now returns to avatārikai – the śrīvaiṣṇavas, who were with āḻvār told him “You are now in your conscious state, without swooning. You should tell us your philosophy”. “In that case, listen to this”, said āḻvār, mercifully explaining his philosophy. Just as mitrabhāvēna is śrī rāma’s philosophy, this pāsuram is āḻvār’s philosophy \[mitrabhāvēna refers to śrī rāma telling sugrīva and others that he would accept anyone who comes to him with a friendly appearance, even if that person has a defect\]. Let us go through the pāsruam and its meanings:*

**īnaccollāyinumāga** – When some people are willing to listen with interest, I will not refrain from saying it. When sarvēśvaran initially saw the sorrow on the face of arjuna, he gave him all the secretive meanings and later grieved over it saying that he had brought out into the courtyard explicitly, what had been kept hidden in a corner. āḻvār shares his misgivings at the forefront itself. *īnaccol* is a word which is not doing any good; in other words, which is not beneficial. It is against the true nature of a *cētana* (sentient entity). It is saying something which he could not do himself. Since he has entrusted all his activities with the omnipotent sarvēśvaran, this is not an impossible activity to engage with. This position is congruent with his nature of being under the control of sarvēśvaran. If one were to hold on to a supreme entity, then there is no possibility for deficiency in knowledge or in strength.

**āyinumāga** – even if what he says does not have a benefit, instead of calling it as a good word in a humble way, since he says *āyinumāga* \[let it be so\], he feels that just as there were a few people who were there to listen to what sarvēśvaran said mercifully \[śrī bhagavat gītā\], there will be a few people to listen to what he has to say. Whether they accept or reject what he has to say, he is not going to step back from saying it. Even if what he says may not be correct and he is desirous of speaking about sarvēśvaran even if he feels that he is in a lowly state, he is not going to step back from speaking it.

Some people who listened to āḻvār saying this, ask him “There is a saying in the world that the money invested in flowers will not yield results. In a similar way, why do you say that what you have to say does not have benefit?” āḻvār responds . . .

**eṟi tirai vaiyam muṟṟum ēnatturuvāy idanda pirān** – it is apt for all the cētanas to hold on to sarvēśvaran alone. But why is this so? During the time of danger \[deluge\] when they are unable to look after themselves, should they not hold on to someone for their protection? This is called as *hētu garbha pratijñai*. This is a vow taken by sarvēśvaran that during times of danger, he would protect them since he is the causative factor for them. How is he the cause?

**eṟi tirai vaiyam** – when pralayam \[deluge\] comes to submerge the earth surrounded by ocean, he transforms himself \[even as a wild boar\] for the sake of others. For protecting others, he transforms himself.

**idanda** – this implies that he is omnipotent.

**pirān** – apart from omnipotency, it is he who has the control to protect them; he is the lord. He has the knowledge for protecting them; he has the strength and control to match that knowledge. The cētana, with his ignorance and lack of strength, has to hold on to sarvēśvaran only. Only the one who owns will take the efforts to attain the owned \[wealth\]. The owned cannot take any effort. Thus, only he is necessary for all. Why should those, who do not think of asking for him but asking for wealth, hold on to him?

**irum kaṟpagam . . .** – for those who carry out good acts here, reach *svargam* (heaven), stand under the *kaṟpagam* tree and seek benefits, only he is required to grant all those. When the cētana carries out good activities, as a result of which he reaches svargam, why is sarvēśvaran required? When the cētana carries out good deeds, their benefits are not given immediately. Those benefits should not get destroyed when the cētana leaves this place. An īśvaran is needed to estimate the deeds carried out by the cētana as well as the cētana himself and provide him the benefits. There are some people who say that due to the effect of *kriyāśakti* and due to *apūrvam* (these are concepts floated by some philosophies but which are not accepted by vēdams). Instead of trying to create new concepts, it would be better to accept sarvēśvaran as the provider of these benefits.

**maṟṟeḷḷāyavarkkum** – apart from those who experience benefits which keep reducing over time, sarvēśvaran is required for nityasūris too who experience permanent benefits which do not reduce over time. Even though they are nityasūris, their existence is essential to enjoy sarvēśvaran.

**ellāyavarkkum** – without knowing as to when this physical form will fall (viz when will death occur), manuśyas (human beings) keep saying that “I am fat” “I am slim” etc. Even for their pleasures and to bestow those pleasures, sarvēśvaran is required. Since those with knowledge and without knowledge are under his control equally, the knowledge which cētana has is considered as *asat* (non-existent) only. Some people claim that only through knowledge *mōkśam* (liberation) can be attained. When such is the case, śouldn’t cētana need knowledge to attain *mōkśam*? It is neither that knowledge is essential nor knowledge is not essential.  The only knowledge to be sought from sarvēśvaran is that which will enable the cētana not to avoid sarvēśvaran when he comes to help. The knowledge needed for providing the benefit is already with sarvēśvaran.

**jñānappirānai** – the only knowledge that cētana has is the unfavourable knowledge that he could protect himself. The knowledge for doing what is to be done to protect others is available with sarvēśvaran.

**allāl illai** – even those who wrote false śāstrams saying that sarvēśvaran does not exist, in their last days, realised that their śāstrams were swept aside, saying that *sahaśruti* pushes out their philosophy. *sahaśruti* is described by taittirīya upaniśat ānandavalli “*sōśnutē sarvān kāmān saha brahmaṇā*” – after attaining *mōkśam*, the muktātmā (the cētana who got liberated) enjoys with brahmam. The atheists too wonder towards the end of their lives whether they had taken the wrong path. Thus, other than sarvēśvaran, neither the cētana himself nor other means would be the means to attain sarvēśvaran.

Once tirukkōttiyūr nambi had come to śrīrangam to witness a temple festival. He wanted to instruct rāmānujar the supreme philosophy that apart from sarvēśvaran, there is no other means and he wanted to instruct that confidentially. Thus, he took rāmānujar to a secluded spot inside the temple. However, he saw a person, who was engaged in cleaning the temple, sleeping soundly there. He instructed rāmānujar the philosophy on another occasion when there was none nearby to hear it. As soon as he heard this supreme philosophy, rāmānuja hurried, in hot weather, to kūrattāḻvān’s tirumāḷigai (divine residence), where kūrattāḻvān was having his lunch.  He told kūrattāḻvān “nambi had told me not to reveal this to anyone else; however I cannot refrain from instructing you this” and shared the philosophy of *sahakāri nairapēkśyam* (sarvēśvaran not expecting any assistance in granting liberation to a cētana). Now, only the nature of cētana who experiences the benefit and the means which he had adopted, remain as assists. āḻvār now removes this too …

**nān kaṇḍa** – since knowledge is composed of both virtues and vices, when virtues surface, the knowledge gets manifested clearly and when vices surface, it gets bewildered. However, this knowledge that I received as bhagavat prasādam (blessed by sarvēśvaran) is not meant to bewilder the others through any deceitful acts. I did not reveal it in order to tell others. I revealed it as an outlet for my enjoyment.

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan

## 100. Nallar navil

*nallār navil kurugūr nagarān tirumāl tiruppēr vallār adikkaṇṇi sūdiya māṟan viṇṇapm seyda sollār todaiyal innūṟum vallār aḻundār piṟappām pollā aruvinai māyavan sēṟṟaḷḷal poynnilattē*

**Пословный перевод:**

nallār – distinguished people\\; navil – speaking (permanently repeating what āḻvār said); kurugūr – known as tirukkurugūr; nagarān – lord of tirukkurugūr; tirumāl – śriya:pati (consort of śrī mahālakśmi), his; tiruppēr – divine names; vallār – śrīvaiṣṇavas who are qualified to recite, their; adi – divine feet; kaṇṇi – garland; sūdiya – one who donned on his head; māṟan – nammāḻvār; viṇṇappam seyda – mercifully sung; sol – through divine words; Ar – complete; todaiyal – garland; innūṟum – these hundred pāsurams; vallār – those who can recite; piṟappām – known as birth; pollā – cruel (capable of destroying ātmā); aru – difficult (to cross over); vinai – being the cause for good and bad deeds; māyam – being deceitful (by creating taste); van – being strong; aḷḷal sēṟu – pressing like a quagmire; poy – being temporary; nilattil – in this prakruti (primordial matter); aḻundār – will not be caught

**Перевод:**

**āḻvār is the lord of tirukkurugūr where distinguished people keep reciting his divine words. He dons on his head the divine feet of śrīvaiṣṇavas who keep reciting the divine names of śrīman nārāyaṇan, the divine consort of śrī mahālakśmi. Those who learn well the hundred pāsurams which are like a garland composed of fully complete words and which are divinely composed by such nammāḻvār, will not get caught in this samsāram which perpetuates birth in it, which destroys the true nature of ātmā and which presses like a quagmire.**

**Комментарий. Автор Periyavāccān Piḷḷai:**

*(Introduction) āḻvār was asked “You have the knowledge to obtain the mercy of sarvēśvaran and the position to clearly state that sarvēśvaran is the means to attain him, as a result of which you have been able to get rid of samsāram which is a hurdle in attaining sarvēśvaran. However, what can samsāris, who do not have your knowledge and who are not fortunate to attain sarvēśvaran, do?” āḻvār responds saying “They may not have knowledge to the extent that I have; it is enough if they recite these pāsurams; they will also attain the same benefit which I attained” Let us go through the pāsuram and its meanings:*

**nallār navil** – all the people in the world keep chanting “āḻvār, āḻvār” just like the people in ayōdyā kept reciting “rāma rāma” as mentioned in śrī rāmāyaṇam yudda kāṇḍam 128-103 “rāmō rāmō rāma:” (while śrī rāma ruled the kingdom, the people kept reciting rāma, rāma, rāma). śrī rāmāyaṇam bāla kāṇḍam 1-16 says “*sarvadā abhigata: sadbi:*” (always accessible to purely good people) – just as ocean is attained by rivers, śrī rāma is attained by sages. When śrī rāma sits in a place to take rest after completing his chores, sages, who keep thinking of others’ good, would reach him. Their purpose is not to remove any shortcoming in śrī rāma – do rivers reach ocean in order to fill it up, which has been filled up already? No, they need to sustain themselves – in the same way, these sages attain śrī rāma in order to get rid of their shortcomings.

**tirumāl . . .** – while the people surround him, chanting his name, āḻvār keeps praising the followers of sarvēśvaran just as he had said in tiruvāimoḻi 3-7-1 “*payilum tiruvudaiyār yavarēlum avar kaṇḍīr emmai āḷum paramar*” (those who carry out kainkaryam permanently are my lords)

āḻvār has as his identity the garland, also known as divine feet of śrīvaiṣṇavas, who have the qualification to recite the divine names of śrīya:pati (consort of śrī mahālakśmi).

**viṇṇappam seyda** – in the first pāsuram of this prabandam āḻvār had said “*adiyēn seyyum viṇṇappamē*” (this is my appeal) and completed the prabandam with *viṇṇappam* (the prabandam composed by him).

**sollār todaiyal** – this \[prabandam\] need not be cherished just because it has been composed by a well-wisher. This should be cherished for the sweetness observed in it, just as it has been said of śrī rāmāyaṇam in bāla kāṇḍam 4-8 “*pātyē gēyē ca maturam*” (it is melodious to read and sing).

**innūṟum** – unlike mahābhāratam which is very expansive or praṇavam which is very brief, this prabandam has only hundred pāsurams, indicating knowledge.

**vallār aḻundār** – first he speaks about the benefit; in what will they not get trapped?

**piṟappām pollā** – the cruel cycle of births. The *ātmavastu* (sentient being) is full of knowledge and full of joy. He is sarvēśvaran’s servitor. Such ātma comes to samsāram to be in association with acit vastu (insentient entities) which is incongruent with it.

**aruvinai** – karma (effect of past deeds, both good and bad) which follows the ātmā, grows continuously and locks up the ātmā in samsāram.

**māya** – creating taste and scent in samsāram

**van sēṟu** – if one falls into this quagmire, the stain made cannot be removed easily.

**aḷḷal** – if one sets foot on it, it will pull down the person further

**poynnilam** – this samsāram does not have a floor. If one stands atop it, the legs will keep exploring below further, searching for firm footing. Those who recite this prabandam will not get caught in such samsāram.

This brings us to the end of this prabandam.

periya pirātti samēda periya perumāḷ tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam

nammāḻvār tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam

emperumānār tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam

periyavāccān piḷḷai tiruvadigaḷē śaraṇam

jīyar tiruvadigaḷē saraṇam

adiyēn kriṣṇa rāmānuja dāsan
